Null links remain null; incomplete source lookup and missing or ambiguous destination identities never fall back to the channel's unrelated `tvg_id`, preserve existing assignments, and increment the named unresolved-link warning for both existing and newly created channels. Lookup failures disclose only their error category, not exception payloads.

- **Raw stream probe and preview failures now explain ECM's network boundary without exposing provider URLs (GitHub #806, bead enhancedchannelmanager-po29f, build 0086).** Raw stream probes and browser previews connect from the ECM container directly to the provider; they do not traverse Dispatcharr's channel proxy or inherit its VPN namespace. Connection failures now give value-free guidance to place ECM on the same VPN/routed network, while channel previews remain explicitly identified as Dispatcharr-proxied. FFprobe diagnostics redact the complete stream URL before logging or persistence, and non-network media failures retain their generic behavior.

- **Pipeline and manual channel-name sorting now preserve numeric brand prefixes (GitHub #838, bead enhancedchannelmanager-g4xhp, build 0085).** With “ignore channel numbers in names” enabled, only explicit generated-number separators (`|`, `-`, or `.`) are stripped; a bare space no longer turns names such as `360 Tunebox` into `Tunebox`. Numeric names with and without spaces now follow the same deterministic natural ordering, including multi-digit values, while descending order, country-prefix handling, case-insensitivity, and stable equal-key ordering retain their existing contracts.

- **Pipeline no-sort selections now survive save/reopen and reruns preserve manual channel numbers (GitHub #833, bead enhancedchannelmanager-pjj97, build 0084).** Editing an existing rule now treats its persisted null stream-sort value as the explicit “No sorting” selection instead of substituting the Smart Sort default; every supported stream-sort mode is pinned through UI and API round trips. Orphan cleanup now closes numbering gaps only when the rule explicitly enables Channel Sort, while fixed/range create numbering still applies to genuinely new channels and existing merge behavior is unchanged.

- **LAN-friendly outbound policy now accepts RFC 6598 shared-address peers (GitHub #836, bead enhancedchannelmanager-z0cqm, build 0083).** Literal IPs and hostnames resolving solely into `100.64.0.0/10`, including the reported `100.80.3.24` cross-instance Sync destination, follow the same explicit mode toggle as RFC1918 and loopback: allowed in LAN-friendly and denied in Public internet only. Redirects are revalidated under that same rule. The carve-out is limited to RFC 6598; mixed DNS answers still reject the whole request, and link-local/metadata, multicast, unspecified, IPv6 ULA, and every other non-global special-use range remain blocked.

- **Frontend asynchronous tests now synchronize on settled operator-visible state (bead enhancedchannelmanager-7zp6g, build 0082).** The Sticky Section Navigation re-arm test waits for the newly requested section to become current before checking its queued scroll, and the outbound-policy default test waits for the loaded radio selection rather than merely observing that its settings request started. This removes two coverage-order races without weakening their behavioral assertions.

- **The final four tab-owned modal callers now complete the 75-caller accessibility inventory (bead enhancedchannelmanager-hr4ft.5, build 0081).** The standard EPG source editor, Logo delete confirmation, Settings probe-results dialog, and Settings reordered-channels dialog now own unique names resolved from visible headings, dialog/modal semantics, and the shared topmost focus lifecycle. EPG save and Logo delete states block every direct dismissal while work is pending; the probe-results re-run intentionally closes its results dialog before starting the background operation. `ModalOverlay` remains neutral. The production-build modal catalog now renders every catalogued state and verifies that each ModalOverlay has exactly one named modal semantic owner, except explicitly enumerated nested stacks which require exactly two.

- **Settings-family dialogs now have caller-owned names, focus lifecycle, and busy-safe dismissal (bead enhancedchannelmanager-hr4ft.6, build 0080).** The Cloud Target editor and delete confirmation, Linked Accounts credential dialog, four Normalization Engine editors/import/apply surfaces, and two Tag Engine dialogs now expose unique names resolved from visible headings and opt into the shared topmost focus containment/restoration helper. The existing descendant-owned Normalization apply confirmation remains the sole nested semantic surface. Async create, link, import, apply, save, test, and delete states prevent Escape, header Close, and footer Cancel from dismissing work in progress; `ModalOverlay` remains neutral. The exact 75-caller manifest changes only these nine settings-family entries from recorded debt to managed semantics.

- **Stacked and stateful modal families now preserve accessible identity and focus order (bead enhancedchannelmanager-hr4ft.2, build 0079).** The Streams bulk-create conflict, Task Editor with both nested schedule editors, and User Menu profile/password states now expose caller-owned named dialog semantics with unique heading references and managed focus. Nested schedule Escape closes only the top dialog and restores its opener inside the parent before a later Escape reaches the parent; topmost Tab containment follows the shared lifecycle. Existing save/create busy states suppress Escape dismissal. User Menu transitions stage focus on the persistent menu trigger so closing either state restores a connected opener. `ModalOverlay` remains neutral, and only these six recorded manifest entries move from debt to managed semantics.

- **Standalone modal dialogs now expose complete caller-owned semantics and focus lifecycle (bead enhancedchannelmanager-hr4ft.1, build 0078).** The 31 recorded standalone callers from Auto-Sync Settings through Stream Profiles, including the VLC protocol helper, now own `role="dialog"`, modal state, unique names resolved from their visible headings, initial in-dialog focus, topmost Tab containment, and opener restoration. Escape and busy-state policy remain caller-controlled, and the shared `ModalOverlay` stays neutral. The closed 75-caller manifest changes only this bounded family from recorded debt to managed semantics; component tests pin accessible-name resolution, Escape behavior, focus containment, and duplicate-instance ID uniqueness.

- **Eleven existing semantic dialogs now expose unique caller-owned accessible names (bead enhancedchannelmanager-hr4ft.4, build 0077).** The three Dummy EPG manager dialogs, five Channel Pipeline dialogs, Event Sync auto-sync confirmation, Event Sync rule discard confirmation, and Rule Builder discard confirmation now bind their visible headings through unique `aria-labelledby` IDs. Overlay-owned dialogs keep their existing role and modal state on `ModalOverlay`; descendant-owned alert dialogs keep the shared overlay neutral, including the nested Rule Builder confirmation. Component tests pin names, duplicate-instance ID uniqueness, nested exactly-two-dialog semantics, and existing busy/Escape behavior. The closed 75-caller manifest changes only these eleven naming states; the remaining recorded accessibility debt is unchanged.

- **Modal accessibility debt now has a closed 75-caller ownership and focus-policy manifest (bead enhancedchannelmanager-hr4ft.3, build 0076).** The former list of only 50 missing callers is replaced by an exact bidirectional inventory of every production `ModalOverlay` use, including current overlay/descendant ownership, independently measured role, modal and naming status, lexical nesting, family, and focus policy. Recorded missing or partial semantics remain explicit debt for the bounded hr4ft child beads, not accessibility exceptions. The AST audit rejects new or stale callers, ownership drift, partial or nested semantic surfaces, empty names, and role/modal/name regressions while keeping the shared overlay neutral. A new caller-opt-in focus lifecycle helper supplies eligible initial focus, a programmatically focusable container fallback when every control is unavailable, topmost Tab/Shift+Tab containment and recapture, and stack-safe opener restoration without taking over Escape policy. `TypeToConfirmDialog`, already a named semantic dialog, is the bounded integration pilot; its busy callback still suppresses Escape, its all-disabled state focuses the dialog container, and nested dialogs restore focus in stack order.

- **Modal responsiveness now follows three reviewed semantic tiers at accessible browser zoom (beads enhancedchannelmanager-79uf8 + enhancedchannelmanager-hw8e3, build 0075).** A 1280px desktop at 200% zoom has a 640 CSS-pixel viewport, so the former 600px rules did not run at the required zoom point. The common zoom/reflow tier now begins at 700px with 8px overlay inset, a 95vh height cap, 8px 16px header padding, 16px body padding, 14px 16px wrapping footer padding, and single-column shared form rows. The 820px complex-layout collapse and 500px compact-content behavior remain separate. VLC, stream-preview, Rule Builder footer/date fields, and Dummy EPG picker content reflow at the zoom tier. M3U filter rows retain exposed, visibly labelled Action and Order values instead of hiding them, while their desktop column headers remain the sole visible labels above the tier. Bulk LCN truncation is responsive only at the compact tier, and Logo's compact rule now has sufficient ownership to render. A caller-derived, bidirectional census follows every direct stylesheet import from every `ModalOverlay` caller, including host-page styles, and records every media block. An exact-build, animation-frozen browser harness pins 640x360 catalog rendering, viewport containment, chrome values, accessibility labels, and boundary behavior.

- **Filter-select chrome is privately owned by each route instead of leaking across eager and lazy CSS chunks (bead enhancedchannelmanager-8epq2, build 0074).** StreamsPane's two single-select alternatives, Journal's three frozen-header filters, and M3U Changes' four frozen-header filters now use distinct owner classes that remain valid after `RouteHeaderSlot` portals the latter controls. The previously effective box, trigger, hover, focus/open, and responsive declarations are preserved under those owners, while the shared `filter-select` token and its three cross-chunk debt entries are gone. A token-aware production census pins zero legacy uses and exact 2/3/4 source ownership. The production route deliberately renders StreamsPane's multi-select branch, so a production-build Playwright guard asserts those two alternatives remain absent there and compares the rendered Journal/M3U controls across forward, reverse, and revisit session orders at 1280x720 and 1920x1080, including wrapper/trigger geometry, chrome, focus/open state, frozen-header height, clipping, and overflow. Responsive behavior is exercised at 768px and 600px. The guard stubs route data only to render deterministic controls; it separately requires both exact-build and checkout-server modes and verifies the fingerprinted CSS asset actually served. Reviewers run the canonical `npm run test:css-guard:filter-select`; the guard remains proportional/manual rather than extending the required Frontend Tests browser step.

- **The final 101 unscaled modal text elements now use semantic type-scale owners (bead enhancedchannelmanager-8rszp, build 0073).** The live 82-dialog catalog was remeasured before editing and still contained the recorded 42 spans, 17 strong elements, 16 paragraphs, 11 summaries, 5 labels, 5 list items, 3 definition terms, and 2 legends at the 16px root default. Each residual is now assigned by meaning: running copy inherits the body token, form and disclosure labels use the label token, review definition titles use the item-title token, and inline emphasis inherits from its corrected container. A reviewed 101-element decision ledger binds every exact measured row and per-owner count to its chosen token; no modal-body-wide default was introduced. The required Frontend Tests context now renders all 82 catalog entries in Chromium at 1280x720, requires every non-gap entry to report a successful harness render with visible production DOM, and rejects a live governed bare-tag residual at 16px, so a production addition or render crash cannot hide behind the historical ledger. The Stream Create empty state's remaining `0.8rem` literal also joins the body scale. Animation-frozen harness captures at 1280x720 and 1920x1080 reduce this exact bare-tag population from 101 to zero without horizontal overflow; the committed historical baseline was not recaptured.

- **Destructive DBAS restore dialogs now expose stable, caller-owned accessible names (bead enhancedchannelmanager-czmph, build 0072).** The uploaded-backup restore, saved-backup restore, and their reusable type-to-confirm gate now carry `role="dialog"`, `aria-modal="true"`, and unique `aria-labelledby` title IDs without changing the neutral `ModalOverlay` primitive or its existing focus behavior. Nested confirmation dialogs retain distinct names and IDs, and the confirmation input still receives focus while Escape remains disabled during a busy operation. A repository AST census accounts for all 75 `ModalOverlay` call sites and explicitly records the 50 remaining semantics omissions as debt owned by enhancedchannelmanager-hr4ft; a recorded omission is not an accessibility exception.

- **The final two modal close-button exceptions now use the shared 32px chrome (bead enhancedchannelmanager-0f2ln, build 0071).** Pending Merges' bulk confirmation and Tag Engine's create-group dialog spelled the control `.modal-close`, bypassing ModalBase's padding, centering, radius, colour, transition, and hover treatment while local CSS compensated only for the glyph size. Both now use `.modal-close-btn`, and the four obsolete private rules are gone. The shared E2E close selector follows the canonical class, while an AST-backed source census rejects statically authored legacy tokens across JSX expression shapes. Component tests retain close, focus-trap, Escape, and focus-restoration behavior. Exact-build browser checks compare both dialogs with a canonical control in normal and hover states at 1280x720 and 1920x1080; both targets are 32x32 with an 18px glyph and no horizontal overflow.

- **Visiting Channel Pipeline no longer restyles textareas in every later modal (bead enhancedchannelmanager-dlcl2, build 0070).** Its lazy stylesheet declared import/export textarea and focus rules beneath the shared `.modal-body` class without a route ancestor, so loading that chunk permanently changed unrelated dialogs for the rest of the browser session. `ModalOverlay` renders in place rather than through a portal, which means both intended YAML textareas remain descendants of `.channel-pipeline-tab`; both rules are now scoped to that root with unchanged declarations. A CSS-structure ratchet pins both selectors, component coverage proves both dialog textareas remain under the root, and a browser regression compares a foreign modal textarea before and after loading the Channel Pipeline chunk.

- **Capped GitHub file lists now fail safe instead of hiding unexamined changes (bead enhancedchannelmanager-cyd0h, build 0069).** The shared changed-path action transports an explicit `{complete, paths}` envelope into the classifier. GitHub documents that compare responses expose at most 300 changed files and pull-request file responses at most 3,000, so an exact-cap response is incomplete: both `code_paths_changed` and `docs_site_affected` become `true`, running every code gate and rebuilding the documentation site. Responses below the boundary retain precise path verdicts, including both sides of renames. Missing, malformed, failed, or incomplete acquisition also fails safe without printing path values. The action retains paginated, slurped JSON transport; deterministic fixtures pin compare 299/300 and paginated pull-request 2,999/3,000 boundaries.

- **Changed-path acquisition has one structured workflow authority (bead enhancedchannelmanager-ifx16, build 0068).** Every `.yml` and `.yaml` workflow that classifies changed paths now invokes the same repository composite action. That action alone owns the pull-request and compare API pagination, lossless JSON transport, rename-source extraction, and classifier call, so adding another workflow cannot silently copy an incomplete filename-only implementation. YAML-structure tests discover the action across every job regardless of job name, require exactly one invocation per registered consumer workflow, and constrain its job to checkout plus the stable action with both outputs bound to that step; a fourth consumer requires an explicit manifest update and review. No additional or shell `run` step is permitted in that authority job. A closed-world reserved-identifier policy rejects the classifier name or path in every workflow `run` scalar, even in shell comments or echoed prose, and rejects noncanonical action references. Every occurrence of either verdict name in parsed workflow keys or scalar values must match an explicit location-and-value manifest covering the authority outputs and the current downstream gates. Every job-output mapping, every job/step `if`, and every job/step `continue-on-error` value across the entire workflow directory is exact-snapshotted by structural location and value, including unrelated cache, release-cut, digest and flake controls. There is no lexical expression filter: fully constructed bracket/format output access and dynamic failure suppression are controls and require deliberate manifest review. Reusable workflows may not export either reserved verdict, and reusable-workflow jobs may not feed those verdicts through `needs`; unrelated reusable outputs remain valid. The composite action separately pins both public output mappings and an exact one-step structure with the stable classifier id, fixed environment, reserved-name occurrences, and one canonical `$GITHUB_OUTPUT` writer across all action run scalars; swapped outputs, added steps, split-key fabrication, or later writes fail. This deliberately accepts review friction to avoid interpreting shell semantics or building a recursive workflow-call graph; real YAML comments remain ignored. The shared action's endpoint-specific jq behavior is validated once. The local classifier command uses the explicit NUL input contract, and the test-dependency fast-fail distinguishes required Python packages from the required `jq` OS executable with separate installation guidance.

### Security
- **Fresh installs now engage authentication as part of creating the first admin (bead enhancedchannelmanager-qg14z, build 0066).** `POST /api/auth/setup` previously committed the user and identity rows without persisting `setup_complete=true`. Because the global middleware enforces only when both `require_auth` and `setup_complete` are true, every admin-gated route remained anonymous until a later status request happened to repair the flag, including the backup download that contains the application database. Setup now writes the gate atomically before it can return success. The HTTP and TLS request-serving processes detect the shared file's changed inode and reload it instead of retaining a process-local first-run answer. A cold process that finds the file missing, unreadable, or invalid permits setup only after proving the user database is empty; durable ownership or an unverifiable database fails closed without creating or overwriting the settings path. The missing-file proof, absence recheck, and initial write share the same host-wide settings lock as every later settings write, so a stale process cannot overwrite a peer's completed setup with open defaults. A database failure restores the prior flag only after an independent query proves that no user became durable; an ambiguous commit remains fail-closed and recoverable. A separate host-wide non-blocking lock prevents two processes from racing first-admin transactions. The frontend re-reads auth status when the setup wizard completes, without relying on a page reload, and routes the new operator to login because setup intentionally creates no session. ASGI integration tests perform setup against isolated database and settings files, then immediately prove anonymous settings and backup downloads are refused; retryable failure paths remain recoverable, while an operator's explicit `require_auth=false` choice remains intentionally ungated.
- **The PII ratchet now also blocks ECM-shaped credentials, backed by a pinned general secret scanner (bead enhancedchannelmanager-h9av3, build 0064).** GitHub's free public-repository secret scanning covers partner/provider patterns but cannot enable its paid non-provider layer, so ECM's own credential formats previously had no preventive control. The existing added-lines guard now recognizes three deliberately precise classes: real-shaped values assigned to ECM's named credential fields (including the MCP `api_key` query form), complete Discord webhook URLs on every host accepted by Settings (`discord.com`, legacy `discordapp.com`, canary, and PTB), and exact-shape Telegram bot tokens. Names, declarations, documentation placeholders, low-entropy identifiers, and generic high-entropy strings do not fire this arm; precision is load-bearing because a noisy guard would be disabled and nothing GitHub-side replaces it. `detect-secrets==1.5.0` supplies the complementary generic/provider layer over changed files with network verification disabled. Its plugin/filter policy and suppression baseline are derived by freshly scanning the merge-base tree, so a PR cannot authorize its own secret, remove a detector, or weaken a threshold; the first-baseline bootstrap is accepted only when its hash-only baseline exactly matches that fresh base scan. Both layers report location and type only, never a value or length oracle; the ECM arm inherits added-lines behavior and fail-closed handling for invalid UTF-8/NUL content. Neither working-tree layer claims to scan history. The completed offline history sweep found two past MCP keys, confirmed both already dead by offline hash comparison without authenticating, and the PO ruled against rewriting history.
- **CI now rejects personal identifiers introduced on added documentation or bead-export lines without republishing them in public logs (bead enhancedchannelmanager-il1xz, build 0063).** The guard covers the identifier classes observed in the repository's PII sweep: personal email domains, real-user home paths, private-share hosts, and a hash-only deny list for known account terms. Hash creation and scanning share one account-token grammar, including hyphen, underscore, and dot separators, without matching inside an alphanumeric component. It deliberately ratchets only added lines, so accepted historical debt and untouched lines do not block unrelated work, while moved or rewritten text is checked again. Reports contain only path, line, rule, and the constant `REDACTED`; they disclose no value fragment or exact length. In-scope invalid UTF-8, NUL/binary, or unreadable files fail closed with path and `unscannable` only. Documentation examples, service identities, placeholder paths, and bead identity fields are precisely allowlisted; source trees remain out of scope because their synthetic-fixture density would make this control noisy. The existing history is not rewritten.
- **`POST /api/backup/restore-initial` no longer lets an anonymous caller take over an instance that already has an admin account (bead enhancedchannelmanager-lf29s, build 0054).** The endpoint restores a legacy ZIP during first-run setup, which means it rewrites `/config/journal.db` wholesale, and `journal.db` holds the `users` table. It sat in `main.py`'s global auth-middleware exemption list with no auth dependency of any kind, and its only guard was `settings.is_configured()`, which reports whether a Dispatcharr URL and credentials are present. That is a Dispatcharr-configuration check standing in for an authentication check, and the two conditions are unrelated. Any instance whose Dispatcharr connection was not yet configured therefore accepted an unauthenticated ZIP upload that replaced every admin password hash and cleared the `tls`, `uploads/logos` and `m3u_uploads` directories, after which the attacker logged in with credentials of their own choosing. The exposed states are a fresh container and, more importantly, a disaster-recovery rebuild sitting empty waiting for its restore, a state this project enters deliberately during backup and restore drills. Reproduced end to end on a disposable instance before the fix: with authentication enabled and enforcing (anonymous `GET /api/settings`, `GET /api/backup/create` and `POST /api/backup/restore` all answering 401), an anonymous `POST /api/backup/restore-initial` returned 200, the real operator's login then failed with "Invalid username or password", and the uploaded account logged in as admin. The endpoint now refuses anyone but an authenticated human admin once the instance holds an operator identity, and the check keys on whether a user row exists rather than on the `setup_complete` configuration flag, so damaged or interrupted configuration persistence cannot reopen the route. A genuinely empty instance still restores without credentials, because serving that case is the endpoint's reason to exist. The static MCP service key is rejected as well, matching `POST /api/backup/restore`. The path is also removed from the middleware exemption list, which costs no first-run reachability (the middleware only enforces once `setup_complete` is true, and the handler already refuses an anonymous caller in that state) and turns the anonymous upload away before FastAPI spools an attacker-controlled ZIP to disk. The frontend needed one change to match: `ProtectedRoute` re-reads `GET /api/auth/status` when the setup wizard reports completion, instead of deciding what to render from the status it cached at mount. `POST /api/auth/setup` issues no session cookie, so the status cached before setup still says setup is incomplete and would render the app with no session. The operator now lands on the login page immediately after creating the admin account, and the restore they run after signing in carries their session. Both the failure and the fix were reproduced in a browser on a disposable instance with no reload between finishing setup and clicking restore.
- **Renaming a code file into a `.md` path no longer hides it from the CI changed-path classifier, so a pull request that deletes code can no longer classify documentation-only and pass every required check having run nothing (bead enhancedchannelmanager-9ogyd, build 0061).** The `detect` job in `test.yml`, `build.yml` and `docs-pages.yml` built its changed-file set with the jq expression `.[].filename`, and `filename` on the GitHub pull-request and compare APIs is the rename DESTINATION only. The source path lives in `previous_filename`, which the expression discarded, exactly as `git diff --name-only` with default rename detection prints only the destination. Verified on this repository's own commit `b47ced96`: `git diff --name-only b47ced96^...b47ced96` lists 18 paths, the same command with `--no-renames` lists 19, and the extra path is the rename source `frontend/src/components/settings/SecuritySettingsSection.css`. Because six of the seven required status checks gate every step on that classifier's `docs_only` verdict, and `dev` branch protection requires no reviews, `git mv backend/tests/unit/test_auth_middleware.py docs/legacy_auth_test_notes.md` titled as a documentation change presented one Markdown path, classified documentation-only, and turned all seven required contexts green without executing a single test, lint or CodeQL query. The three `detect` jobs now use `.[] | .filename, (.previous_filename // empty)` so both sides of a rename reach the classifier. The same omission bit `docs-pages.yml` in the opposite direction: moving a page out of `docs/user_guide/` showed only the destination, so the published site did not rebuild and the removed page lingered. No job name, workflow trigger or required status check name changed, and the classifier script itself was not touched. `backend/tests/unit/test_classify_changed_paths.py` now runs each workflow's real jq expression over a recorded API payload from `b47ced96` and over the rename-into-Markdown reproduction, and fails if either side of a rename is dropped again.

### Added
- **Post-merge publish check, so a merge that never reached the registry stops being invisible (bead enhancedchannelmanager-t8fqg, build 0044).** The published `:dev` tag has silently lagged the `dev` branch four times, from four unrelated causes: a Buildx flake that skipped the multi-arch manifest, a GitHub Actions outage that orphaned queued runs, and two test flakes that correctly gated the publish. Every time the gate behaved correctly and nothing surfaced the drift; on the most recent occasion (PR #793) `dev` carried the fix and the registry did not, for about five hours, until an unrelated merge republished it by accident. `scripts/check_publish.py` closes that gap. It confirms the **Build and Push Docker Image** run for a commit concluded `success`, and reads the published tag's `ECM_VERSION` build marker straight out of the registry's image config (no layer download) to compare it against `frontend/package.json` **at that commit**. `docs/shipping.md` section 6 now ends at a verified image rather than at the merge. It is deliberately **not** a CI gate: a check that runs after the merge cannot gate the merge it follows, and wiring it into the PR flow would add a permanently-failing context to every open PR. Run before a merge it prints a `PRE-MERGE RUN` banner explaining that the mismatch is expected. The restore drill gains a matching Step 0 image gate that reads the same marker with `docker rmi` / `docker pull` / `docker inspect`, for operators without repository access.
- **Em-dash lint ratchet over documentation prose (bead enhancedchannelmanager-3tflw, build 0044).** `docs/style_guide.md` bans the em-dash in prose and had no enforcement, so two documentation passes shipped 144 violations through two merged PRs without three reviewers noticing. `scripts/check_em_dashes.py` now runs as a step of the **Operator Docs** CI job, in both `test.yml` and `docs-only-pass.yml` (the latter is the copy that runs on a Markdown-only PR, which is exactly how the 144 arrived). It is a **ratchet, not a cliff**: it fails only on lines a PR adds, so pre-existing debt is tolerated and reported as a count rather than red-lining CI on day one. It honours the exemptions the rule itself grants: fenced code blocks, inline code spans, link destinations, and URLs in Markdown. Only U+2014 is flagged; en-dashes (`–`) and arrows (`→`) are not em-dashes and are never touched. A genuinely-quoted line can be suppressed with `em-dash-ok: <reason>`, mirroring the existing `fake-test-ok` convention. **Scope: documentation only.** The rule's text also names code comments, but the PO scoped automated enforcement down to Markdown before this shipped: scanning Python and TypeScript comments would have caught 9,211 and 1,551 violations respectively, against 1,271 across the 188-file Markdown scan surface, and the PO judged that surface disproportionate to the risk the guard exists to close. The code-comment clause remains a style-guide convention enforced by review, not by CI.
- **Test Connection now tells you when you're on a Dispatcharr version ECM hasn't been tested against, and every Dispatcharr endpoint ECM calls is now checked against a recorded copy of the real API (bead enhancedchannelmanager-ax0kf, [ADR-014](docs/adr/ADR-014-dispatcharr-api-drift-strategy.md), build 0022).** Three shipped bugs — a schema endpoint that returned YAML (`q6xjl` A), a settings detail route keyed by the wrong field so a restore 404'd on 7/7 keys (`q6xjl` B), and `/api/dvr/rules/`, a path that exists on no Dispatcharr version and silently emptied every DVR-rules backup (`lsa0s`) — all trace to one deferred "verify this later" comment in ECM's Dispatcharr client. Each was found by an operator, never by a test. Two things change. **(1) A contract sweep.** Every `(method, URL template)` the client can issue — 98 call sites over 59 distinct URL templates — is now derived from the client's own source with an `ast` walk and checked against a recorded manifest of the live Dispatcharr 0.28.2 OpenAPI document (all 224 paths, bodies stripped; `scripts/record_dispatcharr_openapi_manifest.py` records it, and never touches the network at test time, so CI stays hermetic). It asserts the path exists, the method is allowed, and the path parameters line up — **not** response shape or semantics, which remain the job of the existing deep recorded fixtures; a green sweep means "no endpoint is imaginary," not "the integration works." An unresolvable path expression, a new raw HTTP call site, or an extractor that stops finding calls all fail the test loudly rather than quietly reducing coverage. **(2) A version advisory.** The **Test Connection** button now also reads `GET /api/core/version/` after the credentials check out, and if the reported version is outside the set ECM's contract is recorded against (`0.28.x` today), it shows a warning notification naming the version. **It never blocks:** the connection still verifies, the settings still save, and a Dispatcharr too old to have the version endpoint — or any timeout or unreadable answer — produces silence rather than a nag you can't act on. Also documented: a re-record recipe in `docs/dispatcharr_api.md`, and an operational rule that a Dispatcharr **major**-version upgrade should be followed by a real backup→restore exercise before you trust it.
- **Restore runbook gets a "Recurring maintenance" section for the highest-care `dbas-backup` component (bead enhancedchannelmanager-nvhg7, build 0021).** `COMPONENTS.md` has tagged the backup/restore subsystem "restore must stay periodically exercised" since onboarding, but nothing prompted or enforced it — the only round-trip exercise this pipeline had ever received was a manual one-off. `docs/runbooks/disaster-recovery-restore.md` now documents a monthly dry-run-preview habit against the actual most-recent scheduled artifact: what to check in the report (category counts, FAILED rows, the settings-key `DEPENDENCY_UNRESOLVED` signal from build 0015, the degraded-backup warning from build 0019), and to file a bead rather than silently re-run on a surprise. Home-lab tier: documentation, not enforced infrastructure — a future CI-wired exercise is noted as a possible path (beads `1zwmr` / `zqtjj`) without implying it's committed. Cross-referenced from the operator guide's `verify-a-backup.md`.
- **Docs: 21 new operator-guide articles land, covering Channels & Streams, Normalization, Notifications, and Troubleshooting (bead enhancedchannelmanager-sbtyw, build 0012).** New articles: `channels-streams/{assign-streams-to-channels,channel-groups-and-tags,logos,streams-overview,the-journal}.md`; `normalization/{apply-to-existing-channels,author-your-first-rule,concepts,condition-and-action-types,rule-groups-and-ordering,when-things-look-wrong}.md`; `notifications/{alert-routing-patterns,discord-webhook-customization,email-recipients-deep-dive,telegram-bot-setup}.md`; and `troubleshooting/{common-issues,escalation-paths,gather-support-information,read-the-logs,recovery-patterns,ui-banners-and-warnings}.md`. Their 24 paired screenshots were individually PII-audited and cleared under bead enhancedchannelmanager-2mluw before landing here; no second audit was performed. `channels-streams/the-journal.md` and `troubleshooting/read-the-logs.md` unblock documentation defects DEF-017, DEF-018 and DEF-020, which could not be fixed while the articles they reference existed on no branch.
- **Startup now warns when your ECM data has nowhere durable to live (bead enhancedchannelmanager-ebl4n, build 0004).** Preflight has always reported `✓ Config directory exists` / `✓ Config directory is writable` — both true even when `/config` is just the container's own writable layer, with no bind mount and no named volume behind it. That is exactly the configuration the operator in the field case above was running, and it means every byte of ECM state (`settings.json`, channel data, `journal.db`, uploaded logos, TLS certificates) is destroyed the moment the container is removed or recreated — including by a Portainer/Unraid/Watchtower "update" and by the `--force-recreate` this release otherwise recommends. Preflight now detects whether `CONFIG_DIR` (or any parent of it) is actually mounted, by reading `/proc/self/mountinfo`, and prints a loud multi-line warning when it is not: what is at stake, what destroys it, how to add a mount, and to take a backup **before** recreating. The warning is advisory and never blocks startup — a first-run or throwaway container without a mount is legitimate — and a correctly-mounted container gets a single `✓ Config directory /config is on a mounted volume` line instead.
- **Cross-instance sync: opt-in logo replication completes the entity slice (bead enhancedchannelmanager-7ipq2.1, build 0001).** Channels + embedded streams already sync every cycle (kcxie); this adds the LOGO slice as a per-`SyncTarget` opt-in (`sync_logos`, default OFF — API/DB flag, migration 0040), implementing ADR-013 S9's exit path rather than forcing logos into the unconditional per-cycle set. When enabled, each cycle 3-tier-matches source logos (files under `uploads/logos`, correlated to the source Dispatcharr logo id/name by URL basename — the same enumeration the backup builder archives) against destination B and streams only the MISSES: the sync plan carries metadata-only logo records (never bytes), and the reused logos importer gains an optional lazy `content_provider` that reads one missed logo's file at a time immediately before validation + upload (D8 — a matched logo never reads its file; the archive-restore path is unchanged). The sync path can never bulk-delete B's logos: the destructive `clear_existing` pre-step is hard-disabled in the sync step, pinned by a stateful round-trip test that also proves convergence, second-run idempotency (zero re-uploads), and that a B-only logo survives. All four security validations (basename/traversal guard, declared+decoded 50MB caps, magic-byte MIME check) run before any upload, provider failures degrade to per-logo path-free VALIDATION_ERRORs, and the run's `sync_outbound` journal row now lists the categories the run actually processed.

### Changed
- **Channel numbers now have one written-down definition, and every place you can type one enforces it (bead `enhancedchannelmanager-ic884.1`, build 0109).** A channel number is a non-negative number with at most one decimal place, because that is Dispatcharr's resolution: only the tenths place carries meaning. Nothing in the stack checked that before. There is no ECM database column for `channel_number` at all (channels live in Dispatcharr, which declares the field a non-unique float and enforces neither uniqueness nor precision), the CSV importer accepted any positive float, and every UI field parsed with a bare `parseFloat`, while the channel list was already formatting numbers to one decimal place on the way out. A value like `1.05` could therefore be typed, imported, or posted, stored as-is, and then displayed as something it was not. The domain and its check now live in exactly one place per side, `backend/channel_number.py` and `frontend/src/utils/channelNumber.ts`, and every entry point consumes them: create channel, edit channel, the inline number editor in the channel list, merge, assign numbers, the edit-mode bulk commit (create, update and assign operations), CSV import and preview, and a Channel Pipeline rule's literal channel number. **Out-of-contract values are rejected, not rounded**, so a CSV import that used to succeed on a bad row will now fail on that row with the reason: surfacing bad data beats silently altering it. The message is the same sentence everywhere, browser or API: "Channel numbers must be 0 or greater and support one decimal place (for example 1.0 or 1.1)." Two long-standing quirks go with it: an unassigned channel's Edit Channel box showed the literal text `null`, and Merge Channels defaulted to `Infinity` when no source channel had a number. Uniqueness is deliberately NOT enforced, since Dispatcharr permits duplicate channel numbers and real lineups have them.
- **Changed-path gating now names and computes the safe decision positively (bead enhancedchannelmanager-gnz19, build 0067).** The shared classifier emits `code_paths_changed`, defaults it to `true` when input is absent, malformed or unreadable, and every workflow and local shipping hook consumes that exact key. The only false verdict is a change made entirely of approved root machine-generated beads state: `.beads/*.jsonl`, `.beads/*.jsonl.bak`, and the exact `.beads/metadata.json`. Every other path is conservatively code-gated, regardless of suffix, case, location, symlink type, or intended use. This intentionally removes documentation and directory-prefix optimizations: reliably proving an arbitrary file inert would require observing every present and future consumer, while treating it as code costs only CI time. Beads configuration, nested artifacts, documentation, images, Graphify output, workflow support files, and undeclared paths all run the gates. Changed paths travel as JSON arrays from GitHub APIs and as NUL-delimited data from local Git, preserving rename sources, destinations, deletions and unusual POSIX filenames without newline splitting, whitespace stripping, or backslash rewriting; malformed or noncanonical input runs all gates. The independent `docs_site_affected` verdict is unchanged: published pages and `mkdocs.yml` still rebuild the site.
- **Claude Code's machine-local permission settings are no longer tracked (bead enhancedchannelmanager-pp84m, build 0065).** `.claude/settings.local.json` is now root-ignored and removed from repository tracking; shared project settings remain in `.claude/settings.json`. This maintained checkout's local file was explicitly preserved byte-for-byte during the branch transition, but Git may delete the formerly tracked file when another existing clone pulls or switches to the updated branch. Contributors with local permission changes should back up the file before updating and recreate it afterward if Git removes it. Once updated, each checkout's local allow-list stays untracked instead of appearing in unrelated application pull requests.
- **M3U account rows and EPG source rows now keep their action buttons at full size and move the setup actions into a per-row kebab menu (bead enhancedchannelmanager-xh33o, build 0058).** Every row action is meant to be a 32x32 target, but the buttons sat in a flex row inside a fixed-width column and were allowed to shrink to fit, so a busy row squeezed them well under the 24x24 minimum that WCAG 2.5.8 asks for: an eight-button M3U account row rendered its buttons 19px wide, and a five-button EPG source row 20.81px. The buttons no longer shrink, and because a row that no longer shrinks would instead be clipped by the list's own `overflow: hidden`, each row now shows only the actions its column actually holds. **M3U account rows** keep Enable/Disable, Refresh, Edit and Delete, and move Refresh VOD, Manage Groups, Manage Account Profiles and Manage Filters into the row's new `⋮` menu. **EPG source rows** keep Enable/Disable, Refresh and Edit, and move Copy URL and Delete into theirs. Nothing is removed; the moved actions are one click further away and are named in full text rather than by icon alone. The EPG Actions column widens from 120px to 144px to hold the four remaining controls, which widens the minimum row width from 880px to 904px before the source list starts to scroll horizontally. Both conditional actions (Refresh VOD appears only for XtreamCodes accounts, Copy URL only when the source has a URL and URL-hiding is off) now live in the menu, so the visible button count no longer varies by row.
- **Checkbox rows are the same height as radio rows (bead enhancedchannelmanager-4nn2o, build 0058).** A checkbox option carried 8px of vertical padding that its radio equivalent did not, so mixed forms rendered checkbox rows at 35.5px against the radios' 24px and lists of checkboxes sat looser than lists of radios for no reason anyone had chosen. The padding is gone and both now render at 24px. The clickable target does not shrink with it: the 24px floor is supplied by the shared rule that makes the whole label a target, not by the padding, and it still measures 24px on both.
- **User guide reworked to read as current operator documentation rather than a build-status page (build 0053).** The landing page (`docs/user_guide/index.md`) now opens with what ECM is and who the guide is for, plus a "Start here" table and sections grouped by task. The Stub / In-progress coverage markers are gone: they were inaccurate, labelling well-covered sections (Normalization, Troubleshooting) as stubs and an in-progress section (Backup & Restore) as complete. Two pages that were internal engineering material rather than operator documentation are removed: the restore-drill procedure (`run-a-restore-drill.md`) and the retained legacy task reference (`legacy-task-reference.md`). Their still-relevant content moved to the sections that own it: VLC protocol-handler setup, password reset, self-deactivation protection, stats auto-refresh, channel-pipeline orphan cleanup and exclusion filters, and a new "Restoring onto an already-populated target" section covering the relink-mode reference. A new page, `channels-streams/stream-preview.md`, documents the stream/channel preview modal. The guide now describes current 0.18.1 behaviour rather than carrying per-build history.
- **Restore drill repinned to run 18 / `0.18.1-0052`, six defects (D1-D6) and three documentation gaps fixed, and four other user-guide pages corrected for the staged-group fixes below (build 0052).** `run-a-restore-drill.md` carried six stale or self-contradicting claims found in doc-following mode: a still-live security-relevant transient the article's own Known-failures table already said was fixed; a quoted Delete-Group dialog string ("Ungrouped") the product no longer shows; a stale empty-state string for the EPG guide picker; a version pin five builds behind; a false claim that the restore-complete modal reloads the Channels pane automatically; and, the highest-value fix, a Step 2 stream-profile curl recipe missing `command`/`parameters`, which Dispatcharr silently accepts as an unrunnable profile and is what built the unplayable channel behind a false P1 filed during run 18. Also documented: the previously-unnamed `POST /api/backup/restore-dbas` endpoint for scripted callers, the single-channel "Move Channel to Group" drag path in `channel-groups-and-tags.md` (previously only the multi-select move was covered), and a Step 8 warning that `preserve` recreates a deleted archived group empty, so the baseline reset must happen before the second restore, not after. A follow-up pass then fixed four pages that still asserted "Create new channel group is not staged" (`channels-overview.md`, `bulk-edit.md`, `your-first-channels.md`, and `channel-groups-and-tags.md`), a claim the `vtapf` fix below falsified inside this same branch, which would otherwise have shipped a contradiction in one PR. Gates run clean: `scripts/check_em_dashes.py --base-ref origin/dev` (no new em-dashes) and `mkdocs build --strict` (no errors, no broken links).
- **Restore drill repinned to `0.18.1-0049` on the back of run 16, and two sentences that had become false are gone (bead enhancedchannelmanager-dqrjn, build 0050).** The article's opening admonition said the fresh-target round trip was last measured by run 12 on `0.18.1-0040` and the populated-target round by run 13 on `0.18.1-0042`, that "no functional change has landed in the restore path since `0.18.1-0042`", and that the round trip had never been re-measured on `0.18.1-0047`. Both of the latter claims stopped being true the same day. Drill run `2026-08-08-run16` drove a **full** round trip on the published `:dev` image at `0.18.1-0047`: both artifact variants reproduced the instance on a freshly-wiped target with **zero inventory-diff findings** (encrypted `outcome: success` with playback baseline-identical on the first restore; standard redacted `outcome: completed_with_failures` recovering in Step 6a's documented two steps, no third restore), the logo-failure round passed all five assertions with `logo_misses: 1` named and nothing rolled back, and the populated-target round showed `preserve` reporting 7 channel-group drifts and moving none against `overwrite` reporting the same 7 and moving all 7, with the drift detail visible on the dry-run preview before either apply. Then `0.18.1-0048` (`bdmby`) changed `backend/task_scheduler.py`, which **is** in the restore path, and `0.18.1-0049` (`o88e9`) changed the channel-group controls Step 8's construction drives. The admonition now pins `0.18.1-0049`, attributes every claim to the build it was actually measured on, and names the two builds of functional change that landed *after* the measurement rather than asserting there was none. Also recorded honestly: run 16 did not exercise `oebpv`, `sdpzy`, `cytzj` or `zt801` at all, and could not complete Step 8's outright-deletion or name-collision sub-constructions, because the `o88e9` defect dead-ended them, so name-collision handling stays proven by construction only as far as run 13's `0.18.1-0042`. `bdmby` and `o88e9` join Known failures with the build each was fixed in, and the running tally is recounted from fifteen beads to **seventeen**, sixteen of them fully fixed. Step 8's Construction step 2 gains a build-scoped note (completable inside ECM on `0.18.1-0049` and later; finish it in Dispatcharr on `0.18.1-0048` and earlier), and Improved reporting gains the `0.18.1-0048` behaviour change for anyone who waits for a restore by polling `GET /api/tasks/dbas_restore`. Documentation only.
- **Restore drill's version pin refreshed to `0.18.1-0046`, and the last 3 em-dashes in the backup-restore section are gone (bead enhancedchannelmanager-wkr09, build 0046).** `run-a-restore-drill.md`'s opening danger admonition still pinned `0.18.1-0040`, five builds behind `dev`. Rather than silently bumping the pin, and asserting claims measured on `0040` were re-measured on `0046`, the admonition now says what's actually defensible: the article is maintained against `0.18.1-0046` (Dispatcharr `0.28.2`), the build this review happened at, the fresh-target round trip was last measured by run 12 on `0.18.1-0040`, the populated-target round was last measured by run 13 on `0.18.1-0042`, and no functional change has landed in the restore path since `0042` (`git diff` over `backend/dbas`, `backend/tasks`, and `frontend/src/components` between those builds is empty), so both measurements still hold. The historical `Fixed as of 0.18.1-00XX` references elsewhere in the article are untouched. Separately, the three em-dashes in the backup-restore section left out of scope by the earlier cleanup (bead 5skhh, which covered `run-a-restore-drill.md` and `troubleshoot-restore.md`) are rewritten per `docs/style_guide.md`, one each in `index.md`, `restore-a-backup.md`, and `verify-a-backup.md`: each was an independent clause joined by an em-dash, now split into two sentences. `docs/user_guide/backup-restore/` is now at zero em-dashes. Documentation only.
- **Restore drill's "not yet proven" language for r1ei7, 3t74w and tddmw retired: run 13 proved all three live against a populated target (bead enhancedchannelmanager-rjroq, build 0045).** `run-a-restore-drill.md` said in four places that the `0.18.1-0041` fixes for those three beads had not yet been proven by a live drill run against a populated target. Run 13 (2026-08-07), against the published `:dev` image at `0.18.1-0042` (digest `sha256:75986c5eee0b80a47ade10e4132b126eb888381e069fbdc52e8f51642225c359`), ran a targeted Step 8 round on a target rebuilt from run 12's own verified artifact and measured all three live: `preserve` reported channel-group drift for all 7 affected channels and moved none, and `overwrite`, re-run from an identical baseline, reported and moved all 7; `already_exists_name_match` appeared exactly 376 times, matching the skipped channel-group count, while every other category still reported `already_exists_identical`; and the preview's `channel_groups` caveat and `Streams: not predicted` row both rendered. The opening danger admonition, the Step 8 introduction, the relink-mode comparison table's grouping-row note, and the r1ei7 row in Known failures are updated to cite run 13 and the `0.18.1-0042` pin. One assertion in Step 8's own checklist, deselecting the `channel_groups` category and confirming the "not checked" note, was found **not reachable from the current restore UI**: the DBAS restore modal exposes no category-selection control at either stage, and `GET /api/tasks/dbas_restore/parameter-schema` returns empty `parameters` and `run_parameters`. The article now says so plainly instead of instructing an operator to do something the UI doesn't expose. Run 13 was a targeted Step 8 round, not a full round trip; the fresh-target round trip remains run 12's result. Documentation only.
- **Backup/restore operator docs brought into line with the style guide's no-em-dash-in-prose rule (build 0043).** `run-a-restore-drill.md`, `migrate-to-a-new-install.md`, and `troubleshoot-restore.md` are edited for punctuation and sentence structure only: every em-dash in prose (introduced by PRs #793 and #794, plus two pre-existing ones from build 0040 in `run-a-restore-drill.md`) is rewritten per `docs/style_guide.md`'s prescribed strategies, independent clauses split into sentences, appositives recast as colons or parentheses, trailing elaborations split into new sentences. No factual claim, version pin, bead id, or hedge changed. Documentation only.
- **Restore drill gains an executable populated-target round, Step 8 (bead enhancedchannelmanager-z9xen, build 0042).** The article's executable procedure was Steps 1-7, all against a freshly-wiped target; "Restoring onto a populated target" was reference material describing behaviour, but no step ever told a drill-runner to construct a populated target and test it, so a drill following the article never exercised that path. Step 8 now has an operator construct the divergence (rename an archived group; delete another after moving its channels out, since Dispatcharr refuses to delete a non-empty group; create a name collision with a different id and different members; diverge one channel's logo and clear another's EPG link so the relink modes are distinguishable) and restore twice from an identical baseline — preserve, then overwrite — asserting by measurement rather than assumption: drift reported non-zero and nothing moved under preserve; reported and moved under overwrite; the dry-run preview showing the drift count and its channel_groups caveat; `already_exists_name_match` for channel groups versus `already_exists_identical` for profiles; and playback verified afterward. Step 8 is what will let a future drill run prove enhancedchannelmanager-r1ei7, -3t74w and -tddmw (fixed in 0.18.1-0041) against a live populated target — this change adds the procedure, it does not itself run it; the article's existing "not yet proven by a live drill" language for those fixes stays true and stays in place until such a run happens. Documentation only.
- **Restore drill's playback verification timeout raised from 40 to 60 seconds, now measured across three runs (build 0040).** `run-a-restore-drill.md` Step 7 previously told operators to use a 40-second hard timeout on the first post-restore playback fetch, sourced from a single measurement (finding D-6, drill run `2026-08-06-run9`, where a 25-second deadline returned 0 bytes and 40 seconds passed). **`2026-08-06-run10-verify` is the run that shows 40 seconds itself is insufficient: a 40-second deadline returned 0 bytes on the first post-restore fetch, and the identical fetch passed at 60 seconds** (run 10's report initially mis-transcribed this failing deadline as 25 seconds and has since been corrected at source). `2026-08-07-run11-published`, against the published `0.18.1-0038` image, used a 60-second deadline throughout and passed — corroborating that 60 seconds works, though it did not retest 40 seconds itself. 40 seconds is confirmed too tight on a cold first fetch and would have caused an operator calibrating on that figure to file a false playback failure — the exact trap the timeout guidance exists to prevent. The article now documents 60 seconds as the floor (stated as what has been observed to work on this drill's host and provider, not a guarantee), explains why the first fetch is slower (a fresh upstream connection to the provider), and states explicitly that a timeout hit before any bytes arrive is inconclusive on a cold fetch and should be retried at a longer deadline before concluding playback is broken. The existing guidance that a read-deadline hit *after* bytes have started arriving counts as a pass is unchanged. Documentation only.
- **Backup/restore docs re-aligned against a live round-trip drill on build 0035, run in doc-following mode (bead enhancedchannelmanager-mlb9v, build 0037).** Drill run 9 held only the published site, no repo access, and found eight documentation defects. The load-bearing one: this article told operators to expect every EPG link to be lost on a restore, in three separate places, when run 9 measured 9 of 9 seeded links surviving on both artifact variants; that guidance is corrected in `run-a-restore-drill.md` and `migrate-to-a-new-install.md`, and the underlying bead (`dfkbn`) is closed. Also corrected: Step 3's status check pointed at the Saved Backups panel, which has no status field at all; the check actually happens in the Notifications panel, now documented. Step 2's seed table named a custom stream profile and user agent without saying where to create them; both are Dispatcharr objects with no create UI on 0.28.2, so the article now documents the REST API path the drill had to use. The playback assertion's "hard timeout" now carries a figure (40 seconds), after a 25-second deadline read a genuine restore as failed. The two backup artifacts share one filename pattern with nothing to tell them apart; the article now promotes the existing `BadZipFile` check to the actual identification method. The `parameter-schema` endpoint warning is retired: as of build 0035 the endpoint documents the encrypted-backup options correctly. The article's own version banner is repinned from `0.18.1-0023` to `0.18.1-0036`, the build these fixes ship in (build 0037, this docs-only PR, does not itself change runtime behavior). Also folded in as measured, not just expected: the genuine non-fatal-logo-failure path was exercised live for the first time and behaved correctly, and the three restore-reporting fixes from PR #788 (`enhancedchannelmanager-k2r7m` logo-miss double-count, `enhancedchannelmanager-cwmid` inverted degraded-restore alert severity, `enhancedchannelmanager-d0bd3` restore-complete UI omitting the no-playable-stream panel) are documented in Known Failures and Improved Reporting. Documentation only.
- **Backup docs no longer tell you to delete your uploaded logos before taking a backup (beads enhancedchannelmanager-xb58a + enhancedchannelmanager-d0agi, build 0035).** Three backup/restore articles carried a prominent warning that a logo uploaded through ECM's own Logo Manager would abort and roll back an entire restore, and that the only way to complete one was to **delete those logo records before backing up** and re-add them by hand afterwards — accepting permanent loss of the logos. That stopped being true in `0.18.1-0024`: uploaded logos have their image bytes archived in the backup and restore intact, and a logo that cannot be restored is counted and named rather than rolling anything back. The warning had gone stale in the operator's *disfavour*, advising them to throw away data the product now preserves correctly, and is replaced with what actually happens. The old behaviour is retained as a version-scoped note for anyone still running an earlier build. The restore drill's own guidance is inverted to match: uploading a logo is now the useful thing to seed, because it exercises a path earlier runs could not reach. Documentation only.
- **Backup & restore troubleshooting docs re-aligned with what builds 0029–0033 actually do (bead enhancedchannelmanager-mlb9v, build 0034).** Five defects found by the live restore drill have since been fixed, and the operator articles still described the broken behaviour — telling people a redacted restore needs three steps when it now needs two, that a preview's health counters cannot be trusted when they now match the apply, and that a repeat restore can silently drop a stream slot when it no longer does. Every stale claim is now version-scoped rather than deleted, so operators on an older build still get the guidance that applies to them, and the advice that outlived its example — verify playback rather than trusting a summary counter — is kept as durable practice. The two-step recovery is documented with its real coverage limits: it is the individual-account **Refresh** and the scheduled refresh task that reattach channels; a refresh-all, or one performed in Dispatcharr's own UI, is picked up on the next scheduled refresh instead. Documentation only.
- **Backup & restore documentation corrected against a live round-trip drill on build 0027 (bead enhancedchannelmanager-zimw5, build 0028).** The run-4 restore drill measured published `0.18.1-0027` against Dispatcharr 0.28.2 and found four operator-facing articles stale or silent. The load-bearing fix: *Troubleshoot a Restore* told operators that re-running a restore "hits the same collision and leaves the same slot behind" for a name-collision placeholder. It does not — the second run clears the slot by **dropping** it (3 streams to 2), silently reorders the survivors, leaves the still-working stream unbound, and reports `outcome: success` with empty `notes[]`. Also newly documented: a second restore's health counters can read `0` while a channel returns HTTP 500 (with the confirming `unique_channel_stream` log check); the dry-run preview's **category** counts are trustworthy while its **health/split** counters under-report on a fresh target (with the populated-target contrast, so operators do not over-correct); an ECM-uploaded logo's bytes live in Dispatcharr's `/data/logos/`, not ECM's `/config/uploads/logos/` as the overview claimed; and authentication turns itself on the first time the ECM web UI is opened, which strands anyone scripting a restore against a new install (stated as observed, not proven). Documentation only — no runtime behaviour changes.
- **Cross-instance sync targets now sync independently and concurrently (bead enhancedchannelmanager-7ipq2.3, build 0003).** Implements ADR-013 S6's per-target trigger model: each `SyncTarget` now owns its own registered task (`dbas_sync_<target_id>`, registered dynamically at startup and on target create/rename/delete) instead of v1's single shared `dbas_sync` id. Under the shared id, one slow or unreachable target B serialized — and, when two targets' schedules came due in the same scheduler tick, silently swallowed — every other target's run; distinct targets now run concurrently while the task engine's per-task_id `ALREADY_RUNNING` guard becomes a true per-target lock (a second run against the *same* B is refused non-silently and retried on its next interval). Concurrency is bounded by a small cross-target cap (`ECM_SYNC_MAX_CONCURRENT`, default 3; excess runs queue rather than being dropped). Per-target registry instances remove cross-target parameter leakage structurally, the 7ipq2.2 one-shot disarm now resets to the *bound* target id (extended red-first to the concurrent case), and a run parameter naming a *different* target than the task is bound to hard-fails without running. Existing `dbas_sync` schedule rows are migrated to their per-target id at startup (rows referencing a deleted target are disabled with a warning, never migrated to a dead id; alert/notification preferences of the legacy shared task row do not carry over — reconfigure per target if you had customized them). Observability sharpens with the id change: `ecm_task_schedule_last_success_timestamp` now tracks each target separately, so per-target drift is visible where the shared id let one healthy replica mask another's — and both sync signals are now keyed per target (the `ECMSyncStalledTargetDrift` alert and `ecm_sync_runs_total`; see the next entry for the exact metrics, which changed in the same release — update your Prometheus rules copy). Per-target sync task ids inherit the same admin gate as the DBAS backup/restore tasks, and the Scheduled Tasks UI now lists one sync task per target (per-target schedules, history, and alert toggles). The "Sync now"/Apply buttons target the per-target task id, and the card now tracks in-flight state per target (previously one scalar marker, so starting a second target re-enabled the first target's row mid-request) — deploy backend and frontend together.
- **Cross-instance sync drift alerting is now apply-only and per-target, and the run counter names the target (bead enhancedchannelmanager-7ipq2.3, build 0003).** Two observability corrections that ship with the per-target work. (1) `ECMSyncStalledTargetDrift` now keys on a new `ecm_sync_last_full_success_timestamp{sync_target_id}` gauge, stamped only by a run that actually APPLIED and returned a clean SUCCESS. It previously used the generic `ecm_task_schedule_last_success_timestamp`, which the task engine stamps on ANY successful run — and a dry-run preview succeeds without writing anything to B, so a recurring preview reset the drift clock while the replica kept diverging. The generic gauge remains as the "is this task running at all" signal; the runbook and SLO doc now state the distinction explicitly. (2) `ecm_sync_runs_total` gains a `sync_target_id` label (the SyncTarget pk — immutable, so renaming a target cannot fork the series), so the runbook's "B unreachable vs applies-but-half-fails" triage step can name WHICH replica is failing now that targets run concurrently; both sync signals are keyed on the same target identifier. Operators with a copy of `docs/sre/prometheus_rules.yaml` must take the updated rule, and any saved dashboard query against `ecm_sync_runs_total` should add a `sync_target_id` filter (an unfiltered query now sums across targets). Also fixed in the same pass: a legacy `dbas_sync` schedule that was actively firing before the upgrade kept firing after it — the migration now preserves the effective enabled state onto the new per-target task (previously the re-keyed schedule landed on a default-disabled parent and silently stopped running), and startup registration never moves an *existing* per-target task's enabled gate in either direction — an enabled sync survives every ordinary container restart, and one an operator switched off is never quietly switched back on — and the live-B validation recipe in `tests/dbas-test-env/README.md` was updated to the per-target endpoint and metric labels.
- **The "Update available" header pill is gone — the same notice now lives in the notification centre, and the container needs GitHub egress to produce it (bead enhancedchannelmanager-nhkd4, build 0005).** The version-update check moved from the browser to the backend: previously only your browser talked to GitHub to check for a new release; now the container does, on a periodic timer and at startup. **Operationally, this means the container now needs outbound network access to GitHub** — if it doesn't have any, the check fails soft (a bounded HTTP timeout, a WARNING log line, previous state left alone) rather than blocking startup or crashing the check loop. The information itself hasn't changed — "a newer version is available" — but it's a notification-centre entry now instead of a pill in the header, and it retires itself automatically once you update, rather than continuing to advertise a version you've already moved past. Moving the check server-side also closes a race across multiple open browser tabs, since the backend is now the single writer.
- **Type scale unified across all ten route pages and the entire modal estate (82 dialogs), and the app's chrome is visibly tighter (beads enhancedchannelmanager-6z299, enhancedchannelmanager-xhldy, enhancedchannelmanager-f4yc7, enhancedchannelmanager-99o0x, enhancedchannelmanager-vh6hh, enhancedchannelmanager-9bpkk, enhancedchannelmanager-sl7dx, enhancedchannelmanager-57pp3, build 0005).** Route pages and dialogs had drifted onto inconsistent, overlapping font sizes — body text, item titles and section headings scattered across 12-18px — because shared CSS classes were being redeclared per page instead of owned by one shared layer, so the same class could render a different size depending which tabs you'd visited that session. Everything now maps onto a small set of named type roles (metric / section / body / item-title / meta / micro / badge) defined once. Visible consequences: the top bar shrank 75px → 45px, dialog header bands shrank 65px → 49px (dialog titles 18px → 16px), route header bands are tighter across all eleven routes, Stats panel headings read sentence case instead of ALL-CAPS, and the notification centre's text — previously inheriting the browser's 16px root size because its panel declared no font-size of its own — is now on the same 13px-body scale as the rest of the app. Channel Manager's two-pane split is rebalanced from 58/42 to an even 50/50 (the old ratio was an unset component default, never a deliberate page decision, and had been read as "wrong" for a while). Checkbox and radio controls render at one consistent 16px size everywhere instead of five different sizes across the app. This consolidation also surfaced and fixed several real rendering bugs along the way: the Manage Channels list was rendering names inside a 32px grid track from a cross-component CSS collision, Channel Manager's stream filters silently picked up Journal's font size after a single visit to that tab, and several row-action toggle buttons rendered 22px instead of their intended 32px after an unrelated CSS hoist changed load order.
- **Settings' drill-in destinations are grouped into six labelled sections instead of one flat list of eighteen, and each section's heading moved into the shared route header (beads enhancedchannelmanager-70u0r.2, enhancedchannelmanager-70u0r.3, enhancedchannelmanager-70u0r.4, enhancedchannelmanager-70u0r.6, enhancedchannelmanager-70u0r.7, enhancedchannelmanager-2896r.16, enhancedchannelmanager-2896r.17, build 0005).** The Settings sidebar previously listed all eighteen destinations as one undifferentiated list ("just one giant blob of things," per PO feedback); they're now grouped as Connections, Channel Processing, Notifications & Reports, Upkeep, Workspace, and Administration. Settings pages no longer render their own heading block — the shared route header above the content pane now carries a third breadcrumb and the section's description, returning that vertical space to the content itself. The "back to Settings" link at the top of the navigation rail is now sticky rather than scrolling out of view: grouping added enough height that, at smaller viewports, Administration — and the only exit from Settings — could scroll off screen.
- **Docs: reverse-proxy / HTTP-2 request concurrency explained where the `Exceeded concurrency limit` log line sends you (bead enhancedchannelmanager-hns2y, GitHub #755, build 0008).** The #755 fix above (see Fixed) explains what broke in that specific case; operators who hit the same `Exceeded concurrency limit` log line for a *different* reason — a genuinely large one-off burst, or another client issuing one request per item — had nowhere to go for **why** the failure shows up behind a reverse proxy and not in direct testing. `docs/runbooks/request-timeout.md` now covers it: a browser talking HTTP/1.1 caps itself at roughly 6 connections per origin, throttling a burst in transit; a reverse proxy speaking HTTP/2 to the browser multiplexes over one connection with no such cap, so the same burst arrives at ECM all at once. **HTTP/2 is not the fix and running behind one is not the problem** — it only removes a throttle the browser was otherwise applying incidentally, which is why the failure can be invisible in direct testing and reproduce every time through a proxy. Indexed in `docs/runbooks/README.md`.
- **Docs: backup/restore guides gain two troubleshooting notes (bead enhancedchannelmanager-ixdaw).** The troubleshoot-restore guide now covers a restore that leaves a multi-stream channel bound to its placeholder **ECM Custom Streams (DBAS restore)** streams: when two of the channel's streams have names that differ only in ways the name matcher normalizes away (capitalization, spacing, punctuation), both resolve to the same real stream, Dispatcharr rejects the resulting duplicate-stream save, and the whole channel falls back to placeholders — with the by-hand reattach steps to repair it. The take-a-backup guide now notes that encrypted and standard artifacts share the same `ecm-backup-<date>_<time>.zip` filename convention; nothing in the filename, the `.sha256` sidecar, or the Saved Backups list tells them apart, so operators need to track which saved files are encrypted themselves.

### Removed
- **Lookup Tables removed entirely — UI, API, template pipe and database table. ACTION REQUIRED BEFORE UPGRADE if you have any rows (bead enhancedchannelmanager-70u0r.1, PO decision D2 on epic enhancedchannelmanager-70u0r).** The **Settings → Lookup Tables** destination, the five `/api/lookup-tables` CRUD endpoints, the `LookupTable` model, `_resolve_lookups`, the `inline_lookups` / `global_lookup_ids` fields on `POST /api/dummy-epg/preview{,/batch}`, and the `{key|lookup:<table>}` pipe in **both** template engines are gone, along with the `lookup_tables` table (destructive Alembic revision `0041`). **Export first:** the Lookup Tables page shipped as a reachable Settings destination in every release from v0.16.0 through v0.18.0, so any instance may hold operator-authored rows, and `alembic downgrade 0040` restores the table's schema but NOT its rows. Full export procedure: [Lookup Tables retired](docs/user_guide/epg/lookup-tables-retired.md). As a safety net the migration writes every row it is about to delete to `/config/lookup_tables_dropped_0041.json` and logs the path at WARNING (best-effort — a read-only or full `/config` makes it fail, and the log line says so). **Your generated EPG output does not change.** The pipe only ever resolved in *preview*: `generate_xmltv()` called `generate_channel_xml` with six positional args, leaving `lookups` at its `None` default, so XMLTV generation raised "unknown lookup table", took `render_template`'s fallback and emitted the RAW template text verbatim as the programme `<title>`/`<desc>`. After removal the same template raises "unknown transform" and takes the same fallback — byte-identical XMLTV. What changes is that preview now agrees with the guide instead of contradicting it, which is why removal was chosen over documenting the divergence. Bookmarked `#settings/lookup-tables` URLs resolve to Settings → General via a compatibility alias rather than the silent unknown-page fallback; the preview endpoints *ignore* the two retired request fields rather than rejecting them, so a stale cached browser tab keeps working. Remaining pipes (`uppercase`, `lowercase`, `titlecase`, `trim`, `strip`, `replace`, `normalize`), conditionals, pattern variants and substitution pairs are untouched.

### Fixed
- **A Channel Pipeline rule that names channel number `0` no longer creates a second channel `0` (bead `enhancedchannelmanager-ay3iq`, build 0110).** `0` is a channel number like any other, and a pipeline run starts by reading your lineup to work out which numbers are already taken. That reading treated a channel numbered `0` as a channel with no number at all, so the slot was recorded as free. A **Create Channel** or **Set Channel Number** action set to `0` then created a duplicate at `0` instead of stepping up to the next free number, every run, for as long as the rule kept firing. It now steps up. Channels that genuinely have no number are still read as unnumbered, which is what they are, and `auto` numbering is unchanged because it has always started at `1`. This one predates the tenths work below and has been present since the engine was written.

- **A Channel Pipeline rule with a full channel-number range no longer lands on a number that is already taken (bead `enhancedchannelmanager-ay3iq`, build 0110).** A **Create Channel** action can be given a range to number from, such as `100-105` or the `Starting from...` field's `100-99999`. The engine walks the range and takes the first free number in it; when every number in the range is already used it deliberately spills past the top rather than failing, which is the behaviour you want. The spill, though, took the number one above the range without ever checking whether *that* number was free. So a rule set to `1-2` on a lineup already holding channels `1`, `2` and `3` was handed `3`, a number the run itself had just recorded as taken, and created a second channel on it. A run that created several channels this way put every one of them on the same number. The spill now keeps searching upward from the top of the range for the first genuinely free number, exactly as `auto` and a plain starting number already did, so `1-2` against `1`, `2`, `3` gives you `4`. Nothing else about ranges changes: a range with room in it still fills from its own bottom up, ranges still count in whole numbers, and a full range still assigns above itself instead of failing the action. This one predates the tenths work below and has been present since ranges were added.

- **Inserting a channel by hand at a number that is already taken now warns you, and "Push channels down" actually pushes (bead `enhancedchannelmanager-fprsq`, build 0110).** The **Create Channel** button in the Channels pane opens the same dialog as creating channels from streams, but the manual path skipped the conflict check entirely. Typing a channel number another channel already held produced a second channel on that number with no warning of any kind, no dialog, and nothing to undo but the change itself. The check was not merely unable to honour "Push channels down" in manual entry, as originally reported: the **Channel Number Conflict** dialog was never shown there at all, so the button was never even offered. Manual entry now runs the same conflict check and, when you choose it, the same push-down planner the bulk path uses, so the channels already sitting at that number move up together and nothing is left holding a duplicate.
