All three answers work: **Push channels down**, **Insert at end** (which used to be doubly broken here, since the manual path ignored the number it hands over and created the channel back at the conflicting one), and **Create anyway** for when the duplicate is what you want. A decimal insert pushes down on the tenths grid exactly as a bulk insert does, so inserting at `38.1` moves `38.1` to `38.2` and carries the ripple on from there rather than jumping to `39`. The dialog's wording is sized for a single channel: it offers to shift by one, not by the zero streams manual entry has selected. Creating a channel with **no** number is still a normal thing to do and never raises the dialog, since an unassigned channel cannot collide with anything. Two smaller things fixed with it: the create dialog no longer previews a channel range for a single manual channel, where it read "Channels 38.1 - 38.0"; and the conflict dialog now closes once the channel is created instead of staying on screen. Manual channel creation is an edit-mode feature and the push-down works by staging, so a push-down requested outside edit mode is now refused with a message rather than quietly creating the duplicate anyway.

- **Starting-number fields no longer turn a typed `1.5` into `1` without telling you (bead `enhancedchannelmanager-j3pyx`, build 0110).** **Sort & Renumber**, **Renumber** on a selection, **Renumber All Groups** and its per-group starting numbers, the group reorder custom number, the **Move to group** custom starting number, and (in the Channel Pipeline rule builder) a **Sort Group** action's **Starting Channel Number** all seed a run of sequential numbers from one value you type. Every one of them read that field with a whole-number parse and then said nothing: typing `1.5` renumbered from `1`, the preview underneath cheerfully announced "Channels will be numbered 1 - 12", and the confirm button stayed live the whole time. You asked for one thing and got another, which is the exact behaviour the channel-number rules were changed to stop (bead `enhancedchannelmanager-ic884.1`). These fields stay whole-number-only, because sequential renumbering counts in whole numbers and there is no sensible run starting at `1.5`. What changes is that they now refuse: one message, the same in every dialog and in the rule builder, saying what is accepted ("Starting channel numbers must be whole numbers of 1 or greater"). The preview and the confirm button are both derived from the same reading of the field as the operation itself, so a value that will not be honoured cannot be previewed and cannot be confirmed. In the rule builder the field goes red and the action is left with no starting number at all, so a rule can never be saved carrying a number you did not type. While the field is red, **Save** and the Enter key both refuse the whole rule and put the cursor back in the offending field, from whichever wizard step you pressed **Save** on. Blocking the save is the other half of refusing the entry: without it you could read the red message, save anyway, and get a rule that renumbers from the group's current lowest, which is not the number you typed either. A blank **Sort Group** starting number is the normal "start from the group's current lowest" behaviour. This does not narrow anything you could do before: channel numbers with one decimal place are still accepted everywhere they were, including when you create channels from streams and when you edit a channel directly. It is only the *starting number of a renumbering run* that has to be whole, and it always was.

- **A Channel Pipeline rule can now be set to a channel number like `1.1`, and the channel actually gets `1.1` (bead `enhancedchannelmanager-ay3iq`, build 0110).** Making one decimal place canonical (bead `enhancedchannelmanager-ic884.1`) meant `1.1` became a valid channel number everywhere you can type one, including a rule's **Set Channel Number** value and a **Create Channel** action's Channel Number. The pipeline engine, though, understood only whole numbers: it read `"auto"`, a whole number, and a whole-number `min-max` range, and quietly numbered everything else automatically. So a rule set to `1.1` was accepted, saved, and then produced a channel numbered `3`, or whatever the next free integer happened to be, with nothing in the execution log to say so. The engine now walks the same tenths grid the Channels pane already uses when you insert at a decimal position: a rule set to `1.1` lands on `1.1`, and if `1.1` is already taken it steps to `1.2`, not to `2`. A rule set to a whole number still steps by whole numbers, exactly as before. The arithmetic is done in whole tenths and converted back once at the end, so a run of decimal assignments cannot drift into a number Dispatcharr will not hold (`1.1 + 0.1` is `1.2000000000000002` in plain floating point). A whole number stays whole: `800.0` is written as `800`, in the payload and in the `800 - Channel Name` prefix, not as `800.0`. Two shapes are still refused when you save the rule, because the engine cannot honour them and silently renumbering is worse than a clear message: a range naming a tenth (`1.1-1.9`, since ranges count in whole numbers) and a number not written as plain digits (`1e3`, `.5`, `+7`). The message says so directly. The **Create Channel** action's **Starting from...** number builds one of those ranges, and it used to quietly drop the decimal before you ever got that far: typing `1.5` there wrote a range starting at `1`, so the save-time refusal for a fractional range could never actually reach you. That field now refuses the entry where you type it, with the same "Starting channel numbers must be whole numbers of 1 or greater" message every other starting-number field uses, and leaves the action with no starting number rather than one you did not ask for. The rule cannot be saved while that field is red, either: **Save** and the Enter key both refuse it and focus the field, so a red message can no longer be saved past into a rule that numbers channels automatically. That refusal now survives editing the action list. Each action card keeps its own identity, so deleting or reordering the actions above a red field leaves the red field exactly where it was, still refusing. Previously, deleting an earlier action moved every card up a slot and took the refusal with it: the red message vanished, and **Save** went straight through with the action carrying no starting number at all, which is automatic numbering, silently, the whole thing being fixed here. The same identity fix cures a longer-standing annoyance with no connection to numbering: reordering actions used to leave each card showing the *previous* action's **Channel Numbering** choice and **Transform Channel Name** toggle, so the cards and the rule disagreed until you closed and reopened the editor. And pressing **Save** from the **Targeting** or **Output & Run** step now actually lands you on the red field: the editor already returned you to **Logic**, but the cursor stayed on the Save button, leaving you on a step change with no visible explanation. If a rule saved before this release carries one of those, the engine now writes a warning to the log naming the value it could not honour, instead of numbering silently. There is one magnitude at which a tenth stops existing at all: above about 562,949,953,421,312, far past any real lineup, the numbers a computer can store are more than a tenth apart, so several tenths collapse onto one stored value. A rule naming a tenth up there is now answered with a whole number and a log line saying why, rather than with the number the engine had just established was taken. One thing deliberately unchanged: a rule whose Create Channel number names a tenth does not drive the rule-level **Channel Sort** renumber pass, which counts in whole numbers, so that pass is skipped exactly as it is for `auto` numbering. The numbers assigned at creation stand.

- **The password-reset CLI accepts the same passwords as the rest of ECM (bead `enhancedchannelmanager-xztoc`, build 0110).** `reset_password.py` is the tool the README and the user guide both point you at when you are locked out, and it carried its own password rules demanding an uppercase letter, a lowercase letter and a digit. ECM dropped exactly those composition rules on purpose, following NIST 800-63B, so the one path an operator reaches for in a lockout was the one path that refused a passphrase the web UI and the API would have accepted. It now calls the same validator everything else does: at least 8 characters, not a common or breached password, and not containing the username. It also prints those rules when it refuses, which it never did before, so you are no longer told only that the password "does not meet requirements" without being told what they are. `--force` still skips validation. The rules themselves are unchanged, and no composition rule was re-added anywhere.

- **The e2e suite no longer defaults to the product owner's live ECM instance (bead `enhancedchannelmanager-536bl`, build 0109).** `playwright.config.ts` resolved an unset `E2E_BASE_URL` to `http://localhost:6100`, which is the PO's live instance holding real channel data; any spec that stages and applies a channel-number push-down, a cross-group move, a merge, a delete, or any other write would have mutated that real lineup for anyone who ran the suite locally without overriding the variable, and nothing warned about it. This is why bead `i85dg` shipped its push-down coverage as a component test instead of an E2E: writing one against the live default was unsafe. `resolveE2EBaseURL()` in `playwright-base-url.mjs` now throws at config load, before any browser launches or any request is made, when `E2E_BASE_URL` is unset and `E2E_EXACT_BUILD` is not `true`; the message names the removed default, the hazard it created, and the exact commands to set the variable or use the self-hosted `E2E_START_SERVER=true E2E_EXACT_BUILD=true` mode instead. The `Screen-Reader-Only Rendering Guard` and `Operator Workspace Release Matrix` CI jobs already run with `E2E_EXACT_BUILD=true`, which builds and serves the checked-out source on its own isolated preview port and never depended on `E2E_BASE_URL`, so both are unaffected; verified by listing each job's exact spec invocation against the changed config with `E2E_BASE_URL` unset. `docs/testing.md` records the new contract everywhere it previously described the `:6100` default.

- **Inserting channels no longer renumbers groups it has no reason to touch (beads `enhancedchannelmanager-i85dg`, `enhancedchannelmanager-nzwtw`, build 0108).** Dropping streams into a group and choosing "Push channels down" was renumbering the target group and then every group above it, even when a wide gap between them made that unnecessary. The cause was the model, not a stray comparison. The push-down treated each channel group as one continuous span of numbers from its lowest to its highest, and cascaded into the next group whenever the target group's highest number, once shifted, reached the next group's lowest. Nothing in ECM, in Dispatcharr's API or in the database makes that model true: a group may contain holes, may sit inside another group's range, and `channel_number` is a plain non-unique number that permits duplicates. So a single stray high number anywhere in the target group, one placeholder at 9999 for instance, inflated the shifted maximum past every later group's minimum and swept the whole lineup. The same model quietly produced duplicates in the opposite case, where a group starting below the target group but reaching past its tail was never examined at all, and its numbers were landed on. It also compared unrounded sums, so 0.7 plus 0.1 compared as less than 0.8 and invented a gap that was not there. Planning now ignores groups entirely and looks only at which numbers are occupied: it walks up from the insertion point over the numbers the insert actually claims, extends the run of channels it moves past anything those claims would land on, and moves exactly the channels in between, each by the same amount. Every occupant of a number moves together, so lineups that already carry duplicates keep them together instead of having them split apart, and no new duplicate is created. **What counts as "in the way" is the numbers the insert takes, not the space between them.** A pre-merge review caught the first version of this planner requiring a whole step-wide stretch of numbers to be clear, which is not the same thing: inserting one channel at 300 takes the number 300, not everything from 300.000 to 300.999. On a lineup holding only 300.5, that moved 300.5 to 301.5 although the slot 300 was free; on 300 and 300.5, it moved both although moving 300 to 301 was enough. The two readings agree for whole numbers on a whole-number step, which is why an all-integer test suite could not tell them apart, so the tests now check the rule with an independent reference and sample decimal and off-lattice lineups as well. A channel sitting off the step INSIDE a run that is moving still moves with it, otherwise it would end up below the channel that was above it. Where the gap between two groups is narrower than the insert, the channels beyond it do move, but by the size of the insert rather than by an amount re-derived from a group maximum, which is how one small overlap used to become a lineup-wide renumber. The conflict dialog now also states how many existing channels "Push channels down" would renumber, which is usually far more than the conflicting channels it reported before, so the size of the operation is visible before it is chosen. Both places that planned this shift, bulk creation from streams and the cross-group channel move, now share one planner; the cross-group move previously had no gap check of any kind and pushed the target group's tail onto the next group's numbers in silence. **"Close gaps in source group" can no longer undo the planner's work either.** Moving a channel out of a group compacts whatever is left behind, and that compaction was calculated separately, against the numbers as they stood before the move: moving channel 15 to a custom number 11 while 10 and 30 stayed behind renumbered 30 to 11 as well, so the move produced the duplicate the planner had just avoided. The two are still separate operations, because closing holes in one group and making room at an insertion point are different things and the planner deliberately knows nothing about groups, but they now share one record of which numbers are taken, so the compaction allocates around the move and the push-down instead of on top of them. A channel the push-down has already moved keeps that number rather than being handed a second, conflicting one. **Numbers finer than three decimals survive a shift intact.** Comparisons are made on a three-decimal grid, and nothing in ECM, the CSV importer or Dispatcharr's contract stops an import from carrying 1.0001 and 1.0004; both land on the same grid point, which is the right answer for deciding whether they are the same slot and the wrong one for deciding what to write back, since it would turn a pair that was merely close into an actual duplicate. Shifted numbers now keep whatever precision they arrived with. The planner is covered by property tests including the reported case, each of which was demonstrated failing against the replaced logic first.

- **The screen-reader-only rendering guard no longer depends on the fail-open defect it shared a branch with (bead `enhancedchannelmanager-9kwzp`, build 0104).** The `Screen-Reader-Only Rendering Guard` CI job builds the checked-out frontend and serves it on an isolated preview port with no backend, so every `/api` call fails, including the two probes that tell the app whether this instance requires a sign-in. It rendered the whole app shell anyway, because an unanswered probe used to resolve to "authentication is not required": the guard had been riding the exact fail-open the entry above removes, and nobody had noticed, because the job was green. With that fixed the app correctly renders its "Cannot reach ECM" screen instead, the shell never mounts, and the fixture's 30 second wait for the navigation rail times out. This was verified by running the job's own command locally and reading the failure screenshot rather than inferring it from the diff. The product code is unchanged and the fail-open fix stands. `e2e/fixtures/css-guard.ts` now states the auth posture itself when `E2E_EXACT_BUILD` is set, which is the flag that means "no backend": it fulfils `/api/auth/status`, `/api/auth/setup-required`, `/api/auth/me` and `/api/session-start` with the same four responses `e2e/operator-shell.spec.ts` and `e2e/filter-select-ownership.spec.ts` already install on the same preview build, describing the same instance the guard has always measured, namely authentication disabled, setup complete, no session. No data endpoint is stubbed, so both routes still render the same empty and error states, and the guard still asserts it measured at least one element per route so it cannot pass vacuously. The six sibling guards that run against a live ECM container are untouched and keep exercising the real login. `docs/testing.md` and the job's own comment recorded the old assumption as fact and now name the stub.

- **Signing in on an instance with authentication turned off now survives a page reload (bead `enhancedchannelmanager-9kwzp`, build 0101).** ECM lets you reach `/login` even when authentication is switched off, because three surfaces (restoring a backup into a fresh instance, the MCP API key, and TLS key material) require a signed-in human admin regardless of that setting. Signing in worked, but the session evaporated on the next page load: the client skipped its "who am I" check whenever the server said authentication was not required, so a refresh or a second tab dropped you back to an anonymous view and the TLS and MCP settings sections went back to reporting "Admin access required". You had to sign in again every single time, which made the route close to useless. The client now resolves an existing session cookie whether or not authentication is required, and skips the check only before first-run setup, when there is no account for a cookie to name.

- **The header now offers a Sign in button on an instance with authentication turned off (bead `enhancedchannelmanager-9kwzp`, build 0101).** Paired with the fix above. On such an instance the app shell showed no sign-in affordance at all, so the login page was reachable only by typing the URL. The button appears whenever there is no active session and the instance has an operator account to sign in as, and it is absent otherwise: before first-run setup there is no account to sign in as, and when authentication IS required the login page replaces the app shell anyway.

- **An ECM the browser cannot reach no longer renders as an ECM that needs no login, and admin navigation no longer hides itself when there is no user to check (bead `enhancedchannelmanager-p388h`, absorbing `ee5f1`, build 0101).** `useAuthRequired()` returned `false` whenever the auth-status probe had not produced an answer, and `ProtectedRoute`'s setup probe did the same in its `catch`, under the comment "If we can't check, assume setup is not required". Two different unknowns both resolved to "no authentication here", so a degraded backend produced the full application shell with no session: every panel erroring, no login prompt (there is no global 401-to-login redirect anywhere in `httpClient.ts`), and `/login` rewritten to `/` so the operator could not reach a sign-in form even by typing the URL. That last part is what makes this a recoverability defect rather than a cosmetic one. Both probes now carry an explicit third state, and an unresolved posture renders a "Cannot reach ECM" screen with a Retry button and a working **Log in** link instead of the application. The opposite polarity is fixed in the same pass: sidebar navigation gated the Administration settings group on `Boolean(user?.is_admin)`, which is permanently false on a `require_auth: false` instance because there is no user object at all, so the group stayed hidden while the backend served it to anyone. `SettingsTab` already read a null user as PERMITTED for exactly that reason (bead `enhancedchannelmanager-9kwzp.10`); the navigation into it was the last place resolving unknown to hidden. **Operator note:** `/login` is now reachable on an auth-disabled instance that already holds an operator account, which is what bead `enhancedchannelmanager-jy006` needs: it made initial restore, the MCP API key and TLS key material require a signed-in human admin even in that mode, and the UI offered no way to sign in. Neither half changes backend enforcement, and the test suite no longer defaults every auth test to the failed-probe state that hid this.
- **The Stats watch-history filter row no longer changes layout depending on which tabs you visited first (bead enhancedchannelmanager-albfq, build 0058).** The row is rendered in exactly one place, the Watch History panel on Stats, but the rule keeping it from being squeezed by the table beside it was being supplied by two dead copies of the same class in the Journal and M3U Changes stylesheets, neither of which renders that row at all. Those stylesheets load only once you visit their tab and are never unloaded, so whether the filter row held its size depended on your navigation history in that session. The dead copies are deleted and the rule now lives with the row it belongs to, which makes the layout the same on every visit. The Journal entry detail pane's JSON boxes were leaking the same way and are likewise now scoped to Journal. Also removes an unreferenced `ChannelDetail` component that nothing had imported for some time; it was not reachable from the UI and nothing changes on screen.
- **Dev-tooling: the agent-side version-advance guard no longer forces a documentation-only pull request to burn a build number (beads enhancedchannelmanager-uf2gh + enhancedchannelmanager-9ogyd, build 0062).** The `.claude/hooks/version-advance-guard.sh` PreToolUse hook blocks `gh pr create` when the build number in `frontend/package.json` has not advanced past `dev`. It performed no path classification, so it fired on changes that carry no build to advance, while the CI half of the same gate has always skipped them: `.github/workflows/test.yml` gates its build-advance step on `needs.detect.outputs.docs_only != 'true'`. The hook was therefore stricter than the rule it mirrors, which its own header describes as "the agent-side half of the build-advance gate" whose false blocks are "a friction bug, not a safety gap". Bead `w9irb`, which created the guard, had already specified scoping it with the existing docs-only classification; only the CI half ever implemented that, so this was a half-finished bead rather than a new requirement. Measured cost: a two-line `.beads/issues.jsonl` redaction was forced to bump `frontend/package.json`, `backend/main.py` and `backend/routers/backup.py`, consuming a build number that an in-flight branch already held, which is a conflict generator whenever several PRs are open against `dev` at once. The hook now calls `scripts/classify_changed_paths.py`, the same script the CI `detect` job calls, against the same baseline ref it uses for the version comparison, and skips the check on a `docs_only=true` verdict while saying why it skipped. Classification trouble fails open with a loud warning rather than wedging shipping, because CI re-enforces the same rule server-side against the real PR head; a missing or unrecognised value for the `docs_only` key is never read as `false`, so a broken classifier can neither skip the gate nor manufacture a block. That warning is delivered rather than merely emitted: the hook contract sends the stderr of a hook that exits 0 to the debug log alone, so every non-blocking message this guard produces now travels as JSON on stdout, under `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext` for the agent and `systemMessage` for the operator. Without that, the fail-open residual would have degraded in silence, which is the failure shape the by-key extraction removed one layer in. The blocking path is unchanged and deliberately stays stderr-only, because a hook that exits 2 has its stdout ignored. The verdict is read by key out of the classifier's `key=value` output rather than by whole-output equality, so the `docs_site_affected` line added by `t4d5w` below does not silently turn the guard into a permanent no-op. Hook routing remains broad for Bash, and the hook performs one conservative raw-literal candidate test for whitespace-separated `gh pr create` anywhere in command text. It deliberately does not interpret quotes, heredocs, substitutions, tokens or command positions: inert prose may run the repository-state check, an accepted friction direction, while candidate presence can never be narrowed into a silent bypass. Diagnostics describe a possible PR creation command rather than claiming execution. Once a candidate is found, the hook resolves the input `cwd` with `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` and performs repository-state checks only; inline `cd ... && gh pr create` is unsupported because the hook runs before the directory change, and the documented workflow opens PRs from the branch checked out in the main checkout. The hook does not parse `--base main`; the shared version checker already accepts release and hotfix versions without a build suffix. Ships with a fixture-based hook state and raw-candidate harness.
- **A documentation-only pull request now gets a check that actually reads documentation, and a green required check says what it ran (bead enhancedchannelmanager-t4d5w, build 0060).** On a documentation-only change, six of the seven required status checks on `dev` correctly gate their expensive steps off and pass having executed nothing, while `Operator Docs` - the one job that examines documentation - is not a required context. Nothing had exercised that path in practice, because `.claude/hooks/version-advance-guard.sh` blocks `gh pr create` unconditionally, so every pull request carries a version bump and none classifies documentation-only; 15 of the last 40 merged PRs would have without it. The hole is not theoretical: `backend/tests/unit/test_check_em_dashes.py` reads a repository `.md` file as a live fixture, and commit `185a4009` exists because deleting a different `.md` broke `Backend Tests`, caught only because that pull request also touched code. Three additive changes, none of which alters which contexts exist or which jobs emit them. `mkdocs build --strict` now runs on every pull request as a step of the ungated `Operator Docs` job, so a broken user-guide link surfaces on the pull request instead of as a failed Pages deploy after the merge. `Operator Docs` is not a required context, so this does not BLOCK the merge: it parks the pull request at `mergeStateStatus=UNSTABLE`, and the enforcement is the shipper reading the rollup per `docs/shipping.md` section "When `UNSTABLE` is the terminal state". Making it required is a branch-protection change and is deliberately out of scope (absorbs bead `enhancedchannelmanager-pb2s4`; it checks something disjoint from `npm run docs:check`, which passed the link that the strict build caught). `scripts/classify_changed_paths.py` gains a second output, `docs_site_affected`, that `.github/workflows/docs-pages.yml` now reads in place of its own `paths:` filter, so the list of paths the published site is built from has one definition instead of two, and a test derives the check from the `mkdocs.yml` nav so a published page added outside the known prefixes fails the pull request rather than quietly disabling its deploy. Every required job writes one line to the run summary naming what it did - `documentation-only change, the pytest suite was NOT run` versus `ran the backend pytest suite. 2147 tests, 0 failed, 0 errored` - with the counts read from the JUnit report by `scripts/ci_junit_summary.py` rather than asserted. Each line reports the real `steps.<id>.outcome` of the step that does the work, so a job that died at lint cannot claim the test suite ran, and a step that was never reached prints `did not run` rather than a count from a stale report. `docs/shipping.md` step 6 no longer tells the shipper to wait on `gh pr checks --watch`, which reports check conclusions only and is blind to merge conflicts; it now reads `statusCheckRollup` per context alongside `mergeable` and `mergeStateStatus`. CI and documentation plumbing only, with no runtime behaviour change.
- **Edit Mode no longer silently drops a channel staged into a group that is still pending in the same batch, and a partially-failed bulk commit is no longer hidden from the operator (beads enhancedchannelmanager-udq1j + enhancedchannelmanager-vtapf + enhancedchannelmanager-75k49, build 0052).** Drill run 18 (`0.18.1-0051`, Dispatcharr `0.28.2`) staged 12 channels in one Edit Mode session, one of them into a channel group still PENDING in the same batch: 11 channels were created, the 12th vanished, Edit Mode exited normally, and the operator was told nothing. The bulk-commit wire protocol resolves new groups by name (`groupsToCreate` up, `groupIdMap` back), but reaching a pending group through "Create in... -> \<pending group\>" put its negative staging id on the wire verbatim, and Dispatcharr answered `400 {"channel_group_id": ["Invalid pk \"-1000\" - object does not exist."]}`. `buildBulkOperations` now rewrites every reference to a staged group as that group's name, on `createChannel.groupId` before the group-creation phase and on `updateChannel.data.channel_group_id` once `groupIdMap` comes back. The identical defect was waiting on a second path: dragging an existing channel onto a pending group's header. A negative id matching no staged group can no longer be posted at all; the commit refuses to start and leaves the session's staged work intact rather than losing it. `_run_bulk_commit` gains a matching backend backstop, refusing a negative `channel_group_id` from any client by name instead of relaying an opaque upstream 400. Separately, the backend already reported `success=False, applied=11, failed=1` correctly for the dropped channel, but the frontend routed that into a header status pill and closed the dialog anyway; a commit that does not fully apply now holds the Exit Edit Mode dialog open on the applied/failed counts and the errors behind them, and it must be acknowledged before the session clears. "Create new channel group" inside Edit Mode called `api.createChannelGroup` directly: written straight to Dispatcharr, absent from the exit ledger, with no undo entry, and Discard could not take it back. Its catch block only logged, so a duplicate-name `400` from Dispatcharr was invisible. It now stages through `stageCreateGroup` like every other edit, and every staging failure raises a toast. The Exit Edit Mode summary line is also corrected: it previously undercounted ("24 pending changes" over lines summing to 26) because it was hand-totalled instead of derived from the buckets the dialog renders; the headline is now `summary.totalChanges`, and every staged change type has a bucket, so none can go unnamed.
- **The Move Channel to Group dialog can no longer show an enabled Move button that silently does nothing (bead enhancedchannelmanager-gddai, build 0052).** Drill run 18: dragging a channel onto another group's header opened the dialog with neither numbering radio checked while the Move button reported `disabled=false`; clicking it did nothing, every time, whenever the destination group was empty. Two faults compounded: the preselected `'suggested'` option had no backing control when the destination held no numbered channel (`suggestedChannelNumber` was null), and `isMoveButtonEnabled` only guarded the `'custom'` case while `handleMoveButtonClick` guarded both and fell silently out of the switch otherwise, so "enabled" and "will do something" were two different predicates that could disagree. `resolveMoveNumbering` now returns either an executable instruction or an operator-readable reason, and the dialog derives its `disabled` state, its click behaviour and a visible explanation from that single call, so an enabled button that no-ops is no longer expressible.
- **The Channels pane stays populated after a restore, and now has the same manual refresh control the Streams pane already had (bead enhancedchannelmanager-eelgi, build 0052).** Drill run 18 applied a restore whose own modal reported "Channels 12 CREATED"; after closing it, with no page reload, the channel-group filter listed every restored group correctly while the pane behind it read "0 Empty" / "CHANNELS 0", though Dispatcharr demonstrably held all 12. `useServerDataInvalidation` had no `channels` key, so the DBAS restore modals published only `channel-groups`. `channels` is now a key, both restore modals publish it on an applied restore (never on a dry-run preview, which changes nothing), and the Channels pane subscribes and refreshes (skipped while Edit Mode is active so a refetch cannot fight a working copy mid-session). The Channels pane also gains its own refresh control, matching the Streams pane, likewise hidden during Edit Mode.
- **A guide download that finishes after you leave EPG Manager now reaches the Edit Channel guide picker without a page reload (bead enhancedchannelmanager-1twap, build 0052).** Bead `3vtim`'s earlier fix published guide-download completion from EPG Manager's own poller, which only runs while that destination is mounted. Drill run 18 walked the ordinary fresh-install sequence, adding an EPG source and then going to link channels, navigating away from EPG Manager while the guide was still parsing. The guide picker kept its empty startup snapshot: "Guide data has not loaded yet," with zero backend requests, for a source that had already reached `status=success` with 14,663 rows. The watch moves to a module-scope service (`services/epgGuideWatch`) with its detection logic unchanged, plus its own bounded fallback poll (five minutes, only while a watched download is genuinely in flight) so completion is still detected with nobody feeding it rows. No standing polling and no refetch-on-focus outside that window, so bead `5z7c9`'s closing condition still holds.
- **A batch containing nothing but new channel groups no longer leaves Edit Mode stuck open (bead enhancedchannelmanager-ius9c, build 0052).** Follow-on to `vtapf` above, found in review rather than in the drill: new groups travel to the backend in `groupsToCreate`, a phase that contributes nothing to `operationsApplied`. Once "Create new channel group" started staging instead of writing straight through, a batch of nothing but groups (create a group, Done, Apply All, exactly how drill run 18 produced `Drill Empty Group`) came back with `operationsApplied: 0`, which the commit read as "nothing happened" and never reset the session. The groups really had been created; Edit Mode just stayed open holding them under their temp ids. A commit that creates groups now counts as a success.
- **Backup/restore drill run17 fixes three restore-path/UI defects and closes two operator-facing copy gaps (beads enhancedchannelmanager-qka89 + enhancedchannelmanager-ayfn9 + enhancedchannelmanager-3vtim, build 0051).** All facts below were measured live on the published `:dev` image at `0.18.1-0050` against Dispatcharr `0.28.2` during backup/restore drill run `2026-08-08-run17`, then re-verified after the fix in a patched container. **(1) Relink `replace` left a stale `tvg_id` (bead qka89).** Restoring with relink mode `replace` restored a channel's `epg_data_id` but not its `tvg_id`, leaving the channel resolving to one guide row while advertising another. Measured: archived ch103 had `tvg_id='KERA(PBS)(KERA).us'` and `epg_data_id=3425`; the operator changed it to `KERA-DT2(HD06)(KERADT2).us` and `3427`; after `replace` the id came back as `3425` but the `tvg_id` stayed at the operator's `KERA-DT2...` value. The artifact carried the archived value all along, in both `tvg_id` and `epg_data_tvg_id`, so this was a restore-path bug, not a backup gap; ECM's own Edit Channel modal writes both fields together. It matters because `tvg_id` is the EPG matching key and what goes out in M3U/XMLTV, so a later refresh keyed on it could silently undo the restore. `backend/dbas/channel_reattach.py` gains `_relink_payload`, which writes the channel's own archived `tvg_id` alongside the resolved `epg_data_id`, never `epg_data_tvg_id` (the two are allowed to disagree). `preserve` is unchanged and still keeps the operator's values. `tvc_guide_stationid` is deliberately not included: the reattach pass never writes it, so it has no half-restored counterpart to desync. **(2) Delete Group silently did nothing, twice over (bead ayfn9).** Deleting a channel group that still contained channels reported success and changed nothing. The confirm dialog promised the channels would move to "Ungrouped"; ECM never reparented them and issued a bare `DELETE`, which Dispatcharr rejected with `400 {"error":"Cannot delete group with associated channels"}`, and the Edit-Mode bulk commit then reported overall success anyway. Two defects: the missing reparent, and a swallowed per-operation failure. The first fix attempt reparented to `channel_group_id: null` and still failed live, because Dispatcharr rejects that too (`400 {"channel_group_id":["This field may not be null."]}`); a channel cannot be group-less, so "Ungrouped" is a render state ECM can read but never write. `backend/routers/channels.py` now resolves a real destination by name (`Default Group`, never by assuming id 1), reparents the members to it, then deletes; it refuses with an actionable message when no such group exists or when the group being deleted is the destination, and an empty group still deletes with no destination needed. The bulk commit's `success` is now `operationsFailed == 0` unconditionally, so one successful operation can no longer launder a failure beside it. The dialog copy now names the real destination. Verified live: 6 channels reparented to `Default Group`, `DELETE ... 204`, `success=True, applied=1, failed=0`, nothing lost. **(3) Stale client caches made present data look missing (bead 3vtim).** After adding an EPG source that reached `status=success`, the Edit Channel "EPG Data" picker reported "No EPG data found" for guide rows that demonstrably existed (14663 rows including `KERA`), with zero `/api/epg/*` requests reaching the backend during the failure, a stale client cache, not a query bug, until a full reload. The channel-group list had the same problem after a DBAS restore. Both now publish on the existing `useServerDataInvalidation` bus (no polling, no refetch-on-focus, so bead `5z7c9`'s condition still holds); guide-download completion is detected on the rows themselves and fed from all three fetch paths, anchored on `updated_at` so a stale `success` reading cannot satisfy it, and a source already downloading when the tab mounts is adopted rather than missed. `EditChannelModal` also gains a distinct "Guide data has not loaded yet" empty state so an unloaded cache no longer renders as "no match". **(4) Two operator-facing copy fixes.** The restore-complete summary pluralises correctly ("1 account needs ... before it will work"), and the unplayable-channels note now points at `stream_reattach_details` (which already carried the names) instead of promising "each named channel" without saying where. Documentation: `docs/user_guide/backup-restore/run-a-restore-drill.md` was updated in the same change to record the three-step Edit-Mode commit, the type-to-confirm restore apply, the two known-defect notes, and the two-step redacted-artifact recovery.
- **An empty channel group can now be renamed and deleted from Channel Manager, instead of only from Dispatcharr (bead enhancedchannelmanager-o88e9, build 0049).** In **Channel Manager → Edit Mode**, the `⋮` **Group actions** menu on a group header is the only place ECM offers **Rename Group** and **Delete Group**. It was hidden for any group with zero channels, so a group lost both actions the moment its last channel left, and a group created by ECM's own **Create new channel group** button never had them at all. Backup/restore drill run 2026-08-08-run16 measured it on published `:dev` `0.18.1-0047` by reading control state out of the DOM: of three groups in one list, the one holding 7 channels had the menu and the two holding none did not. The empty state is exactly the one an operator needs the menu in. An empty group created by mistake, or left behind after its channels were moved elsewhere, could not be removed from the screen that created it, and the group-deletion step of [Run a restore drill](docs/user_guide/backup-restore/run-a-restore-drill.md) (which has you move a group's channels out first, because Dispatcharr refuses to delete a group that still has channels) dead-ended in ECM and had to be finished in Dispatcharr's UI. The `!isEmpty` guard was inherited: it was written for the standalone "sort streams by quality" button this menu replaced in `0.14.0-0021`, where needing channels was correct, and it was carried over wholesale onto the menu that later absorbed Rename and Delete. The menu is now gated per item rather than as a whole. **Rename Group** and **Delete Group** do not depend on members and are offered on an empty group; **Probe Group**, **Sort Streams** and **Sort & Renumber** do, and stay hidden until the group has some. **Delete Group remains restricted to manually created groups.** That was never a member-count rule, and a group backed by an M3U provider is still not deletable from ECM, empty or not, because the next provider refresh recreates it.
- **A restore that completed with warnings no longer reports itself as failed to scripts and log greps (bead enhancedchannelmanager-bdmby, build 0048).** Backup/restore drill run 2026-08-08-run16 measured a DBAS restore that ended `completed_with_failures` with zero failed items in every category and nothing rolled back. Its task-history row said `completed_with_warnings`, its alert was a `warning`, and the restore dialog said "nothing was rolled back". But `GET /api/tasks/dbas_restore` published `progress.status: "failed"` beside `failed_count: 0`, and the log carried `task_scheduler [dbas_restore] Task failed: ...` one line away from `task_engine [dbas_restore] Task completed in a degraded state: ...` for the same event. `progress.status` is the field a script polls to know a restore has finished, and `Task failed` is a string the troubleshooting guide tells operators to grep for, so both gave a false positive on every degraded restore. The live progress status and the finalized progress notification now come from the same single derivation as the history row, so the three cannot disagree, and the scheduler logs "Task completed with warnings" for a run that completed and kept its state. `failed` stays reserved for runs that genuinely failed: a rolled-back restore, an orchestration error, or an exception. A cancelled run now reports `cancelled` rather than `failed`.
- **A required CI check can no longer report green from a job that ran nothing (bead enhancedchannelmanager-5rwzy, build 0047).** `dev` and `main` branch protection require seven status checks. Until now two workflows could emit the same name on one commit: `test.yml` ran on `paths-ignore: ['**.md', '.beads/**']`, and a sentinel workflow, `docs-only-pass.yml`, ran on `paths: ['**.md', '.beads/**']` with jobs named exactly those seven whose only step was an `echo`. Those filters look like complements and are not. A pull request touching both code and Markdown matches both, so every required context existed twice: once real, once permanently green. Every shipped change carries a `CHANGELOG.md` entry, so that is the shape of nearly every pull request in this repo. It went live on PR #797, where `Backend Tests` reported **`failure` and `success` on the same commit**; the failure was a genuine test defect and was caught only because the reviewer read each instance of the context individually rather than the aggregate. The fix is one source of truth per context. Neither `test.yml` nor `build.yml` has a path filter any more, so both run on every pull request and each required name is emitted exactly once. A new `detect` job in each classifies the changed file set through `scripts/classify_changed_paths.py`, which now holds the only definition of the documentation-only rule. Every job carrying a required name **always runs** and gates its expensive **steps** on that classification, so a genuinely documentation-only change does a cheap no-op and passes honestly instead of being covered for by a stub. `docs-only-pass.yml` is deleted. Two deliberate non-choices, both recorded in `docs/testing.md`: skipping the duplicate job would not have worked, because GitHub counts a skipped job as satisfying a required check, and making the duplicate fail would have blocked every mixed pull request. The classifier fails **open**: any input it cannot classify is treated as code, because green-without-running is the only dangerous direction. CI plumbing only, with no runtime behaviour change.
- **Restoring onto an install that already has channels now tells you which channels the backup puts in a different group — and "Replace" puts them back (beads enhancedchannelmanager-r1ei7 / -3t74w / -tddmw).** Every restore drill before run 12 (2026-08-07) restored onto a freshly-wiped target, where every channel is created and the destination has no opinion to disagree with. Run 12 restored the same artifact onto a *populated* target and found three things a clean-looking report was hiding. **(1) Channel grouping was never reconciled and never mentioned.** A channel that already exists is matched and deliberately never overwritten, so its group was never written: seven channels stayed in whatever group the target had them in, in *both* relink modes, while the restore reported `success` with `0 failed` and counts that reconciled exactly. The relink choice now covers grouping as well as guide data and logos: **Keep their current guide data, logos, and grouping** reports every channel whose group differs — naming the channel, the group it is in, and the group the backup assigns it — and still changes nothing; **Replace their guide data, logos, and grouping with the backup's** additionally moves each one back, and still lists what it moved. The count is predicted on the preview, so the decision can be made before applying rather than read about afterwards. And if you leave the **Channel groups** category *unticked*, the grouping check cannot run at all — so the restore now says so in as many words, in the results panel and in the task-history line, rather than showing a zero that looks like a clean bill of health. **(2) A same-named group is no longer reported as "identical".** A channel group carries nothing but a name, and its contents live on the channels, which are restored after it — so at match time there is nothing to compare. The restore still adopts a destination group on its name (that is the only identity a group has across two installs, and refusing would strand every channel pointing at it), but the report now says *matched by name* instead of asserting an "identical" it never checked. **(3) The preview no longer omits the Streams category.** The apply reported `Streams 9 CREATED` while the preview showed no Streams row at all — not zero, absent. Streams genuinely cannot be predicted (they are matched against provider streams that only arrive during the restore itself), so the row now renders as **not predicted** with an explanation, and the Channel groups row carries a note saying its create/skip split can differ from the apply for the same reason.
- **A saved setting, an uploaded logo and a freshly created backup now appear where they belong, without reloading the page (bead enhancedchannelmanager-5z7c9, build 0039).** Drill run 9 hit the same defect three times in one session, and each time the screen said a write had not happened when it had. **(1)** Saving the Dispatcharr connection left the Settings → General summary card reading *"Server URL: Not configured / Auth Method: Username & Password"* while `GET /api/settings` was already returning the saved URL and `auth_method: api_key`. **(2)** A logo uploaded through Logo Manager appeared in Logo Manager and landed in Dispatcharr's `/data/logos/`, but the Edit Channel logo picker kept offering 12 options instead of 13, and searching the new logo's name found nothing. **(3)** An encrypted backup created from the Encrypted Backup card was written and announced with a success toast, yet the Saved Backups list on the same page kept showing only the previous artifact — including after navigating to another settings section and coming back. All three needed a full page reload. One root cause, not three: ECM keeps server data in whichever component renders it, refetched on that component's own mount, and there was no way for a component performing a write to tell a *different* component that its copy had gone stale. The connection modal is rendered both by the Settings tab and by App (which opens it unprompted on a first run) and only one of those refreshed the card; Logo Manager owns its own paged list while the picker reads App's catalogue; the Encrypted Backup card and the Saved Backups list are siblings that had never been introduced. There is now one small invalidation channel: a component that mutates something publishes the affected key, and the components holding a copy refetch. Deliberately **not** a cache, **not** polling, and **not** refetch-on-focus — trading a cosmetic staleness bug for standing background load against Dispatcharr would be the worse deal. A failed mutation publishes nothing, and a plain page view still issues exactly the fetches it always did. The third instance is the one that mattered most: the action an operator repeats when they think a backup failed is "create another credential-bearing encrypted backup".
- **The M3U password field no longer tells you to "leave blank to keep current" when there is no current password to keep (bead enhancedchannelmanager-tsbdq, build 0039).** A standard (redacted) backup deliberately carries no provider credentials, so after restoring one the M3U account's password is genuinely empty — which is correct, is exactly why the documented Step 6a recovery exists, and is named account-by-account and field-by-field in the restore report's `credentials_needing_reentry`. The edit form still showed the generic *"Leave blank to keep current"* hint, advising the one action that guarantees the account keeps failing to authenticate, in precisely the situation the recovery flow puts the operator in. When the account holds no stored password the field now says **"No stored password — enter it"** and carries a warning-level note explaining that a redacted backup does not carry provider credentials, so the empty field is expected and must be filled. When a password really is stored the wording is unchanged, because there it is correct. The signal is the account's own `password` field, which is truthful rather than a redaction sentinel — the drill's inventory captures record it `present` with a stable fingerprint before the backup and `absent` after a redacted restore, on the same account.
- **A backup that archived fifteen categories and missed one no longer reports "0 ok, 1 failed" (bead enhancedchannelmanager-fexq1, build 0038).** When a Dispatcharr category cannot be fetched, the backup is still built, verified and restorable — it is just missing that one category, and the run is reported as a warning naming it. But the numbers alongside that warning were a boolean wearing the shape of item counts: one total item, and one of `ok`/`failed` set to 1. So the Journal row, the task-history row and the progress notification all read **`0 ok, 1 failed`** for a run that produced a perfectly usable artifact, which reads as total failure to anyone scanning at a glance — the same problem the degraded-backup warning was introduced to solve, one surface over. The counts now mean what they say: the item is a **category**, so a backup that archived 15 of 16 reports `15 ok, 1 failed`, and two degraded categories are two failed items rather than one degraded run rounded to a boolean. Even the worst case is honest — when Dispatcharr is entirely unreachable, the local settings, rules and profiles that *did* archive are counted as the successes they are instead of the whole artifact being written off. A clean backup's completion notification now names the unit too ("Backup artifact built. All 16 categories archived in 1.8s" rather than "1 items processed"). Severity is deliberately unchanged and pinned by tests: a clean backup still reports success, a degraded one still reports a warning, and a failed cloud upload still reports an error — the counts got more honest without moving what any run is called.
- **A restore that completed no longer announces itself as "Task Failed", a single failed logo is no longer counted as two, and the restore-complete screen now tells you when nothing can play (beads enhancedchannelmanager-k2r7m + enhancedchannelmanager-cwmid + enhancedchannelmanager-d0bd3, build 0036).** Three operator-facing reporting defects found by the backup/restore round-trip drill on build 0035, all in the exact surface an operator reads to decide what to do next. **(1) One failed logo counted twice.** Two independent passes report the same lost logo — the importer, which knows the logo's name, and the channel-reattach pass that then cannot resolve the reference and only has the archive id. Both reports were correct and both were appended, so a restore whose category counts read `FAILED 1` announced "2 logo(s) could not be reinstated" in the same sentence, sending the operator hunting for a second broken logo that did not exist. Reports of one archived logo now merge into one row keyed on the archive id, keeping the operator-facing name over the synthesized `logo #13 (archived)` label and the union of the affected channels; a miss with no archive id still gets its own row, because under-counting a real loss is the failure this surface exists to prevent. **(2) The severity ordering was inverted.** A restore degraded only by that non-fatal logo miss alerted as `error` / "Task Failed: DBAS Restore", while a restore in which **not one of twelve channels could play** correctly alerted as a warning — same `outcome` value, opposite severity, and the louder one was the cosmetic case. "Task Failed" was also untrue: the restore completed and rolled nothing back, and the docs warn that re-running a restore after a failed attempt starts from an unknown state, so the false alarm could push an operator into a genuinely risky action. The severity now follows the outcome itself — a restore that reached `completed_with_failures` raises a **warning** regardless of which non-fatal category degraded, and `error` is reserved for the two rolled-back/indeterminate outcomes. **(3) The restore-complete screen never said "nothing can play".** The report correctly carried 12 of 12 channels with no playable stream and named every one, and the dialog rendered only the credentials panel and the count grid — while the documentation tells operators to read those panels instead of re-deriving the information by hand. A new panel sits beside the credentials one, naming the affected channels and keeping the two populations apart: channels holding a leftover placeholder slot alongside a real stream still play and are tidy-up, while channels with no URL-bearing stream at all cannot play and are the reason the instance is unusable. **(4) The history row and the alert contradicted each other about the same run (bead enhancedchannelmanager-fexq1).** A run's severity was re-derived at four separate points from two fields that do not carry it — `success` and `failed_count` — so they disagreed: the degraded restore above alerted correctly as a warning while its task-history row read `status: "failed"`, `success: false`, its Journal line read `Failed DBAS Restore`, and the History panel had to guess the middle state in the browser from `success && failed_count > 0` — a guess that cannot see a degraded run whose counts are clean. There is now one derivation (`task_outcome`) producing an explicit outcome — success, warning, error or cancelled — and the notification, the Journal row and the stored history row all map from it. The history vocabulary gains `completed_with_warnings` (migration 0042 widens the column to hold it); the Journal leads with the severity instead of writing `Completed X: 0 ok, 1 failed` beside `success: true`; and the restore modals, which polled for `completed` or `failed` only, accept it too — without that a degraded restore would have polled until its retries ran out and reported "Restore failed" for a restore that completed. Deliberately unchanged: the `ecm_task_schedule_last_success_timestamp` gauge behind the stale-schedule alerts keeps its existing trigger, so a degraded run still does not reset the staleness clock and cannot mask a task that has stopped running. Rows written by earlier builds keep rendering exactly as they did.
- **After restoring a standard backup, re-entering your provider password and refreshing now actually fixes your channels — no second restore needed (beads enhancedchannelmanager-2o0cz + enhancedchannelmanager-juu3c, build 0033).** A standard (redacted) backup carries no provider credentials, so the restore has nothing to attach channels to and leaves every one of them on a placeholder. Re-entering the credential and refreshing the M3U account is the obvious next move — and until now it changed nothing you could see: the real streams appeared *beside* the placeholders and not one channel was rebound, because the reattach pass ran only once, during the restore itself, before the credential existed. Recovering actually required running the entire restore a second time, which nothing told you. A completed M3U refresh now reattaches any channel still holding a leftover placeholder, so the recovery is: **restore → re-enter the credential and refresh.** Measured end to end: 12 channels went from unplayable to all playing after the refresh alone, with the leftover placeholder streams and their synthetic account cleaned up in the same pass. Only ECM's own placeholder streams are ever touched — a custom stream you created yourself is never rebound or removed. Covers the Refresh button and the scheduled refresh task; a refresh-all, or one done in Dispatcharr's own UI, is picked up on the next scheduled refresh rather than instantly. Separately, a dry-run summary now uses future tense throughout — it previously reported profile memberships as "corrected" for changes it would only make on apply.
- **The restore preview now tells you what the apply will actually do, and a restore no longer leaves dead placeholder streams behind (bead enhancedchannelmanager-dgnms, build 0032).** Previewing a restore is the step operators are told to trust before committing, but its health numbers were read off work the preview never does. On a fresh target a dry run reported **0 logos to reattach** where the apply reattached 11, **0 channels needing attention** where the apply reported 12 unplayable, and **0 profile memberships to correct** where the apply corrected 6 — confident zeroes derived from having looked at nothing. The per-category create/update/skip counts were always accurate and are unchanged. Now the logo split and the profile-membership count are genuinely predicted and match the apply exactly; and the two stream-health counters, which cannot be known before the apply's deferred M3U refresh materialises the provider streams, report **null — "not predicted"** rather than a misleading `0`. Separately, every redacted restore cycle used to leave placeholder streams behind: cleanup only removed placeholders created by the current run, so ones stranded by an earlier restore accumulated forever (14 of them, attached to nothing, after a single round trip). A restore now also removes placeholder streams that sit on ECM's own synthetic account, carry no URL, and are referenced by no channel — and drops that synthetic account once nothing is left under it, regardless of which run created it. Nothing an operator created themselves is ever touched.
- **A restore that finishes with warnings no longer also shows you a red error, and the encrypted-backup options are finally discoverable over the API (beads enhancedchannelmanager-asf3n + enhancedchannelmanager-sdpzy, build 0031).** A single task run touches two notification records — the completion notification, and the progress notification created when the run starts and re-typed when it ends — and each decided its own severity. Only one of them knew about the "completed with warnings" rule, so a DBAS restore that degraded showed an operator a **warning and an error side by side for the same event**, with nothing to say which was authoritative. Both now read from one shared severity map, so a run reports exactly one verdict; the wording of each notification is unchanged. Separately, `GET /api/tasks/dbas_backup/parameter-schema` reported *"No configurable parameters"* even though the nested `parameters` body of a manual run is the **only** way to produce an encrypted, credential-bearing backup — and sending those fields at the top level instead is silently ignored, handing back a plain artifact to a caller who believes otherwise. The task now declares them and the endpoint surfaces them under a separate **`run_parameters`** key. They are deliberately kept out of `parameters`: that list is the schedule form and is persisted with the schedule, and a stored passphrase would make every unattended run emit an encrypted credential-bearing artifact.
- **A channel whose streams' names differ only in capitalisation now restores completely, and a repeat restore no longer reports a clean bill of health over a channel that cannot play (beads enhancedchannelmanager-ixdaw + enhancedchannelmanager-oebpv, build 0029).** The restore's exact-name matching tiers compare case-folded names, so a destination pair differing only in case was a single match to them: a channel archived with `TX | DALLAS | PBS KERA` and `TX | Dallas | PBS KERA` had **both** slots handed the same stream while the byte-identical match for the other sat unused. On an encrypted backup that silently cost the channel a stream; on a standard backup the duplicate made Dispatcharr reject the entire channel update (`unique_channel_stream`) and the channel would not play at all. Each exact-name tier now prefers a candidate whose raw name matches byte-for-byte before falling back to case-folded comparison — a restore with no such pair behaves exactly as before. Separately, the "does this channel still play?" audit only ever examined placeholders created by the *current* restore, so a channel left stranded by an **earlier** restore was invisible: a repeat run reported zero channels needing attention while one answered HTTP 500. The audit now covers every restored channel, judged on what it is actually left holding, and names the offending stream properly instead of `<unknown>`. A channel holding a leftover placeholder alongside a working stream still reports success, unchanged. Verified end-to-end on a disposable stack: the full backup → destroy → restore round trip now reproduces the instance with **zero** inventory drift, and the affected channel comes back with all three streams in their archived order and plays on both backup formats.
- **A restored channel carrying two similarly-named streams is no longer left unplayable (bead enhancedchannelmanager-ixdaw, build 0026).** Found on the 2026-08-05 backup/restore round-trip drill (run 3, ECM 0.18.1-0024 against Dispatcharr 0.28.2), reproduced on both artifact variants. Eleven of twelve channels played; the twelfth — the only one with more than one stream — came back bound to three empty placeholder streams and returned an error instead of video, while the restore still reported `success` with zero failures. The seed that triggered it was three streams named `TX | Dallas | PBS KERA`, `TX | DALLAS | PBS KERA` and `TX | Austin | PBS KLRU`: the first two differ **only** in capitalization. When the restore re-runs its stream matcher after your provider's streams have loaded, it compares names case-insensitively — correctly — so both of those slots were pointed at the same provider stream. Dispatcharr does not allow a channel to hold the same stream twice and rejected the update outright, and because that update is all-or-nothing, ECM's own error handling then put the channel back on placeholders **including the KLRU slot that had matched perfectly well**. One ambiguous stream cost the whole channel. The rebind now tracks which streams a channel has already been given — including ones the restore had matched for real earlier in the run — and when a second slot resolves to a stream already taken, that one slot keeps its placeholder and is listed in the restore report's "needs a stream reattached" section like any other unmatched stream. The channel's other streams rebind normally and it plays, the archived stream order is unchanged, and the one ambiguous slot is a named, one-click fix in the UI rather than a dead channel. Re-running the restore no longer helps or hurts — it was previously the same collision every time. The operator workaround documented for build 0025 is no longer needed.
- **Channel EPG links now survive a restore, and a restore no longer resets the guide data and logos of channels you already had (bead enhancedchannelmanager-dfkbn).** The second half of the round-trip drill's `dfkbn` finding. Run 2 (ECM 0.18.1-0023, Dispatcharr 0.28.2) measured all 7 EPG-linked channels coming back with no guide link on both artifact variants, honestly reported as `epg_links_unrestored: 7` but still lost. **The cause was in the BACKUP, not the restore.** A Dispatcharr EPG row's id is instance-local and is re-minted whenever the destination re-downloads its guide, so the restore correctly relinks by `tvg_id` instead. But a channel's OWN `tvg_id` field is not the link, and ECM's own channel API leaves it null when it sets a guide link (verified directly: `PATCH {epg_data_id: 1}` leaves `tvg_id` null). Every archived channel therefore reached the restore with nothing to match on. The backup now resolves each linked channel's guide row at gather time and records THAT row's `tvg_id` alongside the link, in one bounded read for the whole export rather than one per channel. Old backups are unaffected and restore exactly as before; new backups stay readable by older ECM versions. **Second, a choice about channels you already have.** Making the link restorable also made it reach channels a restore MATCHES rather than creates, so restoring into a live, populated install to recover something else (channel-profile membership, say) would have silently reset the guide link and logo of every matched channel to the backup's view, with no way to undo it. The restore dialog now asks, defaulting to **keeping your channels as they are**; replacing them is an explicit choice. On an empty target every channel is created and the two options do exactly the same thing, so disaster recovery is unaffected. The preview reports that split before you commit to anything: how many links land on channels the restore creates versus how many would replace one you already had, naming those channels. It is careful about what a preview can honestly know. It never claims a guide link or a logo is lost, because on a fresh target the guide and the logos are things the restore itself puts there, and a preview that resolved against the not-yet-restored state would report a working restore as a total failure. **Third, backups now tell you when a link cannot come back**: a channel pointing at a guide entry that no longer exists is counted in the backup's task result rather than only logged, and a guide read that hit its size ceiling says so distinctly instead of reporting perfectly good links as broken. That count is informational and does not mark the backup as degraded: a dangling guide reference is common and usually nothing you can act on.
- **Uploaded channel logos are now backed up, a logo failure no longer destroys the whole restore, and the restore preview stops inventing logo failures (beads enhancedchannelmanager-xb58a, enhancedchannelmanager-d0agi, enhancedchannelmanager-dgnms).** Found on the 2026-08-04 backup/restore round-trip drill, which surfaced one chain of defects with three separate causes. **(1) The backup never carried the image.** A logo you upload through ECM's Logo Manager is written to *Dispatcharr's* `/data/logos/`, because Dispatcharr is ECM's source of truth for logos; the artifact's binary subtree only ever collected ECM's own (in practice empty) `/config/uploads/logos/`, so `binary/metadata.json` read `{"logo_count": 0}` on an instance that had an uploaded logo assigned to a channel, and that logo could not be restored on any artifact variant. The premise behind that design, that the bytes were unreachable, was false, and the drill proved it by accident: fetching the logo over Dispatcharr's own cache endpoint with the API key ECM already holds returned a byte-identical copy. The backup now fetches the bytes of every Dispatcharr-hosted logo at gather time and archives them with their filename, one payload at a time through a temp spool so memory stays bounded. Genuinely remote CDN logos are unchanged: they round-trip from their archived URL (verified 10 of 10 byte-identical) and archiving their bytes would be waste. **(2) One unwritable image rolled back the entire restore.** A logo failure was classified as fatal, so a restore that had already created 44 entities was aborted and compensating-rolled-back because a single image could not be written. The drill lost an M3U account, an EPG source, channel groups, a profile, 12 channels and 14 streams to one missing picture. Logos join Dispatcharr users as a non-fatal category: a logo that cannot be restored is counted, named in the report, and the restore completes as *Completed with failures* with nothing rolled back. Nothing else in a restore depends on a logo, and logos run last, so a logo failure could only ever destroy work that had already succeeded. **(3) The preview cried wolf.** The dry run skipped the URL re-create path entirely, so it reported all 11 archived logos as `validation_error: unsafe or empty logo filename` for an artifact whose apply then restored 10 of them, and buried the one genuinely unrestorable logo in ten invented failures that read identically. An operator following the product's own "preview first, always" guidance would reasonably have aborted a restore that was going to work. The preview now simulates the same decision the apply makes and its per-category counts match. Also fixed alongside these: a restored logo is no longer reported to you as lost (the red "N logos are missing after this restore" banner and the summary line now count only logos that actually failed to come back, with the definition written down in one place for all three producers); a stale copy in ECM's own uploads directory can no longer shadow the authoritative Dispatcharr image and silently leave a channel on the wrong artwork; and a backup that could not fetch some logo bytes is reported as a degraded, warning-level run naming the count, instead of a clean success.
- **DBAS restore round-trip drill: six defects that let a "success" restore leave channels unplayable, credentials broken, and settings/logos/EPG links silently dropped (beads enhancedchannelmanager-lvfwd, enhancedchannelmanager-y65si, enhancedchannelmanager-6pilh, enhancedchannelmanager-2o0cz, enhancedchannelmanager-dfkbn, enhancedchannelmanager-cytzj, build 0023).** A round-trip drill (bead a429n, ECM 0.18.1-0022 / Dispatcharr 0.28.2) restored onto a genuinely fresh Dispatcharr and reported "Restore success: created 32, failed 0" for an instance where nothing played. **Two P0 whole-restore aborts** (lvfwd, y65si): a stream profile bound to a custom user agent 400'd the entire restore because user agents imported AFTER stream profiles with the agent id sent unremapped — user agents now import first and the FK remaps through the same table every cross-category reference uses; and a Dispatcharr user-create failure (missing password field) 500'd and rolled the whole restore back — user creation now sends a fresh random password requiring an operator reset, and a user-row failure is the one category that no longer aborts the run (new `RestoreOutcome.COMPLETED_WITH_FAILURES`). **Redacted-artifact credential corruption** (6pilh): restoring a STANDARD (redact-by-default) artifact wrote the literal `***REDACTED***` string into the destination's M3U/EPG password field — worse than leaving it empty, since it reads as "configured" — now stripped at import, with the affected field named in a new `credentials_needing_reentry` report section. **Nothing playing** (2o0cz): the M3U account's enabled-group selection didn't round-trip (a group that was merely enabled, not auto-syncing, restored to PENDING SETUP with 0 groups and never ingested anything), and archived streams synthesized as URL-less placeholders were never rebound once the deferred M3U refresh ran — both fixed, with whatever still can't be resolved now counted and named instead of silently passing. **Silent data loss** (dfkbn): logos, EPG links, channel-profile membership, and ECM's own `settings.json` (timezone, poll interval) were all dropped by the restore with nothing counted as a failure or a skip. Logos, profile membership and ECM settings are restored by this build, and a miss is now reported by name instead of read as zero. **EPG links were NOT fixed by this build.** A second drill run on 0.18.1-0023 measured all 7 linked channels still restoring with no guide link, correctly reported as `epg_links_unrestored`. See the separate EPG-link entry above for the fix that lands it. **Encryption leaking into scheduled runs** (cytzj): a one-off "Create Encrypted Backup" left the passphrase / credentials-included / unrecoverable-acknowledged flags live on the backup task's singleton instance, so every LATER run — including an unattended scheduled one — silently produced an encrypted, credential-bearing artifact under that same passphrase until the container restarted; the transients now reset after every run, success or failure.
- **Dispatcharr developer API docs corrected three stale/missing contracts (bead enhancedchannelmanager-r9oqx, build 0021).** `docs/dispatcharr_api.md` and `docs/architecture.md` said the schema endpoint was `/swagger.json`, "returns YAML despite the name" — wrong for Dispatcharr 0.28.2, where the real endpoint is `GET /api/schema/` (drf-spectacular, YAML by default; `?format=json` is required). This staleness sat behind three shipped bugs before anyone re-checked it against a live instance. Also newly documented: the `/api/core/settings/` list endpoint and its integer-pk-keyed detail route (the `q6xjl` 404 root cause), the list→key→id resolution pattern ECM now uses (one GET per run, bare-list vs. DRF-paginated-envelope handling, a 20-page cap, and a drop-don't-guess rule for unusable rows), and that `/api/dvr/rules/` does not exist on any Dispatcharr version — the real recurring-rules resource is `/api/channels/recurring-rules/` (`lsa0s`), series rules live inside the `dvr_settings` core-setting, and `/api/channels/dvr/comskip-config/` exists but returns only `{path, exists}` on GET and isn't a usable backup source. Added a "how to verify a path against the live schema" note citing the project's recorded fixtures as the in-repo source of truth.
- **Upload-path DBAS restore no longer applies with one click — it now requires typing the uploaded file's name in a confirm dialog, matching the saved-backups path (bead enhancedchannelmanager-d20jf, build 0020).** Follow-on F from the q6xjl investigation (the missing confirmation dialog was called out but explicitly not covered by the q6xjl fix itself, build 0014). `DbasRestoreSavedModal` has gated its "Apply these changes" action behind a `TypeToConfirmDialog` since build 0019 (bead rzhid); `DbasRestoreModal` — the operator-uploaded-file path — never had one (`git log -S TypeToConfirmDialog` on that file was empty; the guardrail was never present, not removed). Two entry points called `start(true)` directly with no confirmation: the post-dry-run "Apply these changes" button, and the configure-step "Apply" radio + primary button, which could skip the dry run entirely with only a static warning banner. Both are now gated behind the same `TypeToConfirmDialog`, requiring the operator to type the uploaded file's name (the saved path's equivalent token is the artifact filename; the upload path has no server-side filename until the file is saved, but the `File` object's own name is present and stable for the whole modal lifetime, so it plays the identical role). The configure-step trigger button reads "Apply restore…" (ellipsis) rather than "Apply restore" so it cannot be confused with the confirm dialog's own "Apply restore" button once both are on screen. Preview (dry run) is unaffected — still one click, no confirmation required.
- **DBAS backup no longer reports a clean SUCCESS when a Dispatcharr category couldn't be fetched — it's now a named "Completed with Warnings" (bead enhancedchannelmanager-zt3kf, build 0019).** Follow-on D from the q6xjl investigation. `_gather_dispatcharr_sections` has always converted an upstream fetch failure into a `{"_warning": ...}` stub written into the artifact in place of that category's real data — but nothing downstream inspected it, so a backup missing an entire category (e.g. every `dvr_rules.yaml` on a run where Dispatcharr errored) still reported a clean `TaskResult(success=True, failed_count=0)`, both in Task History and in the completion notification. The gather is now also isolated per category as an explicit contract rather than an accident of the artifact builder's one-key-at-a-time call pattern: each Dispatcharr section's upstream fetch is wrapped in its OWN try/except, so one failing endpoint degrades only that section, never its siblings requested in the same export call (the legacy `/export?sections=a,b,c` path could previously lose ALL of `a,b,c` to one failing fetch). `build_backup_artifact` now reports which categories degraded on the returned artifact; `DbasBackupTask` threads that into a WARNING-level result (`success=True`, `failed_count>0`) that reaches task_engine's existing "Completed with Warnings" branch — reusing that branch, not a new notification path — with a message naming the degraded category/categories so an operator never has to open the artifact to find out what's missing. A clean gather is unaffected (unchanged clean-success envelope).
