Separately, the PO reaffirmed the DBAS restore rollback policy (parked note from q6xjl): a settings-key `DEPENDENCY_UNRESOLVED` failure still aborts the WHOLE restore and triggers full rollback like any other category failure — no per-key skip-with-warning — and the orchestrator now documents that rule's rationale and adds an explicit report note after such a rollback stating that a retry against the same destination cannot succeed (the archive references a settings key the destination doesn't have; the fix is to edit category selection or restore against a different destination). Two documentation-only parked notes also land: a one-line comment on the duplicate-settings-key last-write-wins assumption in `get_core_setting_id_map`, and an enum-reuse annotation on `FailureReason.DEPENDENCY_UNRESOLVED` noting its settings-key-absent reuse (no new enum value).
- **Operator user guide: defect-sweep fixes from doc-test runs 2-3 land, validated three independent ways (build 0018).** The documentation fixes queued behind runs 2 and 3 of the operator-guide test (`~/ecm/doc-test-runs/2026-08-02-run2/`, run 3's targeted re-verification of those fixes) are shipped as-is per PO decision 2026-08-03. Validation: run 3 re-walked each fixed article and confirmed the specific defect was gone; run 4 then performed a naive full walk with no foreknowledge of what had changed and found the day-one path clean of defects (a first for the series) and a 102-page site-link sweep clean; and `mkdocs build --strict` passes against this exact tree. Three MINOR/MAJOR findings from run 4 itself — a missing Result line on the encrypted-backup walkthrough, a fresh-install no-op in the normalization first-rule article (all 8 shipped rule groups start disabled, so "see what your rules do" has nothing to show), and a Built-in/Custom label mismatch — are deliberately **not** fixed here; they're tracked in bead `enhancedchannelmanager-63lik` for a follow-up pass.
- **Backups no longer ship an empty "DVR Rules" category — ECM was asking Dispatcharr for a URL that doesn't exist (bead enhancedchannelmanager-lsa0s, build 0017).** The third defect from the same doc-test run as q6xjl. ECM fetched DVR rules from `/api/dvr/rules/`, a path guessed when the category was built and never live-verified. Dispatcharr 0.28.2 has no such route, and — because the miss lands on the frontend's single-page-app catch-all rather than the API router — it answered **200 with an HTML page** instead of a 404. The JSON parse then died on the first byte, and the backup builder converted that into a `_warning: Dispatcharr not connected` stub for the category, on **every backup run**: the artifact looked complete, and the DVR Rules category inside it was empty every time. Restoring one silently restored nothing, and a restore *to* Dispatcharr would have failed 405 on the same dead path. The category is now bound to Dispatcharr's real DVR-rule resource, `/api/channels/recurring-rules/` (a recurring recording rule: channel, weekdays, start/end time, date window, enabled flag), which matches what the restore importer already expected — one `channel` foreign key remapped through the restored channels, name-based collision detection, and delete-based rollback. Two neighbouring DVR surfaces are deliberately **not** in this category, both to avoid restoring the same state twice or restoring state that cannot mean anything on the destination: **series rules**, which Dispatcharr stores inside the `dvr_settings` row that the Core Settings category already backs up and restores; and **recordings**, which are individual scheduled or completed recording instances pinned to absolute timestamps and to a media file on the source instance's disk. Three further corrections ship with it. (1) Unnamed rules are now matched by their schedule (channel + weekdays + clock window + date range + enabled state) rather than by name alone — Dispatcharr leaves a rule's name optional, so restoring the same artifact twice used to duplicate every unnamed rule. The enabled state is part of that identity on purpose: an archived rule that is switched *on* is not satisfied by a switched-*off* rule already occupying the same slot on the destination, so restoring it never silently leaves your recordings disabled. (2) A rule whose channel didn't survive to the destination is now reported the same way by a dry-run preview and by a real apply — same `DEPENDENCY_UNRESOLVED` reason, same message; the preview previously called it a skip while the apply called it a failure (the same preview-lies class as y6zg6). (3) A code comment claiming Dispatcharr has no separate comskip endpoint was wrong — `/api/channels/dvr/comskip-config/` exists — but it is still not a backup source: its GET returns only whether a custom `comskip.ini` exists and where, never the file's contents, and its POST takes a file upload. Comskip **settings** continue to come from the core-settings namespace exactly as before. **Existing backups keep restoring**: an artifact taken before this fix carries the warning stub instead of a rule list, and now decodes to an empty DVR Rules category (a reported "0") rather than a gap or an error, with every other category unaffected. Every endpoint claim above is pinned by a recorded live capture from Dispatcharr 0.28.2 (`backend/tests/fixtures/dispatcharr_dvr_recurring_rules_recorded.json`), including what the dead path actually returned.
- **DBAS restore settings hardening: bounded pagination on the core-settings id map, a missing PATCH-failure test, exception-typed resolver logging, and imprecise dry-run wording (bead enhancedchannelmanager-kahzn, build 0016).** Four small follow-ons from the PR #765/#766 review rounds. (1) `get_core_setting_id_map` now fetches every page of a DRF-paginated response to completion instead of reading only page 1 — a non-null `next` is treated as a more-pages signal (never dereferenced as a URL; the client re-requests the same endpoint with an incrementing page number, per this client's path-only request convention), bounded at 20 pages and raising loudly if exceeded rather than silently resolving a partial map (the live recorded response is a bare unpaginated list today; this guards a future/larger settings list). (2) Added the missing regression pin for a settings-category PATCH that fails *after* successful id resolution: that key alone fails `UPSTREAM_API_ERROR`, other keys in the same blob still apply — the exact original q6xjl failure text had no dedicated test. (3) The resolver's fetch-failure warning in `dbas/importers/settings_agents.py` now includes the exception's TYPE name (never its message text) so an operator can distinguish timeout vs 5xx vs unreachable at 3 AM, without any new key-name/URL exposure. (4) The resolver fetch-failure per-key message no longer overclaims "...could not be applied" when the failure happens during a dry-run preview (nothing was ever attempted to apply at that point) — reworded to "...could not be resolved/applied," the same string used on apply (message parity, single source).
- **DBAS restore dry-run preview now reports WOULD-FAIL for a settings key that doesn't resolve on the destination, instead of silently certifying WOULD-UPDATE (bead enhancedchannelmanager-y6zg6, build 0015).** Follow-on to the q6xjl fix directly below: that fix corrected the *apply* path (resolve each `core_settings`/`comskip` key against the destination's own row id before PATCHing), but the counts-only dry-run branch still never contacted upstream at all — it unconditionally incremented `would_update` for every safe key, which is exactly how the original q6xjl incident's preview certified "Settings 7 WILL UPDATE / 0 FAILED" for an apply that then failed 7/7. The dry-run branch now reuses the SAME run-scoped `CoreSettingIdResolver` the apply path uses (still one `GET /api/core/settings/` for the whole run, shared across both blobs) and resolves each safe key before deciding would-update vs would-fail: a key present on the destination is WOULD-UPDATE; a key absent is WOULD-FAIL with the identical `DEPENDENCY_UNRESOLVED` reason and wording the apply path reports, so the preview and the apply verdict can never again disagree. A resolver fetch failure fails every key `UPSTREAM_API_ERROR` on preview too (fail-closed beats a lying preview). Denylisted (credential/auth/instance-identity) keys are unchanged — still would-skip by name, still never resolved. The fetch stays lazy: a dry-run of a backup with no settings categories selected, or an empty settings blob, still costs zero GETs.
- **DBAS restore failed/conflict counts are now visible in the Task History one-liner and the completion notification, not just the rich report UI (bead enhancedchannelmanager-tyei5, PR #766 code review, build 0015).** The y6zg6 fix above corrects the rich dry-run report itself, but two narrower gaps meant a WOULD-FAIL dry-run still read as an unremarkable success everywhere else. (1) `_summary_message`'s dry-run branch summarized "would create N, update N, skip N across N categories" with no failed/conflict count at all — the apply branch already reported `failed`; the dry-run branch now sums the same per-category `failed` field into an "N conflict(s)" phrase, mirroring `dbas_sync.py`'s existing dry-run summary exactly. (2) The task's `TaskResult` never set `failed_count`/`skipped_count`/real `success_count`/`total_items` — they were hardcoded to the 13-stage progress-bar count, not the actual item counts — so `task_engine.py`'s `elif result.failed_count > 0` ("Completed with Warnings") branch could never fire for a DBAS restore no matter how many categories failed. Both fields (and `success_count`/`total_items`) are now summed from the report's real per-category counts via a `RestoreCounts` helper, the same pattern `dbas_sync.py`'s `SyncCounts` already uses, so the human-readable message and the numeric Task History badges can never disagree.
- **DBAS restore: settings no longer fail 7-for-7 with "Upstream rejected applying setting", and Dispatcharr users no longer fail closed on an unreadable schema (bead enhancedchannelmanager-q6xjl, build 0014).** Found in a fresh-install doc-test run against Dispatcharr 0.28.2: a DBAS backup taken and restored onto the *same* instance previewed cleanly (Settings 7 WILL UPDATE, 0 FAILED) and then failed every one of those seven keys on Apply, rolling the whole restore back. Two independent contract mistakes, both now corrected. (1) ECM was PATCHing `/api/core/settings/<key>/` — a URL that matches no route. Dispatcharr's core-settings viewset is a plain DRF `ModelViewSet` with the default primary-key lookup, so the detail route is `/api/core/settings/<integer id>/`, and every key-string PATCH came back 404 (`[DISPATCHARR] API request failed: PATCH - status: 404`, seven times, then rollback). Row ids are per-instance and are deliberately not carried in a backup artifact, so the restore now resolves them against the *destination* at apply time: one `GET /api/core/settings/` per apply run builds a key→id map that both the `core_settings` and `comskip` blobs share (they live in the same Dispatcharr namespace), and each safe key is PATCHed at its own destination row. The denylist that refuses credential/auth/instance-identity keys is unchanged — a skipped key is not even resolved. A key that genuinely does not exist on the destination now fails **that key alone**, by name ("Setting key 'X' is not present on the destination Dispatcharr"), instead of surfacing as an opaque upstream error. (2) The Dispatcharr **users** category failed closed on every restore with `[DBAS-USERS] Could not read User schema for capability check: Expecting value: line 1 column 1 (char 0)`. ECM reads `/api/schema/` to confirm the destination's user-create schema exposes no pre-hashed-password write field before it will restore users; that endpoint is served by drf-spectacular, whose default renderer is **YAML**, so the JSON parse failed on the first byte and the safety check could never confirm anything. ECM now requests the JSON rendering explicitly. The capability check itself is unchanged and still fails closed — it can now actually run. **This entry does not cover the missing "Apply these changes" confirmation dialog, the DVR-rules fetch failure, or the dry-run preview passing where apply fails**, which were reported in the same run and are tracked separately.
- **Operator user guide: the three documentation defects deferred from build 0011 are closed, plus two more caught in the same pass (bead enhancedchannelmanager-mtkw1, build 0013).** `channels-streams/the-journal.md`'s headline task, "Trace the history of one channel," documented a search that doesn't work for every channel: per-channel Journal rows do exist and are searchable by name, but only for changes made through an MCP agent (Source: AI); a change committed through Edit Mode in the UI (Source: UI) writes only a single Bulk Commit summary row per session, with no per-channel rows underneath it, so a UI-only channel's history can't be traced by name today (DEF-017). The article now carries that distinction explicitly, restores the screenshot that shows it (Source column visible, AI rows individually and the one UI row as a Bulk Commit summary), and points at the open product defect, bead `enhancedchannelmanager-r9py9` (unresolved as of this pass, evidence added to it); the "one Bulk Commit row plus the individual rows" claim is corrected to state which path does which. The same article's "Find the rest of a batch" section told operators to reconstruct a batch by calling `GET /api/journal?batch_id=<id>` directly, which is not an action reachable from the browser. It now states the UI limitation (no batch filter, id is not clickable) without presenting a raw API call as the operator's route, referencing bead `enhancedchannelmanager-d2kdg` (DEF-018). `troubleshooting/read-the-logs.md` hardcoded the maintainer's own container name, `ecm-ecm-1`, in all seven example commands with no instruction to substitute; every example now reads `docker logs <container>`, matching `getting-started/first-run.md`'s existing convention (DEF-020). Found separately during this pass: `backup-restore/backup-overview.md` and `backup-restore/configure-cloud-destinations.md` each carried one instance of the wrong sidebar labels DEF-011 already fixed elsewhere ("Settings → Task Schedules" / "Settings → Task History" in place of **Scheduled Tasks** and its per-task **History** expander), now corrected to match `take-a-backup.md`'s wording; and `notifications/index.md` documented the recipient-dedup toast as always reading "Removed N duplicate recipients," when the live UI shows the singular "Removed 1 duplicate recipient" for exactly one removal, now documented as singular/plural-aware.
- **Copying or reordering a Channel Pipeline rule no longer floods the server with one request per rule, and the copy now lands in the right place without a page refresh (bead enhancedchannelmanager-hns2y, GitHub #755, build 0006).** Field-reported on 0.18.0: copying a rule dropped it at the *bottom* of the list and raised an error toast, and dragging a rule to a new position raised another error toast and looked like it had failed — yet after a refresh both operations turned out to have partly worked. The server log told the story: a successful `POST /rules` followed by an unbroken run of `WARNING: Exceeded concurrency limit.`. The rules list was renumbering priorities by issuing **one `PUT /rules/{id}` per rule, all at once** — so a single copy on a 122-rule instance put 123 writes in flight inside 28ms. That exceeds uvicorn's `--limit-concurrency` (default 100), and uvicorn answers everything past the limit with a 503. Measured on a reproduction instance seeded to that size and driven through a reverse proxy (HTTP/2, which multiplexes rather than capping connections the way a browser does): 124 requests, of which **115 came back 503** and 115 matching `Exceeded concurrency limit` warnings landed in the log. The write amplification scaled with rule count, so raising `ECM_LIMIT_CONCURRENCY` would only have moved the failure to the next operator with a larger rule set; the fan-out now collapses to the single bulk `POST /rules/reorder` endpoint that already existed and was simply not being used — the same operation is **2 requests for a copy and 1 for a reorder**, regardless of how many rules you have. The stranded copy was the visible half of the same bug: the list's in-memory update ran only *after* every write resolved, so one rejection skipped it entirely and left the page showing an order the server did not have until you reloaded. The list is now updated with the same rule the server applies, and if a reorder write does fail it resyncs from the server before surfacing the error — so what you are looking at always matches what was actually saved, with no refresh required.
- **Dispatcharr, Emby, Plex and Jellyfin base URLs on `localhost` / `127.0.0.1` can be saved again — a hardcoded blocklist was overriding your outbound-URL policy setting (GitHub #754, bead enhancedchannelmanager-0yh70, build 0007).** Field-reported on 0.18.0 by a first-run operator whose Dispatcharr runs behind a shared gluetun network, where `http://localhost:9191` is the only address that reaches it: **Test Connection succeeded**, and saving the same URL was refused with `Invalid host — loopback and link-local addresses are not allowed`. Their workaround was to stand the stack up outside gluetun, stop it, hand-edit `settings.json`, and put gluetun back — after which ECM ran perfectly well on that URL, which is the tell: the validator was refusing a configuration the running application is happy with. Cause: the save path ran two validation stages, and the first carried its own loopback/link-local denylist that rejected `localhost` unconditionally, before the canonical stage ever ran. That second stage is mode-aware and reads `ssrf_outbound_mode`, which ships as `lan_friendly` and — per its own documentation and ADR-012 D4 — explicitly allows loopback and RFC1918. So the setting existed, defaulted to permissive, was documented, and could not take effect. Host policy now comes from exactly one place: loopback (`127.0.0.0/8` and `::1`) and RFC1918 follow `ssrf_outbound_mode` (allowed under the `lan_friendly` default, refused under `public_only`), while **link-local `169.254.0.0/16` — including the cloud metadata address `169.254.169.254` — CGNAT, IPv6 ULA, multicast and non-`http(s)` schemes stay refused in both modes, with no setting that re-enables them**. That always-on half is what closed the SSRF + key-exfiltration hole in bead kgz3k and it is unchanged. Two related fixes ship with it. (1) IPv6 loopback `::1` moved from the always-on list to the mode-toggled band: it is the same trust domain as `127.0.0.1`, so denying one while allowing the other prevented nothing — and because Docker's generated `/etc/hosts` maps `localhost` to **both** `::1` and `127.0.0.1`, and one denied address rejects the whole URL, leaving it in the always-on list would have left `http://localhost:<port>` broken for every container that is not `network_mode: host` even after the main fix. (2) **Test Connection and Save now apply the same policy.** The Dispatcharr Test Connection endpoint previously applied no host policy at all — which is why you could prove a connection worked and then be refused permission to store it, and also meant it would issue a credential-bearing request to any host it was given, including `169.254.169.254`, while the Emby/Plex/Jellyfin equivalents were guarded. It now routes through the same chokepoint, so what you can test is exactly what you can save. Unreachable hosts are still saveable and testable — a LAN media server that is merely powered off must not become un-configurable — because the runtime client re-validates before it ever connects.
- **Preflight now fails on an unusable uvicorn launcher instead of leaving it to the exec line, and says so when it discards an interpreter override (bead enhancedchannelmanager-wpzo6, build 0004).** Two follow-ups to the interpreter pin below, both reproduced independently during review. (1) `check_python` probed `"$ECM_PYTHON" --version` and `import fastapi, uvicorn` — the *module* — but never checked that `$ECM_UVICORN` was executable, so a container missing the launcher printed an all-green preflight and then died on `exec gosu appuser "$ECM_UVICORN"` with a bare not-found and zero diagnostics. That is precisely the failure class the pin exists to eliminate ("pinning the checks alone would have moved the same failure one line down"), and `print_python_diagnostics` already computed the answer for it (`NOT EXECUTABLE — the app launch would fail here too`) while being reachable only from branches that could never be this case. Preflight now asserts the launcher and routes the failure through those diagnostics, and prints the launcher path on success so a log paste always names what will be exec'd. (2) The `[ ! -x ]` fallback could not distinguish "the default `/opt/venv` pin is absent, fall back" — a supported configuration — from "the operator set `ECM_PYTHON`/`ECM_UVICORN` explicitly and typo'd it", where falling back silently resolves something else and hands them back the very diagnostics that told them to set the variable. An explicit override that is not executable is now reported by name (`! ECM_PYTHON=/opt/venv/bin/pyhton is not executable — falling back to a PATH lookup`) while an absent default still falls back silently.
- **Container no longer refuses to start with "Required Python packages missing" / `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'fastapi'` (bead enhancedchannelmanager-0oi96, build 0004).** Field-reported on 0.18.0: preflight printed a reassuring `✓ Python 3.12.13 found` and then failed on the very next line, with the app never starting. The image was fine — `entrypoint.sh` was locating `python3` and `uvicorn` through `PATH`, and ECM's packages live in the image virtualenv at `/opt/venv`. Any container whose `PATH` does not include `/opt/venv/bin` therefore ran the bare Debian system interpreter, which has none of ECM's dependencies. The real-world trigger is not a hand-edited compose file: an "update/recreate" in Portainer, Unraid, or Watchtower rebuilds the container from the new image while **copying the old container's environment onto it**, so any container created before 0.16.0 (when `/opt/venv` was introduced) inherits a pre-venv `PATH` and breaks on the next update — the reporting operator's container had `ECM_VERSION=0.8.4-0007` baked into a current image. The entrypoint now resolves its interpreter once, pinned to `/opt/venv/bin/python` and `/opt/venv/bin/uvicorn` (both overridable via `ECM_PYTHON` / `ECM_UVICORN`, both falling back to a `PATH` lookup if the virtualenv is absent), and launches uvicorn by that absolute path — `uvicorn` exists *only* in the virtualenv, so pinning the checks alone would have moved the same failure one line down. The Dockerfile healthcheck is pinned the same way. When an interpreter check does fail, preflight now prints what it resolved and why: the interpreter path, its `sys.prefix`, the container's `PATH`, whether `/opt/venv/bin/python` is still present, and what to do about it — enough to diagnose the case from a single log paste. **If you hit this:** recreate the container from your compose file (`docker compose up -d --force-recreate`) rather than using your update tool's in-place update, so it picks up the image's own environment — but read the mount warning below first.
- **Cross-instance sync survives its first contact with a real Dispatcharr-B (bead enhancedchannelmanager-7ipq2.2, build 0002).** The first live two-instance validation (ECM-A + disposable Dispatcharr-B 0.28.2, per the ADR-013 matrix) surfaced five defects the mock tier structurally could not see, all fixed red-first: (1) live-gathered channel rows carried A-side provenance and derived echoes the archive shape never had — `auto_created_by` (an A-local pk) failed EVERY channel create with a 400, and `auto_created=true` would have marked B's replicas for B's own auto-created garbage collection; the channels importer now strips provenance (`auto_created*`, `source_stream`, `override`), derived `effective_*` echoes, and the destination-assigned `uuid`; (2) the custom-stream fallback forwarded A-local `channel_group`/`stream_profile_id` pks (every orphan create 400'd) — it now resolves them through the shared remap and drops what doesn't resolve, and strips live-GET derived echoes (viewer counts, staleness, stats, server hash, `is_custom`); (3) sync-gathered logo records are metadata-only, so uploads fell back to `application/octet-stream`, which Dispatcharr rejects ("Unsupported file type") — the upload content-type is now derived from the already-validated magic bytes; (4) the report claimed "deferred auto-sync applied" even when the sync path's ADR-013 S9 suppressor dropped the settings — the note now counts what the apply fn actually applied — and an apply whose only failures were post-hoc name-conflict details reported `outcome=success` alongside `failed>0`, violating the never-SUCCESS-on-mixed-state invariant (now downgraded consistently); (5) `dbas_sync` is now ONE-SHOT armed: ad-hoc run parameters merged into the singleton task leaked into later runs (observed live as an abort on a stale captured `credential_version`; worst case was a retained `confirm_apply=true` turning an intended dry-run into a source-wins apply) — per-invocation state resets after every run. Also fixed-by-addition: `run_sync` now stamps the persisted per-target status columns (`last_outcome` per realized apply; `last_full_sync_at` only on a FULL success), which existed since migration 0024 but were never written. Validation extras: the reusable live-B recipe (superuser + SECRET_KEY gotcha, teardown) is documented in `tests/dbas-test-env/README.md`; the stalled-drift runbook now records that an unreachable B surfaces as `partial` (per-item fail-soft + rollback), not `failed`; the user guide states the recreate-not-prune replica semantics.
- **Build-advance guard now understands the post-release counter reset (beads enhancedchannelmanager-92fhj, enhancedchannelmanager-w9irb, build 0000).** `scripts/check_version_advances.py` compared BUILD numbers only, so the documented post-release reopen at the next target's `-0000` (shipping.md §Cut Mechanics step 8) was blocked by both the agent-side ship hook and the CI version-consistency job — first hit by the v0.18.0 back-sync PR (`0.18.1-0000` vs baseline `0.17.6-0174`). The comparator is now prefix-aware: a strictly greater `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` prefix passes with the counter reset (`advanced_prefix`), and the converse hole is closed — a prefix *regression* now fails outright (`prefix_regression`) even when its build number exceeds the baseline's, where previously it slipped through the build-only compare. Red-first unit coverage for both directions plus the unchanged same-prefix rules.
- **Destructive database migrations that "succeeded" without actually running are now healed automatically on the next start — you may see previously-orphaned tables disappear on first boot after this upgrade (bead enhancedchannelmanager-nywpw, build 0005).** The startup fast path that skips re-running Alembic when the live schema already matches the current model only checked that every model-declared table and column *existed* — it never checked for tables the model had stopped declaring, so a drop-only migration was invisible to it by construction and got silently stamped over ("Running stamp_revision" in the log) instead of actually executed. This instance hit it twice: migration 0041 (drop `lookup_tables`, see Removed below) logged as stamped rather than run, and revision 0029 had been stamped past months earlier, leaving `ffmpeg_saved_configs`, `playlist_profiles`, `publish_configurations` and `publish_history` behind with nothing to indicate it. Detection is now AST-based — every revision module is parsed for `drop_table` / `drop_column` / `drop_index` / `drop_constraint` calls and DROP / DELETE FROM / TRUNCATE SQL literals, so a drop-only migration is caught structurally rather than relying on its author to remember a marker. On upgrade, each destructive pending revision now replays individually against its real `upgrade()` (with its own row dump where one exists) while non-destructive revisions still stamp over as before. **Self-heal:** an instance already stamped past a destructive revision is detected at boot — the version table claims the revision applied but the table it should have dropped is still physically present — and is healed automatically, with rows already deleted written out first wherever the migration provides a dump path. No operator action is required; this only removes tables the application no longer uses.
- **"Reset Stuck Probe" is no longer hidden by the exact condition it exists to recover from (bead enhancedchannelmanager-xg9gp, build 0005).** Settings gated the entire Reset Stuck Probe section on the probe *not* being in progress — so once a probe actually got stuck (reporting "in progress" forever), the one control built to clear that state disappeared along with it, leaving no in-UI recovery at all short of starting an unrelated probe from a different browser tab. The section now always renders: Reset stays enabled while a probe is running, and Clear All Probe Stats is disabled with a visible on-screen reason instead. Reset has no confirmation dialog — against a healthy probe its worst-case effect is identical to the already-unconfirmed Cancel button one card above it, and it destroys nothing: results already written stay written and the run is re-runnable.
- **Long Stats and Settings pages no longer yank you down the page seconds after you land on a deep link, and Scheduled Tasks links no longer silently break when a task is renamed (beads enhancedchannelmanager-mch8j, enhancedchannelmanager-b32co, enhancedchannelmanager-de6u1, enhancedchannelmanager-ue130, build 0005).** The section-navigation rail used for in-page jump links and `?section=` deep links on Stats and Settings only discovers a section once it renders a heading; several panels rendered a heading-less loading state until their own data fetch settled, so both the rail and any pending deep-link scroll were incomplete for as long as that fetch took — minutes, on a large library mid-probe. Worse, the deep-link scroll re-fired on every rail change instead of firing once, so a reader who had already started reading the top of the page could get yanked as far as 1993px down mid-read, once the last panel's data finally arrived. Panels now expose their section id and label from first paint, and the deep-link scroll now fires exactly once per requested `?section=` value; an unresolvable `?section=` is dropped from the URL and announced via an on-screen notice instead of silently pointing at nothing. Separately, Scheduled Tasks' rail anchors are now pinned to each task's stable internal id instead of a slug of its display name, so renaming a task — or a cross-instance sync target, whose task name embeds the target's own name — no longer silently moves a previously-shared link. Five of seventeen existing task anchors change shape as a result (cleanup, m3u_digest, auto_creation, failed_stream_reprobe, stats_v2_rollup); old links to those five now surface a clear notice instead of landing nowhere without explanation.
- **Accessibility: every checkbox and radio now meets its WCAG 2.5.8 target size or has an accessible name, and the last known contrast failures are cleared (beads enhancedchannelmanager-m26f8, enhancedchannelmanager-7lwe0, enhancedchannelmanager-dlavh, enhancedchannelmanager-0zq1p, enhancedchannelmanager-4pzvg, enhancedchannelmanager-zncyv, build 0005).** A full census of checkboxes and radios across the ten primary routes, seventeen Settings sub-routes and 81 of 82 dialogs found 211 controls rendering at five different sizes (13-18px), none reaching the 24x24 WCAG target; 44 of them had no `<label>` at all, so their bare 16px box was the entire click/tap target. All 44 now sit inside a real target across their five source locations (column-select checkboxes, sort-priority toggles, print-guide group rows, task-schedule toggles, the pending-merges selector), and checkbox/radio boxes are standardized on one 16px size app-wide instead of five. The same census caught something worse than target size: 6 of those 44 controls had no accessible name at all (announced to a screen reader as a bare "checkbox") and 8 more were named only via a `title` attribute, which is invisible to touch and keyboard users — both groups now have real labels or `aria-label`s. Separately, the remaining WCAG contrast failures — the primary navigation's selected-row highlight, the probe-group icon button, and error text in the stream preview dialog — are corrected, each measured on the live rendered page across both supported viewports and all three themes; a bug in the contrast-measuring guard itself (it was compounding layered opacity in the wrong direction, making some passing combinations read as failing) is also fixed. Also fixed: two screen-reader-only status regions — one on the Dashboard, one in Settings — were rendering as ordinary visible text instead of being hidden, because the CSS that hides them only shipped inside the Channel Pipeline page's bundle and hadn't loaded until you'd visited that page at least once in your session.
- **All 211 checkbox and radio controls now clear the WCAG 2.5.8 24x24 target — the boxes themselves are unchanged size, only short rows grew (bead enhancedchannelmanager-3h2u1, build 0006).** Follow-on to the census above: of the 211 labelled checkbox/radio controls, 91 were still failing 2.5.8, every one of them on height only (18-22px tall; none was too narrow). All 211 now clear the floor — 72 of the measurement groups grew to exactly 24px, and the other 84, already at or above it, didn't move at all. **The checkboxes and radio buttons did not get bigger** — they're still their existing 16px box — the fix gives each short row a minimum height instead, so nothing that was already sized correctly changed. Nine Settings sub-pages grew a little taller as a result (up to about 29px) and nine dialogs grew a little too (up to about 12px); nothing that fit on screen before now scrolls.
- **Flaky test defused: the EPG large-file event-loop check no longer fails on a loaded CI runner (bead enhancedchannelmanager-ssvvl, build 0006).** `test_large_xml_index_parse_does_not_block_event_loop` was a wall-clock-budget assertion — the same shape that had already failed CI twice and been fixed elsewhere — so it was only a matter of time before it failed here too on a busy runner. Replaced with a signal-based check that has no clock in its assertion. No production code or user-facing behavior changed; test-only.
- **Operator user guide: 17 documented steps corrected against a live-instance test pass, including one BLOCKER (bead enhancedchannelmanager-i78vd, build 0011).** A documentation test run against a fresh install (`~/ecm/doc-test-runs/2026-08-02/DEFECTS.md`) walked the guide end-to-end and found places where the documented UI did not match the running app. Highlights: `take-a-backup.md` sent operators to a "Backup" card and "Back Up Now" button that do not exist (DEF-010, BLOCKER); it now documents the working path (Scheduled Tasks → DBAS Backup → Run Now) and flags that a one-click control is tracked separately (bead `pui76`). The same article's "Saved Backups" section claimed to list all local backups when it is scoped to YAML exports only and never shows DBAS artifacts (DEF-012), and used the wrong sidebar labels "Task Schedules"/"Task History" for "Scheduled Tasks" and its per-task History expander (DEF-011). `connect-dispatcharr.md` sent operators to a nonexistent "Account → API Keys" path in Dispatcharr 0.28.2; it now documents the real path (User dialog → API & XC → Generate API Key) (DEF-003). `your-first-channels.md` claimed group selection "immediately re-pulls" streams when convergence can take on the order of tens of minutes with no progress indicator (DEF-007), and separately named the wrong fields for XtreamCodes accounts, omitted that gzipped XMLTV feeds work, undersold initial-pull timing, didn't warn that the Channels group filter resets to "No groups selected" on reload, and didn't mention the numbering dialog that opens when dragging a channel onto a group header (DEF-004, DEF-005, DEF-006, DEF-008, DEF-009). `channel-to-epg-matching.md` and `guide/index.md` didn't say that a fresh channel-to-EPG match requires an EPG Refresh task run before it shows in Guide (confirmed as expected product behavior, not a bug), and `guide/index.md` wrongly claimed its own grid Refresh button would surface it (DEF-014); the same matching article also pointed operators at an MCP-tool/API-only audit with no UI path and now says so plainly (DEF-015). `installation.md`'s two ordered lists rendered broken on the published site (a later list item silently glued onto the previous item's trailing paragraph as literal text). Reproduced against the site's actual `mkdocs-material` + `pymdownx.superfences` toolchain, the fix indents continuation content to 4 spaces and blank-lines between items (DEF-001); its companion `getting-started/index.md` promised prerequisites content that `installation.md` didn't have and listed shipped articles as "planned" (DEF-002), so a Prerequisites section was added and the index brought current. `notifications/index.md` didn't document the Alert Methods list at all, including that it does not live-update after saving a channel's settings (DEF-016). `rules-overview.md` and `test-a-rule.md` both described a "⋮ (more actions) menu" on the Channel Pipeline page that does not exist: Dry Run, Import, Export, and Pipeline Debug Bundle are always-visible toolbar buttons instead (DEF-019). `schedules-direct.md` didn't say what to do when an SD account is already at its lineup cap, or that removing a lineup consumes the same daily change quota as adding one (DEF-013). Three findings from the same run remain out of scope here because their target articles (`the-journal.md`, `read-the-logs.md`) are not yet on `dev` (DEF-017, DEF-018, DEF-020); two more are tracked as product defects, not documentation (beads `3p7jg`, `r9py9`).
- **A restore that leaves a channel with no playable stream now reports "completed with failures" instead of success, and raises a warning (bead enhancedchannelmanager-daziw, build 0027).** A DBAS restore could finish reporting `outcome: success, failed: 0` for an instance holding a channel that would not play at all — the round-trip drill measured exactly that, with the channel returning an HTTP 500 and zero bytes while the restore called itself a success. From this build, a restore (or a cross-instance sync, which runs on the same engine) that applies everything and still leaves a channel with **not one real, URL-bearing stream** finishes as `completed_with_failures`, names those channels in the report, and sends a **warning** alert titled "Task Completed with Warnings" rather than a green success. **Nothing is rolled back and nothing is undone** — the restored state is real, kept, and one attached stream away from working; only the label and the alert change. **A channel that still plays is NOT counted.** If a channel kept its real streams and is merely holding one leftover placeholder stream in a slot — the normal, designed outcome when two archived stream names collide, shipped in build 0026 — that channel plays, it is not counted as unplayable, and it does not stop the restore reporting success. Two numbers now appear in the report and in the one-line task summary, and they mean different things: `channels_with_no_playable_stream` (these are dead; this is what downgrades the outcome) and `channels_needing_stream_reattach` (these hold a placeholder slot; most of them play). The old summary wording called the second of those "channel(s) have NO playable stream", a claim it could not support — that phrase now belongs to the first number only. What to expect operationally: if you have alerts configured, a restore or sync that used to look clean while leaving a dead channel will now alert you as a warning, once, naming the channels to fix; if your restores leave every channel playing, nothing changes for you.

## [0.18.0] — 2026-07-26

### Security
- **Release-branch stabilization: cleared the main-target security gates for the v0.18.0 cut (release PR #748).** The release-cut PR's merge-ref scans — which dev-targeting PRs don't run at main's thresholds — surfaced four accumulated items, all fixed on `release/v0.18.0`: (1) `backend/task_engine.py`'s `_param_keys()` sanitizer had a raw-passthrough fallback for non-dict parameters, keeping the `py/clear-text-logging-sensitive-data` taint path alive (CodeQL alerts #1770/#1771, HIGH, open on `dev` since 2026-07-08) — the fallback now returns a `<non-dict:type>` placeholder and a regression test pins that non-dict parameters never reach a log line raw; (2) an unanchored `/example\.com/` regex in `ActionEditor.test.tsx` (CodeQL alert #1769, `js/regex/missing-regexp-anchor`, HIGH) replaced with an exact placeholder-literal match; (3) backend dependency bumps for advisories published since the 0.17.5 cut: `click` 8.3.2→8.3.3 (PYSEC-2026-2132) and `pyasn1` 0.6.3→0.6.4 (PYSEC-2026-3455/3456/3457); (4) frontend transitive bumps via non-`--force` `npm audit fix`: `brace-expansion`→5.0.8 (GHSA-3jxr-9vmj-r5cp, GHSA-mh99-v99m-4gvg), `postcss`→8.5.23 (GHSA-r28c-9q8g-f849), `undici`→7.29.0 (7 advisories) — `npm audit --audit-level=high` now reports zero.

### Changed
- **Docs: BaseException-containment known limitation, and a stale user-guide IA tree fixed (bead enhancedchannelmanager-17v07, build 0172).** `docs/auth_middleware.md` now documents that `BaseExceptionContainmentMiddleware` (registered first/innermost, `backend/main.py:205`) cannot cover the bodies of outer `@app.middleware("http")` functions — including `auth_middleware` itself (`backend/main.py:525-547`) — since each outer middleware body runs outside the guard's task boundary; this is a known, accepted structural ceiling of Starlette's `BaseHTTPMiddleware` model (closing it is a middleware-stack/order redesign — removing the task-boundary layers and placing containment so it actually wraps the affected bodies, then revalidating handler containment), no field occurrence has been observed as of 2026-07-26, and the doc states the exact `docker logs` signature to look for if one ever fires (the atexit `[EXIT-DIAG]` line still logs; what's absent is the containment middleware's own CRITICAL traceback above it). A pointer comment was added at the `auth_middleware` definition (comment only, no behavior change). Separately, `docs/user_guide/README.md`'s "Information architecture" tree was missing six sections added by the gsnw0 per-tab-tutorial epic (`settings/`, `guide/`, `m3u-manager/`, `logo-manager/`, `journal/`, `m3u-changes/`); the tree now lists them, marked **Planned** to match `docs/user_guide/index.md`'s "By tab" table.
- **Channels pane selection is now keyboard-operable (bead enhancedchannelmanager-s8xpd, build 0166).** Follow-on from bead zwhw4's StreamsPane review, which flagged the same bare-span selection flaw in ChannelsPane: the per-channel row selector and the group header's select-all were clickable `<span>`s with no native control semantics — not focusable, no `aria-checked`/`aria-pressed`, no keyboard handler, gating every selection-dependent action (the floating SelectionActionBar, bulk ops, Move-to-group) behind a mouse. Both are now real `<button>`s: the row selector carries `role="checkbox"` + `aria-checked` (mirroring StreamsPane's per-stream selector), and the group header's select-all also carries `role="checkbox"` + a tri-state `aria-checked` (`true`/`false`/`"mixed"`) rather than the boolean `aria-pressed` the first pass shipped, which announced "none selected" and "some selected" identically even though the indeterminate glyph already distinguished them. The tri-state fix was applied to **both** panes' group-header select-all (ChannelsPane's `group-checkbox` and StreamsPane's `group-selection-checkbox`), keeping the two panes' equivalent interactions semantically consistent. A round-2 review also found the select-all button nested inside each pane's group-header `role="button"` div — a focusable-inside-focusable conflict; in both panes the expand/collapse toggle is now its own sibling `<button>` (native Enter/Space handling) instead of the div itself carrying `role="button"`/`tabIndex`, which also let the old bd-6n14l keydown-target-check guard be removed as structurally unnecessary. Both glyphs stay `aria-hidden`, and a `:focus-visible` accent outline was added to each new/changed button.
- **Streams pane: right-click context menu replaced by an accessible "Create in…" menu (bead enhancedchannelmanager-zwhw4, build 0161).** The hand-rolled right-click menu on stream rows and group headers ("Create channel(s) in group / in new group") is gone — it was mouse-only, invisible to screen readers, and unreachable by keyboard. Its capabilities now live in the selection strip at the top of the Streams pane: with streams selected in Edit Mode, a new "Create in…" button opens a keyboard-navigable chooser listing the enabled channel groups (with type-to-filter, matching the Channel Manager selection bar's Move-to-group chooser) plus a pinned "Create in new group…" action. The single-stream duplicate-channel check (dedup prompt) is preserved on the choose-a-group path. Right-clicking a stream row or group header now shows the browser's native context menu, matching the Channels pane.

### Added
- **WebDAV cloud backup targets are now enabled end-to-end (bead enhancedchannelmanager-0i2vt.8, build 0170).** The WebDAV adapter was already built, SSRF-hardened, and registered in `SUPPORTED_PROVIDERS`, but the cloud-target CRUD layer's `provider_type` validation blocked it — creating a WebDAV target was impossible from the UI or API. `webdav` is now accepted across create/update/test requests, and the Cloud Target editor offers it with its `base_url`/`username`/`password` fields. Self-signed endpoints are supported through a validated top-level `insecure` flag (persisted per target, surfaced as an advanced checkbox with an explicit warning): TLS policy derives from that flag ONLY — `insecure` inside the credentials dict is reserved and rejected at the API boundary — so connection tests use exactly the policy scheduled uploads use, and every verification skip writes an audit row on test paths just as on uploads. OneDrive and Dropbox appear greyed out and labeled "not yet supported" in the create dropdown (deferred providers — PO decision 2026-07-25); existing rows of those types still render and edit. Covered by an end-to-end integration test: API create with real credential encryption → saved-target test-connection through the real adapter and SSRF chokepoint → real-adapter streamed upload.
- **Cloud-backup encryption key integrity is probed at every container start (bead enhancedchannelmanager-m40pn, build 0170).** File mode/ownership violations on the Fernet key now surface as an unmissable ERROR log line at startup instead of at the first scheduled backup. Posture is log-loudly-but-boot: the probe never blocks startup. At use time, a decrypt failure reports the credentials as unavailable and the credential-consuming backup operation aborts before any outbound use.
- **Restore logo-miss details now list the affected channels (bead enhancedchannelmanager-cm9bi, build 0170).** Each logo miss in the restore report carries the channels whose archived `logo_id` referenced it — destination Dispatcharr channel id (resolved through the channel remap; channels import before logos) plus the operator-facing name. To make this work on genuine backups, the artifact builder now joins each archived logo file to its source Dispatcharr logo record (by URL basename) and preserves the stable source logo id through `binary/metadata.json`, where the artifact decoder re-attaches it to each logo record — previously real artifacts lost that id and the affected-channel list was always empty. The banner's aggregate copy is logo-oriented ("1 logo is missing…" / "N logos are missing…" — `logo_misses` counts missed logos, one shared logo affecting several channels is one miss) with the per-channel impact in nested rows carrying Dispatcharr links; Dispatcharr's SPA has no per-channel route (channel editing is a modal on `/channels`), so the links open the channels list page. `role=alert` and the colorblind-safe treatment are unchanged, and dry-runs never emit provisional ids.
- **DBAS backup artifacts now carry user agents, DVR rules, core settings, and comskip config — completing round-trip coverage of all 12 categories in the v0.18 scope; plugins remain excluded per ADR-012 D10 (bead enhancedchannelmanager-lc6zu, build 0170).** The restore importers for these categories were fully wired (PR #742) but the artifact builder never produced their sections, so restoring them silently no-opped against a real backup. The builder now emits all four (Dispatcharr has no separate comskip endpoint: comskip keys live in the core-settings namespace and are split out by prefix, matching how the importer applies them per-key), and the artifact decoder maps them into the restore plan. Core-setting values whose key names look credential-bearing (the same conservative denylist the restore importer skips by name) are redacted in the artifact — always, even on credential-carrying migration exports, since the importer would never apply them. The restore summary now labels the Settings category's apply counts.
- **Builtin Quality Tags seed now includes modern UHD labels: `2160P`, `3840P`, `4320P`, `8K` (bead enhancedchannelmanager-lecyo, build 0168).** Follow-up from bead 0tryb: the builtin Quality Tags group predates modern UHD naming, so names like "DE: RTL 3840P" kept their resolution suffix through normalization on fresh installs. The builtin tag sync runs at every startup and tops up missing tags into existing installations, so existing installs gain the new labels on their next restart after upgrading; tags a user already added by hand with the same value are skipped untouched (exact-value dedupe -- no duplicates, no flag changes).
- **Journal Noise Purge covers two more automated-noise buckets (bead enhancedchannelmanager-gjb01, build 0160).** The daily Journal Noise Purge task now also purges run-on-refresh suppression notices (the "auto-creation after M3U refresh skipped" entries written on every refresh while the circuit breaker is tripped) and routine start/complete lifecycle rows from scheduled task runs, both older than the shared retention window (default 3 days). Each bucket has its own toggle in the task's settings (Settings → Scheduled Tasks → Journal Noise Purge), enabled by default. Kept regardless of age: manually-triggered task runs, task cancel/fail/error entries, and every other journal category — and full task execution history remains available in Task History under its own retention. Duplicating a Channel Pipeline rule now also writes a journal create entry (previously a duplicated-then-deleted rule left a delete-only pair in the audit trail).
- **Catch-up (timeshift) badge on channel and stream rows (bead enhancedchannelmanager-sy1sz, build 0155).** Channels and streams that Dispatcharr flags as catch-up-capable now show a compact `replay` badge in the Channels pane and stream lists, with a tooltip stating the archive depth ("Catch-up: 7 days", or "Catch-up available" when the day count is unknown). The badge is driven solely by Dispatcharr's authoritative `is_catchup` flag — a stream reporting archive days without the flag set shows no badge.
- **M3U Auto-Sync groups now honor their channel-profile selection (bead enhancedchannelmanager-y3m6o.3, GH #720 Part B, build 0153).** The Auto-Sync settings modal already let an operator pick which channel profiles an auto-synced group's channels should belong to, but nothing applied that choice — Dispatcharr's default of auto-joining every new channel to every profile stood, so the selection was silently ignored. ECM now reconciles each group's stored selection SUBTRACTIVELY: for every profile in the universe the group's channels are enabled if the profile is selected and disabled otherwise, at a cost of one bulk update per profile regardless of channel count. The **converging backbone** — the M3U change monitor's every-pass sweep plus the post-refresh completion poll — is the ongoing convergence mechanism; the modal save applies it immediately on a best-effort basis and surfaces a warning toast if that apply is incomplete. Selection is **enforced globally per channel-group**: on save it is propagated to every M3U account row carrying that group so it takes effect regardless of which account it was made on (a defensive collapse still resolves legacy rows and flags any residual conflict). Concurrent reconciles are serialized per effective group **within the process** (single-operator assumption; when ECM runs a TLS request subprocess, that subprocess forwards exactly the lock-participating paths — the group-settings save cascade, the single-account M3U refresh, the Channel Pipeline / Auto-Create run endpoints and their `/api/auto-creation` alias, and every background task run AND cancel — to the main process so main stays the sole writer of those paths and the scheduler/lock stay authoritative there; the forward imposes no timeout ceiling of its own so a legitimately long task run is not cut to a false error, and main's request-timeout budget remains authoritative. Direct channel-profile membership endpoints are a separate pre-existing concern, not part of this forwarding — bead nq3ed). Two failure classes are kept distinct: a reconcile that fails AFTER the selection was safely written returns HTTP 200 with a warning in the response body (the save stuck; convergence retries on the next sweep), whereas a **pre-write safety failure** — the lock key is unavailable, or the account list needed to clear a selection cannot be read/enumerated safely — is NOT written and returns HTTP 503 (retry) rather than a false success. A CLEAR that partially applied before a sibling write failed reports the TRUTHFUL partial: a 503 that names which accounts were cleared and which failed, leaves the authoritative account's selection intact (no resurrection), and journals the accounts that actually changed so the operator can re-save to complete. A coalesced periodic sweep (another sweep already in progress) is reported as DEFERRED — a task warning, not a green success — and converges on the next pass. NOTE: reverting ECM to a build before 0.17.6-0153 does not restore prior channel-profile memberships — the membership changes live in Dispatcharr, not an ECM snapshot; re-select in the Auto-Sync modal to recover a state. **Pipeline ownership with handoff:** channels whose profile membership was set by a Channel Pipeline `assign_channel_profile` rule (standard or Event Sync) are excluded — a pipeline choice outranks a group selection — but ownership is automatically handed back to Auto-Sync once that rule is disabled, deleted, or stops assigning profiles (a rule CONDITION change that merely stops matching a channel does not release it — out of scope). Recovery guardrails: an absent or empty selection is a no-op that leaves existing memberships unchanged (never "disable everywhere"); if the entire stored selection references profiles that have since been deleted, the group is left untouched rather than stranded in zero profiles; if a selected-profile write transiently fails, the reconcile aborts before any destructive disable and retries on the next pass; and if the profile universe cannot be fetched the reconcile reports `degraded` (enables applied, exclusivity not enforced) rather than a false success. The modal shows status-specific recovery guidance on an incomplete apply.
- **Recovery note (channel-profile reconcile, GH #720 Part B):** this feature changes Dispatcharr channel-profile MEMBERSHIP as a side effect of M3U Auto-Sync saves and the periodic sweep. Reverting ECM to a build before 0.17.6-0153 does NOT restore prior memberships — the membership changes live in Dispatcharr, not in an ECM snapshot, and ECM's own rollback/undo never claims to reverse profile membership. To recover a specific membership state, re-select the desired profiles in the Auto-Sync settings modal (or adjust the owning Channel Pipeline rule) and let the reconcile re-apply it.
- Public user-guide documentation site: MkDocs + Material scaffold that publishes `docs/user_guide/` to GitHub Pages (developer/internal docs excluded). (enhancedchannelmanager-o8ezw, GH #381)
- **Date-windowed Channel Pipeline rules (bead enhancedchannelmanager-6lhmb, GH #646, build 0147).** Standard and Event Sync rules can now define optional inclusive UTC `active_from` and `active_until` calendar dates. Rules outside their window are excluded consistently from manual, selected, run-on-refresh, and Event Sync auto-run execution without undoing changes made by earlier runs. Both editors expose the window, CRUD/bulk/duplicate operations preserve or clear it explicitly, and YAML export/import round-trips quoted, unquoted, open, and null bounds with atomic validation.
- **Preview-first IPTV ↔ Gracenote guide migration (bead enhancedchannelmanager-96tnl, GH #592, build 0146).** EPG Manager now lets an administrator choose an XMLTV or Schedules Direct target and preview every channel guide assignment before changing anything. ECM maps the current guide through the XMLTV `<gnid>`/`<lcn>` station identifier (Schedules Direct exposes the same identifier as `EPGData.tvg_id`), then classifies each channel as ready, already on target, unassigned, missing LCN, missing target, ambiguous, or unsupported origin. Only explicitly confirmed, unambiguous XMLTV/Schedules Direct rows are changed; unresolved, dummy, and unknown-source rows are never overwritten. Five-minute previews use a domain-separated key derived from the instance's rotatable signing secret and bind the actor, instance/audience, ordered assignments, LCN, and exact current/target EPG identities. Apply returns `202`, runs outside the request timeout, and exposes live counters and per-row outcomes through an actor-bound polling endpoint; the dialog polls until terminal while open with no arbitrary wall-clock cutoff, retains the batch ID and partial results across transient failures, aborts polling on close, and provides reconciliation guidance after a restart. Concurrent applies fail fast with `409`, running jobs are never pruned, and terminal jobs expire 30 minutes after completion. Inside the serialized job ECM rebuilds one bounded XML/source snapshot, re-proves every target is still the exact sole candidate, and refetches each current/target EPG row and channel immediately before PATCH. XML/JSON parsing and large index construction run off the event loop. XMLTV reads use ECM's pinned outbound SSRF transport with per-hop redirect validation, bounded gzip expansion, and DTD/entity rejection. Channel/EPG enumeration stops at cap+1 (1,000/50,000). Audit attempts share a 128-bit Journal batch ID; sessions rollback and close on failure, which is reported as `updated_audit_failed`. Dispatcharr PATCH, HTTP acknowledgement, and Journal commit are not atomic, so interrupted jobs require a fresh preview plus direct Dispatcharr reconciliation.
- **Pending Merges bulk actions (bead enhancedchannelmanager-ixcf1, GH #642, ADR-008 §D10, build 0145).** The Pending Merges queue now provides accessible row checkboxes plus Select all, Deselect all, Merge selected, Clear selected, Merge all, and Clear all controls. Select all spans the complete paginated queue. Merge/clear operations use an accessible focus-trapped confirmation dialog with the exact target count and consequence, then reuse the existing per-row accept/dismiss endpoints sequentially to avoid concurrent Dispatcharr mutations. A persistent accessible progress panel reports completion and failures; Stop cooperatively finishes the request already in flight, skips the remainder, and leaves those records selected for retry. Successful rows disappear as they resolve; a partial failure does not stop later rows, and failed rows remain selected and rendered with their backend error and retry controls. An admin-gated, read-only snapshot endpoint returns the complete pending queue from one ordered database query with a 20,000-row fail-closed cap; Select all, queue-wide actions, and Refresh with an active selection consume that coherent snapshot. Canceling confirmation restores the prior paginated view. Merge all is irreversible within ECM and its exact-count confirmation is the safety boundary.
- **Smart Sort can prefer catch-up-enabled streams (bead enhancedchannelmanager-jnbka, GH #652, build 0144).** Settings → Channel Defaults → Smart Sort Priority now includes an eighth, default-disabled **Catch-up** criterion with the standard history/clock icon and the subheading “Catch-up enabled.” When enabled, streams whose Dispatcharr `is_catchup` flag is true sort ahead of streams without catch-up; ties continue through the remaining enabled criteria. Existing saved priority lists automatically gain the new criterion at the end without changing current behavior. The criterion is also available in channel, group, pane, and selection-bar sort menus; direct **By Catch-up** sorting treats catch-up capability as probe-independent metadata, so an unprobed catch-up stream still ranks ahead of a successfully probed non-catch-up stream.
- **Channel Manager: type-to-filter for the selection bar's Move-to-group submenu (bead hzzcv, build 0143).** The Move-to-group submenu shipped in build 0141 lists every channel group, sorted and scrollable — workable at dozens of groups, painful at the live instance's ~2,940. A filter input now sits at the top of the submenu and focuses automatically the moment it opens (by click or by ArrowRight), narrowing the list as you type. Filtering matches the Channels pane's existing searchable group filter (bead mn8): case-insensitive substring match on the raw, untrimmed query. "Uncategorized" and "New group…" are pinned outside the filter — fixed actions, not filterable named groups — so both stay reachable at any filter state, including a zero-match filter. A filtered-to-nothing state shows `No groups match "<query>"` instead of a blank panel, with a one-click clear button. The existing keyboard contract is preserved end to end: arrows traverse the filtered results, Enter activates the focused group, and Escape closes the submenu only (returning focus to its trigger) without closing the whole menu or clearing the selection — matching-input keys (Left/Right/Home/End) stop propagating to the menu's roving-focus handlers so normal text-cursor editing inside the filter still works. The filter resets each time the submenu closes. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-hzzcv)
- **Channel Manager: floating selection action bar over both panes (bead 09x38.17, build 0141).** Selecting channels in Edit Mode now surfaces a Gmail/Drive-style action bar fixed across the bottom of the viewport — full-width so pane widths are untouched at every breakpoint. Top tier: Delete, Probe, Find Duplicates, Renumber, Assign EPG, Merge (2+ selected only), a '⋮ More' overflow that opens upward, and Clear. The More menu carries three sections: Move ("Move to group" submenu listing every group plus "New group…"), Selection (Normalize Names, Set Logo from M3U/EPG, Sort Streams, Fetch Gracenote IDs), and Profiles (a single "Profile visibility" flyout replacing the duplicate Enable-in-Profile/Disable-in-Profile lists). The hand-rolled right-click context menu on channel rows and group headers is removed with full capability parity (Move-to-group, New-group-and-move, profile toggles, and Merge all live in the bar), the header 'More actions' kebab is reduced to pane-level items only (Channel Profiles, Hidden Groups, Sort All Streams, Renumber All Groups, CSV Template/Export/Import), and the mini "N selected" header strip is absorbed by the bar. Accessibility: all bar buttons are real buttons, the menu is fully keyboard-navigable (arrows/Home/End/Enter, ArrowRight/ArrowLeft for submenus, Escape closes and restores focus), and Escape with no menu open clears the selection. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-09x38.17)
- **Scheduled Tasks: "Enabled, won't run" visibility for tasks whose schedule cannot fire (bead vkktd.4, build 0140).** Task cards now surface the backend's `effective_enabled` state: a task that is enabled but has no enabled schedule that will actually fire shows an amber "Enabled, won't run" status pill (with a one-click "Fix it" action) instead of a green check. The task editor shows an inline warning for the same condition plus a reconcile toast when saving auto-resolves it, and the Channel Pipeline tab gains a dismissible gate banner when the Auto-Create Channels task is disabled or won't run.
- **Journal: automated-noise auto-purge (bead uliyr, build 0140).** New daily scheduled task "Journal Noise Purge" (03:45 UTC default) deletes journal entries older than a settings-visible retention window (default 3 days, PO-decided) for exactly two automated-noise categories: watch start/stop telemetry from the bandwidth tracker, and Channel Pipeline rule create/delete churn from automated test clients. Operator-initiated rule create/delete entries are kept via a new write-time provenance marker (`journal_entries.automated_client`, migration 0037): clients self-declare automation with the `X-ECM-Automated-Client` header (the E2E harness now sends it), requests without the header are operator-classed and never purged, and unmarked pre-marker legacy rows age out until that set reaches zero. All other journal categories are untouched — the manual Purge control remains the tool for everything else. Retention days and both category toggles are editable in Settings → Scheduled Tasks.
- **Event Sync: opt-in promotion of unmatched secondary-only events to ECM-managed channels (bead ti939.4.1, build 0139, GH epic ti939).** The ONE sanctioned exception to Event Sync's "ECM never creates channels" principle, strictly opt-in per rule via `promote_unmatched: true` + a required dedicated `promote_target_group_id` (the master group and secondary groups are refused — ownership rails; absent keys leave the feature completely invisible, byte-identical behavior everywhere). With it on, each unmatched secondary-only event with a complete parsed identity gets its own ECM-managed channel in the target group, with every provider's stream for that event attached (journaled under category `event_sync` with `kind="event_sync_promote"` content-fingerprint provenance — provider id + normalized-name hash + event key; IDs display-only). **Lifecycle is reconciliation-driven (PO decision):** a promoted channel is kept only while its justifying stream is observed in the provider playlist on the current run; Pass 4 orphan reconciliation deletes it per the rule's `orphan_action` (default delete) when it drops out — no wall-clock arithmetic, no timestamps, no run counters; a run that could not observe (provider fetch failure, invalid config) makes no delete decision, so a transient error can never mass-delete promoted channels. **Clustering is exact-event-key only (PO decision):** same-run streams from any provider sharing one normalized event identity form ONE promotion unit (no fuzzy clustering; promoted channels never enter the matcher candidate set); dateless `assume_current_date` parses inherit the t6bin identity semantics — no synthesized date ever appears in the channel name or identity, so the re-run after midnight adopts the same channel. Create-or-adopt idempotence rides the existing group-scoped duplicate lookup; if the event later appears in the master group, its streams attach to the master and the promoted duplicate reconciles away the same run (self-healing). `max_promote_per_run` (default 25, max 200) caps NEW channels per run with a `event_sync_promote_capped` warning on overage; the rule's dummy EPG profile covers promoted channels exactly like masters; rollback of a promotion run uses the standard snapshot path (created channels deleted, master stream lists restored). **Preview parity by construction:** the preview computes the promotion plan with the same pure helper the live run executes (`services/event_sync_promote.py`) — new "Would promote (N)" section + per-unmatched-row annotations in the preview panel and payload, promotion counts in the summary line, and a distinct "Would promote"/PROMOTE block in the MCP `preview_event_sync` renderer. Pinned semantics: an operator never-attach exclusion blocks the ATTACH to that specific master, not promotion — an `excluded_by_operator` stream still promotes; `ambiguous` streams never do (open review questions). Editor: collapsed "Promote unmatched events" subgroup on the Behavior step with explicit copy that ECM will CREATE and DELETE channels in the target group. Docs: `docs/event_sync.md` → "Promoting unmatched events (Phase 3 opt-in)" (the master-as-ceiling section no longer says promotion doesn't exist). (bead enhancedchannelmanager-ti939.4.1)
- **Event Sync: operator team-alias dictionary for the team-token matcher — known equivalences instead of a lower threshold (bead ti939.4.2, build 0138, GH epic ti939).** Recurring missed matches on abbreviation-heavy providers traced to team-name variants the matcher's abbreviation/initialism logic cannot bridge (nicknames like "Red Devils" share no letters with "Manchester United", so the pair HARD-rejects as a team-token conflict at any score). New instance-wide dictionary of alias groups — each a set of known-equivalent team spellings (`Man Utd == Manchester United == MUFC`) plus an optional evidence note — consulted by the team-token layer on BOTH paths: hard-reject rescue (aliased spellings at the same kickoff stop rejecting as `team_token_conflict`) and boost (aliased sides count as full team agreement, so `max(fuzzy, team_score)` lifts the pair into the attach band). This is the safe direction for recall: one declared equivalence instead of lowering the evidence bar for every pair. Safety contract pinned by tests: the lookup is strictly MONOTONIC (an alias can only raise team similarity — different-group or no-group pairs score byte-identically to a no-alias run, so an alias can never CREATE a disagree), the qualifier rail (women's/U21/reserves) outranks aliases, and no admission floor changes. Storage is a JSON setting (`event_sync_team_aliases` on DispatcharrSettings — no DB table, no migration), written only through the new validated + journaled `GET`/`PUT /api/event-sync/team-aliases` endpoints (terms checked against the matcher's own team normalization: ≥2 terms per group, no blank/identity-free terms, one group per term); the general settings POST preserves the field. The matcher loads the dictionary at the `match_streams`/`score_pair` boundary (`team_aliases=None`), so attach runs, previews, and the debug bundle share one dictionary by construction while tests and the frozen corpus inject explicit fixtures — the corpus gate stays byte-stable. Operator surface: new "Event Sync Team Aliases" section on Settings → Channel Pipeline (tag-list group editor with per-group evidence note, own Save); MCP tools `get_event_sync_team_aliases` / `update_event_sync_team_aliases` (full-replace write mirroring the PUT). The shipped dictionary is EMPTY by design — aliases are corpus-gated: add a group only with preview/journal evidence proving the equivalence, because a wrong alias is a new false-positive vector (docs/event_sync.md → "Team aliases (operator dictionary)"). (bead enhancedchannelmanager-ti939.4.2)
- **MCP: four recently-shipped ECM features are now surfaced through MCP tools (bead fltt3, build 0137).** A coverage audit found today's ships partially invisible to MCP — an operator driving ECM through the MCP tool set had no way to reach them even though the REST API and UI already had them. Closed: (1) new `get_stale_stream_ids` tool (`GET /api/streams/stale-ids`, bead po78p) — the cheap Dispatcharr `is_stale` id set, cross-referenced with the heavier `get_stale_streams` report so operators pick the right one; (2) `get_provider_stats` gains a `metric="stream_usage"` branch (`GET /api/stats/providers/stream-usage`, bead n5cwp) rendering the per-provider assigned/total/utilization table, including the synthetic "Unknown" bucket; (3) new `get_m3u_digest_settings` / `update_m3u_digest_settings` tools (`GET`/`PUT /api/m3u/digest/settings`) — the update tool only sends fields the caller passes, including the `account_ids` filter (bead wwovg); (4) `preview_event_sync`'s renderer now surfaces `preflight.warnings` (e.g. the stale-dateless rail-inert warning, bead 2ey2y) and the `stale_suspect_streams` / `freshness_unknown_streams` staleness counts in its summary line. All four follow the existing `Endpoint` contract + `@mcp.tool` pattern, validated against the live backend OpenAPI spec by the MCP tool contract test. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-fltt3)
- **Event Sync: operator never-attach exclusions — durable "never attach this provider stream to that event" standing orders (bead ti939.3.5, build 0136, GH epic ti939).** Stateless recompute meant a false-positive attach the operator manually detached was re-attached on the next run, forever, until the pattern/threshold changed. New `event_sync_exclusions` table (Alembic migration 0036) keyed on content fingerprints ONLY — `(rule_id, provider_id, stream_name_hash, event_key)`, never stream/channel ids (both churn) — so an exclusion survives provider refreshes and stream-ID churn by construction. The shared resolver consults exclusions BEFORE the attach band is honored (and before accept-upgrades: an exclusion OUTRANKS a prior review-queue accept for the same pairing — the two never both apply); an excluded pairing can neither auto-attach nor re-enter the review queue, and reports as the distinct `excluded_by_operator` disposition in preview rows/summary counts, run summaries, and the one-line drift detector. Operator surface: a "Never attach" action on Event Sync Review queue cards (records the exclusion, closes the question) plus a new Never-attach Exclusions panel below the queue to list and remove standing orders (removal makes the pairing matchable again on the next run/preview). Create/delete journal under category `event_sync` (`exclusion_create`/`exclusion_delete`); exclusions are preserved and re-keyed by rule name across config backup/restore, mirroring the review-decision preservation. New `GET/POST /api/event-sync-exclusions` + `DELETE /{id}` endpoints and MCP tools `list_event_sync_exclusions` / `create_event_sync_exclusion` / `delete_event_sync_exclusion` (two-step confirm on delete); `preview_event_sync` MCP output renders the excluded count and per-row EXCLUDED lines. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-ti939.3.5)
- **Stats: new Provider Stream Usage section shows per-provider assigned-stream counts and utilization (bead n5cwp, build 0135, GH-482).** Neither Dispatcharr nor ECM previously surfaced how much of each M3U provider's stream catalog is actually wired into a channel. New `GET /api/stats/providers/stream-usage` endpoint reuses the existing cached reverse stream→channel index (`routers.streams._get_stream_channel_index`) rather than re-sweeping every channel, and is itself cached for 300s. Two counting metrics are surfaced intentionally: `assigned_streams` (PRIMARY) — distinct streams from the provider assigned to at least one channel (a stream in 3 channels counts once); `total_assignments` (secondary) — sum of channel-memberships across those streams (a stream in 2 channels counts twice), surfacing providers whose streams get heavily reused. `total_streams` (the provider's full catalog size) and `utilization_pct` give scale/context; an assigned stream whose `m3u_account` doesn't resolve to a known/current provider lands in a synthetic "Unknown" bucket rather than being silently dropped. Not admin-gated — Dispatcharr-derived catalog/assignment data, the same trust tier as `GET /api/stats/channels`, not per-user watch history like the sibling `/providers/*` telemetry endpoints. New sortable `ProviderStreamUsagePanel` renders the table on the Stats tab. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-n5cwp)
- **Stats: Live Stats provider display condenses to a table when more than 6 providers are configured (bead 49obj, build 0135, GH-481).** Reported on an 11-provider install: the per-provider tile badges in the Live Stats header wrap into several rows and providers scroll out of view once there are more than a handful. Above a 6-provider threshold, the tile badges are replaced by a condensed table (name + live connection count per provider) inside a 220px max-height scroll container, so the header stays a fixed, legible size regardless of provider count; installs with 6 or fewer providers keep the existing tile layout unchanged. A settings-level toggle to force the condensed/tile layout independent of provider count was considered and deliberately deferred as a follow-up candidate — the auto-switch threshold covers the reported case. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-49obj)
- **M3U Change Digest: notifications can now be scoped to selected M3U accounts — change logging stays complete (bead wwovg, build 0135, GH-496).** An operator running a high-churn "FAST" provider (10k+ stream URL changes/hour) alongside slow-changing standard providers wanted that provider's changes excluded from digest emails/Discord without losing its change history. New "Account Filter" section on Settings → M3U Digest (reusing the shared `GroupMultiSelectDropdown` component) lets an operator pick a subset of M3U accounts to include in digest notifications; `M3UChangeLog` DB logging is never filtered by this setting, regardless of selection — only what gets built into an email/Discord notification is scoped. Empty/unset selection means "all accounts" (unchanged default behavior for every existing install). New `M3UDigestSettings.account_ids` column (nullable JSON, Alembic migration 0035) backs the setting; `tasks/m3u_digest.py`'s digest-build query applies the scope via `M3UChangeLog.m3u_account_id.in_(account_ids)` when no single-account filter is already active. The immediate-digest path (fires for one account right after its own M3U refresh) skips sending entirely — without building anything — when that account isn't in a non-empty selection, logging at INFO; the operator deliberately excluded it. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-wwovg)
- **Event Sync preview surfaces staleness-signal counts and warns when the stale-dateless demote rail is inert (bead 2ey2y, build 0132).** The jqwfq stale-dateless demote rail fails open by design when no qualifying M3U snapshot exists (young deployment, change monitor disabled, group not captured) — correct, but previously silent: an operator who turned the rail on had no way to see it wasn't protecting anything. The preview pre-flight now emits an explicit WARNING (check id `staleness_rail_snapshots`) when a rule has `assume_current_date` + `demote_stale_dateless` on but zero secondary streams are covered by a usable previous-day snapshot, and the preview summary carries `stale_suspect_streams` / `freshness_unknown_streams` counts so the tri-state per-stream signal is visible at a glance.
