Chose **"Account Profiles"** for the per-account feature instead — it matches the underlying `M3UAccountProfile` type name exactly and doesn't collide with either existing term. Label-only change, no behavior change: M3U Manager's per-account row-action button title/aria-label goes from "Manage Profiles" to "Manage Account Profiles" (`M3UManagerTab.tsx`, preserving the row's existing "Manage Groups" / "Manage Filters" verb+noun pattern), its modal header goes from "Manage Profiles - {account}" to "Account Profiles - {account}" (`M3UProfileModal.tsx`), and Channel Manager's overflow-menu entry goes from bare "Manage Profiles" to "Channel Profiles" (`ChannelsPane.tsx`), matching `ChannelProfilesListModal`'s own modal title. No other user-facing "Manage Profiles" occurrences found in the frontend or `docs/user_guide/`. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-09x38.6)
- **Per-tab tutorial template + `docs/user_guide/` authoring-convention update — prerequisite for the per-tab tutorial epic (bead enhancedchannelmanager-gsnw0.1, build 0112).** No functional change — this is documentation only. `docs/user_guide/README.md` gains: (1) a literal, copyable "Common tasks" tutorial skeleton — title, a one-sentence audience blockquote, a 2-sentence-max "what this tab is for," numbered goal→steps→**Result** walkthroughs, and a "Going deeper" cross-link section — distilled from the existing `backup-restore/` ("Start here" task-router) and `integrations/` (goal-first setup + explicit end state) sections; (2) a formalized, repo-wide screenshot spec — 1280×720 viewport (matching the existing pin in `e2e/visual-regression.spec.ts`), dark theme, numbered kebab-case `<n>-<short-name>.png` filenames under `docs/images/user_guide/<section>/`, and mandatory inline placement immediately after the step illustrated, never an end-of-article dump — formalizing the pattern already used by `docs/images/event_sync/` (`docs/images/normalization/`'s two pre-existing images are grandfathered, not recaptured); and (3) a capture-mechanics note (Playwright against the dev container, representative seeded data, captured in the same PR as the tutorial bead that needs it). `docs/user_guide/index.md` gains a "By tab" navigation table — one row per UI tab in nav order, linking to the section that owns (or will own) that tab's tutorials, with the net-new sections (`m3u-manager/`, `guide/`, `m3u-changes/`, `logo-manager/`, `journal/`, `settings/`) marked **Planned**. Blocks the 15 remaining per-tab tutorial child beads (gsnw0.2–.16). (bead enhancedchannelmanager-gsnw0.1)
- **Backend test suite self-explains the cryptography-42 venv requirement instead of failing confusingly (bead enhancedchannelmanager-vol5d, build 0109).** No user-facing behavior change. Two engineers independently lost time to 7-9 failures in `backend/tests/unit/test_tls_storage.py` when running the backend suite under a bare system `python3` — its `cryptography` (41.0.7) predates the `x509.Certificate.not_valid_before_utc`/`not_valid_after_utc` properties (added in cryptography 42) that `tls/storage.py`'s `parse_certificate()` reads, and the code's broad exception handling turned the resulting `AttributeError` into confusing assertion failures (wrong subject, wrong validity) rather than an obvious error naming the cause. Fixed both ends: `docs/testing.md` gains a prominent note at the top of the backend-testing section directing agents to `.venv/bin/python -m pytest`; the 9 affected test methods (exactly the ones whose assertions require a genuinely-parsed certificate — directory perms, raw file round-trips, ACME JSON persistence, and the already-invalid/malformed/mismatched/timezone-skew paths are unaffected and still run under any cryptography version) get a `pytest.mark.skipif` gated on a dependency-free `cryptography.__version__` parse, with a reason string naming the fix. Zero new skips under the project venv (cryptography 42+): all 37 tests in the file still pass. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-vol5d)
- **Style guide gains shell-scripting quoting rules and a `docs/user_guide/` kebab-case filename exception (beads enhancedchannelmanager-0zorv / y8vt4, build 0109).** No user-facing behavior change. New Shell Scripting section in `docs/style_guide.md` codifies quoting parameter expansions that flow into exec argv (a class of bug that has appeared twice in `backend/entrypoint.sh`), whitelisting env vars that select behavior (the `ECM_UVICORN_LOOP` case-statement pattern), and failing closed with a warning instead of crash-looping. The Filenames section now documents the existing `docs/user_guide/` kebab-case exception to the general `snake_case.md` rule, matching that subtree's own README. (beads enhancedchannelmanager-0zorv / enhancedchannelmanager-y8vt4)
- **Test-suite hardening from the 2026-07 full-suite audit — batch 2 (beads u8qr6.2/.4/.5/.7, build 0094).** No user-facing behavior change. Tightened weak/smoke assertions that accepted failure (e.g. `status in (200, 500)`) as a pass — including a stream-preview test that was silently vacuous (its payload was SSRF-rejected before the code under test ran); added real behavioral coverage for previously-untested production paths (5 `ConditionEvaluator` condition types, `StruckStreamCleanupTask` error handler + pagination loop, `validate_alert_sources` branches); pinned forwarded args / side-effects in mock-wiring tests (digest `since`/account filter, alert `reload_method`); and added missing-direction regression tests. The reverse-order create+delete test surfaced a real (pre-existing) consolidator bug — captured as a strict-`xfail` linked to bead 9rjgr so it flips to a hard failure the moment the bug is fixed. (beads enhancedchannelmanager-u8qr6.2/.4/.5/.7)
- **Test-suite hardening from the 2026-07 full-suite audit — batch 1 (beads u8qr6.1/.3/.6, build 0093).** No user-facing behavior change. (1) The M3U-digest exclude-filter logic was extracted from `M3UDigestTask.execute()` into a module-level `apply_exclude_filters()` (behavior-preserving) so its tests exercise the **real** production path incl. the `safe_regex` ReDoS/oversize contract — the previous tests ran a hand-copied mirror that used bare `re` and never touched `safe_regex`. (2) Added a real `CertificateStorage` test suite (`tls/storage.py`) covering private-key/cert save/load/parse/validate-pair/delete/ACME persistence, including mismatched-pair rejection and on-disk permission bits (14 previously-untested methods handling key material). (3) De-flaked wall-clock timing assertions (bcrypt work-factor parsed from the hash; event-loop non-blocking checked by in-flight/completion-order; a microbench moved behind `@pytest.mark.slow`). (beads enhancedchannelmanager-u8qr6.1/.3/.6)

### Added
- **Event Sync: preferred-provider stream ordering on master channels via per-rule stream sort (bead io0tv, build 0108).** Event Sync attaches always appended, so the master's own Dispatcharr-synced stream stayed first and secondary-provider streams landed at the bottom — with no way to float a preferred provider's feed to the top (the stream that PLAYS). The rule's existing `stream_sort_field`/`stream_sort_order` columns — previously dead for event_sync rules (the engine's Pass 3.5 reorder never saw event_sync-touched masters) — are now live: the attach phase registers each rule's touched master channels for the Pass 3.5 stream reorder (including idempotent already-attached no-ops, so ordering re-heals on EVERY run — a provider-priority change applies on the next run without detaching anything) and extends the reorder's provider map from the event_sync fetch plus one bulk stream lookup for the masters' own streams, so `provider_order` scoring sees every stream on the channel. Rule editor: new **Behavior → Stream order** subgroup (Provider Order / Quality / Stream Name / Smart Sort; default "No sorting" — existing rules keep append-only behavior unchanged). Recipe (set M3U Manager priorities + rule `provider_order` desc) documented in `docs/event_sync.md`, including the dummy-EPG note: ordering changes which stream plays, not the guide text. Also pins the previously-untested skipped-merge → Pass 3.5 registration side effect on the standard-rule path. Live-verified: a two-provider master reordered `[native, P2, P3]` → `[P3(pri 90), P2(pri 80), native(pri 10)]`, and a fully no-op re-run still evaluated ordering ("already sorted … skipping"). (bead enhancedchannelmanager-io0tv)
- **MCP `list_tasks` now reports whether a task will actually run, not just its toggle (bead vkktd.5, build 0092).** Following the vkktd effective-enabled work, the `list_tasks` MCP tool surfaced only the parent task toggle, so an AI agent saw a gated-off task (task enabled but no active schedule) as plain "enabled" — the same "reads Enabled but won't fire" trap the rest of vkktd removed for the UI/API. It now renders three states from `effective_enabled`: **enabled**, **"enabled but WON'T RUN (no active schedule)"**, and **disabled** (with a safe fallback to the task toggle for older backend payloads). Closes the agent-facing half of the trap. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-vkktd.5)
- **Scheduled tasks that read "Enabled" but never fire are now visible and self-correcting (epic vkktd, build 0091).** A scheduled task only fires when BOTH its task-level toggle AND at least one of its schedules are enabled; opt-in tasks (e.g. Auto Channel Creation) ship with the task toggle off. Previously, when a task was gated off the scheduler skipped it **silently** (no log), the debug bundle reported the always-on child schedule as `enabled: true` (masking the real state), and turning the task "Enabled" only flipped the task gate — so if a schedule was disabled, the task read Enabled yet never ran, with no feedback. This surfaced as "matching isn't running on M3U refresh." Now: **(observability)** the scheduler logs a throttled reason when a due task is skipped because its parent is disabled or unregistered, and logs (throttled INFO) when a refresh watermark is pending but suppressed (no enabled run-on-refresh rule); the M3U-refresh watermark line no longer claims auto-creation "will pick it up" when it's gated off. **(diagnosability)** the debug bundle now reports `effective_enabled = parent AND ≥1 enabled schedule`, and `GET /api/tasks` exposes `effective_enabled`, so a gated-off task can no longer read a bare "enabled". **(self-correction)** enabling a task now auto-reconciles its schedule — it enables the task's schedule (the single one, or the most-recently-created when several exist) so "Enabled" actually runs; MANUAL tasks are never reconciled and disabling never touches schedules. Frontend surfacing of the new `effective_enabled` state is tracked as a follow-up. (epic enhancedchannelmanager-vkktd — beads vkktd.1/.2/.3)
- **Timezone-aware EPG matching — West channels prefer their (Pacific) guide row (bead vznut.2, build 0090).** The EPG matcher was timezone-*blind*: it stripped EAST/WEST when building the match key, and "Pacific" wasn't recognized as a West synonym, so a "…West" channel scored the East entry higher than its correct "(Pacific)" guide row (the root cause behind the shared-EPG-link field reports). Matching is now timezone-*aware*: region words (West≡Pacific, plus Central/Mountain) collapse into the match key so the regional entry becomes an exact candidate, region parentheticals/tokens are neutralized so the Pacific row stays token-symmetric with its base sibling, and a region-consistency tie-break prefers the region that matches the channel (West channel → West/Pacific row; East → East). Region is detected from the tvg_id parenthetical then the trailing name word, skipping quality/format tokens ("AMC West HD"→West) and bare trailing digits; adjective forms ("Eastern"/"Western") are guarded out. Band-preserving — it only re-orders genuine ties and never outranks country/exact matches, and is inert for non-regional channels. Scope: `match_channels_epg` only (auto-creation's separate matcher tracked as follow-up vznut.4). (bead enhancedchannelmanager-vznut.2)
- **Shared EPG-link audit — find channels silently pointing at the same EPG row (bead vznut.1, build 0089).** A read-only audit surfaces every set of channels that share one `epg_data_id` — the fingerprint of the "West feed silently showing East's schedule" bug, where a regional channel is linked to its counterpart's guide row. It appears two ways: a **"shared EPG links"** summary embedded in the EPG match preview (scoped to the channels in the current match set), and a whole-fleet check via the new `GET /api/epg/audit-duplicates` endpoint / `audit_epg_duplicates` MCP tool. Each reported group names the shared EPG row (id, name, `tvg_id`), the channels sharing it, and the count; NULL/unlinked channels are excluded and output is deterministic. Read-only — it reports, it never re-links. See `docs/user_guide/epg/finding-mislinked-channels.md`. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-vznut.1)
- **Build-number ADVANCE gate — CI hard-check + Claude Code agent ship hook (bead w9irb, build 0086).** The `version-consistency` CI job previously proved the three version touchpoints MATCH but not that the build number ADVANCED, so ~9 source PRs shipped to dev at `0.17.6-0084` with no bump, undetected. A new shared checker (`scripts/check_version_advances.py`) parses the `frontend/package.json` build number and fails if it is not strictly greater than the base branch's (`origin/dev`), suggesting the next number in the failure message. It is wired into CI as a required additive step on PRs to `dev` (docs-only PRs stay exempt via the existing `docs-only-pass.yml` classification; release-cut PRs to `main` are exempt because the version legitimately drops its `-BUILD` suffix), and into a checked-in Claude Code `PreToolUse` hook (`.claude/settings.json` + `.claude/hooks/version-advance-guard.sh`) that blocks a `gh pr create` ship when the local build did not advance. A `CHANGELOG.md [Unreleased]` touch check is included but SOFT — it warns, never blocks. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-w9irb)
- **New advisory endpoint `POST /api/channel-pipeline/rules/analyze-body` runs the Rule Analyzer against an UNSAVED rule body (bead m1s38.2, build 0085).** Lets the rule editor surface analyzer findings (guard/no-op/redundant-condition style warnings) while a rule is still being drafted, instead of only after it has been saved. Advisory-only — findings never block or gate save — with a typed frontend binding consuming the new endpoint. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-m1s38.2)
- **New `scripts/deploy-frontend.sh` performs an atomic frontend deploy — build, then clear stale assets, then copy (bead x7pck, build 0085).** Wraps the manual build → clear `/app/static/assets/` → copy sequence into one script, so a deploy can no longer skip the stale-asset cleanup step and leave an old Vite bundle mixed in with the new one. Documents the cleanup requirement alongside the script. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-x7pck)
- **Event Sync matching diagnostics added to the Channel Pipeline debug bundle (bead 03nji, build 0085).** The exportable debug bundle now includes per-stream parse/score/band/disposition/reject-reason detail for Event Sync rules, so an operator troubleshooting a matching problem can export one bundle instead of reproducing the issue live. Also adds gated per-candidate DEBUG logging in the matching resolver. Observability only — no change to matching behavior. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-03nji)
- **Event Sync matcher core now emits per-step DEBUG logging so a user report can be traced line-by-line (bead bbhpy, build 0087).** The matcher (`event_sync_matcher.py`) previously had a logger but zero calls, leaving the parse and scoring decisions dark below the resolver-layer logging from 03nji. It now emits, at `LOG_LEVEL=DEBUG`, one line per name parsed (`parsed` / `title_only_no_time` / `unparsed` with the matched pattern — directly exposing the `no_parsed_time` root cause), one line per scored pair (band/score/fuzzy/team-verdict/team-score/time-delta/reject-reason, covering every branch), a master-side summary (usable vs. unusable masters, naming each unusable one), and — closing a blind spot — one line per time-window-blocked pair (`reject=outside_time_window` with the delta and window), so the "right event, wrong time" case is visible instead of vanishing into a silent unmatched stream. All lines are `logger.isEnabledFor(DEBUG)`-guarded (zero cost when off); observability only — no change to matching behavior. Enable via Settings → backend log level = DEBUG (applied live, no restart). (bead enhancedchannelmanager-bbhpy)
- **Event Sync rules now show discoverable per-rule Run and Test buttons (bead utswf, build 0085).** Run/Test controls for Event Sync rules existed but were effectively hidden; they now appear in the rules list the same way standard-rule controls do. A manual live Run now asks for confirmation first, since it can attach streams. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-utswf)
- **New per-rule "refresh this rule's M3U providers before running" option for Event Sync rules (bead y8yby, build 0085).** Applies to manual Run and Test (auto-runs are excluded); best-effort — a refresh failure does not block the run. Documents the refresh-ordering/self-healing behavior in `docs/event_sync.md`. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-y8yby)
- **Event Sync matching controls: per-rule "ignore time window" toggle, operator-authoritative attach threshold, and acronym/hyphen-aware title scoring (beads krkm4 / amue8 / e0jzj, build 0084).** Three independent matcher improvements, driven by a real multi-provider FloRacing case where the same events are timestamped differently and named with heavy slot/venue noise. **Ignore time window (krkm4):** a new `event_sync_config.enforce_time_window` flag (default **true** — absent means enforced) disables the time-window candidacy gate when set false, so every parsed master event becomes a candidate and matches rank on title/team score alone. It rescues events whose providers publish different start times for the same fixture (one provider listed a race 6 hours off). Editor exposes it under Advanced as "Ignore time window (match on title only)", which greys the window input and warns it is safe only for a single-provider, same-day master group (recurring/serial titles can match the wrong day). The team-conflict/numeric-identity rails and the 0.90 no-teams floor still apply, so borderline pairs route to review, not auto-attach. **Operator-authoritative attach threshold (amue8):** `attach_threshold` is now any value in `[0, 1]` — 0.80 is the DEFAULT the validator fills, no longer a hard floor. `is_event_attachable` honors the operator's value directly; the 0.90 no-teams raise still applies while the threshold stays ≥ 0.80 (default configs and the frozen corpus are byte-identical) but is bypassed once the operator deliberately lowers below 0.80, so the lowering they asked for is not silently vetoed. Team-token conflict and different-event-number hard rejects fire at any threshold. **Acronym/hyphen title bridges (e0jzj):** `_fuzzy_title_score` now normalizes two classes of provider spelling variance before scoring, both corroboration-gated so they only ever ADD token agreement — a hyphenated token splits (`Shangri-La` → `Shangri La`, `Off-Road` → `Off Road`) ONLY when the other title carries that word-run (a blanket split spuriously matched `Pre-Race Show` to `Race Day Live` at the same venue — caught by the frozen corpus and fixed), and an acronym token collapses with the consecutive words it spells (`RoC` ↔ `Race of Champions`, `MUFC` ↔ `Manchester United`, FC-suffix aware) — reusing the same initialism logic the team-token layer already had, extended to teamless titles. The shared `clean_name` (used by dedup) is untouched. Together these took the FloRacing test rule from zero matches to a full clean attach at the default 0.80 threshold. Frozen precision/recall corpus unchanged (8/8). Docs: `docs/event_sync.md` → "Threshold and bands" (How the score is computed / Lowering the threshold / Disabling the time window). (beads enhancedchannelmanager-krkm4 / enhancedchannelmanager-amue8 / enhancedchannelmanager-e0jzj)
- **Event Sync rule editor: master + secondary group pickers default to ENABLED groups only, with a "Show all groups" escape hatch (bead x82s3, build 0074).** A real Dispatcharr instance can carry hundreds of channel groups, and the master-group select and secondary-groups multi-select previously listed every one of them — noisy, and mostly irrelevant to any single rule. Both pickers now default to groups where the group's provider setting is enabled (`M3UGroupSetting.enabled === true`; a group with no provider-settings entry at all counts as not-enabled and stays hidden). A new **Show all groups** checkbox (default off, shared by both pickers) reveals the full list for edge cases — a temporarily-disabled group, or one with no provider settings. The existing secondary name-filter composes with the enabled filter (both apply together). **Round-trip guard:** a group already referenced by the rule being edited — the saved master group, or an already-checked secondary — always renders, marked `(disabled)`, even when the enabled-only filter would otherwise hide it, and is never dropped from the saved config on save. Client-only change (no backend/API changes) — auto-sync pre-flight/guidance semantics are unchanged; this only affects which groups are *listed*. Docs: `docs/event_sync.md` → "Configure master + secondary groups". (bead enhancedchannelmanager-x82s3)
- **Event Sync Phase 2: ambiguous-band review queue keyed on event identity (bead ti939.3.2, build 0073).** Ambiguous-band event_sync matches — a candidate that scored below the attach threshold but above reject, or a contested tie between two masters — now enqueue into an operator **review queue** instead of being silently skipped, so a borderline event consolidates with one click instead of a threshold gamble. **Keying (hard security constraint):** every pending question and every accepted/rejected outcome keys on the **content fingerprint** `(rule_id, provider_id, stream_name_hash, event_key)` — **never** channel or stream IDs. Dispatcharr stream IDs churn on every provider refresh and channel IDs live only while an event's channel exists, so an ID-keyed queue would refill with duplicates of already-answered questions after every refresh; the fingerprint is DB-enforced by a full unique index on the new `event_sync_reviews` table (migration 0032), so an answered question can only refresh its row, never re-insert. Decisions **survive refreshes** and re-apply whenever the same provider string + event identity recurs. **Decisions are an input to the ONE resolver, not a second scorer** (`services/event_sync_resolver.py`, shared by preview and attach — dry-run parity by construction): a prior **accept** upgrades an ambiguous disposition to a queue-driven attach when the matcher still surfaces that master as an attach-/ambiguous-band candidate (accepts never override the matcher's hard rejects — team-token conflict, time window, below the ambiguous floor: precision over recall, the 1,341-merge trust benchmark); a prior **reject** removes the pairing from candidacy entirely, so it can neither attach nor re-enter the queue. **Accept** records the durable fingerprint decision, supersedes sibling pending pairings for the same stream, then best-effort attaches immediately — snapshot channel/stream ids in the display-only `evidence` JSON are re-verified against live Dispatcharr (channel name must still parse to the row's `event_key`; stream name must still hash to `stream_name_hash`) and a failed verification defers the attach to the next idempotent run; the decision is the durable artifact either way. **Journal distinction:** accepts/rejects write `journal_entries` rows (category `event_sync`, action `review_accept`/`review_reject`) and a queue-driven attach writes the standard `merge_stream` entry with `after_value.match.attach_source = "review_queue"` (threshold attaches carry `"threshold"`), keeping the two distinguishable and both covered by the journal-driven surgical unmerge. **UI (`EventSyncReviewQueue`, on the Channel Pipeline tab):** each pairing shows **per-candidate evidence** — both raw names side by side, parsed titles/starts, start-time delta, team-token verdict, score and band (badges are text + icon, never color alone) — because an opaque aggregate score was the human-factors condition that let the original incident scale. The **preview** decorates candidates of a saved rule with `Pending review` / `Accepted` / `Rejected` markers and counts queue-driven would-attaches separately. New endpoints: `GET /api/event-sync-reviews` (paginated, `RequireAuthIfEnabled`), `POST /api/event-sync-reviews/{id}/accept` and `/reject` (`RequireAdminIfEnabled`). Blast-radius controls: per-stream candidate cap and a per-run enqueue cap (default 500, warns and defers the remainder to the next idempotent run). Docs: `docs/event_sync.md` → "Reviewing ambiguous matches (Phase 2 review queue)" + "Review queue keying (ti939.3.2)"; `docs/api.md` → "Event Sync Reviews". (bead enhancedchannelmanager-ti939.3.2)
- **Event Sync Phase 2: dummy EPG auto-assignment for master event channels + guided one-click confirmed auto-sync fix (beads ti939.3.3 / ti939.3.4, build 0072).** **Dummy EPG (ti939.3.3):** an event_sync rule can now reference a dummy EPG profile (`event_sync_config.dummy_epg_profile_id` — optional, absent means off, must reference an existing profile or save fails with a teaching error; the rule editor exposes it under Advanced and API-set values round-trip) that gets assigned to the master group's channels on EVERY run, manual and auto-run, so new event channels get guide data automatically. The assignment rides the EXISTING machinery end to end: standard `assign_epg` execution against the Dispatcharr source serving the profile's XMLTV, with not-yet-covered channels deferring into the existing **Pass 5** refresh-and-retry (auto-adds the master group to the profile, regenerates the XMLTV, refreshes the source, retries — no parallel mechanism, exercised by test). Idempotent (already-assigned channels are no-ops), never clobbers foreign guide data, and degrades gracefully: a disabled profile or missing Dispatcharr source warns and skips only the EPG step — attaches are never blocked. Docs show an event profile whose patterns match the master provider's naming and call out that they can reuse the rule's own parse patterns (author once, paste twice). **Guided auto-sync fix (ti939.3.4, security-constrained):** when the rule editor's live status shows the master group with `auto_channel_sync` OFF or a secondary with it ON, a **Fix** button offers a one-click correction — behind its own confirmation dialog stating exactly what will change and why, including the consequence ("existing auto-created channels from it may be removed by Dispatcharr") and the recovery note (snapshot restore does NOT revert Dispatcharr group settings — the journal entry is the recovery breadcrumb). The toggle is a dedicated, admin-gated endpoint (`POST /api/m3u/accounts/{id}/group-auto-sync-toggle`) that hard-requires `confirm: true`, supports both directions (enable master / disable secondary), journals every toggle with before/after values, and is NEVER called as a side effect of saving a rule or running the pipeline (pinned by tests). It lives on the M3U router, outside the event_sync feature modules — the AST no-group-writes gate keeps proving the attach/preview path itself never writes group settings (and now also scans the new dummy-EPG assignment functions). Docs: `docs/event_sync.md` → "Automatic guide data for master channels (dummy EPG)" and "Guided setup: the confirmed auto-sync fix". (beads enhancedchannelmanager-ti939.3.3 / enhancedchannelmanager-ti939.3.4)
- **Event Sync Phase 2: opt-in auto-run on the refresh watermark (per-rule flag, default OFF) with unattended notifications, gated by the circuit breaker (beads ti939.3.1 / ixujz, build 0071).** An event_sync rule can now opt into UNATTENDED runs on the existing post-refresh watermark task by setting the new `event_sync_config.auto_run` flag — an explicit per-rule opt-in, **default false** (absent means false, so every stored config keeps manual-run-only behavior unchanged; the rule editor exposes it as an Advanced checkbox with honest copy, and API-authored values round-trip). The relaxation is surgical and layered, each layer pinned by its own test: the watermark task's rule query selects only opted-in event_sync rules (standard rules and non-opted event_sync rules are untouched); the engine's new per-rule trigger gate (`event_sync_trigger_allowed`) admits ONLY the watermark trigger for opted-in rules — "scheduled" and unidentified triggers stay denied for every rule (deny-by-default preserved); and the attach phase re-checks the gate as defense in depth. **Circuit breaker (bead ixujz):** a tripped run-on-refresh breaker (crash-sentinel after an abandoned run, or the `ECM_DISABLE_RUN_ON_REFRESH` break-glass) now gates the event_sync auto-run chain too — at the task tick AND inside the attach phase — while manual runs stay available as the recovery surface; reset restores auto-runs (trip and reset both tested end-to-end). **Unattended runs are indistinguishable from manual runs on the audit surfaces** (same journal provenance — category `event_sync`, batch_id = execution id, names+ids plus score/band/time-delta/team-verdict — and the same `event_sync: X attached, …` summary line, asserted field-for-field against a manual run), and misconfigurations notify instead of hiding: attach-cap overage and pre-flight failures raise warning notifications with alerts on (unattended runs pre-flight the Dispatcharr group settings and FAIL CLOSED — e.g. master auto-sync OFF skips the rule that run; manual runs are unchanged and never pre-flight), and the completion notification carries the attach count. The refresh-ordering race stays accepted-not-engineered-around: a watermark run that precedes master-channel materialization attaches nothing and converges next run (now covered on the unattended path too). Docs: `docs/event_sync.md` → "Automatic runs after refresh (Phase 2 opt-in)". (beads enhancedchannelmanager-ti939.3.1 / enhancedchannelmanager-ixujz)
- **Event Sync E2E happy path + documented pre-release manual attach script (bead ti939.2.4, build 0069).** New live-container E2E (`backend/tests/e2e/test_event_sync.py`) covers configure-with-shipped-patterns → Test Patterns parsing → zero-write preview with exactly-reconciling summary counts → manual single-rule run (completes with the `event_sync:` summary line, provably attach-nothing by picking a zero-channel master group) → journal provenance queryable by category + batch_id. The multi-provider attach segment cannot be demonstrated on the dev instance (no auto-sync master group exists, and ECM never toggles that Dispatcharr setting), so it is recorded as a documented pre-release manual script in `docs/event_sync.md` → "Testing & pre-release verification", with the mocked-Dispatcharr coverage (lifecycle + attach-execution unit tests) cross-referenced. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-ti939.2.4)
- **Event Sync Phase 1B: the attach path — event-mode merge execution, manual-run-only, fully reversible (beads ti939.2.1 / ti939.2.2 / ti939.2.3, build 0068).** A **manually triggered** pipeline run now executes event_sync rules through a dedicated attach phase: every secondary-group stream resolves through the SAME resolver the preview endpoint calls (`services/event_sync_resolver.py` — dry-run parity by construction, pinned by a frozen-time test asserting preview decisions equal live-run decisions on identical fixtures), and attach-band matches are attached to their master channel via the existing merge internals — idempotent (already-attached is a no-op, so re-running after every refresh is the intended usage), capped per run (`max_attach_per_run`, overage warns on the execution record), and journaled per attach (category `event_sync`, batch_id = execution id, names alongside IDs plus score/band/time-delta/team-verdict). Ambiguous or contested matches are skipped and counted, never attached. The run writes the operator's drift-detector summary line (`event_sync: X attached, Y ambiguous skipped, Z unmatched, W parse failures`) into the execution log. **Manual-run-only is enforced twice** — the unattended watermark task excludes event_sync rules from its query, and the engine refuses any non-manual/api trigger; `run_pipeline`'s `triggered_by` now defaults to a DENIED sentinel so an unidentified future caller can never execute event_sync rules by accident. **Recovery is verified end-to-end (SRE ship-blocker):** the pre-mutation snapshot includes the master group's channels; execution rollback removes every attachment the run made and never deletes the master channels; master channels are restored with a **streams-only** payload (their Dispatcharr-owned name/group/EPG metadata is never reverted to stale pre-run values); the channel-pipeline circuit breaker covers crashed event_sync runs; and an AST gate proves no event_sync code path writes Dispatcharr group settings. Lifecycle integration tests encode the verified Dispatcharr behaviors (UUID-preserving in-place refreshes, foreign-stream survival, master-scoped deletion) as fixtures: idempotent re-runs, refresh survival, event-end cleanup with no re-attach, refresh-ordering-race convergence, and in-place slot renames. New troubleshooting runbook: [Undo a bad event_sync run](docs/event_sync.md#undo-a-bad-event_sync-run) — including the reminder that a deterministic matcher repeats a bad match until the rule is adjusted. (beads enhancedchannelmanager-ti939.2.1 / ti939.2.2 / ti939.2.3)
- **Event Sync rule editor, shipped default parse patterns, Test Patterns panel, and match preview UI — preview-only, with NO apply/attach control anywhere (bead ti939.1.5, build 0067).** The Channel Pipeline tab's Create Rule flow now opens a rule-kind chooser (Standard vs Event Sync), and event_sync rules get their own editor built around the quick path *pick master → pick secondaries → keep the shipped patterns → Preview*: a master-group select showing LIVE Dispatcharr auto-sync status (guidance text when it's OFF — ECM never toggles group settings this phase), a secondary-groups multi-select with the inverse should-be-OFF validation and the same guidance-not-mutation stance, and a shipped-pattern picker (the two backend built-ins for "Title @ Day-first/Month-first Date Time" including "Fubo Sports Network 07 :"-style slot prefixes — kept verbatim-in-sync with `DEFAULT_EVENT_PATTERNS` and omitted from the saved config when unchanged so backend improvements flow through — plus two generic no-"@" variants and a custom-regex fallback; the whole shipped set lives in a JSON fixture consumed by both the UI and a backend parity test that runs every entry's example through the real `parse_event_name`, so a shipped pattern that cannot parse its own example fails CI) so most operators never write a regex. Advanced knobs stay collapsed: time window (1–1440 min), attach threshold (input clamped to the ≥ 0.80 hard floor), and per-group pattern overrides. A **Test Patterns panel** (pasted sample names or live samples fetched from the rule's groups) runs extraction server-side through the dummy-EPG batch preview — the exact `extract_groups`/`safe_regex` machinery the matcher uses — and renders a native table of raw name → extracted title/date/time per pattern, flagging incomplete date/times as parse failures (start times are never guessed). The **match preview** renders the zero-write preview endpoint's response: keyboard-navigable match cards showing each provider's RAW name side by side with parsed title/time, score, confidence band and team-token verdict as text labels + icons (never color alone), time-delta/disparity flags, a distinct unmatched-secondary-streams list, a parse-failure panel grouped by group/reason, pre-flight failures with their teaching messages, and a reconciling summary line ("42 would attach, 8 ambiguous (skipped), 3 unmatched, 1 parse failure"). Event Sync rules show an "Event Sync" badge in the rules list and hide the run/dry-run controls (they are excluded from pipeline execution entirely in Phase 1A); vitest pins the threshold clamp, the band/pattern-test rendering, and the absence of any apply/attach control. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-ti939.1.5)
- **Event Sync dry-run preview: `POST /api/channel-pipeline/event-sync-preview` + `preview_event_sync` MCP tool — match secondary streams against live master channels with ZERO writes (bead ti939.1.4, build 0066).** Phase 1A of Event Sync becomes exercisable end to end: the new endpoint accepts a saved event_sync rule id OR an inline `event_sync_config` (so the rule editor can preview before saving; inline configs run through the same `validate_event_sync_config` teaching-error rail set as rule save), runs the read-only pre-flight (master group auto-sync ON, secondaries OFF — failures SURFACE in the response but never block the preview, so a misconfiguration is visible alongside what the matcher would still do), fetches the master group's channels and every secondary group's streams from Dispatcharr (paginated, DoS-capped at 2,000 each with a `truncated` flag), and resolves matches through the new shared resolver `backend/services/event_sync_resolver.py` — the EXACT function the future Phase 1B attach path will call, so preview scoring and attach scoring cannot diverge (dry-run parity by construction). The response carries per-stream rows (raw name, provider, group, parsed title + start, disposition, up to 10 scored candidates with master name + current channel id, score, band, team-token verdict, time delta, and machine-readable reject reason), the unmatched-streams list (the master-as-ceiling visibility hedge), parse failures grouped by group/reason with counts and sample names (a silently broken pattern is LOUD), unparsable master channel names, and summary counts (would-attach / ambiguous-skipped / unmatched / parse-failed) that reconcile exactly with the detail rows. Zero writes anywhere in the path: no merges, no channel mutations, no Dispatcharr group-setting toggles (pinned by tests asserting no mutating client method is ever called). The matcher gains an additive `master_patterns` parameter so per-group pattern overrides (`group_patterns`) parse each side with its own group's patterns, and `match_streams` behavior is unchanged for existing callers. Also in this build (PR #611 review riders): the slot-prefix comment in `event_sync_matcher.py` now states the empirically-verified behavior (both sides retaining an unpadded slot prefix hard team-token-conflict — a silent false negative, not graceful degradation), the numeric-identity rail docstring names its false-negative class, and the frozen corpus gains a documented KNOWN RECALL MISS pair ('Lyon'/'Lyons' spelling variants under the 0.90 agree floor). (bead enhancedchannelmanager-ti939.1.4)
- **Event Sync rule kind: schema, write-time validation, migration, and engine guard rails — still preview-only, no execution path (bead ti939.1.3, build 0065).** Auto-creation rules can now carry an `event_sync_config` JSON column (Alembic migration `0031`, one nullable column on `auto_creation_rules`, guarded/idempotent add with a clean downgrade — no new tables per the stateless-recompute decision) that makes the rule the **event_sync kind**: `master_group_id`, `secondary_group_ids[]`, optional shared or per-group parse patterns (title/time/date regexes), `time_window_minutes` (default 30), `attach_threshold` (default 0.80, hard-clamped ≥ 0.80 against the matcher's own `EVENT_ATTACH_FLOOR`), and `enabled`. Write-time validation enforces mandatory scoping (master present, secondaries non-empty, master not in secondaries — the schema-enforced rail from the 1,341-incident trust benchmark), compiles every operator parse regex through `safe_regex` at save time, and rejects unknown keys; every rejection is a teaching error carrying the field, the submitted value, what was expected, and a link to the new `docs/event_sync.md`. Engine wiring is guard-rails-only this phase: event_sync rules are excluded from per-stream Pass 1/Pass 2 evaluation (a run scoped only to event_sync rules reports itself as preview-only instead of silently no-opping) and hard-bypass Pass 4 orphan reconciliation — `managed_channel_ids` is never populated, so the pipeline can never "reconcile" (delete/move) master channels that Dispatcharr owns. A new read-only pre-flight helper (`backend/services/event_sync_preflight.py`) verifies the master group has `auto_channel_sync` ON and every secondary has it OFF, surfacing failures in results — ECM never toggles Dispatcharr group settings. Pre-feature rules load unchanged (column reads back NULL); duplicate-rule, DBAS backup/restore, and YAML export/import all preserve the kind (imported configs route through the same validator; DBAS restore downgrades validation failures to warnings and restores as-is — the raw column keeps the rule excluded from execution); bulk-update refuses the field rather than silently dropping it; `time_window_minutes` is capped at 1440 (24h) so an oversized window cannot re-open the same-teams-different-day false-positive class. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-ti939.1.3)
- **Event Sync groundwork: isolated event-matcher service + frozen regression corpus CI gate (beads ti939.1.1 / ti939.1.2, build 0064).** First slice of the Event Sync epic (one channel per live event across providers). New pure service module `backend/services/event_sync_matcher.py` implements the layered matcher — parse provider event names to (title, start time) reusing the dummy-EPG regex machinery (safe_regex on untrusted strings; both observed date shapes `"11 Jul 06:00 PM ET"` and `"Jan 17 02:45 PM ET"`; year inference across the Dec/Jan boundary; a name with no parsed time is unmatchable, never guessed) → ±30-minute time-window candidate blocking → RapidFuzz token-set scoring of PARSED titles with LOCALS cleaning → order-insensitive team-token check where a clear team conflict (including women's/U21/reserves qualifier mismatches) is a HARD reject at score 0.0 → a structurally separate event admission policy (`EVENT_ATTACH_FLOOR` 0.80, per-rule adjustable, hard-clamped ≥ 0.80; a stricter 0.90 bar when no team tokens corroborate). Master channels are identified by name only — channel IDs are never cached across runs. Guarding it in CI: a frozen, add-only 49-pair corpus (`backend/tests/fixtures/event_sync/matcher_corpus.jsonl`, seeded from live provider event-group names plus engineered traps for the 1,341-false-positive incident shapes) with a pytest gate (`tests/test_event_sync_matcher_corpus.py`) enforcing per-label band correctness and attach-band precision ≥ 97% / recall ≥ 85%. No user-facing surface yet — the preview endpoint, rule schema, and UI land in subsequent Phase 1A beads. (beads enhancedchannelmanager-ti939.1.1 / enhancedchannelmanager-ti939.1.2)
- **`sort_group` channel-pipeline action: renumber a group's channels alphabetically (asc/desc, natural sort, strip-numbers / ignore-country options matching manual Sort & Renumber) as a once-per-group post-run pass; manual Sort & Renumber modal gains a descending option (beads vy4fl, hf8t9, build 0063).**
- **~40 new MCP tools close the MCP-vs-UI coverage gap — normalization CRUD, logo management, tag engine, cloud/sync targets, dummy-EPG profile CRUD, probe lifecycle + circuit breaker, and per-channel CSV export, plus parameter-drift fixes across 8 existing tool families (beads yd6qh / e5n8j / twadj / rv5w1 / omxy5 / dswrl / jcj0f / l3jf7, build 0062).** The MCP coverage audit found whole feature domains an AI client could not reach. New tool modules: **normalization** rule/group CRUD + import/export (e5n8j), **logos** (`tools/logos.py` — list/upload-from-URL/set/clear/cleanup, twadj), **tags** (`tools/tags.py` — tag-engine rule CRUD + test, dswrl), **cloud + sync targets** (`tools/sync_targets.py` — cloud-target CRUD/test-connection and cross-instance sync-target CRUD/run, jcj0f), **dummy-EPG profile CRUD** (omxy5), **probe lifecycle** (pause/resume/cancel + circuit-breaker status/reset, rv5w1), and **per-channel CSV export** (`tools/channels_csv.py`, with a new `ecm_client.get_text` text-response path, l3jf7). Separately, an 8-family parameter-drift sweep (yd6qh) added backend params that existing tools silently lacked (EPG source options, M3U account fields, channel/stream list filters, task-schedule fields, and more). (beads yd6qh / e5n8j / twadj / rv5w1 / omxy5 / dswrl / jcj0f / l3jf7)
- **UI gap closures: one-click restore buttons for saved backups (with a shared type-to-confirm dialog), an Alert Methods settings panel, and a 10-item small-UI sweep (beads rzhid / p4qt8 / hq3de, build 0062).** Settings → Backup & Restore gains restore buttons on saved backup artifacts, backed by a new reusable `TypeToConfirmDialog` and a `DbasRestoreSavedModal` for saved DBAS artifacts (rzhid). A new **Alert Methods** settings section manages notification delivery methods with per-method test buttons (p4qt8). The 10-item sweep (hq3de) adds previously API-only surfaces to the UI, including a Server Groups modal, a Stream Profiles list modal, and a per-channel Stats drill-down modal (`ChannelStatsDetailModal`), among other small additions. (beads rzhid / p4qt8 / hq3de)
- **Find Duplicates can now scan just your selected channels instead of the whole lineup (bead uahp6, build 0062).** The Channels pane's Find Duplicates action now scopes the scan to the checkbox-selected channels when a selection exists (backend `channel_ids` on the find-duplicates endpoint, wired through the UI and the MCP `find_duplicate_channels` tool). The modal header states the scope it scanned. (bead uahp6) and their failures surfaced at run time (beads enhancedchannelmanager-2uwi3 / 3gigl / yom3k / ock7c, build 0061).** A rule's `set_channel_name`-style transform with a `$N` backreference pointing at a capture group the pattern doesn't have (e.g. replacement `$3` against a 2-group pattern) previously saved fine and then failed silently at execution time — the transform just didn't apply, with nothing in the execution log to explain why. Three layers close the gap. **(1) Save-time validation:** creating or updating a rule now validates every `$N` replacement backreference against the pattern's actual group count and rejects the rule with a `422` and an actionable message naming the offending reference and the available groups; `$0` and leading-zero references (e.g. `$01`), which Python's `re` silently reinterprets as octal escapes, are rejected with the same clarity. **(2) Execution-time surfacing:** when a transform still fails at run time, the failure now appears in the execution log's action results, in the Rule Builder's Test output, and as a journal entry — instead of vanishing. **(3) Edit-time preview parity:** the Rule Builder's live name-transform preview now flags an out-of-range `$N` inline instead of rendering it as literal text that looked intentional. (beads enhancedchannelmanager-2uwi3 / 3gigl / yom3k / ock7c)
- **`stream_group_is` channel-pipeline condition: match a stream's provider group (M3U group_title) exactly, with a provider-group dropdown in the Rule Builder (bead enhancedchannelmanager-rgw9p, build 0060).** Rules can now match on the stream's provider group as a first-class condition — exact match, case-insensitive by default with a `case_sensitive` opt-in — instead of regexing the group name. The Rule Builder's condition editor offers "Stream Group is" with a dropdown of the provider groups already loaded, and the Rule Analyzer classifies the condition as a guard. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-rgw9p)
- **Refresh-token rotation grace window — two browser tabs racing the same token refresh no longer log the loser out (bead enhancedchannelmanager-x67qe, build 0059).** `/api/auth/refresh` rotates the refresh token on every use; previously, when two tabs (or a tab and a background poll) raced the same rotation, the second request presented the just-rotated-away token and was hard-rejected, killing the whole session. The backend now remembers the hash of the immediately-prior refresh token and when it was rotated away, and accepts the predecessor within a short grace window with idempotent-refresh semantics. Only ONE prior generation is kept — a normal rotation overwrites both grace fields, so a graced token can never chain to an older one. The frontend additionally serializes concurrent refresh attempts within one browser via `navigator.locks` in `httpClient.ts`, so the race becomes rare to begin with. **Includes a DB schema migration** (`0030_user_sessions_rotation_grace`): two new nullable columns on `user_sessions` (`prior_refresh_token_hash`, `rotated_at`) plus an index, applied automatically on upgrade. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-x67qe)
- **Auth responses now report the access token's lifetime, and the frontend refreshes proactively instead of waiting for a 401 (bead enhancedchannelmanager-3ymo4, build 0059).** Login, `/auth/me`, and `/auth/refresh` responses gain a read-only **`access_token_expires_in`** metadata field (seconds; additive — existing consumers are unaffected). `useAuth` schedules a proactive refresh at 80% of the reported lifetime (with a 30s floor so a tiny/zero lifetime can't hot-loop), so long-lived sessions renew in the background instead of bouncing a real request off a 401 first. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-3ymo4)
- **Accessibility sweep — icon-only buttons are now screen-reader labeled and group/accordion rows are keyboard-operable (beads enhancedchannelmanager-mtklp / 6n14l, build 0059).** 274 icon-only buttons across the app (row actions, modal close buttons, toolbar toggles, chevrons, etc.) now carry `aria-label`s, and a new AST-based regression test (`frontend/src/a11y/iconOnlyButtons.a11y.test.ts`) scans the component sources in CI and fails if a new icon-only button ships without an accessible name — so the sweep can't silently regress. Separately, expandable group/accordion header rows across 9 components are now real keyboard targets: focusable, Enter/Space to toggle, with `aria-expanded` state. (beads enhancedchannelmanager-mtklp / 6n14l)
- **Large groups now render incrementally with a "Show more" control instead of mounting every row at once (bead enhancedchannelmanager-bed9r, build 0059).** A new shared `ShowMoreRows` component caps the initially-rendered rows of very large lists/groups and appends the rest in increments on demand, keeping expansion of huge M3U groups responsive instead of janking the tab while thousands of rows mount. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-bed9r)
- **The Channel Pipeline's per-rule manual-channel protection is now visible and controllable everywhere — MCP, the Rule Builder UI, and blocked merges are now explained in the execution log and journal (beads enhancedchannelmanager-zrte6 / lu3fk / wy6l5, build 0058).** By default, hand-built (manual) channels are protected from Channel Pipeline merges: when a rule's merge target matches a manual channel, the match is treated as "not found" and the rule may create a new auto channel instead. That protection existed in the backend (`allow_manual_channel_merge`, PR #547) but was previously invisible and hard to reach. Three surfaces close the gap: **(1) MCP (zrte6):** `create_channel_pipeline_rule` and `update_channel_pipeline_rule` (and their deprecated `*_auto_creation_*` aliases) now accept a documented `allow_manual_channel_merge` parameter, so agents can opt a rule into merging into manual channels. **(2) UI (lu3fk):** the Rule Builder gains a "Manual channel protection" section with an "Allow merging into manual channels" checkbox (default off) and hint text explaining both modes. **(3) Blocked-merge visibility (wy6l5):** when the protection blocks a merge, the outcome is no longer an INFO-log-only mystery that made rules look like they were "skipping everything" or creating inexplicable duplicates — the execution log's action results now say which manual channel matched, why the merge was withheld, and what happened instead (new auto channel created, or stream skipped), and the journal records a `manual_channel_merge_blocked` entry (deduped to one per blocked channel per run). Merge/skip behavior itself is unchanged — this is control-surface and visibility work only. (beads enhancedchannelmanager-zrte6 / lu3fk / wy6l5)
- **The Stats tab's Active Channels cards now show what's Currently Showing from the EPG (bead enhancedchannelmanager-hoj54, build 0057).** Each active-channel card gains a "Now Showing" row with the program airing right now — title, subtitle when present, and the air-time window (e.g. `Now Showing · SportsCenter · 8:00 PM – 9:00 PM`), with the program description on hover. Programs come from the EPG grid the Guide tab already uses, matched the same way the Guide matches them: the channel's `epg_data_id → EPG tvg_id` hop first (so it works even when the channel's own `tvg_id` differs from the guide's), falling back to the channel's `tvg_id`, and finally to the channel UUID — which is how Dummy EPG programs are keyed, so dummy-EPG channels show their generated program too. Guide data refreshes on a slow 30-minute cadence (which program is "now" is recomputed locally every minute, including in Manual refresh mode), and if EPG data is unavailable the row simply doesn't render. Display-only — no backend or API changes; the shared program-matching helpers were extracted to `utils/epgProgram.ts` and the Guide tab now uses the same code. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-hoj54)
- **The Channel Manager rows are now a two-line layout with resolution capability pills, and the stream URL copies on click (bead enhancedchannelmanager-llosp, build 0055).** Building on the TVG subtitle from builds 0053/0054, each Channel Manager row is restructured into two lines with dedicated left-aligned columns. **Line 1:** channel number (its own fixed column, sized to the largest real channel number) · channel name · the stream URL. **Line 2:** the `EPG · TVG-ID · TVG-name` subtitle (full width, truncating) on the left, with **resolution capability pills** right-aligned — one pill per distinct resolution tier among the channel's successfully-probed streams, highest-first (`4K` purple / `FHD` blue / `HD` green / `SD` gray), derived client-side from the stream-stats the page already loads. The name (line 1) and EPG subtitle (line 2) now share the same left edge. The **stream-count indicator** is a compact `[sources-icon] N` (the neutral `lan` icon for healthy channels, or the existing status icon for no-streams/failed/black-screen/low-fps), with the full "N stream(s)" text moved to the hover tooltip. **Clicking the stream URL copies it** (with the existing copy-feedback toast; the "Copy URL" menu item remains). Also tightened the left-cluster and count-column spacing, and scoped a leaked global `.channel-item` CSS rule (a class-name collision with the Channel Profiles modal) so the fix applies to Channel Manager rows only. Display-only — no backend or API changes. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-llosp)
- **The Channel Manager TVG subtitle now leads with the EPG source name the TVG ID/name tie to (bead enhancedchannelmanager-1nf7u, build 0054).** The per-row subtitle introduced in build 0053 now reads `EPG source · TVG-ID · TVG name` — e.g. `epg.guru UK IPTV · BBCTwo(BBC2).uk · BBC Two` — so an operator can see at a glance not just the channel's TVG identity but which EPG source provides it. The source name appears only when the channel has a linked EPG record whose source resolves; channels showing only their own `tvg_id` (no EPG link) are unchanged. The hover tooltip gains a matching labeled segment (`EPG: … · TVG ID: … · TVG Name: …`). Display-only, resolved client-side from the EPG source list the page already loads — no backend or API changes. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-1nf7u)
- **Channel Manager rows now show the applied TVG ID and TVG name under the channel name (bead enhancedchannelmanager-ciply, build 0053).** Each channel row in the Channel Manager list gains a small muted subtitle beneath the name showing the channel's effective TVG identity — the channel's own `tvg_id` (falling back to the linked EPG record's `tvg_id`) plus the linked EPG record's name, e.g. `BBCTwo(BBC2).uk · BBC Two` — so an operator can see EPG linkage at a glance without opening the channel editor. Rows with no TVG data are unchanged, the subtitle hides during inline name editing, and hovering it shows a labeled tooltip (`TVG ID: … · TVG Name: …`). Display-only, resolved client-side from data the list already loads — no backend or API changes. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-ciply)
- **Exit-path diagnostics instrument GH #546's silent backend crash under concurrent JWT-cookie auth (bead enhancedchannelmanager-0gt2i, GH #546, build 0052).** A well-instrumented user report described the backend process exiting cleanly (ExitCode 0, no signal, no traceback, `PYTHONFAULTHANDLER` silent) under concurrent requests authenticated via the `access_token` cookie — single requests never crash, and the same requests over the MCP static-key path (which bypasses JWT decode) never crash either, pointing at shared state on the JWT-cookie path. The root cause is **not yet pinned**; this build adds instrumentation to make the next occurrence self-diagnosing rather than claiming a fix for the crash itself. New `backend/exit_diagnostics.py` installs an `atexit` hook, `sys.excepthook`, `threading.excepthook`, and an asyncio loop exception handler — all logging a loud `[EXIT-DIAG]` line (chaining to the prior handler, so existing behavior is unchanged) that lets an operator distinguish, from `docker logs` alone, a clean `SystemExit`-class exit (atexit line, no exception above it) from a real unhandled exception (exception logged with traceback) from a hard `os._exit()`/`SIGKILL`/OOM (total silence — no atexit line at all). See the module docstring for the full decision table. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-0gt2i)
- **New admin-only global setting to allow auto-syncing a shared M3U group from more than one provider (bead enhancedchannelmanager-dgs64, GH #591, build 0050).** Dispatcharr channel groups are global entities — a group with the same name on two M3U providers shares one underlying `channel_group` ID — so ECM's `M3UGroupsModal` locks a group's Auto-Sync toggle/Start #/Settings to whichever account enabled it first, to prevent two providers from silently double-creating channels for the same group. A new **Allow multi-provider auto-sync on shared groups** checkbox (Settings → Appearance → Display Options, admin-only) lets an operator opt out of that lock and auto-sync the same group from multiple providers, as Dispatcharr itself allows — an informational shared-ownership icon still marks the overlap in the Manage Groups modal. **Default is off**, preserving today's single-owner lock and its duplicate-channel protection; enabling it is a deliberate admin trade-off, since two providers auto-syncing the same group **can create duplicate channels** if both stream the same content. Changing the field is admin-gated the same way as the existing GH #473 safety caps — a non-admin (including the MCP service principal) attempting to change it is rejected `403`. A value change now also logs and journals a before/after entry (new `settings` journal category) for auditability. See [`docs/runbooks/duplicate-channels-unicode-suffix.md`](docs/runbooks/duplicate-channels-unicode-suffix.md) for a diagnostic pointer distinguishing this overlap from a Unicode-suffix normalization gap, including the rollback caveat that disabling the setting re-engages the lock but does not clean up channels already duplicated. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-dgs64)

- **Channel Pipeline canonical API route and MCP tool names (bead enhancedchannelmanager-dl0kk, build 0046).** Phase 3 of a multi-phase rename (epic enhancedchannelmanager-3udrl) that is preparing the "Auto-Creation" feature for a full rebrand to "Channel Pipeline." The backend now exposes a canonical `/api/channel-pipeline` route (dual-mounted alongside the still-functional `/api/auto-creation`), and the MCP server registers 14 new canonical tool names (`run_channel_pipeline`, `list_channel_pipeline_rules`, `create_channel_pipeline_rule`, and 11 others) as the primary implementation. New integrations should target the new route and tool names. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-dl0kk)

- **Auto-Creation Rule Builder gains a "Does Not Match (Regex)" operator for exclusion filters (bead vmmgy, build 0041).** The backend condition schema already supported inverting any condition (a generic `negate` flag), including regex-matching conditions — but the Rule Builder UI only exposed "Matches (Regex)" with no negated counterpart, and the `create_auto_creation_rule`/`update_auto_creation_rule` MCP tools didn't document `negate` at all. Now Stream Name, Stream Group, TVG-ID, and Channel Name each offer "Does Not Match (Regex)" alongside the existing operators, for excluding streams/channels matching a pattern; reloading a rule with a hand-crafted negated regex condition (e.g. via MCP) now correctly displays it as negated instead of silently showing it as a positive match. MCP tool docs now document `negate` as an available condition-dict key, with examples. (bead vmmgy)
- **M3U account Smart Sort priority is now readable and settable via MCP (bead 0u80f, build 0040).** `list_m3u_accounts` and `get_m3u_account` now surface each account's current Smart Sort priority (the ECM-local `m3u_account_priorities` setting consumed by the "m3u_priority" sort criterion), and a new `set_m3u_account_priority(account_id, priority)` MCP tool lets an agent set (1-100) or clear (0) it — previously only editable from Settings → M3U Manager. (bead 0u80f)
- **Settings → Stream Health now has a "Stale Streams" scan, surfacing streams whose health data has gone quiet or whose provider has stopped seeing them (bead n4g7g, build 0039).** Two independent signals are unioned into one list: **(1) ECM-side staleness** — a stream's own `StreamStats.last_probed` timestamp is older than a configurable N days (default 7), or the stream has never been probed at all; and **(2) provider-side staleness** — Dispatcharr's own per-stream `is_stale`/`last_seen` flags, meaning the source M3U's last refresh no longer re-matched the stream in the playlist (the provider may have dropped or renamed it upstream). Each stream in the results carries a `reasons` list explaining why it was flagged and its associated channel(s), so an operator can tell "just hasn't been probed lately" apart from "the provider may have removed this feed." A new **Stale Streams** section under Settings lets an operator run the scan, review reason badges per stream, and select streams for a non-destructive **Probe Selected** action (which re-probes without any automatic deletion or channel changes). Also available as the `get_stale_streams` MCP tool for AI-driven workflows. (bead n4g7g)
- **Stats tab: per-channel logos, username attribution, and by-user/by-IP viewer toggles (beads 2sfpt / 84l9m, build 0030).** Four operator-facing improvements to the Stats tab. **(1) Active-channel logos:** each currently-active channel row now shows a small channel logo beside its number (resolved from the channel's `logo_id` via the logos cache, matching the Channels pane; null logos render nothing — no broken-image icon). **(2) Recent Events usernames:** the live event feed now shows *which user* connected/disconnected — the backend enriches each event by batch-joining its client IP against ECM's recorded `UniqueClientConnection.username`; when no username is known it falls back to the IP address as before. **(3) Top Viewers by-user/by-IP toggle** and **(4) Channels by Unique Viewers by-user/by-IP toggle:** a shared "Group by: IP / User" control lets the operator group viewers / unique-viewer counts by username (`COALESCE(username, ip)`, so unresolved viewers still fall back to their IP) instead of only by raw IP. (beads 2sfpt / 84l9m)
- **The auto-creation runaway safety cap is now an adjustable, discoverable setting — Settings → Auto Creation — instead of a settings.json-only knob (bead skg35, build 0027).** When a single auto-creation run hits the per-run channel cap (the GH #473 OOM safety valve, default 500), the run stops early with status "capped" and an operator who needed to raise it had no supported way to: `max_auto_created_channels_per_run` lived only in `/config/settings.json`, wasn't in the settings API or UI, and the capped-run message literally told them to "raise max_auto_created_channels_per_run" — a key they couldn't find. Now both that cap and its sibling memory cap (`max_auto_creation_log_entries`) are surfaced on `GET /api/settings`, editable from a new **Runaway Safety Cap** control on the Auto Creation settings page, and persisted via `POST /api/settings`. Helper text explains the default (500), that **0 disables** the cap, and that auto-creation is **idempotent** — re-running a capped run continues from where it stopped (already-created channels persist), so the cap can be left in place. Changing the cap is **admin-only** (it joins the kgz3k field-level admin partition): a non-admin — including the MCP service principal — that attempts to change it is rejected `403`, since lowering/disabling it re-opens the runaway-creation OOM blast radius. The capped-run messages (execution record, run-summary log entry, and "Capped" notification) now point operators to **Settings → Auto Creation** and surface the idempotent-rerun hint instead of naming the raw settings.json key. (bead skg35)
- **Auto-creation now warns when a rule references DISABLED normalization groups instead of silently normalizing nothing (bead e8p1h, build 0025).** Closes a quiet data-quality footgun: a rule could carry `normalization_group_ids` pointing at normalization groups that are globally disabled (or no longer exist), in which case every `[NORMALIZE]` decision applied nothing — provider prefixes/suffixes (e.g. `USA PREMIUM MOVIES:`) were never stripped, stream names never matched the clean channel names, and `merge_streams target:auto` matched almost nothing — with no warning anywhere. Now, when an auto-creation run starts, the engine detects rules referencing disabled/missing normalization groups, logs a `[AUTO-CREATE-ENGINE]` warning, and records the finding on the run summary. The executions detail view shows a prominent amber banner ("Normalization applied no changes — disabled groups referenced") naming the affected rule(s) and group(s) and telling the operator to enable them under Settings → Normalization; a warning toast also fires on an otherwise-clean run. The **Rule Analyzer** (`analyze_auto_creation_rules`, backend + MCP) flags the same condition as a new `RULE_REFERENCES_DISABLED_NORMALIZATION_GROUP` finding when run live. This is detection-only — it does **not** change what gets merged or normalized. Persisted via new migration `0028` (`auto_creation_executions.warnings`). (bead e8p1h)
- **DBAS backup safety: first-run "where can backups go?" prompt, a Settings → Security home for it, and a "not scheduled yet" setup nudge (beads ikv8z/nngkg, build 0024).** Two operator-facing additions for v0.18.0 DBAS Phase 1. **(1) Backup-schedule nudge (ikv8z):** scheduled backups ship OFF by default, so the Backup & Restore page now shows a one-time amber banner — "Backups are not scheduled yet · Set one up →" — whenever no enabled backup schedule exists; the link jumps straight to the schedule editor. Dismissing it persists, and it never nags once a schedule is enabled. Stops a less-engaged operator from silently keeping zero backups. **(2) Backup-destination choice (nngkg):** a first-run modal asks once where ECM may upload backups — "my home network (recommended)" vs "public internet only" — in plain language; the choice is persisted and freely reversible later in the new **Settings → Security** section. Escaping/dismissing the modal accepts the safe home-network default and tells the operator where to change it. The cloud-target editor now shows a "Certificate check off" warning badge on any target with certificate checking disabled. The actual enforcement (blocking risky internal addresses) is always-on in the backend regardless of this choice; the UI only moves the home-network-vs-public band. Persisted via a new admin-gated `PATCH /api/settings/security` endpoint; the mode is also surfaced on `GET /api/settings` and preserved across normal settings saves. (epic 0i2vt, beads ikv8z / nngkg)
- **Anti-fake-test guardrails — convention + CI gate (epic ulp7q, build 0023).** Internal-only, closes out the test-validity audit. Added a "Test validity / anti-patterns" section to `docs/style_guide.md` (the "would it bite?" bar, banned patterns, the good pattern, and real exemplary tests to copy), plus a stdlib `scripts/check_fake_tests.py` guard wired as a `fake-test-guard` CI job that fails on the most blatant fake-test markers (`assert True` in backend/MCP tests, `expect(true).toBe(true)` in frontend tests) with a documented `# fake-test-ok: <reason>` suppression convention. Stops the class of issue the audit found from recurring. (epic ulp7q, bead .9)

### Changed
- **Event Sync master-side parse logging now explains WHY a master is unusable (bead at41p, build 0088).** When an Event Sync run produces no matches because the master group yields no usable events, DEBUG logs previously showed only a bare master name. The matcher now reports, per unusable master, the exact identity string parsed, the parsed title/start, and a reason (`missing-title` / `missing-start` / `missing-title+start`); the resolver echoes the identity-source flags (`parse_master_from_stream`, `include_master_group_streams`); and the fetch path logs the `master_group → effective_master_group` override remap plus channel/identity counts. Observability only (all DEBUG-guarded) — no change to scoring, banding, or attach behavior. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-at41p)
- **Event Sync rule editor reworked into a 4-step wizard (Scope → Matching → Behavior → Review) with a persistent, step-reactive live-impacts rail (bead m1s38.1, build 0085).** The editor replaces its prior single-scroll layout with four focused steps and a right-hand rail whose impact summary (groups, patterns, matches) updates as each step changes, so an operator sees the consequence of a choice before moving to the next step. The generic two-pane modal layout was extracted into shared modal-layout CSS primitives so future multi-step editors can reuse the same structure. No backend/API changes — editor UX only. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-m1s38.1)
- **Standard (conditions+actions) rule builder reworked into a grouped two-pane, Logic-first layout (bead m1s38.3, build 0085).** Consumes the same shared modal-layout primitives introduced for the Event Sync editor (above), grouping conditions and actions together behind an intent sentence that summarizes the rule in plain language, plus advisory Rule Analyzer chips surfaced inline while editing. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-m1s38.3)
- **Event Sync execution details now show an event_sync-aware summary (bead 7wuhd, build 0085).** The execution detail view for Event Sync runs now reports secondary streams evaluated, attached, already-attached, ambiguous, unmatched, and parse failures, including a distinct "fully in sync" state when there is nothing left to attach. Persisted via new execution columns (migration `0033`). (bead enhancedchannelmanager-7wuhd)
- **Confirmed rollback of an Event Sync attach run is now surgical whenever the journal proves it can be (bead sfysz, build 0070).** `POST /api/channel-pipeline/executions/{id}/rollback` with `confirm=true` previously always delegated to the snapshot full-replace when a pre-run snapshot existed — reverting Dispatcharr's own master-stream churn between run and rollback to stale pre-run stream ids that Dispatcharr never self-heals. Now, when the run's journal (category `event_sync`, `batch_id` = execution id) fully covers its attaches — no created entities, every modification a journaled stream attach, entry counts matching 1:1 — the rollback removes ONLY the stream ids the run added from each master's *current* stream list, preserving post-run churn, and returns `surgical_unmerge: true`. Anything short of proven coverage (standard-rule runs, mixed runs, legacy runs, a failed journal write) falls back to the snapshot restore exactly as before; the confirm gate and the standard-rule rollback path are unchanged. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-sfysz)
- **Every hit on the legacy `/api/admin/*` router now logs a deprecation WARNING, pending removal (bead d53lz, build 0062).** The legacy `/api/admin/*` user-CRUD router fully duplicates the canonical `/api/auth/admin/*` surface. Step 1 of a two-step deprecation: a middleware WARNING-logs each request to the legacy prefix (method, path, client IP, user) so operators and integrations can spot any remaining traffic before the router is removed in a later release. Behavior is otherwise unchanged — the legacy routes still work. (bead d53lz) (beads enhancedchannelmanager-3thyn / b3g0r / 7l7wi / juy2e / 9n08a / b2vf5 / jl7sb, build 0059).** A cluster of small consistency fixes from the UX audit. The tab navigation strip now scrolls horizontally with edge chevrons when tabs overflow the window, instead of clipping — keyboard Tab-focus auto-scrolls the focused tab into view (3thyn). Four tab headers migrated onto a new shared `PageHeader` component so title/subtitle/action rows are structurally identical across tabs (b3g0r / 7l7wi). Timestamps across tabs now use one unified time-format helper, so the same moment renders identically everywhere (juy2e). Status pills use a consistent outline style (9n08a). Two previously-unexplained UI elements gained explanatory tooltips: the edit-mode countdown timer (b2vf5) and the EPG priority tie-break behavior (jl7sb). (beads enhancedchannelmanager-3thyn / b3g0r / 7l7wi / juy2e / 9n08a / b2vf5 / jl7sb)
- **The Notification Center now collapses matching start/complete notification pairs into one entry (bead enhancedchannelmanager-ib2w3, build 0059).** Long-running operations previously left both their "started" and "completed" notifications in the list; a new grouping pass (`notificationGrouping.ts`) collapses each start/complete pair into the completed entry so the list shows outcomes, not lifecycle noise. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-ib2w3)
- **The Auto-Creation tab and all its UI text are now "Channel Pipeline" (bead enhancedchannelmanager-xwwe4, build 0047).** Phase 4 of the multi-phase rename (epic enhancedchannelmanager-3udrl) that started with the phase-3 canonical API route above. The frontend now calls the canonical `/api/channel-pipeline` route (the deprecated `/api/auto-creation` alias still works for any other integration, per phase 3); old bookmarked URLs (`#auto-creation`, `#settings/auto-creation`) still resolve correctly via a compatibility redirect. This is purely a rename — no functional/behavioral change to rules, executions, or settings, and all underlying data and Settings field names are unchanged. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-xwwe4)
- **Documentation for the Auto-Creation → Channel Pipeline rename is now complete (bead enhancedchannelmanager-cuktn, build 0048).** Phase 5 of 5 (FINAL) in the multi-phase rename (epic enhancedchannelmanager-3udrl). The user guide, API reference, README, architecture docs, runbooks, and style guide now consistently refer to "Channel Pipeline"; two historical ADRs (ADR-010, ADR-011) received terminology-note addenda rather than being rewritten. No functional change — this is documentation only. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-cuktn)

### Deprecated
- **`/api/auto-creation` and its 14 corresponding MCP tool names are deprecated in favor of `/api/channel-pipeline` and their `channel_pipeline`-named equivalents (bead enhancedchannelmanager-dl0kk, build 0046).** Part of the same phase-3 rename above. The old route and the 14 old MCP tool names (`run_auto_creation`, `create_auto_creation_rule`, etc.) continue to work unchanged — no immediate action needed from operators or existing scripts/agents — but they are hidden from the OpenAPI schema and will be removed in a future dated release once the phase-4 frontend UI rename ships and a follow-up removal bead is filed. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-dl0kk)

### Fixed
- **The standard rule builder no longer silently discards unsaved edits (bead m1s38.3, build 0085).** The header's ×/Escape close previously discarded a dirty rule with no warning, and the dirty-check itself missed deep edits to existing conditions/actions and to option/sort/scope fields, so some edits could be lost even when the discard guard did fire. Both are fixed: closing a dirty rule now prompts for confirmation, and the dirty-check covers every field the builder can edit. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-m1s38.3)
- **Event Sync manual runs no longer show a misleading "Created 0 channels" completion toast (bead utswf, build 0085).** Event Sync runs don't create channels — they attach streams to an existing master channel — so the standard-rule completion toast always read "0" and looked like the run had done nothing even when it attached streams successfully. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-utswf)
- **Event Sync execution details no longer show standard-rule counters that always read 0 (bead 7wuhd, build 0085).** The execution detail view previously reused standard-rule action counters, which have no meaning for Event Sync's attach-based execution and always displayed 0 regardless of outcome — replaced by the event_sync-aware summary described above. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-7wuhd)
- **The Event Sync Test Patterns panel no longer shows 'Parsed' for names the matcher would reject (bead hirm6, build 0070).** The panel's verdict was group-presence only, so `A vs B @ 45 Jul 06:00 PM ET` (day 45) or a custom pattern capturing a garbage month rendered as 'Parsed' while being unmatchable at preview/run time. The dummy-EPG batch preview response (still zero-write) now carries a matcher-level `event_sync_start_valid` flag computed from the matcher's own never-guess semantics (`services/event_sync_matcher.groups_would_build_start` — valid month name, hour ≤ 23, real calendar date), and the panel renders 'Parsed' only from that flag, with a distinct **Invalid date/time** state for captured-but-impossible dates alongside the existing **Incomplete date/time** state. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-hirm6)
- **The Event Sync rule editor no longer silently rewrites API-authored multi-pattern configs on resave (bead z4y4a, build 0070).** Opening and saving a rule whose `event_sync_config` was authored by hand or over the API used to keep only the first non-shipped shared pattern, keep only `patterns[0]` of each per-group override list, and re-add the built-in patterns to an all-custom selection. The editor now round-trips the full `patterns`/`group_patterns` arrays: an untouched save emits the saved arrays verbatim; pattern entries the UI cannot express (a second+ custom shared pattern, a group's second+ override pattern) are preserved unchanged behind the editable ones — surfaced with a read-only indicator, applied in the Test Patterns panel and preview, and never dropped or reordered; edited entries keep their API-authored names; and built-ins (recognized by verbatim equality, not name alone) are never silently re-added.
