Fix: `GET /api/tasks` and `GET /api/tasks/{id}` now source "Next Run" from the earliest enabled `task_schedules.next_run_at` (the real firing source) instead of the stale in-memory value, falling back to the in-memory value only for tasks with no schedule rows; and `_save_task_to_db` no longer overwrites `next_run_at` for tasks that have `task_schedules` rows (those are DB-managed). (GH #468, bd-a80u2, build 0003)
- **Bulk channel creation re-paginated the entire Dispatcharr logo catalog once *per channel*, making large batches O(channels × catalog-pages) and exhausting the request budget (bd-raehx, build 0002).** Every `createChannel` op carrying a `logoUrl` but no `logoId` called `find_logo_by_url()`, which paginates the whole logo catalog (500/page) on every call with no caching. Two real debug bundles confirm the cost: a 441-op SiriusXM batch issued **2,391** logo GETs (~5-page catalog, finished server-side in ~6 min after a 504), and a 113-op batch against a larger ~25-page catalog issued **859** logo GETs and reached only ~channel 22 before the 30s `504`. This is why batches "created channels but didn't assign streams past ~15", why an op reported *failed* while the write still happened (the handler keeps running after the client's 504), and why adding streams manually afterward worked. Fix: `_run_bulk_commit` now builds a per-run `{url → logo}` index **once**, lazily (only the first time an op actually needs a logo lookup, so `validateOnly` and logo-free batches pay nothing), and every subsequent op reuses it; logos created mid-batch are inserted into the index so a later op sharing the same `logoUrl` reuses them instead of creating a duplicate (also fixes a latent duplicate-logo bug). Catalog fetches drop from ~859/2,391 to ~25/5 — one pagination per batch. The per-channel `find_logo_by_url` first-match semantics and the "logo failure still creates the channel without a logo" fallthrough are preserved. (bd-raehx, build 0002)
- **Bulk-commit partial outcomes are now flagged explicitly so the client can distinguish "some applied, some failed" from a total failure (bd-5xciq, build 0002).** The `BulkCommitResponse` envelope gains a `partial` boolean (`operationsApplied > 0 AND operationsFailed > 0`), present on every return path. This makes a partially-applied batch reconcilable via `tempIdMap` instead of reading as a flat failure that prompts a duplicate-creating retry. The frontend `BulkCommitResponse` type carries the field; existing `useEditMode` rendering already derives partial state from the applied/failed counts, so behavior is unchanged and the flag is exposed for future use. (bd-5xciq, build 0002)
- **`POST /api/channels/bulk-commit` returned 504 mid-flight on large batches while the handler kept running, producing duplicates on retry (bd-ggxks, build 0001).** The endpoint was synchronous and pinned behind the 30s `ECM_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` middleware. A 441-op SiriusXM batch (~6 min of sequential Dispatcharr POSTs) caused the middleware to fire `504 Gateway Timeout` to the operator while the handler kept running in the background; the operator retried, each retry committed another ~30 partial channels, and Dispatcharr accumulated duplicates. Fix: bulk-commit now follows the bd-cns7j / bd-enfsy 202+poll pattern. Non-`validateOnly` `POST /api/channels/bulk-commit` returns `202 + {job_id, status: "running"}` immediately and dispatches a supervised background task; clients poll `GET /api/channels/bulk-commit/{job_id}` until the status is terminal (`completed` with the full `BulkCommitResponse` under `result`, or `failed` with an `error` message). `validateOnly` stays synchronous (200 with the response body inline) because pre-commit validation is fast and the frontend uses it for instant feedback. Frontend `bulkCommit()` and the MCP `bulk_commit_channels` / `build_channel_lineup` tools drive the new POST→poll loop transparently — their public shapes are unchanged so all three `useEditMode` call sites and existing MCP consumers keep working. Job state is in-memory with a 30-min TTL prune on every new POST; completed jobs are evicted on first read so RAM stays bounded; failed jobs persist until the TTL so operators can re-poll the error message. (bd-ggxks, build 0001)

## [0.17.3] — 2026-05-25

### Removed
- **Retired MCP OAuth 2.1 feature fully removed from the codebase (bead jir0m, bd-9axgc).** The MCP OAuth 2.1 "Custom Connector" offering — already disabled by PO decision in bd-9axgc because it required public-internet exposure the operator did not want — is now deleted, not just dormant. Removed: the backend OAuth Authorization Server (`routers/oauth_mcp.py`, `routers/oauth_discovery.py`, `auth/oauth_provider.py`, `auth/oauth_store.py`, `auth/oauth_clients.py`, `auth/oauth_rate_limit.py`, `auth/oauth_discovery.py`), the MCP-server OAuth bits (`oauth_discovery.py` and the `verify_oauth_token` HS256 verifier in `oauth_rs.py`), the OAuth config fields/helpers (`mcp_oauth_signing_secret`, `oauth_allow_insecure`, `get_or_create_oauth_signing_secret`, and the MCP-server `get_signing_key*` / `get_oauth_allow_insecure` / `OAUTH_ISSUER` helpers), the frontend consent UI (`OAuthConsentPage`) and OAuth grant/consent API functions, and ~60 OAuth-only tests. **The supported MCP authentication method is unchanged: the static `?api_key=` path.** The security-critical CD1 no-fail-cascade guard is preserved — `looks_like_jwt` still rejects any JWT-shaped Bearer with 401 so it can never fall through to the static-key compare. ADR-009 and `docs/security/threat_model_mcp_oauth.md` are retained (marked Superseded) for decision history. ([bead jir0m], bd-9axgc)

### Added
- **Auto-creation pre-run snapshot + full revert (epic uc51o, ADR-010, builds 0018–0022).** Before each non-dry auto-creation run ECM now captures a point-in-time snapshot of every non-Dispatcharr-auto-created channel and its stream-ID assignments. The snapshot closes the revert gap that existed in the previous rollback: the old facility only undid *created* entities and surgically un-merged run-added streams; it could not restore streams removed from existing channels nor the general pre-run channel↔stream state. **Schema + capture (uc51o.2):** new `AutoCreationSnapshot` table (Alembic 0022), 1:1 FK to `AutoCreationExecution`; stores stream IDs only — never stream URLs — so Xtream-Codes credentials are never persisted in the snapshot. Capture runs in `run_pipeline` after execution-record creation and before any mutations; failure logs a WARNING and the run proceeds without a snapshot. **Restore algorithm + endpoint (uc51o.4):** `POST /api/auto-creation/executions/{id}/restore-snapshot?confirm=true` (admin-gated, `confirm` is the §D5 optimistic-overwrite acknowledgement) deletes run-created channels then full-replaces each snapshotted channel's stream set via `update_channel(streams=[ids])`; partial failures are collected and surfaced — never silent, never fatal mid-run; the endpoint is idempotent (terminal-state guard). **Retention (uc51o.3):** the `CleanupTask` prunes `auto_creation_snapshots` before VACUUM by age window (default 30 days) and count cap (default 50), whichever fires first; `ecm_auto_creation_snapshot_count` / `_bytes` observability gauges published post-prune. **Unified rollback surface (uc51o.5):** `POST /rollback` is now the single revert entry point — it probes for a snapshot and delegates to the full restore when one exists (requiring `confirm=true`), else falls back to the legacy delete-created-only path byte-compatible with pre-uc51o behaviour; `GET /executions` derives a `has_snapshot` boolean per execution in one batched `IN` query (no N+1). **MCP (uc51o.6):** new `restore_auto_creation_snapshot(execution_id, confirm=False)` tool; `confirm=False` (default) returns the optimistic-overwrite warning with channel count and snapshot timestamp before any write; `list_auto_creation_executions` now surfaces a `[snapshot]` / `[no snapshot]` marker per run. **Revert UI (uc51o.7):** "Undo this run" button in Auto-Creation execution history, gated on `has_snapshot`; shows a mandatory overwrite-warning confirm dialog with snapshot timestamp; post-revert result renders `removed_channels` / `restored_channels` / `failed_channels` — partial failures are never presented as plain success. (bd-uc51o, ADR-010, builds 0018–0022)
- **Print Channel Guide: per-group editable channel range, defaulting to each group's first/last used channel number (bd-9eyqp, build 0026).** The previous global From/To range (bd-9q9z0) and a short-lived intermediate redesign (build 0025) are replaced by per-group range inputs rendered inline in each group row. Each group's From/To defaults to that group's minimum and maximum *used* channel number (computed client-side from already-loaded data, no backend change); operators can edit each group's range independently within the modal session. The empty-slot placeholder fill (bd-w532y, build 0025 — em-dash "—" rows for sparse auto-sync ranges, toggle-on by default, 500-slot cap per group) uses each group's own range. The print button is disabled only when *no* selected group has a valid printable range; the global clamp, rangeError hint, and rangeFilteredGroups state are removed. (bd-9eyqp, bd-w532y, builds 0025–0026)
- **MCP: `set_normalization_group_enabled` tool to toggle normalization rule groups (bd-svixy, build 0024).** New MCP tool wraps the existing `PATCH /api/normalization/groups/{group_id}` endpoint to enable or disable a `NormalizationRuleGroup` by id; group ids are discoverable via the existing `list_normalization_rules` output. (bd-svixy, build 0024)
- **`custom_streams` is now a first-class Smart Sort criterion (bead ap1ud, GH #244).** Operators enable and drag operator-added custom streams in the Smart Sort priority order — exactly like `resolution` or `m3u_priority` — instead of tuning a separate M3U-row priority control. It is a binary criterion: a stream scores 1 if it is a custom stream (Dispatcharr `is_custom == true`, i.e. it belongs to the real "custom" M3U account), else 0 (negated for descending so custom streams sort first); ties among custom streams fall through to the next enabled criterion. **Default: present but disabled** (like `m3u_priority`); existing installs auto-merge it (disabled) via the frontend `mergeSortCriteria`/`ALL_SORT_CRITERIA` mechanism, so no operator action is required and saved priority orders are preserved. **Backend:** `smart_sort_streams` (and the auto-creation engine's `_smart_sort_streams`) take an optional `custom_stream_ids: set[int]` threaded through every sort surface and internal caller; each call site builds the set from the stream data it already fetches (`is_custom`), only when the criterion is active — mirroring how `m3u_priority` gates its M3U-account fetch. When `custom_stream_ids` is not supplied the criterion is inert (scores 0 everywhere), so callers degrade gracefully. **Frontend:** the criterion appears in Settings → Smart Sort Priority as a draggable, toggleable row, in the channel/group/toolbar sort dropdowns, and in the `SortMode`/`SortCriterion` types. **This supersedes the bd-4g2t1 "Custom streams" M3U-row priority control** (the row UX the PO disliked); that work never reached a release cut, so the row, its CSS, and its tests were removed in favour of this criterion. **Tests:** 5 backend criterion tests in `test_sort_criteria.py` (prober) + 5 in `test_auto_creation_engine.py` (engine) covering ranks-first-when-top, no-effect-when-disabled, pure-M3U-unaffected, inert-without-ids, and tie-fallthrough; 5 frontend tests in `SmartSortCustomStreamsCriterion.test.tsx` covering render/default-disabled/saved-enabled/toggle/auto-merge. ([bead ap1ud], GH #244)
- **Fuzzy stream→channel matching for OTA/callsign "Local" channels, built on ONE shared backend core + ONE shared name-cleaner (bead jnzst, v0.17.3-0006).** OTA locals never auto-matched because the global merge path is intentionally strict normalized-name *exact* equality (the strictness that ended a prior ~1,341 false-positive merge incident), while providers name locals inconsistently (`CITY:`/`US|`/`WI |` prefixes, `RAW`/superscript quality tags, `GREENBAY` vs `Green Bay`, channel-number prefixes). This adds a scored fuzzy path that does NOT touch the exact rule. **Core (`backend/services/dedup_matcher.py`):** the existing rapidfuzz `token_set_ratio` scorer is extended into `score_one`/`score_all` returning a `ScoredMatch` (score + `callsign_verdict` + `tvg_id_override` + `signal`), and the single cleaner `_normalize(value, mode)` gains a `LOCALS` mode (dotted/`N.N |` number-prefix strip, source/market-prefix strip, `convert_superscripts` reuse, quality-tag strip, run-together token split) while `CONSERVATIVE` (the default every existing dedup/exact caller uses) stays byte-for-byte unchanged. **Rule path (Component A):** `loose_name_match` rules can now opt into scored fuzzy via a required `min_score` (0–1, floored at `CONFIDENCE_FLOOR=0.60`) plus optional `tie_break`/`max_candidates`, scoped to the Local channel groups via the existing `target_channel_in_group` allowlist; per-merge scores + provenance are written to the journal and surfaced in the run summary. **Preview (Component B):** new no-write `GET /api/auto-creation/fuzzy-preview` (admin-gated, paginated, group-scoped, N×M-capped) returns scored `(stream_id, channel_id, score, callsign_verdict)` triples before any write. **MCP (Component C):** `match_streams_to_channels` and `fuzzy_match_stream` are repointed onto the backend core and the MCP-local scorer (`_score_match`/`_generate_variants`/`_normalize_channel`) is deleted; a new `preview_fuzzy_matches(group_ids, min_score)` tool wraps the preview endpoint; `create_auto_creation_rule`/`update_auto_creation_rule` docstrings document the fuzzy `loose_name_match` + `min_score` + `target_channel_in_group` path. **Tests:** 113 new tests incl. the tombstone property (all 4 WBAY variants match the WBAY channel and the WGBA control is excluded at every `min_score` in [0.60, 1.0]); backend suite 5187 passed/69 skipped, mcp-server suite 396 passed/37 skipped. ([bead jnzst], v0.17.3-0006)

### Changed
- **MCP `match_streams_to_channels` now defaults to dry-run (bead jnzst).** It returns ranked candidates + confidence scores by default and writes only with explicit `apply=true`; it accepts a *list* of `group_ids` and gains an opt-in flag to also backfill channels that already have streams (default off = prior behavior). `match_by` remains a deliberate runtime no-op; the real fuzzy lever is `loose_name_match` + `min_score`. ([bead jnzst])
- Internal: resolved CodeQL code-quality findings (unused imports, import-cycles) surfaced during the 0.17.3 release cut (bd-0nabr); test-suite hardening from validity audit — strengthen error-swallow tests, CI anti-silent-skip guard for symlink-escape security tests, test-layer documentation, de-dup health probes (bd-1qnvg, build 0007); e2e test-isolation flake fixed twice (orphaned `create_task` coroutines bd-gqtyf build 0015; auth-fixture 429 retry with `Retry-After` honouring bd-4sned build 0024); worktree-bootstrap EACCES hardening + CLAUDE.md agent-isolation defaults encoded (builds 0016–0017); Stats hot-query unused MAX column drop (bd-ly5sa, build 0016); SLO-6 runbook + user-guide alert rename `rate`→`ratio` (bd-06jyv, build 0023).

### Fixed
- **Stats tab footer hover-scroll pushed the whole UI off-page (bd-tkcmu, build 0012; GH bug).** Hovering the bottom status bar on the Stats tab and scrolling propagated the event to the document root, dragging the entire UI viewport. Fix: `overflow:hidden` added to `html`/`body`/`#root` in `index.css` (an SPA never needs document-level scroll) plus `overscroll-behavior:contain` on `.stats-tab` and `.stats-content` as a belt-and-suspenders scroll-chain guard. (bd-tkcmu, build 0012)
- **`bulk_merge_channels` stale-ID hardening: target-ID 404 now returns 422 with a refresh hint, `source_names` made complete, non-404 errors re-raised correctly (bd-4xxax, build 0009).** The source-ID 404 guard (bd-ozhkf) was not mirrored on the target ID; a stale target fell through to the per-item catch-all, returning 200 + a failed count instead of the 422 refresh prompt. Fixed. Also: `source_names` is now populated in the 422 branch (one entry per requested source ID) to prevent a misaligned audit record if the path ever becomes partial-success; 410/500 upstream errors on both source and target correctly re-raise rather than being misclassified as stale-ID 422. (bd-4xxax, build 0009)
- **`get_channels(channel_group=0)` silently fetched all channels (bd-d5z9u, build 0009).** The `if channel_group:` guard in `dispatcharr_client.py` is falsy for `0`, so requests scoped to group 0 returned the unfiltered channel list. Fixed to `if channel_group is not None:`. (bd-d5z9u, build 0009)
- **Modal error banner suppressed when backend returns `detail: []` (bd-p3jpl, build 0024).** `extractDetail()` returned an empty string for empty FastAPI validation arrays, which the `{error && …}` render guard silently hid. Now trims the assembled string and falls back to `'Server returned an empty error'` so callers always receive a renderable message. (bd-p3jpl, build 0024)

### Security
- **Operator-only: all channel-configuration write endpoints now require admin (bd-um30y + bd-v7n9f, builds 0013–0014; auto-creation writes bd-757hc, build 0009).** ECM is an operator-only system; Dispatcharr-federated users are created `is_admin=False` by default, so authenticated non-admin principals could previously invoke destructive or bulk channel operations. Three separate gating passes closed this. **Build 0009 (bd-757hc):** 11 mutating `auto_creation.py` endpoints gated with `RequireAdminIfEnabled` (create/update/bulk-update/delete/reorder/toggle/duplicate rule, run pipeline, run rule, execution rollback, import YAML); read-only and validation endpoints stay ungated. **Build 0013 (bd-um30y):** destructive/bulk `channels.py` class gated — `clear-auto-created`, merge, bulk-merge, bulk-commit, assign-numbers, import-CSV. **Build 0014 (bd-v7n9f):** PO decision that ECM is operator-only extended the gate to all remaining channel-config writes — `create_channel`, `update_channel`, `delete_channel`, add-stream, add-streams, remove-stream, reorder-streams, and logo CRUD (create/upload/update/delete logo). Read-only GETs and preview/validation scan endpoints (`preview-csv`, `normalize-preview-batch`, `find-duplicates`) remain ungated. `RequireAdminIfEnabled` is a no-op when auth is disabled, so single-user deployments are unchanged. (bd-757hc, bd-um30y, bd-v7n9f, builds 0009/0013/0014)
- **SSRF denylist on media-server saved-URL test-connection endpoints (bd-fbc50, build 0024).** `_sanitize_base_url` in `routers/settings.py` already blocked non-`http`/`https` schemes but still permitted loopback and link-local hosts, leaving an admin-gated SSRF surface on the Emby/Plex/Jellyfin "Test Connection" endpoints. Two new stdlib-only helpers (`ipaddress` + `socket`, no regex): **`_ip_is_blocked`** denies loopback (`127.0.0.0/8`, `::1`), link-local IPv4 (`169.254.0.0/16`, including the AWS/GCP `169.254.169.254` metadata endpoint), link-local IPv6 (`fe80::/10`), and IPv4-mapped IPv6 unwrapping (`::ffff:127.x.x.x`, `::ffff:169.254.x.x`); RFC 1918 private ranges are intentionally left reachable (Plex/Emby/Jellyfin run on the operator's LAN). **`_host_is_blocked`** applies the IP check to literal IPs, blocks the `localhost` alias by name, and performs best-effort hostname resolution so a hostname resolving to a blocked IP is also caught; resolution failures do not block (offline LAN servers stay testable). Denied hosts return the same `{ok: false, error: <msg>}` envelope as the scheme-block path. (bd-fbc50, build 0024)
- **Reintroduced fuzzy matching is gated so it cannot recreate the ~1,341 false-positive merge incident (bead jnzst).** The admission decision is a single shared chokepoint (`dedup_matcher.is_admissible`) that the rule executor, the preview endpoint, and — transitively — both MCP write tools all funnel through, so no consumer can apply a score-only policy and re-admit an incident-class false positive. Invariants enforced + test-pinned: **callsign hard-reject** (when both names parse a callsign and they differ, the pair is rejected before any threshold, beating `min_score` and the tvg_id override); **callsign required on both sides by default** for Locals fuzzy (no-callsign pairs admitted only via explicit opt-in and only at score ≥ 0.90); **refuse unscoped scored fuzzy** (schema validation rejects a `min_score` rule without a non-empty `target_channel_in_group`; legacy `loose_name_match` rules without `min_score` are unaffected); **tvg_id-callsign override fails closed** under a name-callsign conflict; **mandatory dry-run** with a test asserting the preview path performs zero writes; **journaled scores + provenance** (score, effective threshold, signal fired, both parsed callsigns, rule id, allowlist) for forensic reconstruction; and **journal-driven surgical rollback** that removes only the stream-ids a fuzzy merge added (preserving edits made after the merge), falling back to snapshot-restore only when journal data is missing. The new preview endpoint is `RequireAdminIfEnabled`-gated with `group_ids` validation/caps to bound the N×M scoring surface. **ReDoS hardening** on the `LOCALS` cleaner: input is length-capped before any regex and whitespace is collapsed once up front so the source-prefix regex is linear-time (the prior pattern was O(n²) on whitespace-padded, attacker-controllable M3U names). The global exact-match rule and the `match_by` no-op are untouched. ([bead jnzst])

## [0.17.2] — 2026-05-23

### Added
- **MCP: expose the 0.17.0/0.17.1 Stats v2 + media-server attribution features through Claude (epic co5wh, v0.17.2-0002).** These features shipped to the UI/API but were never queryable via MCP. New/updated tools: `get_channel_stats` now renders the media-server attribution fields the Active Channels endpoint already returns (`emby_user_name`/`plex_user_name`/`jellyfin_user_name` + `*_viewers`, `client_ip`/`client_ips`, `provider_name`) so Claude can answer "who's watching channel X" (co5wh.1); a new `get_provider_stats` tool wraps the Providers panel (`/api/stats/providers/{buffering,watch-time,channel-heatmap,bitrate}`, GH-59) via a `metric` selector (co5wh.2); `get_user_watch_time` + `get_user_channel_breakdown` wrap the Users panel and per-source breakdowns (`/api/stats/watch-time`, `/users/dispatcharr/{id}`, `/users/emby/{id}`, GH-62; surfaces `attribution_source`) (co5wh.3); `get_trending` + `get_channel_popularity` wrap `/popularity/trending` and `/popularity/channel/{id}` (co5wh.4); and `get_activity` + `get_channel_bandwidth` round out stats coverage (co5wh.5). 8 new tools (124 total). All new contract entries match the backend's untyped-list OpenAPI shape (`response_is_list` default) to satisfy the cross-repo contract test. **Tests:** 30 new mcp-server tests; mcp-server suite 250 passed, contract test 120 passed. Live-verified against the real backend (provider watch-time, per-user totals with "via dispatcharr" attribution, activity feed). ([epic co5wh])

### Deprecated
- **`ECM_TELEMETRY_EXCLUDE_USERS` env var is deprecated; slated for removal in v0.18.0 (bead ye075).** The var was a bd-uqbob workaround for the ECM/Dispatcharr user-id namespace collision in the Stats v2 Watch Time panel — ECM's local `users` table and Dispatcharr's `users` table have separate, coincidentally-overlapping integer IDs, so a Dispatcharr viewer could be mis-attributed to an ECM login. bd-gsn3r's migration 0011 (`session_telemetry.dispatcharr_username` + FK drop) fixed that collision at the source, so the var is no longer needed for its original purpose. It still works for ad-hoc "exclude this Dispatcharr viewer" needs, but ECM now logs a one-time `[BANDWIDTH] ECM_TELEMETRY_EXCLUDE_USERS is set … but is DEPRECATED … will be REMOVED in v0.18.0` WARNING the first poll the var is observed set. Operator guide updated (`docs/user_guide/stats/stats-v2-history-cutover.md` → "Excluding a specific viewer"); v0.18.0 removal tracked under bead lbpl7. **Tests:** 2 new unit tests in `test_bandwidth_tracker_exclude_users.py` (warning fires exactly once when the var is set, silent when unset). ([bead ye075])

### Fixed
- **MCP display/data-rendering tail from the full test-plan sweep (epic lq38l: lq38l.11 + lq38l.13, v0.17.2).** `get_journal` returned "No journal entries found" for every call — it unwrapped a nonexistent `entries` key instead of the backend's `results` pagination envelope; it now reads `results` (None-safe, and appends `entity_name`). Plus a 12-item display/cosmetic cluster: channel numbers no longer render a `.0` float suffix (`list_channels`/`get_channel`/`create_channel`/`update_channel`); `get_streams_for_channel`/`get_streams_by_ids` resolve provider + group names instead of raw ids; `get_epg_grid` resolves real channel names via `channel_uuid` (was `[Unknown]`, and channel_id-filtering now returns results); `get_auto_creation_rule` extracts the create-channel descriptor (was `create_channel: ?`); `run_auto_creation` dry-run renders real sample names and a "N more" count consistent with the created total; `get_top_watched` shows `N views` from `watch_count` (the metric the endpoint provides) instead of `? unique viewers`; `create_export_profile` rejects an empty/blank name; `dismiss_channel_merge` returns a clean `MERGE_NOT_FOUND` envelope (matching `accept_channel_merge`); `list_pending_channel_merges` coerces `candidate_channel_id` to int; `get_probe_results` reports "No probe results available." when none completed; `cancel_probe` drops the contradictory "cancelled" wording when nothing is running; and `create_auto_creation_rule` gains `quality_tie_break_order` + `match_scope_target_group` params. All fixes verified against confirmed backend response shapes. **Tests:** 27 new mcp-server tests (`test_lq38l_journal_and_nits.py`); mcp-server suite 328 passed, contract test 122 passed. ([epic lq38l])
- **MCP integration: a batch of 21 fixes across the MCP tool surface, found by a live test sweep of all 116 tools (epic bd-1wq7z, v0.17.2-0001).** Two cross-cutting blockers plus a wave of per-tool correctness fixes. **Auth (bd-1wq7z.1):** the static `mcp_api_key` was rejected (401 "Malformed token") by every route guarded with `RequireAuthIfEnabled`/`RequireAdminIfEnabled` because the route dependency only accepted a JWT — `get_current_user` now also accepts the static key (constant-time compare) as an admin-equivalent service principal, aligning the dependency layer with the grant the global middleware already makes; this revives the dedup tools, `add_stream` prompt/merge modes, and all backup tools. **Connectivity (bd-1wq7z.2):** documented that host-networking deployments must set `ECM_URL=http://localhost:6100` (the `ecm` service name doesn't resolve on a shared host network) — the symmetric partner to the existing `MCP_HOST` callout. **Data loss (bd-1wq7z.3 + bd-1wq7z.25):** `reorder-streams` and the bulk-commit reorder path REPLACED a channel's stream set, so a partial list silently detached the omitted streams; both now validate the list is a true permutation of the channel's current streams and reject (HTTP 400 / per-op error) instead of detaching. **Crashes from un-unwrapped response envelopes (bd-1wq7z.4):** `get_orphaned_groups`, `get_auto_created_groups`, `get_groups_with_streams`, and `list_tasks` iterated the backend's `{...: [...]}` dict and raised `'str' object has no attribute 'get'` — all now unwrap correctly. **Wrong result keys (bd-1wq7z.5):** `match_channels_epg` (read `matched`/`unmatched` vs the real `exact`/`multiple`/`none`, and sent no request body → 422), `delete_orphaned_groups` (`deleted`/`groups` vs `deleted_groups`/`failed_groups`), and `rollback_auto_creation` (`deleted` vs `entities_removed`) all reported zero/garbled results. **Other tool fixes:** `add_stream` now prefers an exact stream-name match instead of the first substring hit (bd-1wq7z.6); `update_m3u_group_settings`/`bulk_update_m3u_group_settings` send the backend's structured `{group_settings:[{channel_group:<int>,enabled}]}` body instead of a name-keyed dict (bd-1wq7z.7); `run_auto_creation` polls the execution to completion instead of reporting the 202 accept envelope as a finished "0 channels" run (bd-1wq7z.8); `create_epg_source` sends the required `source_type` (bd-1wq7z.9); `list_epg_sources` no longer crashes on a null `url` (bd-1wq7z.10); `list_channels(group_id=…)` works again — the backend now resolves the group id to the group name Dispatcharr filters on (bd-1wq7z.11); `cancel_task` reports the real status instead of always "cancelled" (bd-1wq7z.12); `apply_profile_to_channels` sends an explicit `enabled` instead of silently enabling every targeted channel (bd-1wq7z.13); `delete_all_notifications` can target unread via `include_unread` (bd-1wq7z.14); `delete_channel_group` reports "hidden" vs "deleted" for M3U-synced groups (bd-1wq7z.15); `cleanup_struck_out_streams` surfaces channel-deletion failures instead of swallowing them (bd-1wq7z.16); `probe_single_stream` uses a 300s timeout like the bulk variants (bd-1wq7z.17); `clear_auto_created` requires explicit `group_ids` or `all_groups=True` so an empty list can't trigger a system-wide clear (bd-1wq7z.18); `create_task_schedule` accepts a `timezone` (bd-1wq7z.19); a new `list_publish_configs` tool gives `publish_export` a config-id discovery path (bd-1wq7z.21); and several backend routes (`create_channel`, `get_channel`, `delete_channel_group`, `create_epg_source`) now surface the upstream Dispatcharr 4xx detail (400/404) instead of an opaque 500 (bd-1wq7z.22). **Tests:** ~80 new unit/integration tests across the mcp-server and backend suites; mcp-server suite green (220 passed), backend suite green (4805 passed, 69 skipped). ([epic bd-1wq7z])

### Security
- **MCP static-key hardening tail (1wq7z.24 + i3axt, v0.17.2).** Low-severity hardening on the PO-locked permanent static-key auth path (threat model EP2; none reachable by the real MCP client). The static-key comparisons in `mcp-server/server.py` and `backend/main.py` now use `hmac.compare_digest` (constant-time) instead of `!=`/`==`, removing a theoretical timing oracle; the MCP resource server also parses the `Bearer` scheme case-insensitively and strips credential whitespace per RFC 6750 §2.1 (malformed/empty headers still fail safe with 401). Self-mutation auth routes (`PUT /api/auth/me`, `POST /api/auth/change-password`) now reject the transient MCP service principal with a clean 403 instead of raising a 500 (`session.refresh` on a non-DB `User`). The MCP threat model gains an EP3 row documenting that `mcp_api_key` is admin-equivalent **including ECM user-account management** (confirmed redacted in backups via `_SETTINGS_CREDENTIAL_FIELDS`). **Tests:** 9 backend + 25 mcp-server security tests. ([1wq7z.24], [i3axt])

## [0.17.1] — 2026-05-22

### Added
- **Stats: surface the real requestor device IP from media-server sessions as a separate "Client IP" field (bd-7ncci, v0.17.1-0072).** The Stats → Active Channels → Connected Clients view shows the Dispatcharr-observed connection IP, which for a media-server-mediated stream is the media server's / Docker bridge's IP — not the device the human is actually watching on. Emby and Jellyfin sessions expose the real device IP in `RemoteEndPoint`; Plex exposes it in `<Player address=...>`. ECM already captured these into `EmbySession.remote_endpoint` / `JellyfinSession.remote_endpoint` / `PlexSession.remote_endpoint` but dropped them at the attribution boundary. This change threads the matched session's real client IP through the full attribution pipeline and surfaces it as a NEW, SEPARATE field — the existing Dispatcharr connection IP display is unchanged. **Step 0 live verification (ecm-ecm-1, all three sources enabled):** Emby `RemoteEndPoint` confirmed live as a bare IP (`gbsweep`=`159.112.42.143`); Jellyfin/Plex confirmed via the identical capture paths + existing client-parse tests (no active session at probe time). Normalization strips a `host:port` suffix, unwraps bracketed IPv6 (`[2001:db8::1]:51514` → `2001:db8::1`), takes the first hop of an XFF-style list, and validates the result is a real IP (junk → blank). **Plumbing:** new source-agnostic `normalize_client_ip()` + `rollup_client_ips()` in `services/attribution_reconciler.py`; `client_ip` added to `EmbyAttribution`/`JellyfinAttribution`/`PlexAttribution` (threaded from each matched session's `remote_endpoint`) and to the reconciler's `CandidateUser`, so it rides through every assignment shape. **API (live path `routers/stats.py`):** each per-client connection now carries a `client_ip` (the single attributed viewer's real device IP) and `client_ips` (the distinct set for a server-proxy or Option-B rollup connection), plus a `client_ip` key inside each `*_viewers` dict; both seeded blank (`null` / `[]`) so the TypeScript shape is stable for unattributed and direct-XC connections. **Persisted path (`bandwidth_tracker.py`):** `client_ip` rides inside the existing `*_viewers` JSON columns (no schema migration). **Frontend (`StatsTab.tsx` + `types/index.ts` + `StatsTab.css`):** a separate "Client IP" element renders next to the connection IP when present (single IP, or comma-joined set for proxy/rollup), blank otherwise. **Attribution correctness is unchanged** — this is a display-only field; all bd-mlcla/bd-rools invariants (anti-collapse, anti-broadcast, account-identity discriminator, B1 direct-first/proxy-remainder, Option-B rollup) are untouched. **Live end-to-end (ecm-ecm-1, deployed code, real Emby session):** the production `_enrich_channels_with_attribution` path attributed `gbsweep` and stamped the real device IP `159.112.42.143` into the new `client_ip` field while the connection's observed `ip_address` stayed unchanged; an unassigned connection showed `client_ip` blank. **Tests:** resolver client-IP capture + normalization (host:port/IPv6/missing) per source; reconciler `client_ip` single + `client_ips` rollup set + unattributed→none; stats live-path attributed→populated / unattributed→blank / proxy→IP set; persisted-path `client_ip` in viewers JSON (populated + null); frontend renders the field when present, omits when absent, and renders the rollup set. Full backend + frontend suites green. ([bd-7ncci], v0.17.1-0072)
- **CI: gate mcp-server/tests/ suite before any OAuth work (bd-ak7xa, v0.17.1-0067).** Hygiene precondition for the OAuth 2.1 epic (bd-buiqr). `mcp-server/tests/` (72 tests, 4 files, ~1.6k LOC) was not gated by CI — `.github/workflows/test.yml` only ran `backend/tests/` and frontend, so any new OAuth tests would have rotted silently. **`.github/workflows/test.yml`**: new `mcp-server` job (check name "MCP Server Tests") mirrors the backend job structure — checkout, Python 3.12 setup with pip cache keyed on `mcp-server/requirements.txt`, install runtime deps + pin `pytest pytest-asyncio pytest-cov`, run pytest with `--tb=short --no-header -p no:warnings --cov=. --cov-config=.coveragerc --cov-fail-under=26 --junitxml=junit.xml`, upload `mcp-server-coverage` artifact, upload `junit-mcp` artifact (30-day retention to match the flake-pr-comment aggregator window). **`mcp-server/.coveragerc`** (new): `source = .` with `tests/` and `__pycache__/*` omitted so the self-coverage of test files doesn't inflate the TOTAL. Baseline measured 2026-05-19 at **28%** line coverage on dev tip; floor set at **26%** per the 2-point regression buffer in `docs/testing.md` § "Coverage ratchet cadence". **`scripts/aggregate_flakes.py`**: `JUNIT_ARTIFACT_NAMES` extended with `junit-mcp` so the existing flake-pr-comment workflow picks up mcp-server failures alongside backend and frontend. **Operator-visible follow-up**: branch protection on `dev` (and `main`) does not include the new "MCP Server Tests" check by default — the PO must add it via the GitHub branch-protection UI after this PR lands so the gate becomes blocking. Until then the job runs and reports status but cannot block a merge. **Tests:** all 72 mcp-server tests pass clean locally (`72 passed in 2.37s` with `Required test coverage of 26% reached. Total coverage: 28.14%`). ([bd-ak7xa], v0.17.1-0067)

### Fixed
- **SMTP "Test Connection" failed with 530 Authentication Required even though scheduled notification emails sent fine (bd-air4z, GH #380, v0.17.1-0090).** The Settings UI never re-sends the saved SMTP password — it is masked and cleared on load (`setSmtpPassword('') // Never load password from server` in `SettingsTab.tsx`) — so a Test Connection request normally arrives with `smtp_password=""`. The `POST /api/settings/test-smtp` handler called `server.login()` only `if request.smtp_user and request.smtp_password`, so with the empty password it skipped authentication entirely and Gmail rejected the send with `530 5.7.0 Authentication Required`. The scheduled-notification path (`tasks/m3u_digest.py`, `alert_methods_smtp.py`) reads the stored password directly and was unaffected — exactly matching the reporter's observation that overnight digest emails arrived while the test kept failing. **Fix:** `test_smtp_connection` now resolves credentials with the same preserve-on-omit contract as `update_settings` — an empty `smtp_user`/`smtp_password` in the request falls back to the stored settings before `server.login()`. A password typed into the test form still takes precedence (operators validating new credentials before saving). **Tests:** 2 new cases in `test_settings.py::TestTestSMTP` — `test_falls_back_to_stored_password_when_omitted` (asserts `login()` is called with the stored secret when the request omits the password) and `test_uses_request_password_over_stored`. Full backend suite green (4859 passed, 1 skipped). ([bd-air4z], GH #380, v0.17.1-0090)
- **Per-connection Dispatcharr-account discriminator: a genuine direct XC subscriber no longer absorbs a media-server viewer it shares a channel with (bd-rools, re-fix for the bd-cat70 direct-client cross-attribution, v0.17.1-0071).** Fix-forward for bd-4w9w6 (PR #366, already merged into `dev` at v0.17.1-0070). bd-4w9w6 fixed the "User #0" bug by setting `has_url_identity=False` for every proxy connection — correct, but because the bd-mlcla resolver is no longer IP-gated (it returns the channel's full matched-user set for ANY source IP), removing the only discriminator re-exposed the **bd-cat70 direct-client cross-attribution**: a genuine direct XC subscriber (e.g. `kmfelmer`, identified by a real Dispatcharr account `user_id`) sharing a channel with a media-server viewer (`MotWakorb`) became eligible for reconciliation and, via the B1 direct-first ordering, ABSORBED the media-server user — `kmfelmer`'s row showed `MotWakorb` and the real Emby/Jellyfin viewer dropped to "User #0". **Root cause:** the bd-4w9w6 commit had NO per-connection discriminator at all — the Dispatcharr ACCOUNT identity present on the `/proxy/ts/status` client dict (`user_id`/`username`) was read for the username-join but never carried into the reconciler's `Connection`. **Fix:** a real per-connection discriminator `Connection.has_account_identity`, set at BOTH call sites (`routers/stats.py` `_build_attribution_connections` and `bandwidth_tracker.py` `_build_persisted_connection`) from the Dispatcharr account `user_id`/`username` via the shared `services.attribution_reconciler.has_dispatcharr_account_identity()` helper. A connection with a genuine account identity (positive `user_id`, or a real non-sentinel `username`) is a direct-IPTV subscriber: it is excluded from `eligible_connections()` and never flagged `is_server_proxy`, keeping its existing Dispatcharr/bd-gy5nd attribution. Anonymous connections — Dispatcharr `user_id` `"0"`/`0`/`None`, which is how Dispatcharr serves EVERY media-server pull (the transcoding proxy AND NAT'd browser-direct playback) — carry no account identity and stay eligible. The `bandwidth_tracker` persisted snapshot now threads `user_id` through `client_meta` so the persisted path applies the identical discriminator. The channel-upstream URL is no longer used for eligibility (bd-4w9w6); the bd-gy5nd `provider_name` label remains independent. **Step 0 live data verification (Dispatcharr `172.16.0.19:9191`):** the active media-server pull on the live channel carried `user_id="0"` (anonymous); the Dispatcharr account list confirms genuine subscribers (`kmfelmer`=`user_id 3`, etc.) carry distinct positive ids — the two populations are cleanly separable by account-identity presence. **Live end-to-end verification (ecm-ecm-1, deployed code, real Emby with 7 active sessions):** (a) TSN5/`172.18.0.1` anonymous NAT'd browser-direct → `MotWakorb` (User#0 fix holds); (b) a shared channel with `kmfelmer` (user_id 3, ranked first) + an anonymous pull → `kmfelmer` keeps `emby_user_name=None` (NOT cross-attributed) and the anonymous pull gets `MotWakorb`; (c) jkaisersoze (4 distinct anonymous connections, 1 user) → exactly ONE connection attributed, the rest User #0 (no collapse). **Tests:** repaired the bd-cat70 regression test that previously passed for the WRONG reason (it used an IP-gated resolver mock — old semantics) — `test_direct_xc_subscriber_does_not_absorb_media_server_viewer` and `test_single_user_not_broadcast_to_every_eligible_connection` now use realistic non-IP-gated resolver semantics and assert the account-identity protection; both proven to FAIL on pre-fix code and PASS after. New `TestAccountIdentityHelper` + eligibility cases in `test_attribution_reconciler.py`, and persisted-path `test_nat_browser_direct_no_account_stays_eligible_persisted_bd_rools` + `test_direct_subscriber_does_not_absorb_media_viewer_persisted_bd_rools`. All bd-mlcla invariants (anti-collapse jkaisersoze, anti-broadcast, B1 direct-first/proxy-remainder, Option-B rollup) and the bd-4w9w6 User#0 end-to-end tests remain green. Full backend suite green (4573 passed, 1 skipped). ([bd-rools], re-fix for [bd-cat70], fix-forward for [bd-4w9w6]/[bd-mlcla], v0.17.1-0071)
- **Attribution integration gap: an XC-credentialed UPSTREAM channel URL silently excluded every media-server viewer from reconciliation → "User #0" (bd-4w9w6, fix-forward for bd-mlcla, v0.17.1-0070).** Live bug on the PO's container, deployed v0.17.1-0069: the Jellyfin resolver correctly matched the browser-direct TSN5 connection (`[JELLYFIN-RESOLVER] tier_match ecm_channel='494 | TSN 5' match_count=1 top_user=MotWakorb`, client `172.18.0.1` NAT'd through the Docker bridge, `ip_is_server=False`), but the Stats page still showed "User #0" and ZERO reconciler result-log lines appeared — the resolver's match never reached the connection. **Root cause:** bd-mlcla's "no-URL-identity" discriminator was derived from the channel's **upstream provider URL** (`ch["url"]`, e.g. `https://infinity.gives/live/mot/16118141/108495.ts`). That URL embeds the operator's **Xtream-Codes provider account** credentials (`/live/<user>/<pass>/<id>.ts`), which `url_embeds_username()` matches — so `has_url_identity` was `True` for the channel, `eligible_connections()` filtered the connection out, and `_enrich_one_source` returned early at the empty-`eligible_ips` guard before ever calling the reconciler. Because virtually every channel in an XC-sourced deployment carries such a URL, this excluded **all** media-server-mediated viewers, not just genuine direct-IPTV clients. The discriminator conflated the channel's upstream SOURCE (a per-channel provider account, shared by all viewers) with the client's IDENTITY. Dispatcharr `/proxy/ts/status` connections are proxy clients — their payload carries only `client_id`/`ip_address`/`user_agent`/`connected_at`, never per-client credentials — so the upstream URL is never a per-client signal. **Fix:** both call sites (`routers/stats.py` `_build_attribution_connections` and `bandwidth_tracker.py` `_reconcile_persisted_attributions`) now set `has_url_identity=False` for every proxy connection; the channel-upstream URL no longer gates reconciliation. The bd-gy5nd provider/hostname label is computed independently and continues to surface alongside the media-server user (the two are not mutually exclusive — a viewer can watch an XC-sourced channel through Jellyfin/Emby/Plex). All bd-mlcla invariants are unchanged (anti-collapse, anti-broadcast, direct-first/proxy-remainder B1, Option-B rollup, no phantom clients) — only the (always-wrong) eligibility gate is removed. **Diagnosability:** the reconciler now emits a concise INFO `[RECONCILE] channel=… conns=N eligible=N candidate_users=[…] assignments={client_id->user} unattributed=N` result line on every reconciliation (it previously had NO result logging, which is why this class of bug produced zero downstream log lines). **Live verification (ecm-ecm-1, v0.17.1-0070, real HTTP `/api/stats/channels` through the auth stack):** the TSN5 / `172.18.0.1` connection now returns `jellyfin_user_name="MotWakorb"` + `jellyfin_viewers=[{user_id:72be7…, user_name:MotWakorb}]`, with `provider_name="Infinity"` coexisting, and `emby_user_name`/`plex_user_name` correctly `None` (those sources run and don't match — the multi-source case where one match survives others' empty results). **Tests:** the synthetic bd-mlcla call-site tests used a non-credentialed Dispatcharr proxy URL as `ch["url"]`, which is why they passed while the real path (XC upstream URL) was broken — root-caused and corrected. New end-to-end regressions drive the realistic XC upstream URL through the full `_enrich_channels_with_attribution` (live path) and `_collect_stats` (persisted path) wiring: `TestStatsBd4w9w6XcUpstreamUrl` (live TSN5/Jellyfin multi-source match survival + `[RECONCILE]` logging assertion), the retargeted `test_xc_sourced_channel_still_attributes_via_media_server`, and `test_xc_upstream_url_jellyfin_match_persisted_bd4w9w6` — all proven to FAIL on pre-fix code (`None`/NULL attribution, no `[RECONCILE]` line) and PASS after. Full backend suite green (4548 passed, 1 skipped). ([bd-4w9w6], fix-forward for [bd-mlcla], v0.17.1-0070)
- **Media-server attribution is now networking-agnostic — per-channel set reconciliation replaces the brittle IP gate (bd-mlcla, supersedes bd-podx3/bd-ost8o/bd-cat70 attribution model, v0.17.1-0069).** Live bug on the PO's container: browser-direct Jellyfin playback (Jellyfin Web) is NAT'd through the media box's Docker bridge gateway, so ECM observed source `172.18.0.1` instead of the configured `172.16.0.19`. The strict resolver IP gate restored by the bd-podx3 revert (`if ecm_session_ip != server_ip: return []`) rejected it → "User #0". **Core change:** Dispatcharr `/proxy/ts/status` connections carry a stable `client_id`; media-server sessions carry a stable `session_id` + `user_name` + `last_activity_date`. The two sides' source IPs do NOT correlate across the NAT boundary, so attribution is now a per-channel **set reconciliation** keyed on stable ids, NOT an IP join. **Resolver layer (`backend/services/{emby,jellyfin,plex}_resolver.py`):** the hard `return []` IP gate is removed — the resolver runs the existing Tier 1 (channel-name strict) + Tier 2 (channel-number strict) match for ANY source IP and returns the distinct matched-user set for the channel. Tier 3 (fuzzy stream-name) stays gated to the server-IP case only (`allow_fuzzy_tier3=ecm_session_ip==server_ip`) — fuzzy matching with no IP signal is the documented bd-ost8o VOD-onto-live false-positive vector. Source IP becomes a soft RANKING hint, never a reject; the `resolver_call` INFO log now carries `ip_is_server=<bool>` (rank-not-gate) instead of the old `ip_mismatch` reject. **Reconciliation layer (new `backend/services/attribution_reconciler.py`, used by both `routers/stats.py` `_enrich_one_source` and `bandwidth_tracker.py` `_resolve_attributions`):** eligible connections = clients with NO URL identity (channels whose active stream URL embeds XC/M3U credentials keep the bd-gy5nd provider path and are excluded — the discriminator that replaces the IP gate); candidate users = the resolver's distinct set. **Each user is assigned to AT MOST ONE connection** (structural anti-collapse — the jkaisersoze regression stays fixed). **Direct (browser-direct/NAT'd) connections reconcile FIRST**, each consuming a distinct user; a server-proxy connection (source IP == resolved media-server IP) then carries the **remaining (unconsumed)** users as its rollup (bd-mlcla B1 — PO directive "never drop the viewer"). This guarantees a browser-direct viewer sharing a channel with a tuner/proxy pull always gets its own distinct name when an unconsumed matching user exists (the TSN5 mixed case), while a proxy serving N app-viewers with no direct connection still carries the full set unchanged (preserves the bd-r5f0c.9 multi-viewer-on-one-proxy case). The `is_server_proxy` flag is exact source-IP-equality with no corroborating signal, so a browser-direct connection whose observed IP happens to equal the server IP is mis-flagged as the proxy; the direct-first ordering bounds the blast radius to that one connection's display label. Count behavior: `users==connections` → exact 1:1; `users<connections` → fill top-ranked, rest stay User #0 (never broadcast — the bd-cat70 fix); `users>connections` → surplus surfaces only in the channel-level `*_viewers` list (no phantom clients). **Residual ambiguity = Option B:** when a group of browser-direct connections is genuinely indistinguishable (same IP-priority bucket, no per-connection signal) AND there are 2+ candidate users, each row shows a `"N viewers: <comma-list>"` rollup instead of a possibly-wrong single pinned name. **Soft IP ranking (`backend/config.py`):** new operator setting `trusted_media_networks` (CIDRs/IPs, default empty) + auto-detected local Docker bridge gateways feed candidate RANKING only — getting them wrong changes tie-break order, never which user attributes (asserted by a flip-the-gateway test). New Settings → Trusted Media Networks field + TS types. **Live verification (ecm-ecm-1, v0.17.1-0069):** TSN5 / `172.18.0.1` browser-direct now attributes to `MotWakorb` (no more User #0); CNBC server-mediated from `172.16.0.19` still attributes; jkaisersoze (3 distinct IPs, 1 user) attributes to exactly 1 connection, never broadcast. **Tests:** new `test_attribution_reconciler.py` (40 cases — anti-collapse/anti-broadcast/no-phantom/Option-B/topology-agnostic/IP-ranking-correctness-neutral), `test_config_trusted_media_networks.py`, `test_bandwidth_tracker_mlcla_attribution.py` (persisted-path bridge-NAT/anti-collapse/anti-broadcast/Option-B), bd-mlcla stats reconciliation cases in `test_stats_attribution.py`, retargeted resolver IP-gate tests (off-server traffic now strict-matches; the bd-podx3 `TestPodx3DistinctIpsDoNotCollapse` class supersedes the gate contract — collapse-prevention moved to the reconciler), plus an Option-B rollup render test in `StatsTab.test.tsx`. Full backend suite + frontend suite green. ([bd-mlcla], supersedes [bd-podx3]/[bd-ost8o]/[bd-cat70], cross-ref [bd-gy5nd], v0.17.1-0069)
- **Stats attribution collapse: distinct client IPs all mapped to one media-server user — revert bd-ost8o two-mode dispatch (bd-podx3, fixes bd-ost8o, v0.17.1-0068).** Live production regression: on the PO's Stats page, four connected clients — each from a DIFFERENT IP — were all attributed to the single Emby user ``jkaisersoze``. **Root cause (confirmed by live container logs):** bd-ost8o (v0.17.1-0062) removed the strict IP-gate short-circuit (``if ecm_session_ip != server_ip: return []``) in all three resolvers (``emby_resolver.py`` / ``jellyfin_resolver.py`` / ``plex_resolver.py``) and replaced it with a two-mode dispatch. Its new ``direct_fallback`` mode (Mode B, run for any IP that is NOT the media-server IP) matched an ECM client to a media-server session by **channel name/number alone, with no signal tying the client to the session.** Every ECM client watching channel ``679 | Milwaukee Brewers`` — regardless of its physical IP (``98.144.168.47``, ``192.168.1.108``, ``172.16.0.19``) — resolved to whichever Emby user (``jkaisersoze``) was watching that same channel. bd-ost8o's ``ambiguous_skip`` guard only fired when 2+ *media-server sessions* matched; it did nothing about the actual failure mode (1 session, N ECM clients). The pre-bd-ost8o strict gate was the load-bearing correctness guarantee: only traffic egressing through the media server's IP can be a genuine media-server viewer. bd-cat70's per-IP enrichment scoping in ``stats.py`` was verified correct and is NOT the cause — the collapse was entirely in the resolver returning a positive match for non-viewer IPs. **Fix vs. forward-fix decision:** reverted bd-ost8o's resolver behavior change (restored the strict IP gate; removed ``mode`` dispatch, the ``direct_fallback`` branch, the ``ambiguous_skip`` guard, and the Mode-B tier-3 gate) in all three resolvers. A forward fix preserving browser-direct-play would need a real per-client identity signal (the captured-but-unused ``EmbySession.remote_endpoint`` client IP), which is net-new design unsuitable for a live hotfix — browser-direct-play attribution is deferred to a fresh bead. Mode B was unsound by construction: channel-name matching cannot identify a specific viewer, so it systematically mis-attributed every co-channel non-media-server viewer. The functional resolver code is now byte-identical to the pre-bd-ost8o (v0.17.1-0061) version. **Live verification (PO's ecm-ecm-1 container):** after deploy + restart, ``mode=`` log markers are gone (new code live) and ``[EMBY-RESOLVER] resolver_call`` now fires ONLY for the Emby server IP ``172.16.0.19`` — the non-server client IPs short-circuit at the restored gate and ``jkaisersoze`` is no longer broadcast across four IPs. **Tests:** reverted the bd-ost8o Mode-B test additions across all three ``test_*_resolver.py`` files (restored the strict-gate test contract), plus a new regression class ``TestPodx3DistinctIpsDoNotCollapse`` in ``test_emby_resolver.py`` reproducing the live scenario — three distinct non-server client IPs on the same channel as a matching Emby session each return ``[]`` (proven to FAIL against the bd-ost8o resolver and PASS against the fix), the genuine server-IP client still attributes correctly, and the non-server path does not consult the session cache. Resolver suites: 194 passed; stats suites: 265 passed. ([bd-podx3], fixes [bd-ost8o], v0.17.1-0068)
- **README: surface MCP_HOST upgrade callout for host-networking operators (bd-0eimc, v0.17.1-0066).** Operators running ECM and ecm-mcp with `network_mode: host` who pull dev after v0.17.1-0064 will silently break: the probe default changed from `localhost` to `ecm-mcp`, and `ecm-mcp` does not resolve on host-networking topologies. The existing "Reaching the MCP container from ECM" paragraph in README covered the configuration option but read as new-installer guidance, not an action item for upgraders. Added a distinct blockquote callout immediately below that paragraph labelled "Upgrade note (v0.17.1-0066+)" explaining the behavior change, who is affected, and the exact fix (`MCP_HOST=localhost` in the ECM service's environment block).
- **MCP Integration: copy buttons silently failed on plain-HTTP deployments + missing Node.js prerequisite docs (bd-rowlk + bd-fpjz3, v0.17.1-0065).** Two related Settings → MCP Integration fixes shipped together. **bd-rowlk (copy buttons):** the four copy buttons on the MCP Integration panel (API Key, MCP Endpoint URL, Claude Desktop config, Claude Code config) used `navigator.clipboard.writeText` directly. `navigator.clipboard` is gated behind a secure-context check by the browser, so on plain-HTTP deployments (the typical ECM home-server shape on a LAN IP) the API is undefined; the call silently rejected and the "Copied to clipboard" toast fired regardless. **`frontend/src/components/settings/MCPSettingsSection.tsx`**: `handleCopy` now uses the project's existing `copyToClipboard` utility (`frontend/src/utils/clipboard.ts`) which falls back to `document.execCommand('copy')` on HTTP origins where `navigator.clipboard` is undefined. The other ECM components (`StreamListItem`, `ChannelListItem`, `PreviewStreamModal`, etc.) already use this utility; `MCPSettingsSection` was the outlier. The four call sites now pass a label argument and surface a `notifications.error('Failed to copy to clipboard')` toast on failure instead of falsely claiming success. **bd-fpjz3 (Node.js prereq docs):** Claude Desktop does not bundle Node.js, but the `mcp-remote` bridge in our published `claude_desktop_config.json` snippet runs via `npx`. Operators without Node hit `spawn npx ENOENT` in Claude Desktop's logs after pasting the config — README and the in-app Settings panel did not call out the prerequisite. **`README.md`**: new prerequisite callout under the Claude Desktop config block explaining Node.js LTS 18+ is required, with install pointers (`winget install OpenJS.NodeJS.LTS`, `brew install node`, `apt install nodejs npm`). Also adds a "Why not Claude Desktop's Custom Connectors (no-Node path)?" note explaining that Claude Desktop's built-in Custom Connectors UI would let you skip Node entirely but requires OAuth 2.1 with PKCE per the MCP spec — ECM's MCP server currently uses a static API key, so the Custom Connector path is **not supported yet** (tracked as enhancedchannelmanager-buiqr). Claude Code is unaffected by the Node requirement — it talks Streamable HTTP directly. **`frontend/src/components/settings/MCPSettingsSection.tsx` + `.css`**: matching in-app callout (`.mcp-prereq-note` class with accent-bordered styling) above the Claude Desktop config snippet so operators see the same warning in the UI without reading the README. **No new tests** — the existing `MCPSettingsSection.test.tsx` suite covers the Server Status diagnostic panel (bd-ix1g6); copy-button behavior is covered indirectly by the `copyToClipboard` utility's own test suite (`clipboard.test.ts`). Full frontend suite stays green. ([bd-rowlk] + [bd-fpjz3], v0.17.1-0065)
- **MCP Integration status badge always showed "not reachable" on canonical Docker compose (bd-d2171, v0.17.1-0064).** `GET /api/settings/mcp-status` hard-coded `http://localhost:6101/health` as the probe target. Under canonical bridge networking (`docker-compose.mcp.yml` — MCP runs as a separate `ecm-mcp` container), `localhost` from inside ECM resolves to ECM itself, not the MCP container, so the probe always failed and the Settings → MCP Integration badge displayed "MCP server not reachable" even when MCP was healthy. The bug was invisible to operators running `network_mode: host` on both containers because the host network namespace is shared. **`backend/routers/settings.py`**: new `MCP_HOST` env var, defaults to `ecm-mcp` (matches the canonical compose service name), URL is built as `http://{MCP_HOST}:{MCP_PORT}/health`. **Operator action for `network_mode: host` deployments**: set `MCP_HOST=localhost` on the `ecm` service in your compose file to restore the previous behavior. **README**: one paragraph under "With MCP Server" explains the default and the host-networking override. **Tests:** 2 new tests in `backend/tests/routers/test_settings.py::TestMCPStatusHostResolution` lock both contracts — unset env → `http://ecm-mcp:6101/health`, `MCP_HOST=localhost` → `http://localhost:6101/health`. Uses `monkeypatch.setenv`/`delenv` and a captor stub for `httpx.AsyncClient`. Full backend suite (3808 passed, 1 skipped) stays green. ([bd-d2171], v0.17.1-0064)
- **Stats page shows "Unknown" provider on live channels — replace broken proxy-session-UUID channel-streams fallback with URL-hostname matching against M3U accounts (bd-gy5nd, v0.17.1-0063).** PO upgraded to v0.17.1-0062 and the Stats page still showed "Unknown" for the provider on the channel they were watching. Live probe of the PO's ecm-ecm-1 container showed the actual failure: ECM's resolver was calling ``GET /api/channels/channels/<uuid>/streams/`` with the proxy-session UUID from ``/proxy/ts/status`` (the ``channel_id`` field on that payload, e.g. ``"893c18f3-5aaf-4254-b1d0-3b926deb4c78"``), but that endpoint expects a Dispatcharr REST channel id (integer or REST UUID — different identifier space). Dispatcharr returned the SPA ``index.html`` (200 + ``text/html``) because no API route matched, JSON parse blew up on ``<`` and the resolver logged ``[STATS_V2] provider_resolution_failed reason=channel_streams_lookup_raised`` every 10s. The original bd-5g7kx channel-streams URL-match fallback (which depended on ``get_channel_streams(<channel_uuid>)``) has never worked for proxy-session UUIDs and is structurally broken — superseded by the bd-gy5nd path. The original bd-ef1go framing diagnosed this as "Dispatcharr returns empty body" — wrong; the body is the SPA HTML, not empty. ``backend/bandwidth_tracker.py``: new ``_match_provider_from_url(url, m3u_accounts)`` derives provider identity from the ``url`` field that is already in the ``/proxy/ts/status`` snapshot — parses the URL hostname (``infinity.gives``), matches against each M3U account's ``server_url`` hostname (case-insensitive, port-tolerant), and returns the matched account's ``id``/``name`` plus the bare hostname. When no M3U account matches the hostname, the function still returns the bare hostname so the response always carries a meaningful provider string for the operator. ``resolve_active_channel_streams`` now fetches the M3U accounts list once per poll (best-effort, empty list on failure — never blocks the resolver) and uses the URL-hostname match as the fallback path when the stream-id direct lookup misses. The legacy ``_resolve_via_channel_streams`` channel-streams fallback that called ``client.get_channel_streams(<channel_uuid>)`` is removed because the identifier is wrong — that endpoint never resolved for proxy-session UUIDs. ``ProviderResolution`` NamedTuple gains two trailing fields ``provider_name: Optional[str]`` and ``hostname: Optional[str]`` (defaults preserve existing call sites), populated whenever URL parsing succeeds even when no M3U match exists. ``backend/dispatcharr_client.py``: ``get_channel_streams(channel_id: int)`` keeps its existing integer-ID contract — only the proxy-session-UUID misuse goes away. ``backend/routers/stats.py``: the Active Channels endpoint surfaces ``m3u_account_id``/``provider_name``/``provider_hostname`` per channel; the prior ``stream_name`` field stays populated when the direct-id path resolves. ``frontend/src/components/tabs/StatsTab.tsx``: when a channel carries a backend ``provider_name`` (M3U source name or bare hostname), the badge derivation uses that string; only truly null provider attribution (no URL, no hostname) falls into the existing "Unknown" bucket. The 10s WARN spam is replaced with an INFO-level ``[STATS_V2] provider_resolved channel=<uuid> host=<hostname> source=<account-name-or-null>`` line per channel when URL-hostname resolution succeeds, and a single ``provider_resolution resolved=N unresolved=N`` SLI summary per poll as before. **Critical premise correction**: the brief assumed an ECM-side ``M3USource`` model in ``backend/models.py``; reality is that M3U accounts live in Dispatcharr (``/api/m3u/accounts/``) and ECM consumes them live via ``client.get_m3u_accounts()``. The fix design is functionally identical — the source of truth is Dispatcharr, not ECM's DB. **Tests**: 7 new tests in ``backend/tests/unit/test_bandwidth_tracker_url_provider.py`` lock the URL-hostname match contract (M3U match by hostname, port-tolerance, scheme-tolerance, query-string-tolerance, no-match returns bare hostname, malformed URL returns None triple, M3U accounts fetch failure degrades gracefully). 4 new tests in ``backend/tests/routers/test_stats.py::TestChannelStatsUrlProvider`` lock the endpoint contract (resolved M3U name surfaces, bare hostname surfaces when no M3U match, ``stream_name`` still resolves via stream-id direct path, no ``provider_resolution_failed`` WARN emitted for the URL-hostname path). Existing ``channel_streams_fallback_*`` tests retargeted to the URL-hostname path — the bd-5g7kx tests have served their purpose and the underlying code is gone. Existing ``test_get_channel_streams_with_int_id_still_works`` integration in ``test_channels.py`` preserved (the REST API method is correct for integer channel ids; only the proxy-session-UUID misuse goes away). 5 retargeted tests in ``frontend/src/components/tabs/StatsTab.test.tsx`` (the new ``provider_name`` display contract) plus 3 new tests for the badge sum invariant under the URL-hostname path. Full backend + frontend suites green. ([bd-gy5nd], v0.17.1-0063)
- **Resolver IP-gate too strict for browser-direct-play attribution (bd-ost8o, v0.17.1-0062).** PO upgraded to v0.17.1-0061 (bd-dok7u NowPlayingQueue fallback + forensic logging) and Jellyfin attribution still showed "User #0". The new forensic logs revealed the actual remaining gap: ``[STATS-ENRICH] channel_trace decision=proceed`` fires with ``client_ips=['172.18.0.1']`` (the Docker bridge gateway — the PO's browser egress), but then ZERO ``[JELLYFIN-RESOLVER] resolver_call`` INFO logs follow because the resolver's IP-gate short-circuits before fetching sessions. The gate assumes the ECM client comes from the source server's IP — TRUE for server-mediated playback (Emby/Jellyfin/Plex transcodes the stream from ECM and re-serves to the user) but FALSE for browser-direct-play (the operator opens Jellyfin Web in their browser, the browser fetches the IPTV stream URL directly from Dispatcharr, ECM sees the browser's egress IP, NOT the Jellyfin server). **`backend/services/jellyfin_resolver.py`** / **`emby_resolver.py`** / **`plex_resolver.py`** now implement two-mode dispatch: **Mode A (server-mediated, existing fast path)** runs when ``ecm_session_ip == source_server_ip`` — behavior unchanged, all tiers active including tier-3 fuzzy. **Mode B (browser-direct fallback, new)** runs when the IPs differ — fetch sessions and run tier-1 (channel-name strict) and tier-2 (channel-number strict) only; tier-3 fuzzy is disabled because in a multi-user setup a sufficiently generic stream name would cross-attribute viewers without the IP gate's implicit tiebreaker. Plex's bd-ma6r3 EPG cross-reference tier remains active in Mode B (it is a Tier-1 alternative relying on strict EPG ``channelCallSign`` equality, not fuzzy matching). When Mode B produces >1 candidate, the resolver returns ``[]`` and emits an ambiguous-skip diagnostic INFO log (``result=ambiguous_skip matched_sessions=N matched_users=[...]``) rather than attributing arbitrarily — the IP gate's "the server vouched for the playback" tiebreaker is no longer available in direct-play, so a coin-flip attribution would risk credit theft. The existing ``resolver_call`` INFO log gains a ``mode=server_ip|direct_fallback`` field so operators can confirm which dispatch branch ran. The no-match WARN is now emitted on Mode B no-match paths too (previously the IP short-circuit silenced it on those calls) — direct-play debugging needs the forensic surface as much as server-mediated debugging does. The Jellyfin bd-dok7u NowPlayingQueue resolution happens at cache-fetch time and is therefore already populated by the time Mode B's tier-1/tier-2 run, so the PO's exact symptom (browser-direct-play of ``'430 | Tennis Channel'``) now resolves to ``MotWakorb`` end-to-end. **bd-dok7u + bd-f68c8 retrospective:** both prior fixes remain correct as defense-in-depth — bd-dok7u (queue-resolved session data) and bd-f68c8 (ECM-side pipe-prefix parsing) operate at orthogonal layers (data shape and tier-comparison respectively). Mode B exposes their value: without them, tier-1/tier-2 in Mode B would have nothing to match against for the PO's data shape. Neither is obsoleted by bd-ost8o; rather, bd-ost8o is the dispatch-layer fix that lets the prior two finally take effect on the operator's direct-play path. **Hard rules respected:** ``_resolve_<source>_server_ip`` helpers retained (Mode A still uses them), Mode A behavior unchanged, ``stats.py`` enrichment untouched (per bd-cat70), bandwidth_tracker / SessionTelemetry writer untouched, Plex EPG path untouched (per bd-ma6r3), no new resolver tiers added, no channel renames. **Tests:** 7 new tests per source-resolver test file in ``backend/tests/services/test_jellyfin_resolver.py::TestModeBDirectFallback`` (+ ``test_emby_resolver.py`` + ``test_plex_resolver.py``) cover Mode B Tier-1 match, Tier-2 match, no-match diagnostic, ambiguous-skip diagnostic, Tier-3 fuzzy explicitly disabled (Mode A control + Mode B control), and the ``mode=direct_fallback`` log marker. Jellyfin gets an additional end-to-end test reproducing the PO's exact Tennis Channel scenario with a queue-resolved session ending in a ``MotWakorb`` attribution. Plex gets a bonus EPG-tier-in-Mode-B test confirming the bd-ma6r3 path works without modification when ECM client IP ≠ Plex server IP. Existing IP-mismatch / hostname-non-match tests were retargeted to reflect Mode B's new "cache IS consulted" contract (the old "assert_not_awaited" semantics intentionally no longer hold — that was the bug). Full backend suite + frontend suite stay green. ([bd-ost8o], v0.17.1-0062)
- **Jellyfin attribution: resolve NowPlayingQueue item IDs when NowPlayingItem is absent + comprehensive enrichment forensic logging (bd-dok7u, v0.17.1-0061).** Third attempt at the PO's "Jellyfin shows User #0" — the prior two (bd-f68c8, bd-r5f0c.11) were resolver-tier fixes that compiled cleanly against the brief but did not reach the production data shape. Live API probe against the PO's container (this PR's investigation step) showed that the PO's Jellyfin Web 10.11.8 sessions are reported with ``IsActive=True``, ``NowPlayingItem`` absent entirely, and ``NowPlayingQueue=[{Id: <channel-id>, PlaylistItemId: "playlistItem0"}]``. No combination of resolver-tier work could fix this; the resolver had no name / number / channel data to match against. The channel data is reachable via a second API call: ``GET /Users/{user_id}/Items/{item_id}`` returns the Live TV channel item including ``Name`` (e.g. ``"| ABC: WBAY Green Bay"``), ``ChannelNumber`` (``"2.1"``), and ``Type=TvChannel``. The user-scoped path is required — the unscoped ``/Items/{id}`` returns 400 for ``Type=TvChannel`` items.

  **`backend/jellyfin_client.py`**: ``JellyfinSession`` gains ``now_playing_queue_item_id: str | None`` (default ``None``). ``_map_session`` extracts ``NowPlayingQueue[0].Id`` when the queue is non-empty. New method ``JellyfinClient.get_user_item(user_id, item_id)`` calls ``GET /Users/{user_id}/Items/{item_id}`` and returns the raw dict on 200 or ``None`` on any failure (empty input, non-200, network error) — best-effort by design so the fallback never raises into the resolver hot path.

  **`backend/services/jellyfin_cache.py`**: after ``get_sessions()`` returns, new helper ``_resolve_queue_items`` walks the list and for any session whose ``now_playing_item_name`` is empty AND ``now_playing_queue_item_id`` is populated, calls ``get_user_item`` and rebuilds the session via ``dataclasses.replace`` with ``Name`` → ``now_playing_item_name``, ``ChannelName`` → ``now_playing_channel_name``, ``ChannelNumber`` (or ``Number``) → ``channel_number``. Sessions with NowPlayingItem already populated skip the lookup (zero extra round-trips for working clients). Lookup failure passes the session through unchanged so the resolver's existing no-match path takes over — the pre-bd-dok7u behavior is the worst-case here, not a regression. One INFO summary line per cache miss reports ``NowPlayingQueue fallback resolved <succeeded>/<attempted> sessions`` for production observability.

  **Choice A vs B vs C** (per brief): implemented **Option A** (alternative endpoint). The ``/Sessions?activeWithinSeconds=N`` and ``/Sessions/Playing`` endpoints were probed live; ``?activeWithinSeconds=`` returned ``[]`` for the PO's setup (PlayState reports the sessions as not actively playing even when ``IsActive=True``), and ``/Sessions/Playing`` returns 405 on Jellyfin 10.11.8. ``NowPlayingQueue[0].Id`` was the only signal that survived. Option B (RemoteEndPoint-based IP attribution) would not have known WHICH channel the user was on — only THAT they were active — and the PO's RemoteEndPoint values (``172.16.0.2``, ``192.168.1.61``) are end-user IPs, not the Jellyfin server IP that ECM clients arrive from. Option C (defense-in-depth combining A + B) was rejected because option A is sufficient and the implementation surface is one extra HTTP call per stale-NowPlayingItem session per cache miss (5-second TTL) — at PO scale that is 0-2 extra calls per 5 s.

  **Forensic logging — `backend/services/jellyfin_resolver.py`** (and parallel additions to ``emby_resolver.py`` + ``plex_resolver.py``): every resolver call on the IP-matched + sessions-available path now emits three INFO-level traces so any future "User #0" can be diagnosed from production logs without DEBUG. (1) ``[<SOURCE>-RESOLVER] resolver_call ip=... ecm_channel=... ecm_channel_number=... ecm_stream=... sessions_count=N sessions_with_now_playing=M`` — entry-point snapshot. (2) ``[<SOURCE>-RESOLVER] session_inspect user=... has_now_playing=<bool> item_name=... channel_name=... channel_number=... last_activity=... [queue_item_id=...]`` — one line per cached session exposing exactly what the matcher sees. (3) ``[<SOURCE>-RESOLVER] tier_match ecm_channel=... match_count=N top_user=... top_user_id=...`` — fires only on successful match (parallel to the existing no-match WARN, so the success path is no longer silent). IP-mismatch short-circuits remain DEBUG-only (hot path called on every non-source IP — INFO there would flood the log). **`backend/routers/stats.py`**: ``_enrich_channels_with_attribution`` adds ``[STATS-ENRICH] channel_trace`` (one line per channel — ``decision=proceed|skip:no_data|skip:no_clients``) + ``[STATS-ENRICH] source_dispatch`` (one line per proceeding channel — ``emby_enabled=<bool> plex_enabled=<bool> jellyfin_enabled=<bool>``). Operators can now distinguish "Jellyfin disabled" from "Jellyfin enabled but no match" from "channel short-circuited before resolver" in one log read.

  **Expected production log sequence after deploy** (PO's exact channel 2.1 scenario):
  ```
  [JELLYFIN] NowPlayingQueue fallback resolved 1/1 sessions (bd-dok7u)
  [STATS-ENRICH] channel_trace ch_id=c0c84148... stream_name='WI | Green Bay | ABC 2 WBAY' channel_name='2.1 | ABC: WBAY Green Bay' channel_number=None client_ips=['<jellyfin-server-ip>'] decision=proceed (bd-dok7u)
  [STATS-ENRICH] source_dispatch ch_id=c0c84148... emby_enabled=True plex_enabled=True jellyfin_enabled=True (bd-dok7u)
  [JELLYFIN-RESOLVER] resolver_call ip=<jellyfin-server-ip> ecm_channel='2.1 | ABC: WBAY Green Bay' ecm_channel_number=None ecm_stream='WI | Green Bay | ABC 2 WBAY' sessions_count=2 sessions_with_now_playing=2 (bd-dok7u)
  [JELLYFIN-RESOLVER] session_inspect user=MotWakorb active_session_id=b2d89d65 has_now_playing=True item_name='| ABC: WBAY Green Bay' channel_name=None channel_number='2.1' last_activity=2026-05-19T02:33:36Z queue_item_id=2b0159285ef4370ec3c8c91534bc076d
  [JELLYFIN-RESOLVER] tier_match ecm_channel='2.1 | ABC: WBAY Green Bay' ecm_stream='WI | Green Bay | ABC 2 WBAY' match_count=1 top_user=MotWakorb top_user_id=72be7fac4ca046cf8198573ad285c963 (bd-dok7u)
  ```

  **Tests:** 8 new tests in ``backend/tests/unit/test_jellyfin_client.py`` cover NowPlayingQueue capture (present / absent / empty list), ``get_user_item`` happy path + non-200 + network error + empty-input short-circuit. 6 new tests in ``backend/tests/services/test_jellyfin_cache.py::TestNowPlayingQueueFallback`` cover the PO's exact production shape (queue-only session resolves to ``"| ABC: WBAY Green Bay"`` + ``channel_number="2.1"``), already-populated sessions skip the lookup (zero extra round-trips), idle sessions with no queue pass through, lookup failure passes through unchanged, multi-session independent resolution (one succeeds, one fails — both surface), INFO summary line emitted. 6 new tests in ``backend/tests/services/test_jellyfin_resolver.py::TestForensicInfoLogging`` cover the three INFO trace shapes — entry-point resolver_call payload, per-session session_inspect payload, tier_match on success, absence on no-match, queue_item_id surfaced in session_inspect, IP-mismatch hot path stays DEBUG-only. 5 new tests in ``backend/tests/routers/test_stats_attribution.py::TestEnrichmentForensicLogging`` cover the two STATS-ENRICH trace shapes — channel_trace decision=proceed, decision=skip:no_data, decision=skip:no_clients, source_dispatch emitted only on proceed. Total: 25 new backend tests. Full suite: 4448 backend tests + 1 skipped + 1492 frontend tests stay green.

  **bd-f68c8 retrospective**: the prior fix (Tier-2 ECM pipe-prefix parse) is correct as additive defense-in-depth and should NOT be reverted — it covers the divergent-name-but-matching-number case for all three sources and the bd-dok7u fix is structurally orthogonal (one operates at the JellyfinSession data layer, the other at the tier-comparison layer). Keeping both means future Jellyfin attribution survives both NowPlayingItem-absent AND name-divergence failure modes. ([bd-dok7u], v0.17.1-0061)
- **Stats: Connected Clients header counts actual upstream viewers, not ECM-side proxy connections (bd-w9c2a, v0.17.1-0060).** PO was watching a channel with 1 Dispatcharr XC client ('Hap') + 1 Emby-proxy connection carrying 2 deduped Emby users ('MotWakorb' + 'jkaisersoze') — 3 humans total — and the Active Channels card's Connected Clients section header read "Connected Clients (2)". Root cause: `frontend/src/components/tabs/StatsTab.tsx` rendered the count as `channel.clients.length`, which is the ECM-side connection count. The transcoding-proxy pattern (Emby / Plex / Jellyfin server fans out one upstream connection back to ECM that carries N viewers) was undercounted because the W9 / bd-r5f0c.9 per-client `emby_viewers[]` / `plex_viewers[]` / `jellyfin_viewers[]` payloads weren't being read at the count site. The per-client renderer already used those payloads correctly (each viewer surfaces with an AttributionBadge), and the channel-header `(N viewers)` rollup at lines 911-929 already aggregated them — only the Connected Clients section header was stale. **Fix:** the count expression now reduces over `channel.clients` and adds `Math.max(emby_viewers?.length ?? 0, plex_viewers?.length ?? 0, jellyfin_viewers?.length ?? 0)` per client (or 1 when all three source lists are empty/absent, preserving the pure-Dispatcharr semantic). MAX-across-sources rather than SUM-across-sources because a single ECM client carrying viewers from two source servers concurrently would imply the same humans being visible to two resolvers at once (rare; would double-count). Matches the per-client renderer's source-precedence semantic (emby > plex > jellyfin in lookup order). **Out of scope (verified):** the top-level "Connected Clients" summary stat at `StatsTab.tsx:751` reads `totalClients = sum(channel.client_count)` — a different aggregate (cross-channel sum of backend-supplied connection counts, not per-channel `clients.length`).
