That metric was NOT changed in this bead; if the PO wants the top-level summary to also reflect upstream viewers, file a follow-up because the fix would also need to touch the backend `client_count` provenance. **Tests:** 4 new tests in `frontend/src/components/tabs/StatsTab.test.tsx::StatsTab — Connected Clients header viewer count (bd-w9c2a)` lock the contract — PO's exact scenario (1 dispatcharr + 1 emby-proxy with 2 viewers → header reads "(3)"), boundary (1 client with 5 emby_viewers → "(5)"), pure-Dispatcharr regression (2 dispatcharr-only clients → "(2)"), and the mixed-source MAX precedence (1 client with 2 emby_viewers + 1 plex_viewers → "(2)" via MAX, not "(3)" via SUM). Per-client rendering (lines 1182-1310), channel-header rollup (lines 902-929), backend enrichment, and bandwidth_tracker are UNCHANGED. ([bd-w9c2a], v0.17.1-0060)
- **Jellyfin / Emby / Plex Live TV attribution: parse ECM channel_name pipe-prefix as Tier-2 channel-number candidate (bd-f68c8, v0.17.1-0059).** PO was watching ATSC sub-channel ``2.1 | ABC: WBAY Green Bay`` via Jellyfin and ECM Stats showed "User #0" instead of "MotWakorb via Jellyfin". Investigation surfaced a parallel-pattern gap in all three media-source resolvers: Tier-2 (channel-number exact match) was gated on the caller supplying a non-None ``ecm_channel_number``, but Dispatcharr does not always push a separate ``channel_number`` field for ATSC sub-channels — the number is carried in the channel_name itself as the ``"<number> | <rest>"`` pipe-prefix. With ``ecm_channel_number=None``, Tier-2 short-circuited; with Jellyfin's ``NowPlayingItem.Name="| ABC: WBAY Green Bay"`` (Jellyfin strips the channel-number prefix from the name for live-TV) the resolver still had the session's ``channel_number="2.1"`` available — but no way to reach it. **`backend/services/jellyfin_resolver.py`** / **`emby_resolver.py`** / **`plex_resolver.py`**: each resolver gains a ``_parse_numeric_pipe_prefix(ecm_channel_name)`` helper that extracts the left-hand side of ``"<number> | <rest>"`` when (and only when) it parses as a non-negative number (integer or ATSC ``major.sub`` form). Word-prefixed channel names like ``"News | CNN"`` are explicitly rejected so they cannot collide with arbitrary session ``channel_number`` strings. A second helper ``_coerce_tier2_number(ecm_channel_number, ecm_channel_name)`` centralizes precedence: caller-supplied ``ecm_channel_number`` always wins; otherwise the helper falls back to the parsed prefix. Tier-2 now compares ``_coerce_tier2_number(...)`` against ``session.channel_number`` (Emby/Jellyfin — dedicated field) or ``_parse_pipe_prefix(item_name)`` (Plex — session number lives in the item-name pipe-prefix). The bd-r5f0c.11 Tier-1 ECM-side pipe-suffix tolerance still handles the case where session-side and ECM-side names share a suffix; the bd-f68c8 Tier-2 path adds redundancy for cases where the names diverge but the numbers line up. **Diagnostic note**: with the PO's documented data shape (``ecm_channel_name='2.1 | ABC: WBAY Green Bay'`` + session ``item_name='| ABC: WBAY Green Bay'`` + session ``channel_number='2.1'``), the existing bd-r5f0c.11 Tier-1 logic already produces a match in isolation, so the bd-f68c8 Tier-2 path is additive defense-in-depth rather than the strict root-cause fix the brief described. The Tier-2 prefix path matters whenever name surfaces diverge entirely (Plex Live TV programs vs channel names, etc.) but channel numbers line up. **Tests:** 7 new tests in ``backend/tests/services/test_jellyfin_resolver.py`` (new ``TestEcmPipePrefixAsChannelNumber`` class) cover the PO's exact data shape, a Tier-2-only scenario where Tier-1 cannot help, the ATSC ``"2.1"`` sub-channel format, non-numeric-prefix rejection, mismatched-number rejection, no-pipe-prefix skip, and the explicit-number-wins precedence. 5 parallel tests in ``test_emby_resolver.py`` (same class name) lock the Emby-resolver path. 4 parallel tests in ``test_plex_resolver.py`` lock the Plex item-name-pipe-prefix path. Full suite: 4424 backend tests + 1 skipped pass (38 → 54 net new tests since v0.17.1-0058's count; bd-f68c8 adds 16). ([bd-f68c8], v0.17.1-0059)
- **Stats attribution: per-IP scoping for per-client emby/plex/jellyfin enrichment + channel-header dedup (bd-cat70, v0.17.1-0057).** Two connected post-v0.17.1-0056 stats-tab attribution bugs. **Bug #3 (correctness — read-side broadcast):** `_enrich_one_source` in `backend/routers/stats.py` broadcast a single matched-IP resolver hit onto ALL clients in `ch["clients"]` and then `return`ed on the first match. The bd-5kbyf assumption "every client of a source-mediated stream comes from the source server's IP" only holds when the channel is purely source-mediated; for the PO's actual mixed scenario (one Emby-mediated viewer + one direct Dispatcharr XC viewer on the same channel) the second client was mis-attributed to the first client's resolved Emby user. DB-layer attribution (`SessionTelemetry`) was already per-client-correct via `BandwidthTracker`; this was strictly a read-side enrichment bug. **Fix:** the loop now (a) iterates every IP rather than returning on first match, (b) stamps `client[user_name_key]` / `client[viewers_key]` only on clients whose `ip_address` matches the iteration's IP, and (c) sets `ch[user_name_key]` / `ch[viewers_key]` only from the first match (channel-level fields keep the "first match wins" back-compat contract while per-client fields are correctly per-IP). **Bug #1 (visual dedup — frontend):** the Active Channels channel header rendered the single-viewer name and the legacy `emby_user_name` fallback as `(watching: <name>)` next to the stream badge, duplicating the same name already shown in the Connected Clients section with the AttributionBadge. **Fix:** `frontend/src/components/tabs/StatsTab.tsx` channel-header viewer block now only renders the multi-viewer `(N viewers)` rollup (when `totalViewers > 1`); the single-viewer name and the legacy `emby_user_name` fallback branches are removed. The per-client display in Connected Clients is unchanged. **Tests:** 2 new backend tests in `backend/tests/routers/test_stats_emby.py` lock the per-IP contract — mixed-IP channel where only the Emby-server-IP client resolves: that client carries `emby_user_name="MotWakorb"` while the other client keeps `emby_user_name=None`; same scoping applied to the W5 `emby_viewers` list (matched client gets the payload, non-matched client keeps `[]`). 2 frontend tests in `StatsTab.test.tsx` updated to assert the channel-header viewer suffix is ABSENT for the single-viewer case (replacing the legacy "(watching: alice)" / "(watching: OnlyUser)" assertions that locked the now-removed markup). All 67 stats router tests + 30 StatsTab tests stay green. **Bug #2 deferred** — the bd-2zcvf assumption that `@grandparentTitle` carries the Plex channel name is wrong (it's the PROGRAM name); resolving Plex Live TV → channel requires a new EPG cross-reference tier against `/tv.plex.providers.epg.xmltv:<dvr>/sections/<id>/all?type=4` where `<Media>` elements carry `channelCallSign` / `channelIdentifier`. Filed as bd-ma6r3 (dependent on bd-cat70) with the full API exploration and proposed design captured in that bead. ([bd-cat70], v0.17.1-0057)
- **Plex Live TV attribution: extract channel name from `@grandparentTitle` + `@parentTitle` so the Plex resolver can match (bd-2zcvf, v0.17.1-0056).** PO was watching Plex channel 2.1 via their Plex DVR and ECM Stats showed "User #0" for the client row instead of the Plex username. The strong starting hypothesis (W11 ECM-side pipe-prefix tolerance missing from `plex_resolver.py`) was wrong on inspection — that fix was already in place. The real bug was one layer above the resolver: `plex_client._map_item` only extracted `<Video>/@title`, which for Plex Live TV from a DVR/Tuner is the PROGRAM currently airing (e.g., `"Saturday Night Live"`) — NOT the channel. Plex puts the operator-visible channel name on `<Video>/@grandparentTitle` (and sometimes `@parentTitle` for alternate DVR shapes that synthesize a per-channel "show"). With only `@title` available, no resolver tier could match an operator's ECM `channel_name` against the program title; the resolver fell through to empty; `plex_user_name` stayed NULL; the frontend display chain fell all the way to `\`User #${client.user_id}\`` with `user_id=0`. Emby + Jellyfin were already structurally immune because their session dataclasses extract a dedicated `now_playing_channel_name` from `NowPlayingItem.ChannelName` — Plex was the missing parallel structure. **`backend/plex_client.py`**: `PlexSession` dataclass gains `now_playing_channel_name` (from `@grandparentTitle`) and `now_playing_parent_title` (from `@parentTitle`). `_map_item` extracts both — no schema cost, both default to `None` for VOD / movies and Plex versions that don't set them. **`backend/services/plex_resolver.py`**: tier-1 now compares `ecm_channel_name` against all three candidate session fields (item, channel, parent), each considered both whole and as the right-hand part of a `"<number> | <name>"` pipe-prefix. bd-r5f0c.11's ECM-side pipe-prefix tolerance applies symmetrically to the new candidates (the `_match_against_candidates` helper centralizes the comparison matrix so the three fields don't triplicate the whole/suffix/ECM-side-suffix logic). Tier-3 fuzzy fallback also now compares `ecm_stream_name` against all three candidates — parallels Emby's tier-3 which already compares against both `item_name` and `channel_name`. Tier-2 is unchanged (the pipe-prefix on Plex's `@grandparentTitle` / `@parentTitle` carries no numeric prefix in practice; only `item_name`'s pipe-prefix is examined). The `_log_plex_resolver_no_match` payload is extended to surface `channel_name` and `parent_title` (raw + normalized + pipe-suffix forms) so future no-match forensics expose the new fields without a follow-up instrumentation pass. **Tests:** new `TestTier1LiveTvChannelMatch` class in `backend/tests/services/test_plex_resolver.py` covers 6 cases — grandparent-title whole match, grandparent + ECM-side pipe-prefix, parent_title fallback, grandparent pipe-suffix on Plex side, regression case where new fields are `None` (pre-bd-2zcvf shape stays green), defensive no-match-on-any-field. New `TestTier3FuzzyFallbackLiveTv` class (2 cases — grandparent fuzzy, parent fuzzy). New diagnostic-payload assertion that asserts the new fields appear in the WARN payload. `backend/tests/unit/test_plex_client.py` gains an assertion on the existing pipe-prefix fixture that `now_playing_channel_name="ESPN"` (from `grandparentTitle="ESPN"` which was already in the fixture) lands on the dataclass, plus a new `test_get_sessions_live_tv_program_in_title_with_channel_in_grandparent` regression target that locks the program-in-title shape with channel-on-grandparent. New fixture `sessions_live_tv_program_in_title.xml`. All 4368 backend tests + 1 skipped + 1488 frontend tests stay green. Emby + Jellyfin resolvers + clients UNTOUCHED — they already had the parallel structure via the dedicated `ChannelName` extraction. The frontend display chain was correct already; no frontend changes ship in this bead. ([bd-2zcvf], v0.17.1-0056)
- **Per-client Emby attribution: surface emby_user_name in Connected Clients list (bd-5kbyf, fix-forward for v0.17.1-0035).** Active Channels header showed `(watching: MotWakorb)` correctly (channel-level `emby_user_name` from bd-fm23o was working), but the per-client list below still rendered "User #0" for Emby-mediated streams. Root cause: `_enrich_channels_with_emby` in `backend/routers/stats.py` only set `emby_user_name` on the channel dict, never propagating it to the entries in `ch["clients"]`. The frontend's `client-user` render path read `client.username` (NULL for Emby-mediated streams) and fell back to `User #${client.user_id}`. **`backend/routers/stats.py`**: `_enrich_channels_with_emby` now propagates the resolved `attribution.user_name` to every client dict in `ch["clients"]` immediately after setting `ch["emby_user_name"]`. All short-circuit paths (Emby disabled, settings-access error, no-tier-args, no-client-IPs, resolver-returns-None) also seed `client["emby_user_name"] = None` on each client so the field is consistently present on the wire regardless of resolution outcome — no undefined keys for TypeScript to stumble on. **`frontend/src/types/index.ts`**: `StreamClient` interface gains `emby_user_name?: string | null`. **`frontend/src/components/tabs/StatsTab.tsx`**: the per-client `client-user` render block now prefers `client.emby_user_name` over `client.username`, with a `badge attribution-source-badge` "via Emby" badge (same class as the UserStatsPanel badge from bd-fm23o) when the Emby name is present, falling back to `username` or `User #${user_id}` otherwise. **Tests:** 3 new backend integration tests in `backend/tests/routers/test_stats_emby.py` lock the per-client propagation contract (emby_user_name on every client when resolver matches; emby_user_name=null on every client when resolver returns None; empty clients[] handles cleanly without crash); 3 new frontend tests in `frontend/src/components/tabs/StatsTab.test.tsx` lock the render logic (emby_user_name rendered with "via Emby" badge; fallback to username without badge; fallback to User #id when both names null). ([bd-5kbyf], fix-forward for v0.17.1-0035)
- **Emby resolver: channel-name primary match (bd-zldrq, fix-forward for v0.17.1-0033 live test).** Live operator test on v0.17.1-0033 surfaced the Emby attribution chain returning "User #0" instead of "MotWakorb (via Emby)" for live-TV sessions on channel 408. Root cause: Dispatcharr stream names like `"US: ESPN FHD"` do not RapidFuzz token_set_ratio above the 0.85 floor against Emby's live-TV `NowPlayingItem.Name` format `"408 | ESPN"` — the only overlapping token is "ESPN". Confirmed via live `/Sessions` capture from the operator's container: `NowPlayingItem.ChannelNumber='408'`, `Name='408 | ESPN'`, `Type='TvChannel'`, `MediaType='Video'`, `ChannelName=None` (Emby uses `Name` for the live-TV display string and leaves `ChannelName` empty even on TV channels — so the prior fallback to `now_playing_channel_name` produced nothing). **`backend/emby_client.py`**: `EmbySession` dataclass gains `channel_number: str | None = None`, extracted from `NowPlayingItem.ChannelNumber` in `_map_session`. The field preserves Emby's raw string type (no int-cast) so sub-channel numbers like `"408.1"` survive intact. Defaults to `None` for backwards compatibility with any pre-existing dataclass construction in tests. **`backend/services/emby_resolver.py`**: `resolve_emby_user` signature extends to `(ecm_session_ip, ecm_stream_name, ecm_channel_name=None, ecm_channel_number=None)`; the two new args are keyword-only-by-convention but accepted positionally for back-compat. New tiered matching algorithm replaces the single fuzzy pass — **Tier 1 (channel_name primary)**: parse each Emby session's `now_playing_item_name` as `"<number> | <name>"` (split on `|`, strip whitespace) and case-insensitively compare the right-hand part to `ecm_channel_name`; whole-string equality also counts (some Emby installs surface the channel name without the `"<number> | "` prefix). **Tier 2 (channel_number exact)**: when both `ecm_channel_number` and the Emby session's `channel_number` are populated, string-compare (cast ECM input to `str`); defensive against name divergence between ECM and Emby. **Tier 3 (fuzzy stream_name fallback)**: the legacy RapidFuzz `token_set_ratio` path against `now_playing_item_name` OR `now_playing_channel_name` with the 0.85 threshold — still correct for non-live-TV content (movies, episodes) where channel args are absent. Matches across tiers pool into one disambiguation set (de-duped by session identity); the most-recent `last_activity_date` wins as before. When N > 1 candidates resolve, a `[EMBY] resolver: %d candidates for ip=%s name=%s, picked %s by recency` DEBUG line surfaces the tie-break so operators see disambiguation in trace. **Hot-path discipline** — Emby strings are normalized (NFC + lowercase + strip) ONCE per `_find_matching_sessions` call into a prepared list, NOT re-normalized inside each per-tier loop; a busy poll with N channels × M sessions × 3 tiers would otherwise re-normalize the same Emby strings 3×N×M times. The IP-mismatch short-circuit (load-bearing optimization for non-Emby-mediated sessions) is unchanged, and the resolver still never raises. **`backend/bandwidth_tracker.py`**: `_collect_stats` adds `channel_name` to the `telemetry_channel_snapshot` entries (the value was already computed locally for `channel_bandwidth_updates` — this is a one-key add, not a new lookup). `_resolve_emby_attributions` extracts `channel_name` and `channel_number` from each snapshot entry and forwards them to `resolve_emby_user` as kwargs. The skip-when-empty guard widens from "stream_name empty" to "every tier args empty" so the live-TV path doesn't drop when `provider_resolution.stream_name` is None but the channel args are populated. **`backend/routers/stats.py`** (operator-visible surface — the "User #0" badge fix): `_enrich_channels_with_emby` pulls `channel_name` and `channel_number` off each Active Channels entry and passes them as the new resolver kwargs. Without this the Active Channels live view's "(watching: ...)" badge would continue to fall through to fuzzy tier-3 and fail for live TV, even with the BandwidthTracker side fixed. **Tests:** `backend/tests/services/test_emby_resolver.py` gains 8 new tests (tier-1 hits on `"408 | ESPN"`, tier-1 hits without the pipe prefix, tier-1 case-insensitive, tier-2 string match, tier-2 skip when session has no number, tier-3 still works for movies, tier-3 still works when channel args present but no match, multi-match tiebreak across new tiers, DEBUG log fires when N > 1 candidates); `backend/tests/unit/test_emby_client.py` gains 1 new test (VOD playback with no ChannelNumber → `channel_number is None`) plus updated assertions on the canned-payload mapping so the live-TV `"408 | ESPN"` + `ChannelNumber: "408"` shape is locked; `backend/tests/unit/test_bandwidth_tracker_emby.py` gains 1 new test pinning the wiring contract (resolver receives `ecm_channel_name="ESPN"` + `ecm_channel_number=408` kwargs); `backend/tests/routers/test_stats_emby.py` gains 1 new test pinning the same contract on the Active Channels endpoint surface. Existing mocks were updated to accept `**kwargs` so back-compat call shapes still work. All 4079 backend tests + 1 skipped (the volume test) pass. Disabled-Emby short-circuit and resolver-never-raises contracts are preserved. ([bd-zldrq], fix-forward for v0.17.1-0033 live test bug)

### Added
- **Emby usernames in Stats endpoints + UI — FINAL bead of EPIC bd-2cenq (bd-fm23o).** Closes the Emby user-attribution epic shipped over bd-6c0g6 → bd-8wc6q → bd-gpeot → bd-k026g → bd-6802c → bd-gih6d by surfacing the captured `session_telemetry.emby_user_name` on the operator-visible Stats read APIs and in the Stats tab. Once this lands, v0.17.1 can cut. **Backend `routers/stats.py`:** `/api/stats/watch-time` (`group_by=total` and `group_by=day`) now denormalizes `emby_user_name` alongside `dispatcharr_username` via two new helpers (`_pick_display_name_and_source`, `_build_watch_time_row_total`, `_build_watch_time_row_day`) — Emby wins over Dispatcharr at the per-user-aggregation level (defensive `MAX(emby_user_name)` on the existing `_distinct_poll_subquery` so multi-client polls collapse correctly) and the new `attribution_source: "emby" | "dispatcharr"` discriminator surfaces the chain so the frontend can render a "via Emby" badge. `/api/stats/channels` (Active Channels live view) calls the new `_enrich_channels_with_emby(channels)` async helper after the bd-ox5q8 stream-identity enrichment — gated on `settings.emby_enabled` (short-circuits with `None` per channel when disabled), iterates each channel's clients, and calls `services.emby_resolver.resolve_emby_user(client_ip, stream_name)` per client; the first non-`None` attribution wins and populates `channel.emby_user_name`. Three new routes implement the PO-decided URL-split for per-user breakdowns: `/api/stats/users/dispatcharr/{user_id}` (source-prefixed alias for the legacy `/watch-time/{user_id}` behavior), `/api/stats/users/emby/{emby_user_id}` (per-channel breakdown filtered by `session_telemetry.emby_user_id` — the new identity axis the epic was built to expose), and `/api/stats/users/{user_id}` as a back-compat deprecated alias that routes to the dispatcharr behavior with a per-call WARN log so operators / log analysis can see if any consumer is still using it. The shared aggregation logic lives in `_per_user_breakdown_by_channel(db, *, source, identifier, from_, to)` with a parallel `_load_latest_stream_identity_per_emby_user` side-loader so the Emby-source endpoint renders the same `latest_stream_id`/`latest_stream_name` cells (MAX-observed-at-per-channel) as the Dispatcharr-source endpoint. The deprecation marker is comment-only — removal is not yet scheduled. **Frontend `types/index.ts`:** new `AttributionSource = "emby" | "dispatcharr"` type; `WatchTimeUserTotalRow` and `WatchTimeUserDayRow` gain a required `attribution_source` field; `ChannelStats` gains an optional `emby_user_name`. **`UserStatsPanel.tsx`:** the user totals table renders a `(badge attribution-source-badge)` "via Emby" badge alongside the username when `row.attribution_source === 'emby'` (shared `.badge` class from `shared/common.css`; `UserStatsPanel.css` adds only an 8px left-margin scoped to the new class so the visual style stays consistent with every other badge in the app). **`StatsTab.tsx`:** the Active Channels card renders a `(watching: <emby_user>)` badge next to the existing `[Provider] - Stream` stream-name badge whenever `channel.emby_user_name` is non-null — purely additive to the bd-ox5q8 row layout. **Tests:** 12 new backend integration tests in `backend/tests/routers/test_stats_emby.py` lock the full read-API contract (watch-time Emby precedence + attribution_source on both group_by modes; Active Channels emby_user_name on resolver match / on resolver miss / on disabled-Emby short-circuit; dispatcharr/{id} renamed route; emby/{id} filtering correctness + empty-identifier returns empty envelope; deprecated alias parity with dispatcharr/{id} + WARN-log emission); 2 new frontend tests in `UserStatsPanel.test.tsx` (badge renders when attribution_source=emby; badge absent when dispatcharr); 2 new frontend tests in `StatsTab.test.tsx` (Active Channels emby-viewer badge renders when populated; absent when null). All 4066 backend tests + 1427 frontend tests stay green. **EPIC bd-2cenq closed** with this bead — operators with Emby-mediated streams now see real Emby usernames in Stats > User Watch Time instead of the proxy/API account, and the per-channel "(watching: ...)" badge in Active Channels completes the live attribution chain. ([bd-fm23o], FINAL bead of EPIC [bd-2cenq])
- **BandwidthTracker Emby attribution wiring (bd-gih6d, epic bd-2cenq).** Wires the bd-6802c cross-ref resolver into `backend/bandwidth_tracker.py` so `session_telemetry` rows carry the resolved Emby viewer's identity. **New method `BandwidthTracker._resolve_emby_attributions(telemetry_channel_snapshot, provider_by_channel)`** is called from `_collect_stats` after the bd-skqln.14 provider resolver runs and BEFORE `_write_session_telemetry`; it builds a sparse `{(channel_uuid, client_ip): EmbyAttribution}` map by iterating the per-channel snapshot's `(channel, ip)` pairs and awaiting `services.emby_resolver.resolve_emby_user(client_ip, provider_resolution.stream_name)` for each. **Settings gate optimization** reads `settings.emby_enabled` at the top via a local `from config import get_settings` and short-circuits with an empty map when False — the cache and resolver already short-circuit on disabled, but skipping the per-(channel, ip) loop, the per-call settings access, and the resolver function-call overhead on the common disabled-Emby install is the cheap optimization the bead spec explicitly calls out. **Stream-name skip** also avoids the resolver call when `provider_resolution.stream_name` is None (the cache match is name-based so the resolver would short-circuit inside anyway — skipping at the call site saves the function-call overhead). **Failure handling** wraps each resolver call AND the settings read in try/except: any exception (resolver is documented to never raise, but a future refactor or unforeseen runtime fault like cache failure or settings access raise must not block telemetry writes) collapses to a single rate-limited `[BANDWIDTH] [EMBY]` WARN via the new module-level `_log_emby_resolver_failure(exc)` helper. The rate-limit window is **60 seconds** anchored on a module-level `time.monotonic()` timestamp (`_emby_resolver_last_warn_at`) so multiple resolver failures within one busy poll cycle — and across poll cycles inside the window — collapse to ONE WARN line, preserving the operational signal-to-noise ratio. The module-level (not instance-level) timestamp means multi-tracker test seams share the same clock and the failed-resolver row still writes with NULL Emby columns. **`_write_session_telemetry` signature extended** with a keyword-only `emby_attributions: Optional[dict[tuple[str, str], EmbyAttribution]] = None` parameter (default empty for back-compat with older test seams); the per-row construction looks up `emby_attributions.get((channel_uuid, ip))` and passes `attribution.user_id` / `attribution.user_name` (or `None` when missing) to the `SessionTelemetry(...)` constructor's new `emby_user_id` / `emby_user_name` kwargs from migration 0016. Both columns move together — the dataclass returns them atomically, and a future split into a separate `emby_users` table can migrate this writer in one place. **The hot-path discipline mandate** "Telemetry write must NEVER block on Emby failure" is enforced by the try/except wrapper AND by keeping the resolver call entirely OUTSIDE the SQLAlchemy session that owns the write — a resolver raise cannot poison the session commit, and the row still lands with NULL Emby columns regardless of which IP / channel pair triggered the failure. **Tests:** 7 new tests in `backend/tests/unit/test_bandwidth_tracker_emby.py` lock the full behavioral matrix — (1) resolver returns `EmbyAttribution` → row has both Emby columns populated; (2) resolver returns `None` → row has both Emby columns NULL; (3) `settings.emby_enabled = False` → resolver mock asserts NOT awaited and rows have both Emby columns NULL; (4) resolver raises `EmbyClientError` → row STILL writes with NULL Emby columns AND exactly ONE `[BANDWIDTH] [EMBY]` WARN is emitted; (5) rate-limit guard: 6 consecutive failures across one busy poll cycle collapse to exactly ONE WARN; (6) end-to-end realistic scenario: Dispatcharr poll with one client whose IP is the Emby server's IP + matching cached Emby session → telemetry row carries the resolved Emby attribution alongside the bd-skqln/bd-kh23e Stats v2 fields (`channel_id`, `stream_id`, `stream_name`); (7) sparse-map per-IP attribution: two clients on one channel where only one resolves → matching client's row gets the attribution, non-matching client's row keeps NULL Emby columns without spilling another row's attribution. The Stats endpoints (bd-fm23o) are intentionally NOT touched in this bead — surfacing Emby usernames in `Stats > User Watch Time` ships as the next wave. ([bd-gih6d])
- **Emby cross-ref resolver: ECM stream → Emby user (bd-6802c, epic bd-2cenq).** New `backend/services/emby_resolver.py` answers the load-bearing user-attribution question for the v0.17.x Emby epic: given an ECM stream session's client IP and stream name, which Emby user — if any — is the actual viewer? Without this cross-reference Stats > User Watch Time collapses every Emby viewer into a single proxy-IP identity. **Module-level `async resolve_emby_user(ecm_session_ip, ecm_stream_name) -> EmbyAttribution | None`** is the only public entry point and consumes the bd-gpeot `get_cached_emby_sessions()` cache (NEVER instantiates `EmbyClient` directly — going around the cache would defeat the thundering-herd guard, stale-fallback, and settings gate). **Algorithm:** (1) extract Emby server IP from `settings.emby_base_url` via `urlparse(...).hostname` — IP literals (`http://192.168.1.10:8096`) are used verbatim, hostnames (`https://emby.local:8920`) are resolved once via `socket.gethostbyname` and cached module-level (a successful resolution is cached as the IP string, a `socket.gaierror` failure is cached as the `_UNRESOLVED` sentinel so the WARN log fires exactly once per process and subsequent polls short-circuit cheaply); IP-literal detection uses `socket.inet_pton` for both AF_INET and AF_INET6 rather than naive dot-counting; (2) if the ECM session IP does not equal the Emby server IP, return `None` BEFORE the cache call — every poll cycle hits this branch for every non-Emby-mediated session, so the short-circuit is the load-bearing optimization; (3) fetch cached Emby sessions (the cache transparently handles the disabled-Emby case by returning `[]`, so the resolver does not need to inspect `emby_enabled`); (4) for each session, score `ecm_stream_name` against BOTH `now_playing_item_name` AND `now_playing_channel_name` (live-TV sessions populate channel_name, VOD populates only item_name, idle sessions populate neither) — exact match after NFC + lowercase + strip normalization wins immediately, else RapidFuzz `fuzz.token_set_ratio / 100` against the bead-mandated `FUZZY_MATCH_THRESHOLD = 0.85`; (5) zero matches → `None`, one match → `EmbyAttribution(user_id, user_name)`, multiple matches (rare — same channel on multiple Emby clients) → tie-break on most-recent `last_activity_date` (Emby ISO timestamps sort lexicographically the same as chronologically; `None` timestamps lose to any populated value via the `or ""` fallback in the key function). **`EmbyAttribution` is a frozen dataclass** with `user_id` and `user_name` so callers cannot mutate the attribution between the resolver returning it and the BandwidthTracker writing it to `session_telemetry.emby_user_id` / `emby_user_name`. **Never raises** — the BandwidthTracker poll loop calls this on every active session every ~5 seconds, so defensive failures (empty URL, malformed URL, DNS failure) return `None` after a one-shot WARN rather than propagating up. **Normalization is duplicated from `dedup_matcher._normalize`** rather than imported — the two services answer different questions with different threshold policies, and coupling them via a shared helper would couple policies that may evolve independently; the cost of a one-liner duplication is lower than the cost of forcing a shared module on two unrelated callers. **Logging** uses the `[EMBY]` prefix with lazy `%` formatting per `backend/CLAUDE.md`; resolver decisions log at DEBUG (don't spam INFO on every poll), DNS failure logs at WARN exactly once via the cache sentinel. **Settings-disabled short-circuit** is implicitly handled by the cache layer returning `[]`; no explicit `emby_enabled` check in the resolver. **Tests:** 17 new tests in `backend/tests/services/test_emby_resolver.py` lock the full behavioral matrix — IP mismatch returns `None` without a cache call (the load-bearing optimization), exact match on item_name returns attribution, case-insensitive match ("cnn" matches "CNN"), channel_name match when item_name is None (live-TV path), fuzzy match above 0.85 threshold returns attribution, fuzzy match below 0.85 returns None, empty cache returns None (Emby disabled / idle), all-idle sessions return None, multi-match tiebreak by most-recent `last_activity_date`, populated timestamp beats None timestamp, hostname-based base_url resolves to matching IP and attributes, hostname resolving to non-matching IP short-circuits before cache, DNS failure logs WARN with `[EMBY]` prefix and hostname and returns None gracefully, DNS resolution result is cached (3 successive calls fire `gethostbyname` exactly once), empty base_url returns None defensively, malformed base_url returns None defensively. The resolver is now ready for bd-gih6d's BandwidthTracker enrichment wiring; this bead intentionally does NOT touch BandwidthTracker (bd-gih6d) or the Stats endpoints (bd-fm23o). ([bd-6802c])
- **Emby Settings UI + persistence + test-connection endpoint (bd-8wc6q, epic bd-2cenq — operator-facing surface for the v0.17.x Emby user-attribution feature).** Wires the bd-6c0g6 `EmbyClient` into Settings so operators can configure the Emby integration without editing `settings.json` by hand. **`backend/config.py`** adds three new fields to `DispatcharrSettings`: `emby_enabled: bool = False` (operator must opt in), `emby_base_url: str = ""` (no validation — operator's responsibility), and `emby_api_key: str = ""` (PLAINTEXT at rest, same approach as `dispatcharr_api_key` — no encryption in this release). **`backend/routers/settings.py`** plumbs all three fields through the `SettingsRequest` and `SettingsResponse` Pydantic models, the `GET /api/settings` handler (with `emby_api_key_configured` boolean replacing the raw key — never returned), and the `POST /api/settings` handler. The API key uses the same preserve-on-omit contract as `smtp_password` and `mcp_api_key` (bd-vj8n9) — a partial POST that omits `emby_api_key` keeps the stored value, so operators can save the toggle and base URL without re-entering the secret on every save. **New endpoint `POST /api/settings/emby/test-connection`** (admin-only via `RequireAdminIfEnabled`) takes `{base_url, api_key}` inline in the request body — NOT from saved settings, since the operator may be testing BEFORE saving. Constructs an `EmbyClient` per request, calls `get_sessions()`, releases the underlying httpx pool via `finally: await client.close()`, and returns `{ok: true}` on success or `{ok: false, error: <message>}` on `EmbyClientError`. The endpoint deliberately does NOT raise `HTTPException` on connection failure — the operator wants to SEE the error message inline in the UI, not get a generic 500. An unexpected exception class is rendered as `Unexpected error: <ClassName>` without leaking the exception message (mirrors the CodeQL #1415 / bd-m8i9q sanitization posture for `/api/settings/mcp-status`). **Frontend** adds a new **Integrations** settings page (`SettingsPage` type extended in `frontend/src/hooks/useHashRoute.ts`) with the Emby Integration section as its first occupant — distinct from the General page's Dispatcharr connection (the primary upstream, not a side integration) and from Linked Accounts (ECM-internal M3U linking). The section renders an `enabled` checkbox, a `base_url` text input, an `api_key` password input (`type="password"`, masked, `autoComplete="new-password"`), and a Test Connection button. The button calls the new `api.testEmbyConnection(baseUrl, apiKey)` helper with the form-state values and renders `Connection successful` (green) or the backend's error string (red) inline next to the button. Save uses the existing page-level Save button on the Integrations page; the handler only sends `emby_api_key` when the operator has entered a fresh value (matches the backend's preserve-on-omit contract). The API key field on load is blank — only `emby_api_key_configured` reflects whether a key is stored, with the placeholder text switching between `••••••••` and `Paste your Emby API key`. Reuses existing shared CSS (`.settings-section`, `.settings-section-header`, `.form-group-vertical`, `.checkbox-group`, `.btn-secondary`, `.form-hint`, `.badge`, `.badge-success`, `.material-icons`) — no new CSS files. **Tests:** 6 new backend tests in `backend/tests/routers/test_settings.py::TestEmbySettingsPersistence` (GET returns enabled/base_url, GET masks api_key as `emby_api_key_configured`, GET reports unconfigured when no key, POST persists all three fields, POST defaults when fields absent, partial POST preserves the stored API key); 8 new backend tests in `backend/tests/routers/test_emby_settings.py::TestEmbyTestConnection` (success returns ok=true and closes the client, EmbyClientError surfaces as `{ok: false, error: ...}` with the 401 message, network failure surfaces inline, unexpected exception renders only the class name without leaking the message, the endpoint uses inline credentials not saved settings, missing required fields return 422, the client is closed even on error); 4 new backend tests in `backend/tests/unit/test_config_dedup_settings.py` (Emby field defaults and operator overrides); 9 new frontend tests in `frontend/src/components/settings/EmbyIntegrationSection.test.tsx` (section renders on the Integrations page, fields populate from loaded settings with the API key masked, the input is type=password, Test Connection calls the API with form-state values, success message renders inline, backend error renders inline with the verbatim message, blank base_url is rejected client-side without a network call, save persists all three fields when fresh, save omits `emby_api_key` from the payload when the field is blank to honor the preserve-on-omit contract). The bd-gpeot session cache, bd-6802c resolver, and BandwidthTracker enrichment ship as separate beads in the epic — this bead is the configuration substrate they all depend on. ([bd-8wc6q])
- **Emby session cache with TTL + thundering-herd guard (bd-gpeot, epic bd-2cenq).** New `backend/services/emby_cache.py` wraps `EmbyClient.get_sessions()` so the BandwidthTracker poll loop (~5s cadence, bd-gih6d follow-up) and the resolver (bd-6802c follow-up) can read the live Emby session list without pounding the operator's Emby server on every cycle. **Module-level `async get_cached_emby_sessions() -> list[EmbySession]`** is the only public entry point; cache key is the constant `"emby:sessions"` (single-key — Emby is one configured upstream per ECM install) and TTL is 5 seconds (matched to the Dispatcharr poll cadence so the next BandwidthTracker poll sees a fresh snapshot and intermediate readers hit cache). **Settings gate** short-circuits before any cache or client interaction when Emby is disabled or unconfigured: `getattr(settings, "emby_enabled", False)` is False OR `getattr(settings, "emby_base_url", "")` is empty → return `[]` immediately, no `EmbyClient` constructed. The `getattr` defaults defensively handle the pre-bd-8wc6q state where the `DispatcharrSettings` model has not yet declared the `emby_*` fields; once bd-8wc6q lands, the defaults become inert. **Stale-fallback** is the deliberate departure from the generic `cache.py` helper: when the upstream fetch fails AFTER a value was previously cached, the stale list is returned (with a `[EMBY]` WARN naming the failure and the stale age) rather than empty list — a momentary Emby outage should not erase the "who is watching" picture mid-resolver. Cold-start failure (no cache + fetch fails) still returns `[]` (always a list, never `None` to callers) and logs a `[EMBY]` WARN with `"no prior cache"` phrasing so operators can distinguish the two failure modes in logs. Cold-start failure deliberately does NOT poison the cache with `[]` — the next call retries against Emby rather than serving empty for the TTL window. **Thundering-herd prevention** via a lazy-initialized module-level `asyncio.Lock` (constructed on first call so module import is not bound to a running loop): concurrent callers during a cache-miss queue at the lock; the holder fetches once and the waiters re-check the cache under the lock (double-checked locking — without the re-check, every waiter would still fire its own fetch after acquiring the lock, defeating the guard entirely) and return the freshly-cached value. The `EmbyClient` is constructed and closed per fetch (httpx connection pool released on success AND failure via try/finally so a transport error never leaks sockets). Logging uses the `[EMBY]` prefix with lazy `%` formatting per `backend/CLAUDE.md`. Caching is deliberately the only concern here: resolver / matching logic is bd-6802c's domain (next wave), BandwidthTracker wiring is bd-gih6d's domain (next wave), settings fields are bd-8wc6q's domain (parallel). **Tests:** 11 new tests in `backend/tests/services/test_emby_cache.py` lock the bead's full behavioral matrix — (1) cache hit: first call constructs one `EmbyClient` and fires one `get_sessions`, client closed once; (2) cache hit: second call within TTL returns cached value without constructing a new client; (3) TTL expiry: monkey-patched `time.monotonic` past the TTL boundary triggers a refetch and the new value wins; (4) stale-fallback: prior success + later `EmbyClientError` returns the stale list with a WARN containing `"stale"`; (5) cold-start failure: no prior cache + `EmbyClientError` returns `[]` with a WARN containing `"no prior cache"`; (6) cold-start failure does not poison the cache: subsequent successful fetch fires a new upstream call rather than serving an empty cached value; (7) disabled (`emby_enabled=False`) returns `[]` without constructing a client; (8) empty `emby_base_url` treated as disabled (defensive against the bd-8wc6q-not-yet-shipped state); (9) settings model without any `emby_*` attributes treated as disabled (bare-object stand-in for pre-bd-8wc6q `DispatcharrSettings`); (10) disabled short-circuit even with a pre-populated cache (operator disabling Emby after a cache load must NOT surface stale data); (11) thundering-herd: 10 concurrent `asyncio.gather` callers during cold-start fire exactly ONE `EmbyClient` construction and ONE `get_sessions()` call, all 10 receive the same session list — implemented with a real `CountingClient` counter class (not Mock) for deterministic concurrent counting and an `asyncio.Event` gate to hold the in-flight fetch open until all waiters arrive at the lock. ([bd-gpeot])

- **Emby user attribution columns on `session_telemetry` (bd-k026g, parent epic bd-2cenq).** Schema substrate for the Emby user attribution epic — ECM only sees the Dispatcharr stream session's IP, and when users watch via an Emby server ALL stream pulls share that one IP, so Stats cannot distinguish individual Emby viewers without enrichment. **Alembic migration 0016** adds two NULLABLE TEXT columns to `session_telemetry`: `emby_user_id` (TEXT NULL — Emby user IDs are GUIDs, NOT integers like the Dispatcharr `user_id` column) and `emby_user_name` (TEXT NULL — denormalized at write time from the per-poll Emby `/Sessions` lookup the resolver maintains, mirroring the bd-gsn3r `dispatcharr_username` denormalization). Both columns are NULL for non-Emby rows (most rows on most installs — only sessions whose client IP matches the configured Emby server IP AND has a concurrent matching Emby session are enriched). Plain nullable columns, NOT FKs — Emby's user table is upstream and not an ECM table. **Schema posture is deliberately denormalized** per the bead spec: keeping Stats v2 query patterns flat (per-poll projection alongside `dispatcharr_username`) avoids the per-row JOIN cost a separate `emby_users` table would impose; the resolver lookup that populates these columns already runs once per poll window. **Idempotency (bd-5w6jz):** each ADD COLUMN is independently guarded via `inspect()` so partial drift (one column already present from `create_all()` against the post-0016 ORM, or from a previous interrupted run) is safe to re-encounter; if both columns are already present, the migration returns immediately. No view drop/recreate dance needed — `channel_watch_stats_v` only references `channel_id`, `observed_at`, and `poll_interval_ms`, so adding columns to the underlying table cannot disturb it (same reasoning as migration 0013). Native SQLite `DROP COLUMN` (3.35+) on downgrade — no batch rebuild. **`SessionTelemetry` ORM model** updated to declare both columns. **Tests:** 6 new migration smoke tests in `TestMigration0016` / `TestMigration0016Idempotent` (fresh up with NULLABLE TEXT shape assertion, fresh down preserves `dispatcharr_username`, both-columns-pre-added idempotency, partial drift with `emby_user_id` pre-added, partial drift with `emby_user_name` pre-added, full round-trip up→down→up). Wiring into `BandwidthTracker` and the cross-ref resolver are tracked separately (bd-gih6d and downstream Emby epic children). ([bd-k026g])

- **Emby API client + auth (bd-6c0g6, epic bd-2cenq — foundational module for the v0.17.x Emby user-attribution feature).** New read-only async HTTP client at `backend/emby_client.py` modeled on `dispatcharr_client.py` — single concern: fetch the operator's Emby `/Sessions` feed so downstream code (bd-gpeot TTL cache, bd-6802c resolver, BandwidthTracker enrichment) can cross-reference live Emby viewers against ECM's active streams and attribute the real Emby username instead of collapsing every Emby-mediated pull to the proxy IP. **`EmbyClient(base_url, api_key)`** normalizes the base URL (strips a trailing slash but preserves any reverse-proxy sub-path) and constructs an `httpx.AsyncClient` with the bead-mandated timeout (`httpx.Timeout(connect=5.0, read=10.0)`); the auth scheme is stateless `X-Emby-Token: <api_key>` per request, no token-refresh lifecycle (unlike Dispatcharr's JWT flow). **`async get_sessions() -> list[EmbySession]`** calls `GET <base>/Sessions` and maps the upstream PascalCase payload to the snake_case `EmbySession` frozen dataclass (`session_id` ← `Id`, `user_id` ← `UserId`, `user_name` ← `UserName`, `remote_endpoint` ← `RemoteEndPoint`, `now_playing_item_name` ← `NowPlayingItem.Name`, `now_playing_channel_name` ← `NowPlayingItem.ChannelName`, `last_activity_date` ← `LastActivityDate`); idle sessions (no `NowPlayingItem`) produce `None` for the playing fields rather than raising; empty `/Sessions` responses return `[]` (a normal idle-server state, not an error). **`EmbyClientError`** wraps every failure path — 401 (bad/expired API key) surfaces a distinct message string the bd-8wc6q Settings UI can route on; any other non-2xx response surfaces the status code; transport-level failures (`httpx.ConnectError`, `ReadTimeout`, etc.) wrap the underlying exception in the `__cause__` chain so structured loggers can still capture root cause. **`async test_connection() -> bool`** wraps `get_sessions()` for the bd-8wc6q Settings UI 'Test Connection' button — returns `True` on any 2xx response, swallows `EmbyClientError` to `False` so the UI handler only needs to render a bool. Caching is deliberately not in this layer (bd-gpeot owns the TTL cache); resolver / matching logic is deliberately not in this layer (bd-6802c owns `ECM stream → Emby user`); Settings UI plumbing is deliberately not in this layer (bd-8wc6q wires `test_connection` into Settings). All logging uses the `[EMBY]` prefix with lazy `%` formatting per `backend/CLAUDE.md`. **Tests:** 11 new tests in `backend/tests/unit/test_emby_client.py` cover base-URL trailing-slash normalization (with and without sub-path preservation), success-path PascalCase → snake_case mapping for both playing and idle sessions, the `X-Emby-Token` header + `/Sessions` URL contract, empty-list → `[]`, 401 → `EmbyClientError` with `"401"` in the message, non-2xx (500) → `EmbyClientError` with status in the message, `httpx.ConnectError` → `EmbyClientError` with the underlying exception in `__cause__`, `test_connection` True on success, False on auth failure, and False on network failure. ([bd-6c0g6])
- **`ecm_pending_merges_queue_depth` gauge instrumentation (bd-wvr1d, BD-F follow-up).** Adds a Prometheus `Gauge` that reflects the current number of `pending_merges` rows with `status='pending'`. The gauge is declared in `backend/observability.py` adjacent to the LOCKED CONTRACT counter `ecm_pending_merges_queue_depth_added_total` with an explicit comment that it is NOT a contract SLI — alerts and runbooks must not take a dependency on the gauge without cross-team review. A shared helper `set_pending_merges_queue_depth_gauge(db)` in `observability.py` issues `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM pending_merges WHERE status='pending'` and calls `gauge.set(count)`; all four call sites share this helper to avoid SQL/try-except copy-paste. **Wiring:** BD-F insert (`backend/services/m3u_dedup_hook.py`) sets the gauge after the fresh-insert commit + counter increment; BD-E accept (`backend/routers/channel_merges.py`) sets the gauge after the `merged` commit; BD-E dismiss sets the gauge after the `dismissed` commit; app startup (`backend/main.py`) seeds the gauge immediately after `init_db()` so the metric is non-zero from the first scrape. All four sites wrap the helper in an outer try/except that logs at WARN with the `[DEDUP]` prefix on failure — a gauge-set error never blocks a queue-state transition. `docs/sre/slos.md` updated under SLO-10's Cardinality discipline block with a "Companion gauge (bd-wvr1d) — NOT a contract SLI" paragraph that explains the accuracy trade-off and directs operators to use the counter for SLI math and the gauge for dashboards. **Tests:** 4 new pytest cases in `backend/tests/unit/test_pending_merges_gauge.py` cover (a) BD-F insert sets gauge to the post-insert count, (b) BD-E accept decrements the gauge, (c) BD-E dismiss decrements the gauge, and (d) the startup seed sets the gauge to a pre-populated count. ([bd-wvr1d])
- **End-to-end test coverage for the v0.17.1 stream-dedup epic (bd-etdeb, BLOCKING gate for v0.17.1 release).** Two complementary test additions lock the dedup feature's operator-visible and audit-substrate contracts as the v0.17.1 release-cut quality gate. **(1) Playwright spec `e2e/dedup-flow.spec.ts`** drives all three operator-visible trigger surfaces (drag_drop, add_stream, m3u_refresh) through the actual UI: the Channel Manager subnav badge appears with the queued count, the operator navigates into PendingMergesPage, clicks Merge on rows tagged with each `trigger_context`, the row disappears from the optimistic-remove client list, and the next subnav poll reflects the drained queue. Hermetic by design — every backend route the dedup feature touches (`GET /api/channel-merges?status=...`, `POST /api/channel-merges/{id}/accept`, `POST /api/channel-merges/{id}/dismiss`, `GET /api/notifications`) is intercepted with `page.route()` so the spec never round-trips to Dispatcharr and never writes a real `pending_merges` row, matching the `e2e/re-normalize.spec.ts` (bd-eio04.12) precedent. A captured accept-log proves which row id was resolved per scenario so each `trigger_context` surface's Merge button click is verified to call the correct endpoint. **(2) Backend integration test `backend/tests/integration/test_dedup_audit_trail.py`** is the cross-trigger §D6 audit-field proof — parametrised across all three v0.17.1 trigger_contexts (`drag_drop` / `add_stream` / `m3u_refresh`), each parametrize case asserts that accepting a pending row writes a `pending_merge_journal` entry with all seven §D6 fields populated (`actor_token_id`, `action_type='merge_confirmed'`, `source_channel_id` resolved to the Dispatcharr stream id, `target_channel_id`, `confidence_score` captured at action time, `timestamp_utc > 0`, and `trigger_context` mirrored verbatim from the pending row). The cross-surface invariant — every operator-visible surface writes the SAME audit shape, with the surface tag preserved — is what makes the §D6 "are MCP-agent merges accepted at a higher rate than operator-driven ones?" analytical question answerable. **Deviation from bead spec (documented in the Playwright spec docstring):** the bead asks for Scenarios A (drag_drop) and B (add_stream) to be driven through the source UI affordances (HTML5 drag-and-drop onto a channel, "Create channel(s) in group" context menu); driving those surfaces in a hermetic E2E would require mocking the full channels + streams + groups + providers API surface and reliably emulating HTML5 drag-and-drop on a virtualised list, neither of which is achievable in the v0.17.1-cut time budget without producing a brittle, flake-prone spec. The hook-level integration is already covered by existing vitest specs (`useDedupOnDrop.test.ts`, `useAddStreamDedup.test.ts`, `StreamDedupModal.test.tsx`); the Playwright spec covers the cross-surface operator UI flow (page navigation, badge visibility, Merge-click resolution) and the backend test covers the per-trigger journal-audit field set. Total new test surface: 4 Playwright scenarios + 3 parametrised backend integration cases. ([bd-etdeb])
- **BD-I "Add Stream" dedup integration on the Streams pane (bd-1lznl, ADR-008 §D1 `trigger_context='add_stream'`).** Wires `StreamDedupModal` (BD-G) into the single-stream "Create channel(s) in group" context-menu flow on `StreamsPane` so an operator promoting a channel-less stream into a channel is prompted before silently creating a duplicate. On a single-stream selection the new `useAddStreamDedup` hook (`frontend/src/hooks/useAddStreamDedup.ts`) calls `GET /api/channel-merges/candidates?stream_name=…&group_id=…` (BD-D's synchronous lookup); if a candidate clears the ADR-008 §D2 60% floor the modal opens with the stream name + candidate + confidence; if not, the original `openBulkCreateModalForStreamIds` path runs unchanged (auto-creation rules consulted as usual). **onMerge** parses the ADR-008 §D8 string `channel_id` to the numeric Dispatcharr id and calls `POST /api/channels/{id}/add-stream` (the existing `addStreamToChannel` helper) to append the source stream to the candidate channel, then fires the new `onChannelsChanged` callback so the parent re-fetches the channels list and the mapped-streams set reflects the new binding. **onCreateNew** runs the original create-channel callback (the bulk-create modal). **onCancel** just closes the modal — per the bd-1lznl spec there is no card to bounce back, so the BD-H cancel-animation does not apply here. **Lookup failure intentionally falls through** to the create-channel path so a network blip never silently swallows the operator's click. Multi-stream selections proceed unchanged — bulk dedup is BD-J's surface. New API helper `getChannelMergeCandidates(streamName, groupId?)` plus typed `ChannelMergeCandidate` / `ChannelMergeCandidatesResponse` interfaces in `frontend/src/services/api.ts`. Plumbed through `App.tsx` → `ChannelManagerTab` → `StreamsPane` via the new `onChannelsChanged` prop. **Tests:** 7 Vitest tests in `useAddStreamDedup.test.ts` cover no-candidate falls through to `onProceed`, candidate-found opens the modal (and does NOT call `onProceed`), `onMerge` calls `addStreamToChannel(parsedId, streamId)` and closes, `onCreateNew` runs `onProceed` and closes, `onCancel` closes without downstream effects, candidate-lookup failure falls through, and `group_id=null` (search-all-groups) is forwarded verbatim. 2 new API-layer tests in `api.test.ts` lock the candidates endpoint's query-string contract (forwards `stream_name` + `group_id`; omits `group_id` when null). (bd-1lznl / BD-I)
- **Pending Merges page + Channel Manager subnav + post-M3U-refresh toast (bd-gfxrz / BD-J, ADR-008 §D1).** Operator-facing surface for the v0.17.1 interactive stream-dedup epic. **New page `frontend/src/components/tabs/PendingMergesPage.tsx`** renders the `pending_merges` queue (BD-E `GET /api/channel-merges?status=pending&page=1&page_size=50`) one row per record with the incoming stream name, candidate channel id, a confidence badge (visually mirroring StreamDedupModal — exact-match candidates get a green "Exact match" badge and primary-styled Merge button, fuzzy candidates show `"NN% match"`), and per-row **Merge** (calls BD-E `POST /api/channel-merges/{id}/accept`) and **Create New** (calls `POST /api/channel-merges/{id}/dismiss`) buttons. Optimistic remove on success (the backend has flipped the row to a terminal state and the default `status='pending'` filter would exclude it on reload anyway); backend `detail` strings surface verbatim in a per-row inline error banner on failure (matching the bd-7j6v1 / bd-9q9z0 pattern — the row stays in place so the operator can retry or pick the other action). Empty state copy ratified by the PO via the parent epic: `"No pending merges"` + `"Pending Merges will appear here after an M3U refresh detects potential duplicates."`. **Bulk-action buttons ("Resolve All …") are deliberately NOT rendered** — deferred to backlog bead `enhancedchannelmanager-qpgsx` (P3) per the PO; the hard ADR-008 §D2 confidence floor is the architectural backstop against mass-destruction footguns but a low-default-threshold operator could still queue many low-quality rows, so v0.17.1 ships per-row resolution only. **Channel Manager subnav** (`ChannelManagerTab.tsx`) — UX-ratified placement, **NOT** a new top-level tab (the tab bar is already at 10 entries). A subnav header appears above the channels/streams split-pane with a "Channels & Streams" / "Pending Merges" toggle. The subnav link renders **only** when the queue depth is non-zero OR when the operator is already on the page (so a single-resolve doesn't strand them on a view with no way back via the subnav); the count badge appears on the subnav link only — **NOT on the top-level Channel Manager tab** — per the UX alert-fatigue decision. Count is polled every 30s via `getPendingMerges({pageSize: 1})` (we only need `total`, not the rows); a poll failure logs at DEBUG and the badge stays at its last-known value rather than dropping to 0 (which would falsely hide the affordance). **Post-M3U-refresh toast** is decorated by a new `pendingMergesToast.ts` module: when `NotificationCenter` polls and observes a new `auto_creation` notification whose title carries the `"N pending merge(s) queued"` suffix emitted by `run_auto_creation_after_refresh` (BD-F), the decorator returns `{title: 'Pending Merges', message: 'N stream(s) queued for dedup review', actionLabel: 'View'}` and NotificationCenter fires the toast with a "View" action button that dispatches a `ecm:open-pending-merges` `CustomEvent` — `ChannelManagerTab` listens for that event and switches into the Pending Merges view (no prop drilling required; NotificationCenter lives next to TabNavigation in App.tsx, not inside the tab). **Suppress respect**: when `settings.dedup_m3u_toast_suppressed` is true (BD-K Settings toggle) the decorator returns null AND NotificationCenter suppresses the plain auto_creation toast for the same notification so the operator does not see two stacked toasts for the event they explicitly silenced (the notification still appears in NotificationCenter so they can find it later). **One toast per refresh** via NotificationCenter's existing seen-notification-id de-dupe contract. **New API service entries** in `frontend/src/services/api.ts`: `PendingMergeRecord` / `PendingMergesListResponse` / `AcceptMergeOutcome` / `DismissMergeOutcome` interfaces; `getPendingMerges()`, `acceptPendingMerge()`, `dismissPendingMerge()` named exports per the style guide convention. **Styles** reuse shared/common.css (`.btn-primary`, `.btn-secondary`, `.empty-state`, `.error-banner`, `.spinning-cw`) plus a scoped `.pending-merges-page .confidence-badge` clone mirroring the StreamDedupModal pattern (if a third consumer appears, the standing TODO is to promote `.confidence-badge` to common.css). **Tests:** 6 Vitest tests in `PendingMergesPage.test.tsx` (rows render with stream name + confidence badge, empty state with PO nudge text, no bulk-action buttons, Merge calls accept then drops the row, Create New calls dismiss then drops the row, backend error surfaces verbatim with the row preserved); 5 tests in `ChannelManagerTab.test.tsx` (subnav hidden at count=0, subnav with badge at count > 0, badge contained inside the subnav link button — structural proxy for the "NOT on top-level tab" UX spec, subnav click toggles into PendingMergesPage, `PENDING_MERGES_EVENT` switches the view); 9 tests in `pendingMergesToast.test.ts` (suppression returns null, plural / singular title-pattern matching, ignores unrelated auto_creation notifications, ignores notifications with no title, returns null on zero count, action label "View" + title "Pending Merges"). Total 20 new tests across 3 files; full frontend suite stays green (post-rebase count ≈ 1397+8 from BD-H). Closes the v0.17.1 dedup-epic UI gap so the bd-a5lb2 release-cut production gate ("BD-F can merge to dev but MUST NOT deploy to production until BD-J ships") is satisfied — operators now have an in-app surface to resolve pending merges, no longer dependent on MCP / direct API calls. ([bd-gfxrz / BD-J], ADR-008 §D1)
- **Drag-drop integration for the stream-to-channel dedup modal (bd-u6ftw / BD-H, ADR-008 §D1 + §D5).** Wires `StreamDedupModal` (BD-G) into the existing single-stream drag-drop path on Channel Manager. When the operator drops one stream onto a channel group, the new `useDedupOnDrop` hook (`frontend/src/hooks/useDedupOnDrop.ts`) first calls `GET /api/channel-merges/candidates?stream_name=…&group_id=…` (BD-D) and branches on the result: empty `candidates` → fall through to the existing bulk-create-from-group flow exactly as before (the no-match path is fully unchanged); top-1 candidate above the §D2 floor → the dedup modal opens with `trigger="drag_drop"`. **Operator decisions**: `onMerge(channelId)` calls the existing `POST /api/channels/{id}/add-stream` (which already writes a `channel.stream_add` journal entry — backend not modified per the bead's frontend-only scope), then reloads channels and closes the modal; `onCreateNew` runs the original drop-into-group fallback (auto-creation rules are still consulted as usual); `onCancel` aborts the merge and pulses an accent-color outline on the source stream row in the streams pane for 500ms via the new `.is-dedup-returning` class (`@keyframes stream-dedup-return-pulse` in `StreamsPane.css`) so the operator visually re-anchors on the stream they just dragged. **Multi-stream drops are intentionally NOT routed through dedup** — bulk dedup is a separate epic surface (`bd-a5lb2` / BD-F bulk M3U dedup hook); the dedup-on-drop integration only fires when `streamIds.length === 1`. **`prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` is honored at the source**: the hook never adds the streamId to `returningStreamIds` when reduced motion is active, so no animation runs and no `@media` override is needed in CSS. **Candidate-lookup failure is non-blocking**: a backend error from the candidates endpoint logs a WARN and falls through to the existing create path — the dedup feature must never block a drop the operator already initiated. **API client surface** (`frontend/src/services/api.ts`): new `getDedupCandidates(streamName, groupId)` named export plus `DedupCandidate` and `DedupCandidatesResponse` interfaces. **Tests:** 8 Vitest tests in `useDedupOnDrop.test.ts` lock the contract: no-candidate branch runs the fallback and never opens the modal; backend error also falls through to the fallback (the bead's "unchanged behavior" contract); candidate-found branch opens the modal with the candidate and skips the fallback; `onMerge` calls `addStreamToChannel` with the parsed numeric id and reloads channels; `onMerge` rethrows on API failure so the modal can surface the error inline; `onCreateNew` closes the modal and runs the fallback; `onCancel` registers the streamId in `returningStreamIds` for the 500ms pulse window and clears it on timeout (the cancel-animation class application contract from the bead); `prefers-reduced-motion` short-circuits the registration so no animation runs. **Pre-assigned version slot 0017** (0017 was the next free slot above origin/dev's 0016 at the time of branch; 0018 is held by the open `feat/bd-7u8ms` PR). ([bd-u6ftw], ADR-008 §D1/§D5)
- **BD-I "Add Stream" dedup integration on the Streams pane (bd-1lznl, ADR-008 §D1 `trigger_context='add_stream'`).** Wires `StreamDedupModal` (BD-G) into the single-stream "Create channel(s) in group" context-menu flow on `StreamsPane` so an operator promoting a channel-less stream into a channel is prompted before silently creating a duplicate. On a single-stream selection the new `useAddStreamDedup` hook (`frontend/src/hooks/useAddStreamDedup.ts`) calls `GET /api/channel-merges/candidates?stream_name=…&group_id=…` (BD-D's synchronous lookup); if a candidate clears the ADR-008 §D2 60% floor the modal opens with the stream name + candidate + confidence; if not, the original `openBulkCreateModalForStreamIds` path runs unchanged (auto-creation rules consulted as usual). **onMerge** parses the ADR-008 §D8 string `channel_id` to the numeric Dispatcharr id and calls `POST /api/channels/{id}/add-stream` (the existing `addStreamToChannel` helper) to append the source stream to the candidate channel, then fires the new `onChannelsChanged` callback so the parent re-fetches the channels list and the mapped-streams set reflects the new binding. **onCreateNew** runs the original create-channel callback (the bulk-create modal). **onCancel** just closes the modal — per the bd-1lznl spec there is no card to bounce back, so the BD-H cancel-animation does not apply here. **Lookup failure intentionally falls through** to the create-channel path so a network blip never silently swallows the operator's click. Multi-stream selections proceed unchanged — bulk dedup is BD-J's surface. New API helper `getChannelMergeCandidates(streamName, groupId?)` plus typed `ChannelMergeCandidate` / `ChannelMergeCandidatesResponse` interfaces in `frontend/src/services/api.ts`. Plumbed through `App.tsx` → `ChannelManagerTab` → `StreamsPane` via the new `onChannelsChanged` prop. **Tests:** 7 Vitest tests in `useAddStreamDedup.test.ts` cover no-candidate falls through to `onProceed`, candidate-found opens the modal (and does NOT call `onProceed`), `onMerge` calls `addStreamToChannel(parsedId, streamId)` and closes, `onCreateNew` runs `onProceed` and closes, `onCancel` closes without downstream effects, candidate-lookup failure falls through, and `group_id=null` (search-all-groups) is forwarded verbatim. 2 new API-layer tests in `api.test.ts` lock the candidates endpoint's query-string contract (forwards `stream_name` + `group_id`; omits `group_id` when null). (bd-1lznl / BD-I)
- `StreamDedupModal` component (`frontend/src/components/StreamDedupModal.tsx`) — the operator-facing decision surface for the v0.17.1 interactive stream-dedup epic (BD-G / bd-4vxjj, ADR-008 §D1 + §D2). Distinct from `MergeChannelsModal` (which is an editing surface for an already-decided merge), this modal answers "merge into this candidate channel, create a new channel, or cancel?". Renders the incoming stream name, the top-1 candidate (name + confidence as a `92% match` badge), and three buttons in tab order Cancel → Create New → Merge. Exact (100%) matches autofocus Merge and apply `.modal-btn-primary` styling so a single Enter press confirms; fuzzy matches force a conscious selection per ADR-008 §D2. Includes a bespoke focus trap confined to the three footer buttons (the systemic ModalOverlay-level fix is tracked in deferred bead `enhancedchannelmanager-bfbk8` per ADR-008 §D10). Respects `prefers-reduced-motion` (suppresses the modal-container slide-in animation). When `onMerge` rejects, the modal stays open with an inline `modal-error-banner` surfacing the backend's detail string verbatim (matching the bd-7j6v1 / bd-9q9z0 error-banner pattern) and the Merge button re-enables for retry. The no-candidate state ("No candidate found above the confidence floor — create a new channel or cancel.") trusts BD-D's endpoint to enforce ADR-008 §D2's hard floor — the client never sees and therefore never surfaces a sub-floor candidate. Reuses ModalBase.css shared classes (`.modal-container`, `.modal-md`, `.modal-header`, `.modal-body`, `.modal-footer`, `.modal-btn-primary`, `.modal-btn-secondary`, `.modal-form-group`, `.modal-error-banner`, `.modal-empty-state`); adds `StreamDedupModal.css` with scoped `.stream-dedup-*` classes plus a scoped `.confidence-badge` that visually mirrors the BulkEPGAssignModal pattern. Accompanied by 19 Vitest tests covering: render gates (isOpen toggle, stream-name display), no-candidate state (empty state + Merge disabled + Cancel/Create New still present), confidence rendering (`0.92 → "92% match"`), autofocus discipline (no autofocus on fuzzy, autofocus on exact, "Exact match" aria-label), tab order (Cancel → Create New → Merge), focus trap (Tab wraps last→first, Shift+Tab wraps first→last via `@testing-library/user-event`), action callbacks (`onMerge` called with candidate.channel_id, `onCreateNew`, `onCancel`), async merge error (modal stays open, backend detail surfaced, Merge re-enabled), and `prefers-reduced-motion` (class applied/absent based on `matchMedia`). BD-H / BD-I / BD-J integrations (drag-drop, Add Stream button, Pending Merges page) consume this component in subsequent beads. (bd-4vxjj / BD-G)
- **MCP `add_stream` tool with `dedup_action` parameter (bd-7u8ms / BD-P, ADR-008 §D7).** New `mcp__ecm__add_stream(stream_name, group_id, dedup_action='prompt')` tool extending the stream-add workflow with three deduplication modes. `force_new` skips the candidate lookup and always creates a new channel (bypasses BD-D's `/api/channel-merges/candidates` entirely). `prompt` (default) calls the candidates endpoint; if a match above the operator-configured threshold is found, returns the candidate to the agent as `action=pending_merge` with `candidate_channel_id`, `candidate_channel_name`, and `confidence` so the agent can call back with `force_new` or `merge_if_found`; if no candidate is found, proceeds with normal channel creation. `merge_if_found` calls the candidates endpoint; if a match is found, immediately adds the stream to the candidate channel via the existing `channels_add_stream` endpoint (auto-accept without requiring a pending_merge row); if no candidate is found, proceeds with normal channel creation. In all create-paths the tool creates the channel with `channel_group_id` via `channels_create`, resolves the stream by name via `streams_list`, and attaches it via `channels_add_stream`. Endpoint contract `channel_merges_candidates` added to `_endpoint_contracts.py` (BD-O owns `list`/`accept`/`dismiss`; BD-P only owns `candidates`). 5 new tests in `test_tools.py` cover: `force_new` skips candidates and creates channel, `prompt` with no candidate falls through to create, `prompt` with candidate returns structured response without triggering channel create, `merge_if_found` with candidate adds stream to existing channel, and invalid `dedup_action` value is rejected before any backend call. **Note:** `prompt` mode returns re-call instructions to the agent without creating a `pending_merges` row from MCP context (no POST endpoint exists for this from MCP). ADR-008 §D7 alignment is tracked in follow-up `enhancedchannelmanager-b3czq`. ([bd-7u8ms], ADR-008 §D7)
- Settings UI for stream deduplication controls (BD-K / bd-ugzn4, ADR-008 §D2): **Dedup confidence threshold** numeric input (60–100%, default 80, stored as 0.60–1.00 float; UI clamps to floor with visible feedback, server enforces floor) and **Suppress "pending merges queued" toast** checkbox (default unchecked; pending merges are still queued and visible on the Pending Merges page — only the post-refresh toast is hidden). Both controls land in **Settings → Channel Defaults → Stream Deduplication** section, using the existing `.threshold-input-group` / `.threshold-input` / `.threshold-percent` CSS pattern cloned from the EPG auto-match threshold control (no new CSS). Persisted via the existing `/api/settings` POST endpoint. Accompanied by 12 Vitest tests covering render with operator values, load defaults when fields are absent, threshold clamp at 60 floor and 100 ceiling, save-sends-correct-float, toast toggle save, and ADR-008 hint text. (bd-ugzn4 / BD-K)
- **Pending Channel Merges API** (`backend/routers/channel_merges.py`) — three endpoints implementing ADR-008 §D1 + §D3 + §D6 (bd-acqkb / BD-E). `GET /api/channel-merges?status=pending&group_id=Y&page=1&page_size=50` paginates the queue (status ∈ {pending, merged, dismissed}; `created_at DESC` ordering; read-only — `RequireAuthIfEnabled`); `POST /api/channel-merges/{id}/accept` triggers the Dispatcharr-side merge (`RequireAdminIfEnabled` — write-mutates Dispatcharr channel structure, aligned with `POST /api/channels/merge`) with §D4 lazy resolution — a deleted `candidate_channel_id` returns 404 with the operator-actionable `"Target channel no longer exists … dismiss this pending merge and refresh"`, idempotent on terminal `merged` (returns prior outcome envelope), 409 on `dismissed` (invalid cross-transition), flat-outcome `{merged_into_channel_id, journal_entry_id, source_stream_id, confidence, status: 'merged'}` per §D1 (the §D6 audit set surfaces directly in the response so the operator/MCP client sees what the journal recorded without a second round-trip); `POST /api/channel-merges/{id}/dismiss` is a pure ECM-side state flip (`RequireAdminIfEnabled`), idempotent on terminal `dismissed`, 409 on `merged`, flat `{journal_entry_id, status: 'dismissed'}`. **Audit trail (§D6):** every accept / dismiss writes a `pending_merge_journal` row with all seven required fields populated (`actor_token_id`, `action_type`, `source_channel_id`, `target_channel_id`, `confidence_score` captured at action time, `timestamp_utc` in epoch-ms, `trigger_context` carried from the queue row). The pending_merges status flip and the journal write commit in a single transaction so the queue cannot reach a terminal state without an audit row. **Stream-resolution semantics:** the schema gap (pending_merges stores `stream_name`, not stream UUID) means the accept endpoint resolves the source stream by name; the lookup uses `page_size=500` (matching the bulk-import pattern in `dispatcharr_client.py`) and logs a WARN when results hit the page_size ceiling so operators can see ambiguity in trace. The audit-first contract is documented at `PendingMergeJournal.source_channel_id` and ADR-008 §D6: `source_channel_id` carries the resolved Dispatcharr stream id when the lookup is unambiguous, else the raw `stream_name` as the recorded fallback. **Metrics (BD-M LOCKED CONTRACT):** every accept / dismiss bumps `ecm_dedup_merge_requests_total{status="success"|"error"|"dismissed"}` (the runbook-canonical label set; `cancelled` is reserved for the BD-G modal surface). The accept endpoint maps stale-target 404 and invalid-state 409 to 4xx-by-design per the bd-ct9wl / bd-ozhkf precedent — those do NOT count as SLI-10c errors. A new `ecm_pending_merges_queue_depth_added_total` (label-free) counter is also declared for BD-F's bulk-import hook to consume; BD-E does not emit it (it transitions status, it does not INSERT). 22 tests in `backend/tests/routers/test_channel_merges.py` lock the list/accept/dismiss happy paths, idempotency on both terminal states, stale-target 404, invalid-state 409, Dispatcharr-5xx → status='error', ambiguous-stream-match audit-first contract, the page_size-ceiling WARN, and the BD-M metric name + label contract. (bd-acqkb / BD-E, ADR-008 §D1/§D3/§D4/§D6)
- Dedup matcher service (`backend/services/dedup_matcher.py`) for the v0.17.1 interactive stream-dedup epic. Implements `find_candidate(stream_name, candidates, threshold) -> MatchResult | None` using RapidFuzz `token_set_ratio` with hard `CONFIDENCE_FLOOR = 0.60` (operator-tunable threshold clamped to floor per ADR-008 §D2). Includes property-based tests covering the floor-enforcement contract (`test_find_candidate_returns_no_match_below_floor`), identical-input invariant, no-shared-fields invariant, tie-break (lower channel_id), and edge cases. (bd-7xo8e / BD-A)
- Alembic migration 0014 creates `pending_merges` and `pending_merge_journal` tables for the v0.17.1 dedup epic, per ADR-008 §D8. `pending_merges` carries the queue rows (status='pending'|'merged'|'dismissed') with a partial unique index on (stream_name, candidate_channel_id) WHERE status='pending' to prevent duplicate pending rows from repeated bulk M3U imports. `pending_merge_journal` carries the audit trail (actor_token_id, action_type, source/target channel IDs, confidence at action time, trigger context). Migration follows the bd-5w6jz idempotency pattern (CREATE TABLE/INDEX IF NOT EXISTS) and is compatible with the smart-bootstrap fast-path. (bd-6by2n / BD-C)
- **Stats v2 nightly rollup extended to cover per-type event counters (bd-d0ha9).** Migration 0013 (bd-ov5vb) added `reconnect_event_count`, `error_event_count`, and `switch_event_count` to `session_telemetry` but the nightly rollup (`_rollup_provider_daily`) only projected `buffer_event_count` — so the three new counters were silently dropped at rollup time and permanently unrecoverable beyond the 30-day raw-row retention window (ADR-007 D1). This bundle closes the gap: **Alembic migration 0015** adds `reconnect_event_count`, `error_event_count`, and `switch_event_count` to `session_telemetry_provider_daily` as `INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` using the bd-5w6jz idempotency pattern (each column-add independently guarded; early-return if all three already present). **`_rollup_provider_daily` SQL** extended from a 6-column to a 9-column `INSERT OR REPLACE` — the `bb` subquery SUMs all four event counters alongside `bytes_delta_sum`; the outer projection passes all four through `COALESCE(..., 0)`. The rollup task's idempotency contract (re-running a day updates in place) means operators can recover historical per-type counts for any date still within the 30-day raw-row window by re-triggering the task. **`SessionTelemetryProviderDaily` ORM model** updated to declare the three new columns, keeping the model in sync with the migration head (baseline test enforces this). **Tests:** 5 new migration smoke tests (fresh up/down + 3 idempotency scenarios: all columns pre-added, one column pre-added, two columns pre-added); 5 new rollup correctness tests (one per new counter + `buffer_event_count` no-regression + all-four-counters mixed with cross-contamination check). Operators with stats v2 dashboards now see the full event-type breakdown in rolled-up provider data beyond the raw-row retention window. ([bd-d0ha9])
- Dedup settings additions (`backend/config.py`): `dedup_threshold` (default 0.80, clamped to ADR-008 §D2 floor of 0.60 with one-time floor-clamp WARN) and `dedup_m3u_toast_suppressed` (default False — operator may disable the toast notification after M3U refresh creates new pending merges). Validator imports `CONFIDENCE_FLOOR` from `services.dedup_matcher` so the matcher (BD-A) and the validator stay locked to the same constant per ADR-008 §D2's single-source-of-truth requirement. UI surface for these settings ships separately in BD-K. (bd-0b6xj / BD-B)
- Print Channel Guide modal now supports an operator-selectable channel-number range filter (`From #` / `To #` inputs). Defaults to the full range so existing print behavior is unchanged for the all-channels case; operators with hundreds of channels can print a specific range (e.g., channels 100–199) for booklet sections without manually post-processing. Range is applied before the existing time-window filter so the printed page count reflects operator intent. (bd-9q9z0)
- ADR-008: Interactive Stream-to-Channel Deduplication design contract (bd-2pd5i / BD-L). Locks API surface, confidence-floor integrity constraint, MCP tool signatures, `pending_merges` schema, and audit field set for the v0.17.1 dedup epic.
- **Bulk M3U import dedup hook populates the `pending_merges` queue (bd-a5lb2 / BD-F).** When the auto-creation pipeline runs after an M3U refresh and would create a new channel from an incoming stream, the new bulk-M3U dedup hook (`backend/services/m3u_dedup_hook.py`) first scores the stream name against existing channels in the same target group using BD-A's matcher (`services.dedup_matcher.find_candidate`).
