A confidence above the operator-configured `dedup_threshold` (clamped to ADR-008 §D2 floor of 0.60 by the matcher itself) enqueues a `pending_merges` row with `trigger_context='m3u_refresh'` and signals the executor to SKIP the channel-creation API call — the pending row encodes the deferred operator decision for BD-J's UI (and BD-E's accept/dismiss endpoints) to resolve. The hook fires AFTER the executor's existing exact / normalized lookups (`_find_channel_by_name`) have failed and BEFORE the new-channel `create_channel` API call, so all current "merge into existing" and "skip if exists" paths are unchanged. **ADR-008 §D5 partial-unique-index idempotency:** repeat M3U refreshes of the same stream against the same candidate produce one INSERT then catch `IntegrityError` on subsequent attempts, logging at INFO (`[DEDUP] Pending merge for stream=... candidate=... already queued; skipping`) and returning "skip channel creation" because the prior pending row is still authoritative — no crash, no duplicate row. **ADR-008 §D9 broker-free:** writes go directly to `pending_merges` via the existing ECM session; no Celery, no Redis, no scheduler daemon. **Hook is gated to `triggered_by='m3u_refresh'`:** scheduled / manual auto-creation runs predate the dedup epic and stay on legacy "always create" semantics — they are not one of the four ADR-008 §D1 interactive trigger surfaces. **Dry-run preview** never mutates the queue. **LOCKED CONTRACT metric (BD-M):** new `Counter` `ecm_pending_merges_queue_depth_added_total` registered in `backend/observability.py`, +1 per fresh INSERT (collisions do NOT increment — they are not new rows). The companion gauge `ecm_pending_merges_queue_depth` is not in BD-M's locked contract; gauge maintenance (cross-cutting BD-F insert + BD-E accept/dismiss) is filed as the P3 follow-up bead `enhancedchannelmanager-wvr1d`. **Engine wiring:** `triggered_by` threads from `AutoCreationEngine.run_pipeline` → `_process_streams` → `ActionExecutor.__init__`, so the hook only activates on the M3U-refresh path. **Operator-facing toast plumbing:** `ExecutionContext.pending_merges_added` aggregates the per-stream enqueue count; `_process_streams` rolls it up onto the pipeline result as `pending_merges_added`; `run_auto_creation_after_refresh` surfaces the count in the post-refresh notification title (`"Auto-Creation: 0 created, 3 pending merges queued"`) so operators see the signal even before BD-J's UI lands. **Tests:** 14 service-level tests in `backend/tests/services/test_m3u_dedup_hook.py` (constants, dry-run + non-m3u_refresh + empty-candidates short-circuits, fresh-insert happy path, integer→string id stringification, NULL group_id, metric increment, below-threshold silent refusal, hard-floor clamp, §D5 collision with INFO log, collision-does-not-increment-metric, terminal-state row does not block fresh pending) and 8 executor-integration tests in `backend/tests/unit/test_auto_creation_executor_dedup_hook.py` (fuzzy-match enqueues + skips create, no-candidate falls through, high operator threshold falls through, parametric `triggered_by`-gate, §D5 collision via two real executor calls, dry-run bypass). **Release-cut production gate:** BD-F can merge to `dev` independently but MUST NOT deploy to production until BD-J (Pending Merges page UI) ships — otherwise `pending_merges` rows accumulate with no operator-facing UI to resolve them. MCP-only resolution lands with BD-E. This is a release-cut concern, not a PR concern. ([bd-a5lb2])
- **SLO-10 + Prometheus alert rules for the v0.17.1 dedup epic (bd-ft3hk / BD-M).** New `SLO-10: Channel Deduplication` section in `docs/sre/slos.md` codifies three SLIs: SLI-10a candidate lookup p99 latency (<500ms / 7d / 99%), SLI-10b pending merge resolution rate (95% of merges added to the pending queue reach a terminal state — `success` or `dismissed` — within 24h), SLI-10c merge API error rate (<1% / 5m / 99%). Companion alert rules ship in a new `ecm_dedup` group in `docs/sre/prometheus_rules.yaml`: `ECMDedupCandidateLookupLatencyHigh` (warn), `ECMDedupPendingMergeResolutionStale` (warn), `ECMDedupMergeApiErrorRateHigh` (**page** — the merge endpoint is the load-bearing write path of the dedup epic; a failed accept potentially leaves a channel in a half-merged state). The metrics `ecm_dedup_candidate_lookup_duration_seconds`, `ecm_dedup_merge_requests_total{status}`, `ecm_pending_merges_queue_depth_added_total` do not exist yet — they will be emitted by BD-D/E/F as the dedup epic ships. Alert rules are intentionally vacuous (no data) until then; this is deliberate per the team-plan SRE position: rules-without-emitters fire nothing and cost nothing, and the rule definitions are the contract that tells the engineer wiring the emitters which metric names and labels to produce. Three runbook stubs ship at `docs/runbooks/dedup-candidate-lookup-latency.md`, `docs/runbooks/dedup-pending-merge-resolution-stale.md`, `docs/runbooks/dedup-merge-api-error-rate-high.md` — each is explicitly self-identified as a stub, with section headers, summary, metric names in the triage queries, and TODO placeholders for the resolution procedures that fill in as the team accumulates real incident experience. **Also closes the v0.17.0 alert-rules gap:** `ECMTaskSchedulerNextRunNull` was shipped commented-out at v0.17.0 cut so existing operators would not page themselves before Bundle H's heal (bd-1weac) reached their installs; v0.17.0 has now shipped, the heal is in operators' hands, and the alert is now live. Operators using their own Prometheus + Alertmanager get page coverage for the bd-p5b8i regression class going forward. Audit of the other four v0.17.0 metrics flagged in standup (`ecm_task_schedule_last_success_timestamp`, `ecm_database_size_bytes`, `ecm_database_wal_size_bytes`, week-over-week growth) confirmed they already had corresponding alerts shipped at v0.17.0 (`ecm_task_scheduler` and `ecm_database_size` groups respectively); the NextRunNull uncomment was the only genuine gap. ([bd-ft3hk])
- **`GET /api/channel-merges/candidates` — synchronous dedup candidate lookup (bd-kbqwb, BD-D).** New router `backend/routers/channel_merges.py` under the `/api/channel-merges/*` family (ADR-008 §D1 D4-override plural-noun path). Accepts `stream_name` (required), `group_id` (optional — forwarded to Dispatcharr as `channel_group` filter to restrict the candidate pool), `page`, and `page_size`. Fetches channels from Dispatcharr, builds the candidate tuple list, and delegates to `services.dedup_matcher.find_candidate` (BD-A) with the operator-configured `dedup_threshold` from settings (BD-B). Returns top-1 as a 1-element `candidates` list (or empty list on no match) in the ECM flat-outcome envelope with pagination fields always present for forward-compat. Confidence floor enforcement is fully delegated to the matcher (ADR-008 §D2 — no duplicate check in the router). Metric `ecm_dedup_candidate_lookup_duration_seconds` Histogram (BD-M locked contract, SLO-10 latency SLI) emitted on every request wrapping the matcher call. Auth via `RequireAdminIfEnabled` consistent with all protected endpoints. 13 new tests in `backend/tests/routers/test_dedup.py`. `observability.py` extended with the locked metric. ([bd-kbqwb], ADR-008 §D1)

- **MCP dedup tools: `list_pending_channel_merges`, `accept_channel_merge`, `dismiss_channel_merge` (bd-70ylc / BD-O, ADR-008 §D7).** Three new MCP tools in `mcp-server/tools/dedup.py` expose the interactive stream-to-channel dedup queue to AI agents. `list_pending_channel_merges(group_id?, status?)` paginates the `/api/channel-merges?status=…` queue with `status` defaulting to `'pending'` per the ADR §D7 locked contract; accepts all three lifecycle states (`pending` / `merged` / `dismissed`) so agents can inspect history as well as the live queue. `accept_channel_merge(merge_id)` wraps `POST /api/channel-merges/{id}/accept` — on success returns the flat outcome envelope `{merged_into_channel_id, journal_entry_id, source_stream_id, confidence, status: 'merged'}`; on 4xx returns a structured `{error: {code, message}}` envelope the agent can act on rather than raising (ADR-008 §D7 error-envelope contract): `code: 'TARGET_NOT_FOUND'` (404, stale target channel) includes a `dismiss_channel_merge` recovery instruction in the message per §D4 self-recovery guidance; `code: 'INVALID_STATE'` (409) signals a terminal-state collision. `dismiss_channel_merge(merge_id)` wraps `POST /api/channel-merges/{id}/dismiss` — same 4xx → structured-error semantics (409 → `INVALID_STATE`); this is the §D4 cleanup path for a stale-target accept. 5xx from either POST is re-raised as `ToolError` (uncatchable upstream error). `actor_token_id` in the `pending_merge_journal` audit row flows from the MCP bearer token through the ECM auth layer server-side — the tools do not pass identity explicitly. All three tools route through `_endpoint_contracts.ENDPOINTS` via `call_endpoint` (three new contract entries: `channel_merges_list`, `channel_merges_accept`, `channel_merges_dismiss`). `mcp-server/tools/__init__.py` registers the `dedup` module alongside the existing 14 tool modules. 11 tests in `mcp-server/tests/test_dedup.py` cover: list happy-path (pagination envelope), default-status-is-pending, status filter forwarded, group_id filter forwarded, accept happy-path (status injected), accept 404 → TARGET_NOT_FOUND (message contains recovery instruction), accept 409 → INVALID_STATE, accept 5xx → ToolError, dismiss happy-path (status injected), dismiss 409 → INVALID_STATE, dismiss 5xx → ToolError. ([bd-70ylc])

### Documentation
- **Worktree bootstrap script + docs for agents spawned into `.claude/worktrees/agent-*` (bd-scv37).** Adds `scripts/worktree-bootstrap.sh` — an idempotent 45-line Bash script that symlinks the main checkout's `frontend/node_modules` into the active worktree so frontend tooling works without a full `npm install`. The script uses `git worktree list --porcelain` to locate the main checkout path and prints a `PATH` export hint for `fnm`/`nvm` node. Updated `docs/shipping.md` §6 with a "Worktree quirks" subsection documenting the root cause (sparse-checkout + no npm on PATH), the bootstrap command, the direct `.bin/` invocation pattern for `vitest`/`eslint`/`tsc`/`vite`, and the node PATH export. Updated `frontend/CLAUDE.md` with a "Worktree workaround" quick-reference section near the top, cross-linking to the full explanation in `docs/shipping.md`. ([bd-scv37])
- **Stream deduplication documentation for v0.17.1 (bd-0lsas / BD-N, ADR-008).** Three new/updated artifacts covering the full dedup feature surface: (1) `docs/user_guide/channels-streams/stream-dedup.md` — new operator-facing guide covering all three trigger paths (drag-drop, Add Stream button, bulk M3U refresh), the Pending Merges page workflow (navigation via Channel Manager subnav badge, per-row Merge/Create New/error handling), Settings controls (dedup threshold 60–100%, suppress M3U toast), and MCP agent access. Terminology: "merge into existing channel" for the modal action, "candidate channel" for the match result, "dedup" as the feature name — distinct from the existing two-channel merge surface. (2) `docs/api.md` additions — `GET /api/channel-merges/candidates` (query params, response shape, metric emitted), `GET /api/channel-merges` (status filter, pagination envelope), `POST /api/channel-merges/{id}/accept` (auth, flat outcome envelope, audit fields, idempotency contract), `POST /api/channel-merges/{id}/dismiss` (auth, flat outcome), error codes `TARGET_NOT_FOUND` (404, stale target) and `INVALID_STATE` (409, invalid transition). (3) `docs/architecture.md` additions — new "Stream Deduplication Pipeline" section covering the `pending_merges` + `pending_merge_journal` database tables, the matcher service, the `/api/channel-merges/*` router family, the bulk M3U hook wiring, interactive trigger flow diagram, MCP tools, and the boundary with the auto-creation pipeline's own unattended collision detection. Router count updated (20 → 21); MCP tool module count updated (14 → 15). ([bd-0lsas])

### Fixed
- **Auto-creation Smart Sort: account-less streams now participate in priority ordering via a `"custom"` fallback key (bd-sgtmx, GH #244).** Previously the Smart Sort logic gave streams with no M3U account (`m3u_account_id is None`) no way to be ranked by the `m3u_priority` criterion. This added a reserved `"custom"` key in `Settings → M3U account priorities` so such account-less streams can be assigned a priority; `{"1": 100, "custom": 200}` places account-less streams above M3U account 1, and when `"custom"` is unset the default is `0` (no behavioral change for operators who leave it unset). The fix applies to both the auto-creation engine (Pass 3.5 stream reorder) and the stream-prober smart sort. **Correction (bd-o529v / bead ap1ud):** operator-added custom streams are NOT account-less — they belong to the real Dispatcharr "custom" M3U account and carry that account's `m3u_account_id`, so they never reach this `"custom"` fallback. They are now ranked by the dedicated `custom_streams` Smart Sort criterion (bead ap1ud). The `m3u_priority` `"custom"` key described here is therefore a vestigial defensive fallback that only fires for genuinely account-less streams. ([bd-sgtmx], [GH #244])
- **`settings.json` `api_key` field-name collision with the MCP integration (bd-jmi1c, GH #273).** Resolves GH #273 by renaming the Dispatcharr REST API token field from `api_key` to `dispatcharr_api_key`, with one release of back-compat for the legacy name. Three distinct credentials shared a confusingly-overloaded namespace in `settings.json`: ECM read `api_key` as its Dispatcharr REST API token, the MCP "Generate API key" flow correctly writes `mcp_api_key`, and the Settings UI labels both as "API Key". Operators following the redeployment procedure (Settings → MCP Integration → Regenerate Key, copy the new key) were placing the MCP key in `api_key`, which made ECM send the MCP key to Dispatcharr and return 401 on every channel/stream operation. The v0.17.0 `bd-ix1g6` `/health` self-diagnosis helped operators recognize a missing MCP key but did not address this collision. The rename eliminates the lexical hazard at the source. **Backend (`backend/config.py`):** new canonical `dispatcharr_api_key` field on `DispatcharrSettings`; legacy `api_key` field retained as a deprecated alias for v0.17.x with a one-time-per-process `[CONFIG] Reading deprecated 'api_key' field as Dispatcharr token — please rename to 'dispatcharr_api_key' in settings.json` WARN on first read; `load_settings()` silently migrates legacy → canonical in the loaded dict (canonical wins when both are populated); `save_settings()` mirrors canonical → legacy on write so external scripts that read `settings.json:api_key` directly (the workaround in the GH #273 issue body) keep working until the legacy field is removed in a future release. **Backend (`backend/dispatcharr_client.py`):** the X-API-Key header reads `dispatcharr_api_key` with a legacy fallback; the `_settings_hash` includes both fields so the singleton client recreates on any rotation. **Backend (`backend/routers/settings.py`):** `SettingsRequest` accepts either field name (canonical wins when both are present); `SettingsResponse` surfaces both `dispatcharr_api_key_configured` (canonical) and `api_key_configured` (legacy alias) so older cached frontend bundles keep working; `TestConnectionRequest` accepts either field name. **Backend (`backend/routers/backup.py`):** `_SETTINGS_CREDENTIAL_FIELDS` lists both `dispatcharr_api_key` and `api_key` so the backup redactor never leaks either form. **Frontend (`frontend/src/services/api.ts`, `SettingsModal.tsx`, `SettingsTab.tsx`):** type definitions and POST bodies switched to the canonical field; the indicator-state hook prefers `dispatcharr_api_key_configured` with a fallback so a new frontend bundle still works against an older backend mid-rollout. **Tests:** 12 new unit tests in `backend/tests/unit/test_config_dispatcharr_api_key_migration.py` cover the canonical-only read, legacy-only read with WARN, both-populated canonical-wins, one-WARN-per-process discipline, save-time canonical→legacy mirror, and full load→save→load idempotency. 4 new integration tests in `backend/tests/routers/test_settings.py` verify the POST endpoint accepts both field names, picks canonical when both are sent, and GET returns both indicators. **Operator migration path:** existing v0.17.x operators see one `[CONFIG] Reading deprecated 'api_key' field …` WARN on the first start after upgrade; the next save (any settings change, or the next MCP key rotation) silently mirrors the value into the new canonical field. No manual `settings.json` edit is required. Manual cleanup (removing the legacy `api_key` line) is allowed once the operator has confirmed `dispatcharr_api_key` is populated. **Field-rename scope is intentionally narrow:** `mcp_api_key` is correctly named and unchanged; the `auth_method: "api_key"` enum value is unchanged (it is the mode name, not the field name); the bd-ix1g6 `/health` MCP self-diagnostic is unchanged. Legacy 'api_key' field is deprecated and will be removed in v0.19.0 (see bd-ewm4h). ([bd-jmi1c], [GH #273])
- **Bulk channel merge: pre-validate source IDs to return 422 instead of silently producing DELETE 404 noise on stale rows (bd-ozhkf — follow-up to bd-ct9wl).** The `bulk_merge_channels` endpoint had the same bare-`except` footgun as the single-channel `merge_channels` endpoint before bd-ct9wl: a `get_channel` 404 was swallowed and execution fell through to `delete_channel`, generating `[DISPATCHARR] API request failed: DELETE 404` log noise for every stale source ID. The fix mirrors the bd-ct9wl pattern exactly: catch `httpx.HTTPStatusError` with `status_code == 404`, collect missing IDs, raise HTTP 422 with the same detail string (`"Source channels [...] no longer exist — refresh the channels list and try again"`) before any mutations. Two regression tests in `TestBulkMergeChannelsStaleSourceIds` assert the 422 path and that `delete_channel` is not called on stale IDs.
- **Channel merge UX: surface backend 422 detail to operator instead of swallowing into generic "Merge failed" error (bd-7j6v1 — follow-up to bd-ct9wl).** The `httpClient.ts` `fetchJson` function already extracts the FastAPI `detail` field from non-2xx responses and throws it as `HttpError.message`, so `MergeChannelsModal` and `FindDuplicatesModal` already propagate the 422 detail string to the error banner through their `setError` catch handlers — no code change required. Added regression tests to lock this contract: `MergeChannelsModal.test.tsx` and `FindDuplicatesModal.test.tsx` each assert 422 detail renders verbatim in the error banner, a non-422 `HttpError` renders its message, and a non-`Error` thrown value falls back to the generic copy.

- **Plex user attribution (bd-r5f0c.2/r5f0c.4).** Connected Clients and User Stats now show Plex usernames for sessions routed through a Plex Media Server. Configure under Settings → Integrations → Plex Integration. Uses a server-local Plex token (not a plex.tv account token) scoped to a single server.
- **Jellyfin user attribution (bd-r5f0c.3/r5f0c.4).** Connected Clients and User Stats now show Jellyfin usernames for sessions routed through a Jellyfin server. Configure under Settings → Integrations → Jellyfin Integration.
- **Multi-viewer attribution display (bd-r5f0c.9).** When multiple users watch the same channel through the same media server simultaneously, all their names are listed in Connected Clients. The previous behaviour surfaced only the most-recent user's name; the new `*_viewers` JSON arrays capture every concurrent viewer. The legacy `*_user_name` singular fields remain for back-compat.
- **`AttributionBadge` UI component (bd-r5f0c.5).** Renders "via Emby" / "via Plex" / "via Jellyfin" badges in the Connected Clients list, keying on the `attribution_source` field in the stats API response.
- **Operator integrations documentation (bd-r5f0c.7).** `docs/user_guide/integrations/index.md` — side-by-side setup guide for all three media-server integrations, including Plex server-local token guidance, multi-viewer behaviour, privacy posture, and troubleshooting. Partially addresses bd-jn30g (Emby docs gap).

### Security

- **SSRF mitigation on test-connection endpoints (bd-r5f0c.4).** `POST /api/settings/test_emby_connection`, `POST /api/settings/test_plex_connection`, and `POST /api/settings/test_jellyfin_connection` now apply a scheme allowlist (`http`, `https`) and reconstruct URLs using netloc-only (no path interpolation), preventing SSRF via operator-supplied base URLs.

### Changed

- **`_resolve_emby_attributions` → `_resolve_attributions` (bd-r5f0c.4).** Multi-source `asyncio.gather` replacing the Emby-only implementation. The old name is preserved as an alias.
- **`_enrich_channels_with_emby` → `_enrich_channels_with_attribution` (bd-r5f0c.4).** The Active Channels endpoint now calls the unified multi-source helper. Legacy alias `_enrich_channels_with_emby` preserved for any external callers.

## [0.17.0] — 2026-05-16

### Added
- **Debug logging for the channel-edit-modal logo-selection path (bd-nh50y).** Operators reported "logos not loading in the edit-channel modal" but the existing code path had zero observability between the API response and the rendered grid — backend access logs showed `GET /api/channels/logos` returning 200 OK in ~60ms per page, the App.tsx pagination loop and EditChannelModal render path were silent, and the `<img onError>` handlers swapped `cache_url` → `url` without logging the failed URL. This bundle is **instrumentation only** — no fix attempt, deliberately, because the PO chose "add logging then diagnose" over a blind fix. **Backend (`backend/routers/channels.py` `get_logos`):** new single-line INFO diagnostic `[CHANNELS-LOGO] GET /logos page=X page_size=Y search=Q returned N logos in MMM.Mms next=true|false` fires once per request — operator-grepable, names every input field plus the result count, elapsed ms, and next-page flag. Per-row logging deliberately avoided (page_size=500 would flood). **Frontend API layer (`frontend/src/services/api.ts` `getLogos`):** wraps the fetch with DEBUG before (`[LogoApi] GET /logos page=X pageSize=Y search=Z`), DEBUG after (`[LogoApi] Received N logos, next=URL_OR_NULL`), and ERROR on catch (params + error). **Frontend pagination loop:** extracted to `api.getAllLogos(pageSize)` so the App.tsx and LogoManagerTab callsites share one log-line contract — INFO on start (`[LogoLoader] Starting logo load (pagination, pageSize=N)`), DEBUG per page (`[LogoLoader] Page N: fetched X logos, hasMore=Y`), INFO on completion (`[LogoLoader] Loaded TOTAL logos across PAGES pages`), ERROR on catch with the failing page number + partial-results length so operators see how much of the list was lost. **Modal (`frontend/src/components/EditChannelModal.tsx`):** mount DEBUG (`[LogoPicker] Mounted — received N logos in props`), `logos.length`-dependent useEffect (`[LogoPicker] logos prop updated — now N logos`) — `[logos.length]` not `[logos]` to avoid firing on every parent render — and render-time DEBUG (`[LogoPicker] Rendering grid: X filtered (of Y total) logos, search="Q"`). **Critical onError instrumentation:** the logo grid and current-logo preview `<img>` tags now log ERROR with the failed src + cache_url + url BEFORE the existing fallback chain fires, so silent 404s become visible — previously a broken `cache_url` swapped silently to `url` and a broken `url` produced no signal at all. **Test contracts:** two new frontend tests in `frontend/src/services/api.test.ts` (`getAllLogos (debug-logging contract — bd-nh50y)`) lock the happy-path log sequence across two pages and the mid-pagination-error contract (ERROR line must name failing page + partial-results length). One new backend test in `backend/tests/routers/test_channels.py` (`test_emits_per_request_info_diagnostic`) locks the INFO line shape against caplog. A future refactor that silently drops any of these log lines will fail the tests rather than dropping observability into production. **Operator action on the next repro**: reload the channel edit modal once the container update lands, then share browser DevTools console output (filter on `[LogoPicker]`, `[LogoLoader]`, `[LogoApi]`) and `docker logs ecm-ecm-1 | grep '\[CHANNELS-LOGO\]'` for the time of the repro. ([bd-nh50y])

### Fixed
- **Ghost source channels after "Merge channels" in edit mode (bd-ct9wl).** With edit mode active, selecting 2+ channels and choosing "Merge channels" merged them server-side (target channel got the streams, sources were deleted) but the source rows remained visible in the channels grid. The cause was the `useEditMode` working-copy sync effect only adding new channels — it never removed channels that disappeared from the API. The downstream consequence was operator-visible 404 noise: a user who then re-selected ghost rows for a second merge sent the now-stale source IDs to the backend, which forwarded the DELETEs to Dispatcharr and got `404 Not Found` per source (logged as `[DISPATCHARR] API request failed`), leaving the operator with no useful error to act on. The sync effect now also removes channels with persisted IDs that vanish from the API, while preserving locally-created not-yet-saved channels (negative temp IDs from `stageCreateChannel`) so an in-progress local-add does not get nuked by a routine API refresh. Two paired regression tests in `frontend/src/hooks/useEditMode.test.ts` lock the fix and the persisted-ID guard. Defense in depth: `/api/channels/merge` now catches a 404 from `get_channel(source_id)` and returns 422 with `"Source channels [...] no longer exist — refresh the channels list and try again"` instead of falling through to a generic 500. Backend regression test in `test_channels.py::TestMergeChannelsStaleSourceIds`.

- **Stats v2 "Buffering events" metric was a no-op on real installs (bd-ov5vb + bd-1x5v0).** ECM's previous "Buffering" metric returned 0 on every poll because Dispatcharr's `channel_buffering` event only fires on rare ffmpeg-speed threshold trips. The events that actually represent channel-health problems are `channel_reconnect`, `channel_error`, and `stream_switch` — which ECM was filtering out at the API call (`event_type=buffering` was passed as a hard query-param filter). This bundle broadens the ingest to all 4 event types, surfaces them as 4 distinct per-channel counters in `session_telemetry`, and reframes the Providers panel UI so operators see meaningful data. Discovered during the bd-p5b8i investigation when verifying buffer-event filter against the PO's live instance returned `fetched=0` on every poll (the PO's actual 100-event Dispatcharr window had zero `buffering` events but 8 `channel_reconnect`, 4 `channel_error`, and 3 `stream_switch`). ([bd-ov5vb], [bd-1x5v0])
  - **Backend ingest (`backend/bandwidth_tracker.py`):** renamed `_collect_buffer_events` → `_collect_channel_events`, dropped the `event_type=buffering` API filter, buckets the response client-side into four counters (`channel_buffering` / `channel_reconnect` / `channel_error` / `stream_switch`), and drops noise event types (`client_connect`, `login_success`, `epg_*`, `m3u_refresh`, `channel_start/stop`) BEFORE the LRU dedup lookup so the bounded working-set stays focused on the four health types. Return shape changes from `dict[str, int]` to `dict[str, dict[str, int]]`; the writer (`_write_session_telemetry`) now writes per-type counters with the same first-row-wins attribution rule the legacy buffer counter used. New regression-guard test asserts the helper never re-introduces the API-side `event_type` filter.
  - **Schema migration 0013 (`backend/alembic/versions/20260515_2000_0013_session_telemetry_channel_event_counters.py`):** adds `reconnect_event_count`, `error_event_count`, `switch_event_count` to `session_telemetry` as `INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`. The pre-existing `buffer_event_count` is preserved verbatim — pre-bd-ov5vb read paths (the GH-59 buffering chart, `SessionTelemetryProviderDaily.buffer_event_count`, `test_perf_provider_stats`) keep returning the same shape with no contract break. Migration follows the bd-5w6jz idempotency pattern: each column-add is independently guarded, partial drift (one or two columns already present from `create_all()`) returns cleanly without raising. Five new migration smoke tests cover fresh up, fresh down, full drift (all three columns pre-added), partial drift (1 column), partial drift (2 columns). Schema-parity boot-guard (`_assert_schema_matches_models`) locks model and head migration in sync; the existing `TestSessionTelemetryMigration::test_upgrade_creates_table_indexes_and_check` head-shape assertion is extended to require all three new columns. The migration's revision-id was bumped from 0012 → 0013 after rebase to avoid a collision with the bd-lbkck `task_schedules_interval_positive` migration that landed on `dev` concurrently.
  - **SLI log line:** renamed from `[STATS_V2] buffer_event_ingest fetched=X deduped=Y attributed=Z` to `[STATS_V2] channel_event_ingest fetched=X deduped=Y attributed_buffer=A attributed_reconnect=B attributed_error=C attributed_switch=D` so SRE's log-derived counter can grow per-type series. Per-channel "unattributed events" warning line gains a per-type breakdown for the same reason. The `[STATS_V2]` prefix is preserved.
  - **API response shape (`GET /api/stats/providers/buffering`):** additive change — the row shape now carries `reconnect_event_count`, `error_event_count`, `switch_event_count`, and a pre-summed `total_event_count` alongside the pre-bd-ov5vb `buffer_event_count`. URL path stays `/buffering` for back-compat with any external dashboard or alerting integration that hard-coded the path. Two new integration tests assert the four-counter shape and the pre-summed total.
  - **Providers panel UI (UX option A — single column with breakdown):** chart title relabeled "Buffering events by provider" → "Channel events by provider"; chart value switched from `buffer_event_count` to `total_event_count` (pre-summed by the backend so the render path stays simple); data-table fallback gains four per-type columns (`Reconnect | Error | Switch | Buffer | Total`) so screen-reader users see the same breakdown sighted users would get from hovering chart points; chart description names the four event types operators care about. Option A was chosen over option B (three separate columns) because it preserves the chart's visual real estate, keeps the legend coherent (one series per provider rather than per provider × event_type), and avoids breaking the visual-baseline screenshot more than necessary.
  - **Out of scope (filed as future work if discovered to matter):** stream-unhealthy log lines (`Stream unhealthy for channel X - no data for Ns`) are upstream-log-only — never written to `SystemEvent`, so not retrievable from any Dispatcharr API today; would require an upstream PR. Cursor-based polling (`since=<id>`) on the system-events endpoint would be cleaner than ECM's current offset-pagination but requires an upstream Dispatcharr PR; not blocking this bundle.

### Added
- **Schema CHECK constraint + Pydantic validator preventing `task_schedules` interval/0 placeholder rows (bd-lbkck, defense in depth for bd-p5b8i).** The DBA spike's verbatim conclusion on the bd-p5b8i scheduling subsystem regression: "interval/0 as a valid state is always a bug." Two-layer defense closes the bad-row attack surface so the placeholder bug Bundle H (bd-1weac) fixes cannot recur via backup-restore or a future code path. **Layer 1 — Alembic migration 0012** adds `ck_task_schedules_interval_positive` CHECK constraint: `schedule_type != 'interval' OR (interval_seconds IS NOT NULL AND interval_seconds > 0)`. SQLite enforces CHECK on INSERT/UPDATE so the placeholder bug becomes `IntegrityError` at the write call site instead of silently corrupting the scheduling subsystem. Migration uses `batch_alter_table.create_check_constraint` (SQLite has no `ALTER TABLE ADD CONSTRAINT`); pattern mirrors bd-gsn3r migration 0011. **Pre-flight repair**: the constraint cannot be added if existing rows violate it. Since Bundle H's `_heal_task_schedules_null_next_run_at` runs in `_run_migrations` AFTER `alembic upgrade head`, the migration cannot rely on Bundle H having already fixed the data. Pre-flight `DELETE` removes any violating row unconditionally; `task_registry._create_default_task_schedule` rebuilds correct-shape rows on the next startup sync. DELETE rowcount logged at WARNING for operator visibility. **bd-5w6jz idempotency**: re-run detection via substring check on `sqlite_master.sql` DDL for the constraint name; downgrade also skips cleanly when the constraint is already absent. **Smart-bootstrap fast-path caveat (documented in the migration docstring)**: `database._schema_matches_head` only inspects columns + tables, NOT constraints, so on long-running installs where every model column is already present, the fast-path stamps `alembic_version` forward without running this migration — the CHECK constraint does NOT land on existing operators' DBs. This is acceptable defense in depth: Layer 2 (the Pydantic validator) covers existing operators' write surface; the constraint primarily protects fresh installs and DBAS backup-restore (which initializes the target DB from scratch and runs migrations). **Layer 2 — Pydantic `model_validator(mode="after")`** on `TaskScheduleCreate` and `TaskScheduleUpdate` in `backend/routers/tasks.py` rejects `schedule_type='interval'` with NULL or `<=0` `interval_seconds` at the API surface (HTTP 422 with message `"interval_seconds must be > 0 when schedule_type is 'interval'"`). The PATCH handler in `update_task_schedule` adds a final cross-check against the loaded row to cover the half-PATCH case where only `interval_seconds=0` arrives (no `schedule_type` in the body) and the existing row is already an interval schedule — that case can't be caught at the request-shape validator without DB context. **Regression lock**: `TestMigration0012` in `test_alembic_smoke.py` covers fresh up, drift (pre-existing interval/0 rows repaired pre-add), and idempotency (re-run is a no-op). New API tests in `test_tasks.py` cover all five rejection paths (POST with interval/0, POST with interval/NULL, PATCH with interval/0 in same body, PATCH with type-change-only into interval, PATCH with interval_seconds-only=0 onto existing interval row). ([bd-lbkck])
- **Task scheduler health observability (bd-qxi02, recommendation from bd-p5b8i SRE spike).** The scheduling subsystem was silently broken for 39+ days on the PO's install (4 months across all operators) and existing observability (`ecm_telemetry_rollup_last_success_timestamp` from bd-7i2vv) did not catch it — that gauge fired only for rollup-specific tasks, no alert rule consumed it, and no Prometheus deployment was ingesting the metric. Three layers of "the signal exists, nobody sees it." This bundle closes the detection gap with two new generic gauges in `backend/observability.py`: `ecm_task_schedule_last_success_timestamp{task_id}` (Unix-epoch seconds of the most recent successful execution per task, label cardinality bounded by the task_registry's ~15 code-constant task IDs) — stamped by `task_engine._execute_task` immediately after every `success=True` TaskResult via the new defensive `observability.record_task_success(task_id)` helper — and `ecm_task_schedule_next_run_null_count` (label-free count of `task_schedules` rows where `next_run_at IS NULL AND enabled=1 AND schedule_type != 'manual'`) — the canonical "scheduler subsystem is broken" signal, the exact bd-p5b8i symptom. The null-count gauge is published at startup post-`_run_migrations` in `database.init_db` and re-emitted on every `TaskRegistry.sync_from_database` (so Bundle H's heal is visible on the next scrape without a container restart), via the new `observability.update_task_schedule_null_count(count=None)` helper that lazily queries the DB when no count is provided. Both helpers are defensive — observability instrumentation must not break the task engine, registry sync, or boot path it's wrapping; failures log at DEBUG. New alert group `ecm_task_scheduler` in `docs/sre/prometheus_rules.yaml` ships 5 alerts and 1 recording rule: `ECMTaskSchedulerNextRunNull` (count > 0 for 5m → page — but ONLY useful AFTER Bundle H's heal ships; pre-heal it would page on every existing operator's first scrape), per-task staleness warnings sized at 2× each task's documented cadence (`ECMTaskScheduleStaleStatsRollup` 25h budget, `ECMTaskScheduleStaleCleanup` 8d, `ECMTaskScheduleStaleM3UMonitor` 12h, `ECMTaskScheduleStaleStreamProbe` 48h), and recording rule `ecm:task_schedule:hours_since_success` for dashboard / ad-hoc PromQL stability. New SLO entry in `docs/sre/slos.md` ("Capacity planning: Task scheduler health") documents the capacity-planning posture (not a numbered SLO — operator-configurable task cadence makes a portable commitment impossible) with explicit deploy-order callout. New runbook `docs/runbooks/task_scheduler_stalled.md` covers the NULL `next_run_at` diagnostic SQL, Bundle H heal recovery path, manual one-shot recovery (UPDATE setting `next_run_at = now+1m` for healing pre-Bundle-H installs), per-task triage from `task_executions` history, and validation queries to confirm recovery. 14 new unit tests in `backend/tests/unit/test_observability_task_scheduler.py` lock the gauge contract (install, label cardinality, per-task isolation, last-write-wins semantics, defensive no-raise on bad input), 2 new integration tests in `backend/tests/integration/test_task_engine_success_metric.py` lock the success-path wiring (success TaskResult stamps the gauge; failure TaskResult does NOT). Bundles H, I, J are parallel — this metrics + alerts bundle works regardless of when they land, but the page-severity NextRunNull alert should only be enabled in production AFTER Bundle H deploys the heal. ([bd-qxi02], [bd-p5b8i])
- **`ecm_database_size_bytes` + `ecm_database_wal_size_bytes` Prometheus gauges (bd-ygoqr).** Two new label-free gauges in `backend/observability.py` expose the on-disk size of `journal.db` and `journal.db-wal` so operators can answer "is my DB growing?" without shelling into the container. Sampling is wired at the two points that already do real DB I/O: `database._perform_maintenance` (after the boot-time VACUUM) and `tasks.cleanup.CleanupTask.execute` (after the weekly VACUUM, regardless of VACUUM success — the operator cares more about "what is it now?" than "did the most recent VACUUM succeed?"). Cost per emit is two `os.path.getsize` calls — single syscalls each. Companion alert rules in `docs/sre/prometheus_rules.yaml` (new group `ecm_database_size`): `ECMDatabaseSizeWarn` (body > 500 MB / 30m / warning), `ECMDatabaseSizePage` (body > 2 GB / 10m / page), `ECMDatabaseWALSizeWarn` (WAL > 200 MB / 15m / warning), `ECMDatabaseSizeGrowthAnomaly` (week-over-week delta > 200 MB / 24h / warning). New recording rule `ecm:database_size_bytes:weekly_delta` is the stable input for the growth-anomaly alert. New SLO entry in `docs/sre/slos.md` documents this as capacity-planning class (NOT a numbered SLO — disk-pressure failure modes are environment-dependent and ECM cannot make a portable commitment about them). New runbook `docs/runbooks/database-size-warn.md` covers the WAL-vs-body triage table, the `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` recovery, the per-table size-attribution query via `dbstat`, and the explicit step for "operator on pre-bd-ygoqr persisted MANUAL schedule" who needs to flip the cleanup task to CRON via the Settings UI. Cross-references bd-ej995 (WAL checkpoint at startup, in-flight), bd-7i2vv (Stats v2 retention), and bd-dmu8w (journal-entry retention). Six new unit tests in `backend/tests/unit/test_database_size_metrics.py` lock the helper contract on a synthetic file layout (both files present, WAL absent, body absent, defensive no-raise on uninitialized registry, render output, default-path resolution from `database.JOURNAL_DB_FILE`). ([bd-ygoqr])
- **v0.17.0-0035 polish round (bd-9rtlc + bd-8axhi):** corrected the `TOUCHPOINTS` type annotation in `scripts/check_version_consistency.py` from the `callable` predicate to `Callable[[str], str | None]` (added `from collections.abc import Callable`), added a parametrized regression test in `backend/tests/unit/test_check_version_consistency.py` (`test_main_surfaces_both_files_on_simultaneous_mismatch`) that mirrors the actual production divergence — `package.json="0.17.0-0034"`, `backup.py="0.17.0-0033"`, `main.py="0.16.0-0003"` — and asserts the diagnostic surfaces both divergent file names + values plus names the canonical reference, and added an inline scope comment above the `isinstance(e, OperationalError) and _is_schema_drift_error(e)` gate in `backend/bandwidth_tracker.py` documenting that the gate is deliberately SQLAlchemy-only (sqlite3 errors arrive wrapped) while the helper itself walks the chain so future scope expansions only need to loosen the gate. Per code review.
- **CI guard against version-bump skew across the three hand-edited touchpoints.** The version literal lives in `frontend/package.json` (canonical), `backend/routers/backup.py` (`APP_VERSION`), and `backend/main.py` (FastAPI `version=` kwarg). Today's bump workflow requires editing all three in lockstep; historically it didn't — PR #277 caught `backend/routers/backup.py` at `"0.16.0"` while `frontend/package.json` had been bumped 27 times to `"0.17.0-0027"`, and the bd-9rtlc audit grep surfaced a second long-standing skew where `backend/main.py` was at `"0.16.0-0003"` while `frontend/package.json` was at `"0.17.0-0033"` (the FastAPI version only shows in the OpenAPI schema, which no external consumer cited, so nobody noticed for ~30 builds). New script `scripts/check_version_consistency.py` reads each touchpoint, compares against the canonical `frontend/package.json` value, and exits non-zero with a diagnostic naming the divergent file + both versions on mismatch. New `version-consistency` job in `.github/workflows/test.yml` runs the script on every push + PR to `main`/`dev`, so the next missed bump fails the PR before merge. Both pre-existing skews are corrected at this bump (all three touchpoints now `"0.17.0-0034"`). When a fourth touchpoint is added, document it in the new "Touchpoints" section of `docs/versioning.md` and add it to `TOUCHPOINTS` in the script. 13 new unit tests in `backend/tests/unit/test_check_version_consistency.py` lock the extractor + main() exit-code contract on a synthetic repo layout (so the tests don't churn on every version bump). ([bd-9rtlc])
- Visual regression coverage for the Stats v2 Channels-by-Provider heatmap. New Playwright config (`playwright.visual.config.ts`) screenshots the `Heatmap` chart primitive in isolation via a standalone Vite fixture page (`frontend/visual-fixture-heatmap.html` + `frontend/src/visual-fixture/heatmap-fixture.tsx`) and diffs against a committed baseline PNG (`e2e/visual/__screenshots__/heatmap.spec.ts/heatmap-providers-panel.png`). The fixture mounts only the `Heatmap` with a deterministic dataset (6 providers including "Unknown" × 24 channels including the ~145px-wide "917 | Milwaukee Brewers" label that exposed `bd-wdpve`) so the rendered pixels are reproducible across runs and OSes. Tolerance is `maxDiffPixels: 500` — empirically catches the bd-wdpve-class regression (~3360 differing pixels when the column-label rotation is wrong) while tolerating cross-OS font-hinting noise (<100 differing pixels). New CI job `Visual Regression` in `.github/workflows/test.yml` installs Chromium with the standard `actions/cache` Playwright pattern and runs the visual gate on every PR — defense in depth on top of the existing jsdom-based DOM rotation lock in `Heatmap.test.tsx`, which catches the rotation *attribute* but not the rendered *pixels*. The fixture page is dev-server-only and does not appear in `vite build` output. Follow-up to `bd-wdpve`. ([bd-lln2x])
- Stats v2 Active Channels live stream-identity badge: `GET /api/stats/channels` now enriches each active-channel row with `stream_name` and `m3u_account_id` resolved at request time via the same resolver chain the BandwidthTracker poll uses (direct `stream_id` → URL-derived → channel-streams cross-ref). UI: StatsTab Active Channels section renders a `[<provider>] - <stream_name>` badge next to the channel name (e.g., `[Infinity] - US: TNT`) using `streamLabel` helper from `bd-kh23e` and the `getM3UAccounts` side-load pattern from `bd-vjv7k`. Edge cases: when `m3u_account_id` is unmapped or NULL, falls back to bare `<stream_name>` (no `[Provider <id>]` leak); when `stream_name` equals the channel display name, suppresses the badge to avoid duplication; when both are absent, no badge. Shared resolver entry point `resolve_active_channel_streams()` extracted to `bandwidth_tracker.py` as a free function; the endpoint instantiates a fresh `ChannelStreamsCache` per request and passes `emit_metrics=False` so on-demand calls don't dilute the cyclic-poll SLI signal owned by the polling hot path. Closes the "I don't see the stream name next to the active channels" gap (`bd-kh23e` covered historical aggregates only, not the live view). ([bd-ox5q8])
- Stats v2 stream-identity capture: `session_telemetry` persists `stream_id` and `stream_name` per row (Alembic `0010`). Resolver refactored to return a `ProviderResolution(provider_id, stream_id, stream_name)` NamedTuple per channel. APIs `GET /api/stats/watch-time/{user_id}` and `GET /api/stats/providers/channel-heatmap` gain `latest_stream_id` + `latest_stream_name` (picked via `MAX(observed_at)` per channel / per `(provider, channel)`). UI: Users panel per-user breakdown adds a "Stream" column; Providers panel heatmap data-table shows `[<provider>] - <stream>` (e.g., `[Infinity] - US: TNT`). Format helper at `frontend/src/utils/formatting.ts` falls back to bare stream name when provider context isn't available. Closes the "we don't show which stream is playing" gap. ([bd-kh23e])

### Fixed
- **Root-cause fix: CRON-default scheduled tasks have been silently not firing on every operator install for 4 months (bd-1weac, bd-p5b8i, GH #243).** `task_registry.TaskRegistry._create_default_task_schedule` was added in v0.8.7-0023 (2026-01-29) when every registered task had either an `INTERVAL` or `MANUAL` default. The CRON branch fell through to a default that wrote `schedule_type='interval', interval_seconds=0` into `task_schedules`. `schedule_calculator.calculate_next_run` returns `None` for `interval_seconds <= 0` (see `_calculate_interval_next_run`), so the row's `next_run_at` column was `NULL` from the moment it was written. `task_engine.check_and_run_tasks` filters `WHERE next_run_at IS NOT NULL` (see `TaskEngine.check_and_run_tasks`), so the row exists in the DB and shows up in the Settings → Tasks UI but **never fires**. Tasks have been silently not firing on every operator install for 4 months due to bd-p5b8i; this restores execution. Affected operators see the heal log line on next container restart; no operator action required. **Affected tasks**: `CleanupTask` (after bd-ygoqr flipped its default to CRON `0 2 * * 0`), `StatsV2RollupTask` (since it landed at CRON `30 3 * * *` per bd-7i2vv) — and any future task that ships with a CRON default. The bd-ygoqr fix flipped the constructor default to CRON but never reached the load-bearing `task_schedules` row that `task_engine` actually reads; bd-ifmr5's database migration flipped existing-operator `scheduled_tasks` rows to CRON but again never reached `task_schedules`. **Why not an Alembic migration**: same constraint as bd-ifmr5 — the bd-5w6jz smart-bootstrap fast-path in `database._bootstrap_alembic` stamps `alembic_version` forward when `_schema_matches_head()` returns True, so an Alembic data migration would be SILENTLY SKIPPED on every existing v0.17.0 install. The `_run_migrations` pattern in `backend/database.py` runs unconditionally on every startup with WHERE-clause idempotency, which is exactly the shape this fix needs. **Fix scope**: (1) `task_registry._create_default_task_schedule` now delegates CRON parsing to `database._convert_cron_to_schedule` (the same helper that powers the v0.8.7 `scheduled_tasks → task_schedules` migration), so a `CleanupTask` CRON `0 2 * * 0` produces a `task_schedules` row at `schedule_type='weekly', days_of_week='0', schedule_time='02:00'` with `next_run_at` populated, and a `StatsV2RollupTask` CRON `30 3 * * *` produces `schedule_type='daily', schedule_time='03:30'` with `next_run_at` populated. Unknown cron expressions fall back to daily at the configured time (no longer the interval/0 silent-skip trap). (2) New `_heal_task_schedules_null_next_run_at()` helper wired into `_run_migrations` after `_migrate_cleanup_task_manual_to_cron`. It scans `task_schedules` for `next_run_at IS NULL AND enabled = 1`, identifies rows in the pre-fix `interval/0` shape and rewrites them from the registered task class's `schedule_config`, and for operator-customised schedules (e.g., the cleanup row was set to `daily 06:30 America/New_York` via the UI but `next_run_at` is NULL for a separate reason) just recomputes `next_run_at` from the existing columns — operator intent survives the heal. Operator-visible INFO log `[DATABASE] Healed N task_schedules row(s) with NULL next_run_at — class defaults rehydrated (bd-1weac)` fires only when N > 0, so the second container restart and every restart thereafter is silent (same contract as bd-ifmr5). Disabled rows (legitimately NULL by design — no point computing a run time for a disabled schedule) are NOT touched. **Regression lock**: 12 new unit tests across two files. `backend/tests/unit/test_task_registry_cron_default_schedule.py` (5 tests) locks the Part 1 write-path fix: `test_cron_weekly_translates_for_cleanup_task_sunday_0200`, `test_cron_daily_translates_for_stats_v2_rollup_03_30`, `test_interval_passthrough_unchanged_for_m3u_change_monitor` (no-regression guard for the path the bug didn't touch), `test_noop_when_row_already_exists` (idempotency — preserves operator UI customisations), and `test_pre_fix_symptom_does_not_recur_no_interval_0_no_null_next_run` (direct sentinel against the bd-p5b8i shape). `backend/tests/unit/test_heal_task_schedules_null_next_run_at.py` (7 tests) locks the Part 2 heal: `test_heal_rewrites_broken_cleanup_row_to_weekly_with_next_run_at` (the GH #243 happy path for existing operators), `test_heal_rewrites_broken_stats_v2_rollup_row_to_daily`, `test_heal_is_idempotent_across_restarts` (the production contract — second run logs nothing), `test_heal_preserves_disabled_rows`, `test_heal_preserves_healthy_interval_row` (no-regression guard for m3u_change_monitor and other interval/300 tasks), `test_heal_preserves_operator_customised_schedule_only_recomputes_next_run` (operator intent survives), and `test_heal_noop_when_table_missing` (defensive, matches the rest of this file's table-existence guards). All tests run against real file-backed SQLite (bd-ej995 pattern) — not mocks, so the UPDATE is observably hitting actual SQLite. **Operator recovery**: automatic on next container restart — no manual SQL, no settings change, no UI workaround. ([bd-1weac], [bd-p5b8i], GH #243)
- **`auto_creation_executions` BLOB + `health_checks` + `notifications` retention added to `CleanupTask` (bd-ia28g, bd-p5b8i DBA spike attribution correction).** The bd-f9gd8 spike's `session_telemetry` retention story was the wrong target — `session_telemetry` already has its own retention via `StatsV2RollupTask` (bd-7i2vv). The bd-p5b8i DBA re-attribution against the PO's 165MB operator DB found the actual large tables: **`auto_creation_executions` BLOBs are 127MB / 77% of the DB**, `health_checks` is 20MB / 53k rows / 14%, and `notifications` is 2.2MB. None of these three had retention. This bundle adds three new prune blocks to `backend/tasks/cleanup.py` against the actual offenders, with three companion config keys exposed via `get_config()` / `update_config()` and editable from Settings → Tasks → Database Cleanup (`TaskEditorModal.tsx`).
  - **Block 1 — auto_creation_executions BLOB null-out (`auto_creation_blob_days`, default 30):** the four large JSON BLOB columns (`execution_log`, `dry_run_results`, `created_entities`, `modified_entities`) on rows older than the cutoff are NULLed via a single `UPDATE ... SET ... WHERE started_at < cutoff AND (execution_log IS NOT NULL OR dry_run_results IS NOT NULL OR created_entities IS NOT NULL OR modified_entities IS NOT NULL)`. The summary row (id, status, started_at, completed_at, statistics counts) is **preserved** for audit history per DBA recommendation — operators keep their "what ran when, what was the result" trail while the multi-MB per-row payloads age out. The OR'd `IS NOT NULL` filter across all four BLOB columns is the idempotency gate: a second cleanup pass on the same row is a strict no-op (rowcount=0), and the gate correctly catches the empty-execution-log / non-empty-entities shape that `auto_creation_engine.set_execution_log(log)` produces when the per-stream log list is empty but channels were still created (filtering on `execution_log` alone would miss those rows). **Operator log line distinguishes deleted vs nulled:** the post-run summary now reads `Cleanup completed. Deleted N records, nulled M auto-creation BLOBs.` — `auto_creation_blobs_pruned` is excluded from the rolled-up "deleted" count because nulling a BLOB column is not the same as deleting a row.
  - **Block 2 — health_checks DELETE (`health_checks_days`, default 7):** the `health_checks` table is **NOT** in `models.py` — it's a legacy table created by the v0.11.6 Background Health Check Service spec (bd-lq8dq.1.13). The service itself was removed from the codebase, but the table persists on long-running installs (53k rows / 20MB on the GH #243 operator). The prune uses raw SQL with an `inspect()`-based table-existence check so fresh installs (which never had the legacy table) are silent no-ops, not error-noise. The default of 7 days follows the DBA recommendation: high-frequency polling data loses diagnostic value quickly. The block auto-detects which timestamp column is present (`created_at` → `checked_at` → `timestamp`) since the legacy schema didn't standardize, falling back to skip-with-warning if none match. The dynamic-column DELETE is documented inline as safe because `ts_column` is drawn from a hardcoded allowlist tuple, not user input.
  - **Block 3 — notifications DELETE (`notifications_days`, default 30):** uses each row's `expires_at` if set (auto-expiring notifications honor their own TTL), otherwise falls back to `created_at` + cutoff. This avoids deleting a notification deliberately given a long `expires_at` ("keep this for 90 days") just because it happens to be older than 30 days by `created_at`. The SQL OR-clause encodes this exactly: `(expires_at IS NOT NULL AND expires_at < now) OR (expires_at IS NULL AND created_at < cutoff)`.

  **Frontend:** three new number inputs added to the existing retention-fields section in `TaskEditorModal.tsx` for the `cleanup` task, matching the established `retention-item` / `form-hint` pattern; each input documents what its block does so operators understand the trade-off before lowering the value. **Tests:** new file `backend/tests/unit/test_cleanup_task_blob_health_notifications_retention.py` with 16 tests across real file-backed SQLite (bd-ej995 pattern, not mocks) covering: config schema round-trip (3 tests), BLOB null-out happy path + fresh-row preservation + idempotency + the null-execution-log/populated-entities regression (4 tests), health_checks delete + absent-table no-op + alternate-timestamp-column + idempotency (4 tests), notifications by created_at + by expired TTL + future-TTL protection + idempotency (4 tests), and one end-to-end test asserting all 7 cleanup steps run (was 4: probe + tasks + journal + vacuum). Companion frontend tests in `frontend/src/components/TaskEditorModal.test.tsx` (4 tests) lock the three input render contracts and the save-handler round-trip into `api.updateTask`. **Operator recovery:** automatic on next weekly cleanup cron run (Sunday 02:00 UTC per bd-ygoqr default) — no manual SQL, no settings change required. Operators with persisted MANUAL cleanup schedules are migrated to CRON by bd-ifmr5 (already shipped at v0.17.0-0041), so this fix lands for the GH #243 population that needed it most. ([bd-ia28g], [bd-p5b8i])
- **Existing installs now actually get the bd-ygoqr CRON default (bd-ifmr5, GH #243 follow-up).** _Note: this `scheduled_tasks` data flip didn't reach existing operators because of the broader bd-p5b8i `task_schedules` placeholder bug. The bd-1weac fix above is the load-bearing piece that actually starts cleanup running for existing operators — bd-ifmr5 flipped the right source-of-truth (`scheduled_tasks`) but `task_engine` reads `task_schedules`, which stayed at the pre-fix interval/0/NULL placeholder shape until bd-1weac shipped._ bd-ygoqr (PR #289) changed the `CleanupTask` constructor default from `ScheduleType.MANUAL` to `ScheduleType.CRON` (Sunday 02:00 UTC) so journal entries (90d hot retention per bd-dmu8w), task-execution history (30d default), and failed/timeout `stream_stats` rows (30d default) actually enforce. But that fix is reached ONLY on **fresh** installs — every existing install (including the PO's own 130MB instance and the original GH #243 reporter's container) has a persisted `scheduled_tasks` row written at first-boot with `schedule_type='manual'`, and `task_registry.TaskRegistry.sync_from_database` faithfully rehydrates it into the constructor, so the bd-ygoqr default is never reached for exactly the population the fix was for. **Why not an Alembic migration**: the bd-5w6jz smart-bootstrap fast-path in `database._bootstrap_alembic` stamps `alembic_version` forward when `_schema_matches_head()` returns True (every model table + column exists). For every existing v0.17.0 install, the `scheduled_tasks` table + columns are already present, so a regular Alembic data migration would be SILENTLY SKIPPED by the fast-path's stamp-forward — the data fix would never apply. The codebase's `_run_migrations` pattern in `backend/database.py` runs unconditionally on every startup with idempotency enforced via WHERE clauses, which is exactly the shape this fix needs (same pattern as `_migrate_normalize_names_to_normalization_group_ids`, `_add_unique_client_connections_user_columns`, and the rest of the in-place data migrations). **Fix scope**: new `_migrate_cleanup_task_manual_to_cron()` helper wired into `_run_migrations` after `_add_unique_client_connections_user_columns`. SQL is one statement — `UPDATE scheduled_tasks SET schedule_type='cron', cron_expression='0 2 * * 0' WHERE task_id='cleanup' AND schedule_type='manual'` — and the WHERE clause is the idempotency gate: already-CRON, already-INTERVAL, and absent-row installs are all natural no-ops. Operator-visible INFO log `[DATABASE] Flipped cleanup task schedule MANUAL -> CRON Sunday 02:00 UTC for N existing operator(s) (bd-ifmr5)` fires only when the rowcount is non-zero, so the second container restart and every restart thereafter is silent (no false "your schedule got changed again" signal in logs). **Operator caveat — read this before upgrading**: if you deliberately set Database Cleanup to MANUAL via Settings → Tasks (rare — most operators never touched the schedule because the UI didn't surface the retention consequence), this one-time migration will flip you to CRON Sunday 02:00 UTC. Re-set MANUAL from Settings → Tasks after upgrading; the migration assumes MANUAL was the historical default (it was) and not an explicit operator choice (it usually wasn't, per GH #243). **Regression lock**: 6 new unit tests in `backend/tests/unit/test_cleanup_task_manual_to_cron_migration.py` against real file-backed SQLite (the bd-ej995 fixture pattern — not mocks, so the UPDATE is observably hitting actual SQLite): `test_flips_existing_manual_row_to_cron_and_logs_count` (the GH #243 happy path), `test_preserves_existing_cron_row_with_different_expression` (operator's custom CRON like `30 4 * * *` survives untouched), `test_preserves_existing_interval_row` (INTERVAL stays INTERVAL), `test_noop_when_no_cleanup_row_exists` (fresh install — constructor default already handles it, migration must not invent a row), `test_is_idempotent_across_restarts` (the production contract — second run logs nothing, third run logs nothing, …), and `test_noop_when_scheduled_tasks_table_missing` (defensive — matches the rest of this file's table-existence guards). **Operator recovery**: automatic on next container restart — no manual SQL, no settings change, no `UPDATE scheduled_tasks SET schedule_type='cron' WHERE …` workaround. ([bd-ifmr5], GH #243)
- **Startup WAL checkpoint prevents bloated-WAL migrations from tripping the Docker health check.** A user (GH #274) reported a long-running install where `journal.db-wal` had grown to 1.4 GB+ — typically because some connection had been pinning a read transaction across the SQLite auto-checkpointer's would-be merge window. The bloated WAL stretches every read because SQLite walks the WAL to satisfy the read, and the v0.16.0 `normalize_names → normalization_group_ids` migration took 15-20s to complete. With the Dockerfile HEALTHCHECK `start_period` at the upstream default of 5s, the container was marked unhealthy mid-migration; `ecm-mcp` (which `depends_on: { ecm: { condition: service_healthy } }`) refused to start. The rule_lint_findings half of GH #274 was fixed by bd-ax3uj (PR #280) — this entry closes the WAL half. **Fix scope**: new `_wal_checkpoint_truncate()` helper in `backend/database.py` runs `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` as the first action in `init_db` after the engine is created — BEFORE `_bootstrap_alembic` and `_assert_schema_matches_models` so the migration timeline runs against a clean baseline. Pattern + try/except shape lifted from `backend/routers/backup.py:_create_backup_zip`, which already does this same checkpoint before backup. Logs WAL size before/after at INFO (`[DATABASE] WAL checkpoint: X.X MB -> Y.Y MB`) for operator visibility. Failure mode (file lock, disk full, read-only filesystem) is non-fatal — WARN-and-continue matches the backup.py posture; bootstrap will still run, just against the same bloated WAL the user is already living with. **Regression lock**: 4 new unit tests in `backend/tests/unit/test_database_bootstrap.py::TestWalCheckpointTruncate` — `test_truncate_reduces_dirty_wal_to_zero_bytes` (real file-backed SQLite + pinned reader fixture so the WAL is genuinely dirty pre-call; asserts WAL shrinks to 0 bytes post-call — mocks alone would not have proved SQLite actually truncated), `test_truncate_is_safe_when_no_wal_file_exists` (fresh-install path), `test_truncate_failure_is_non_fatal` (broken-engine raise must be swallowed as WARN), `test_init_db_invokes_wal_checkpoint_before_bootstrap` (ordering contract — captures the call order via a side-effect list so a future refactor cannot silently invert WAL-checkpoint vs bootstrap-alembic). **Operator recovery**: automatic on next container restart — no settings change, no manual SQL. **Polish round (post-review)**: `_wal_checkpoint_truncate` now inspects the `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` return tuple `(busy, log, checkpointed)` and logs at WARNING (`[DATABASE] WAL checkpoint: X.X MB -> Y.Y MB (incomplete -- WAL busy)`) when `busy=1` — SQLite couldn't acquire the exclusive WAL lock and the WAL was NOT fully truncated, which is exactly the GH #274 disease vector and must not hide behind an INFO log. Same return-inspection extended to `backend/routers/backup.py:_create_backup_zip` for consistency (a busy checkpoint there means the zipped journal.db may not contain the most recent writes). New regression tests `test_busy_checkpoint_logs_warning_not_info` (unit) and `test_wal_checkpoint_busy_logs_warning` (router) lock the WARN-on-busy contract on both call sites. Test fixture `_build_engine_with_dirty_wal` reorganized so `reader.close()` runs in a guaranteed-execution `finally` block instead of leaking until GC if `writer.executemany` raises. Per code review. ([bd-ej995])
- **Docker HEALTHCHECK `start_period` bumped 5s -> 120s to absorb edge-case slow migrations.** Defense in depth on top of bd-ej995's startup WAL checkpoint: even after the WAL truncate, a particularly large migration on a long-running install may still take longer than the upstream-default 5s window. The Dockerfile `HEALTHCHECK` directive (the canonical source — there is no `docker-compose.yml` in the repo; image consumers pick up the baked-in healthcheck) now uses `--start-period=120s` with an inline comment documenting the rationale: WAL checkpoint addresses the common case; this start_period absorbs the edge case where a particularly large migration still runs longer than the default. Bumped consistently across `Dockerfile`, `Dockerfile.dev`, and the README docker-compose example block (which gains a `healthcheck:` comment pointing operators at the override pattern if their installs have consistently fast startups). Operators on installs with consistent fast startups can lower this; the default favors safety over startup time. ([bd-ul0ah])
- **`CleanupTask` default schedule flipped from MANUAL → CRON (Sunday 02:00 UTC) for fresh installs (bd-ygoqr).** _Note: this default-flip didn't reach existing operators because of the broader bd-p5b8i `task_schedules` placeholder bug. The bd-1weac fix above is the load-bearing piece that actually starts cleanup running for existing operators — the constructor change here was correct, but `task_registry._create_default_task_schedule` was miscompiling the new CRON default into a `task_schedules` row that `task_engine` silently skipped._ The single most impactful fix in v0.17.0 polish for GH #243 per the bd-f9gd8 DBA spike: a fresh-install ECM previously left the database cleanup task on `ScheduleType.MANUAL`, so journal entries (90d hot retention per bd-dmu8w), task-execution history (30d default), and failed/timeout `stream_stats` rows (30d default) accumulated unboundedly until the operator discovered the Settings → Tasks panel and configured a schedule by hand. The constructor's default in `backend/tasks/cleanup.py:CleanupTask.__init__` now ships as `ScheduleType.CRON` with cron expression `0 2 * * 0`. **Existing operators are NOT clobbered**: `task_registry.TaskRegistry.sync_from_database` rehydrates the persisted `ScheduleConfig` from the `ScheduledTask` DB row and passes it to the constructor, so the new CRON default is reached ONLY on installs that have no DB row yet (the `if schedule_config is None` branch). Six new unit tests in `backend/tests/unit/test_cleanup_task_default_schedule.py` lock both halves of the contract: fresh installs get the CRON default; explicit MANUAL/INTERVAL/CRON-with-other-cron configs are preserved across reconstruction; and `sync_from_database` round-trips a persisted MANUAL config without falling through to the new CRON default. **Operator guidance (per DBA spike):** the weekly VACUUM in `CleanupTask.execute()` holds an exclusive SQLite lock for seconds-to-minutes on multi-GB databases, which can disrupt streaming. The Sunday 02:00 UTC default is chosen to put the lock in an off-hours window. If your DB is large enough that the weekly VACUUM disrupts streaming OR if your install runs at non-US-aligned hours, override the schedule via Settings → Tasks → Database Cleanup. The new `ecm_database_size_bytes` gauge (above) and the `ECMDatabaseSizeGrowthAnomaly` alert give operators the leading indicator that they may want to do this. Bundle D's bd-ul0ah bumped the healthcheck `start_period` to 120s, which absorbs the worst case for the very first VACUUM after a long-running install upgrades to this default. **Polish round (post-review, v0.17.0-0040):** VACUUM moved off `session.execute` to a raw `engine.connect()` context (matching the established `database._perform_maintenance` pattern) — the pre-existing SQLAlchemy 2.0 implicit-transaction hazard would have raised `OperationalError: cannot VACUUM from within a transaction` on every fresh-install weekly cron run, hidden until now because every cleanup test set `task.vacuum_db = False`; the MANUAL→CRON flip raised the risk profile high enough that the polish round had to fix it. New regression test `TestCleanupTaskVacuumExecutesAgainstRealEngine.test_vacuum_completes_without_operational_error` exercises the real VACUUM path against a file-backed SQLite engine WITHOUT disabling `vacuum_db` (with an explicit sentinel asserting it stays enabled, so a future maintainer can't accidentally re-mask the bug). Companion fix: added `slo: database_capacity` label to all four `ecm_database_size` alerts in `docs/sre/prometheus_rules.yaml` (`ECMDatabaseSizeWarn`, `ECMDatabaseSizePage`, `ECMDatabaseWALSizeWarn`, `ECMDatabaseSizeGrowthAnomaly`) — every other alert group in the file carries an `slo:` label, and Alertmanager routing rules that match on `slo=~".*"` would have silently dropped these (matches `ecm_stats_v2_storage`'s `slo: stats_v2_storage` style for the other capacity-planning class without a numbered SLO). Per code review. ([bd-ygoqr])
- **Defense-in-depth for the hot-deploy hazard: BandwidthTracker now loud-fails on runtime schema drift.** The bd-zaaey loud-fail at `init_db` (`_assert_schema_matches_models`) catches model-vs-DB column drift at boot, but cannot catch drift introduced AFTER the process is already running — for example, the hot-deploy workflow where a developer `docker cp`'s a new alembic migration + writer code into a live container without `docker restart`. The boot guard already passed on the previous restart; the runtime drift surfaces only when the new writer first executes against the still-unmigrated schema, raising `OperationalError: no such column: <name>`. Until now the writer's existing try/except swallowed this as WARN, where it blended into ordinary noise and the telemetry stream silently dropped rows for hours. New `_is_schema_drift_error()` helper detects the SQLite signature (`"no such column"` / `"no such table"`, walking the exception chain so a SQLAlchemy wrapper around a sqlite3 inner is detected on either layer), and `_write_session_telemetry`'s except block escalates the FIRST such error to ERROR-level with an actionable recovery path naming `docker restart ecm-ecm-1` and pointing at bd-zaaey for the structural diagnostic. A one-shot armed flag (`self._schema_drift_alarm_armed`) prevents log flooding — subsequent failures fall back to WARN while the operator repairs the container, and the alarm re-arms on the next successful write so a future drift event triggers another ERROR rather than silently degrading. Non-drift `OperationalError`s (e.g., `database is locked`) and unrelated `RuntimeError`s stay at the existing bd-skqln.12 WARN level — only the schema-drift signature escalates. 10 new unit tests in `backend/tests/unit/test_bandwidth_tracker_schema_drift.py` lock the detector behavior and the escalate/suppress/re-arm contract. ([bd-8axhi])
- **Stats v2 Providers heatmap column labels now render fully (bd-wdpve).** bd-yteek shipped `rotate(-45)` for column labels; in SVG's y-down coordinate system that sends rotated text DOWN-AND-LEFT from the pivot — directly onto the cells below the label band, leaving only the rightmost 1-2 chars visible at the anchor. Fix: flips to `rotate(+45)` (UP-AND-RIGHT in y-down, labels project diagonally into the band above the grid) and widens `COLUMN_LABEL_HEIGHT` 80px → 140px to contain labels like "917 | Milwaukee Brewers" (~95-100px projected height at 45°). Hot-patched in container 2026-05-14; this commit is the proper PR. Test updated to assert `rotate(45)` and explicitly assert NOT `rotate(-45)`. ([bd-wdpve])
- **`ecm_session_telemetry_row_count` gauge semantic conflict resolved (bd-ae58c, Option B).** bd-skqln.12 (BandwidthTracker) set the gauge to per-poll batch size (~10); bd-7i2vv (nightly rollup task) set it to post-prune table total (~250k). The resulting oscillation would trip the `ECMStatsRowCountGrowthAnomaly` alert spuriously. Fix (Option B): BandwidthTracker no longer writes the gauge — `StatsV2RollupTask` is now the sole writer, setting it once per nightly run to the post-prune table total. Per-poll batch size is derivable from the `ecm_session_telemetry_writes_total` counter rate. Alert rule comments updated; gauge docstring updated; `test_successful_write_publishes_row_count_gauge` renamed and inverted to assert BandwidthTracker does NOT touch the gauge. ([bd-ae58c])
- **`docs/security/threat_model_stats_v2.md` §7.8 and §9 updated to reflect bd-gsn3r FK drop (bd-qum5e).** bd-gsn3r (PR #283, migration 0011) dropped the FK on `session_telemetry.user_id`, making the §7.8 data-deletion control ("account deletion scrubs the behavioral trail via FK ON DELETE SET NULL") and the §9 sign-off condition "(a) user_id FK keeps ON DELETE SET NULL" stale. §7.8 now documents the new contract: viewer privacy is operator-managed via ADR-007 D1 raw-row retention (default 30 days); `session_telemetry.user_id` is an opaque Dispatcharr-side identifier with no FK to ECM `users`. §9 condition (a) is struck through and marked superseded, with a forward reference to bd-gsn3r. Schema table updated to remove the old FK annotation. ([bd-qum5e])
- **Stats v2 live header badges no longer silently undercount active channels.** A user reported 5 active channels in the page-header counter but the per-provider live-stats badges summed to only 4 (HD Homerun 2/4 + Infinity 2/10 + Lumen 0/4 + Strong 0/0 = 4). Root cause: the `StatsTab` `m3uConnectionStats` aggregator iterated over `channelStats.channels` but only incremented a provider counter when `ch.m3u_profile_id` was truthy AND the profile mapped to a known account — any channel whose live resolver chain (`bandwidth_tracker._resolve_provider_ids`) couldn't pin down a provider was silently dropped from the badge sum. The 1-missing pattern matched a known resolver gap from the post-`bd-ox5q8` era: when Dispatcharr's `/proxy/ts/status` payload omits `stream_id` AND the URL-derived id misses (`bd-kbgey`/`bd-5g7kx` fallbacks didn't catch the upstream-id case), the backend now returns `m3u_account_id: null` for that row, and the old aggregator just dropped the row instead of surfacing it. **Fix scope**: the aggregator now prefers the backend-resolved `m3u_account_id` (bd-ox5q8's authoritative live result) over the legacy `m3u_profile_id` → account-map fallback, and any channel that doesn't attribute to a displayed provider — null `m3u_account_id`, account filtered out as "Custom"/`is_active=false`, or account not yet side-loaded into `m3uAccounts` — falls into a new "Unknown" bucket rendered with a warning-tinted border + `help_outline` icon next to the real provider badges. **Regression lock**: backend `TestChannelStatsBadgeSumInvariant` (2 tests) pins the endpoint contract — every channel in `GET /api/stats/channels` MUST surface the `m3u_account_id` key (possibly `None`), and resolver-failure mode must still return every active row with `m3u_account_id: None` instead of stripping the row, so the frontend can route unattributed channels to the Unknown bucket. Frontend `StatsTab — provider badge sum invariant (bd-lhxfu)` (3 tests) locks the user-visible invariant: provider badge sum + Unknown bucket count == active-channel count, in three resolver-outcome scenarios (resolved + unresolved mix, all resolved, attributed-to-unknown-account side-load gap). The Unknown badge is also a visible signal of resolver attribution gaps that until now hid in the silent-drop noise — any non-zero Unknown count points at an active stream the resolver couldn't attribute, which is the leading signal for SLO-8 (provider attribution rate, warn-only) regressions. **Operator recovery**: automatic on next browser refresh — no settings change, no DB rewrite. ([bd-lhxfu])
- **Stats v2 User Watch Time panel: architectural fix for the namespace-collision bug bd-uqbob worked around with an env-var exclude filter.** ECM's local `users` table (auth identities for ECM logins) and Dispatcharr's `users` table (stream viewers) are two separate namespaces with coincidentally-overlapping integer IDs. `session_telemetry.user_id` previously had a FK to ECM `users.id` (per `models.py` `SessionTelemetry`) and the read APIs joined against ECM's `User` to surface "username" — but the values written into that column came from Dispatcharr's namespace. When the integer IDs collided (e.g., Dispatcharr viewer id=3 + ECM admin "claude" at users.id=3), the panel attributed real human viewing sessions to the ECM admin. **Fix**: new Alembic migration `0011` adds `session_telemetry.dispatcharr_username TEXT NULL` and drops the FK on `user_id`. The writer (`BandwidthTracker._write_session_telemetry`) populates `dispatcharr_username` directly from the per-poll Dispatcharr `user_id → username` map it already maintains for the bd-uqbob exclude filter — zero extra round-trips, denormalized at write time. The read APIs (`GET /api/stats/watch-time` + `GET /api/stats/watch-time/{user_id}`) read `dispatcharr_username` straight from the telemetry row's DISTINCT-collapse subquery — no more joins against ECM `users`. The `_load_usernames` helper (which queried `User`) is removed. PO directive 2026-05-13: "we should be able to get the active user account from the Dispatcharr API; we never need to touch a table." **Backfill policy**: pre-migration rows have `dispatcharr_username = NULL` and surface as "Unknown viewer" on the frontend (per the Stats v2 history-cutover policy from bd-skqln.3). No automatic backfill — operators who want a clean slate can purge stale rows with the existing one-liner from the bd-uqbob CHANGELOG entry. **bd-uqbob env var (`ECM_TELEMETRY_EXCLUDE_USERS`) is now moot for its original purpose** (the namespace collision goes away) but stays in place — operators may still want to exclude a specific Dispatcharr viewer for any other reason; no harm. Migration 0011 follows the bd-5w6jz idempotency pattern: column add and FK drop are independently guarded so a long-running install where `create_all()` materialised the post-0011 ORM shape (or where the smart-bootstrap fast-path stamped past 0011 without dropping the FK) re-applies cleanly. New regression tests in `backend/tests/integration/test_alembic_smoke.py`: `TestMigration0011` (fresh up + fresh down) and `TestMigration0011Idempotent` (column-already-added drift + FK-already-dropped drift). Existing watch-time integration tests (`backend/tests/integration/test_api_watch_time.py`) updated to seed `dispatcharr_username` on telemetry rows instead of relying on the dropped FK to ECM `User` rows. ([bd-gsn3r])
- **Alembic migrations 0006-0010 are now idempotent against pre-existing artifacts, and a new smart-bootstrap fast-path ends the whack-a-mole architecturally.** A user reported a second outage in the same family as `bd-ax3uj` (which fixed 0003/0004): `sqlite3.OperationalError: table session_telemetry already exists` during `Running upgrade 0005 -> 0006`, aborting container startup (post-`bd-zaaey`, bootstrap loud-fails — prober and everything else went with it). Same disease vector on a different migration: `init_db`'s `Base.metadata.create_all()` materialises ORM-declared artifacts from `models.py` ahead of the migration timeline on long-running installs.
