The risk inventory across 0006-0010 is broad — 0006 creates `session_telemetry` + 5 indexes from `SessionTelemetry`, 0007 alters `session_telemetry.channel_id` (post-0007 ORM shape lands ahead of the alter), 0008 creates the `channel_watch_stats_v` view, 0009 creates 3 rollup tables (`session_telemetry_user_daily`, `session_telemetry_provider_daily`, `telemetry_rollup_state`) + 3 indexes from their ORM models, 0010 adds `stream_id` and `stream_name` to `session_telemetry` (both columns declared on the ORM model). All five migrations now `inspect()` the live schema and skip `op.create_table` / `op.create_index` / `op.add_column` / `op.alter_column` for any artifact already present — `op.execute("CREATE VIEW ...")` becomes `CREATE VIEW IF NOT EXISTS` (SQLite-supported); 0007's alter checks the column type + nullability and skips when `channel_id` is already `String(64) NOT NULL`; 0010's add-column dance skips the entire view-drop/recreate block when both target columns already exist. **Strategic fix in `database.py`**: new `_schema_matches_head()` helper diffs `Base.metadata` against the live DB's tables + columns; new fast-path in `_bootstrap_alembic` detects "alembic_version lags head AND live schema covers the model shape" and stamps forward instead of running `upgrade head` — ends the whack-a-mole for any future migration that creates an ORM-declared artifact (the per-migration idempotency guards stay as defense in depth). The fast-path is guarded against fresh installs (`current_rev` is unset) and the historical bd-fwpzw stamped-at-HEAD scenario (the existing `_BOOTSTRAP_CANARIES` self-heal still covers that). New regression tests in `backend/tests/integration/test_alembic_smoke.py`: `TestMigration0006Idempotent` (drift scenario + fresh-fix path), `TestMigration0007Idempotent`, `TestMigration0008IfNotExists`, `TestMigration0009Idempotent`, `TestMigration0010Idempotent`, and `TestSmartBootstrapFastPath` (locks the fast-path triggers on schema-matches-head AND skips on fresh installs). Affected users recover automatically on the next container restart — no manual SQL required, no `UPDATE alembic_version SET version_num='0010'` workaround. Polish round (v0.17.0-0031): tightened `TestMigration0007Idempotent` post-upgrade assertion to verify both the type and length of `channel_id` (not just nullability), standardized bd-5w6jz drift-test class naming with an `Idempotent` suffix, and added safety comments in `database.py` documenting the symmetric guard direction, the concurrent-startup assumption on the `command.stamp` write, and the WARNING (vs INFO) log-level choice on the fast-path branch — per code review. ([bd-5w6jz])
- **Alembic migrations 0003 and 0004 are now idempotent against pre-existing artifacts.** A user reported `sqlite3.OperationalError: table rule_lint_findings already exists` during `Running upgrade 0002 -> 0003`, aborting container startup (post-`bd-zaaey`, bootstrap loud-fails — the user's prober and everything else went with it). Root cause is the same class of bug `bd-m2k7p` previously fixed in migration 0005: on a long-running install, `Base.metadata.create_all()` materialises the `rule_lint_findings` table + its two indexes (from the `RuleLintFinding` ORM model in `models.py`), and `idx_journal_batch_id` + `idx_journal_entity` on `journal_entries` (from `JournalEntry.__table_args__`), while `alembic_version` lags at 0002. The next start re-runs 0003 / 0004 and SQLite raises "already exists". Both migrations now `inspect()` the live schema and skip `op.create_table` / `op.create_index` for any artifact already present (matching the 0005 pattern). New regression tests in `backend/tests/integration/test_alembic_smoke.py` (`TestMigration0003`, `TestMigration0004`) lock in the fresh-fix path and the drift path (`create_all()` first, then `alembic upgrade head`) — both pre-fix migrations would have failed these tests with `OperationalError: ... already exists`. ([bd-ax3uj])
- **MCP `/health` is now self-diagnosing** when `api_key_configured` is false. The endpoint returns a new `api_key_status` field that distinguishes the four ways the key can appear missing: `file_not_found` (settings.json absent on the MCP container's `/config` mount — typically a volume mount mismatch between ECM and MCP), `invalid_json` (file present but corrupted), `field_missing` (legacy settings.json that predates the MCP feature and was never re-saved), or `field_empty` (no key generated yet, or key was revoked). The MCP Settings UI's Server Status panel now surfaces the status code plus a remediation hint tailored to the cause, so operators reporting "I generated a key but `/health` still says `api_key_configured: false`" can diagnose the failure mode from the UI alone instead of needing container shell access. Closes the diagnostic gap that motivated `bd-ix1g6`: code-only audit of the v0.16.0 generate→save→reload path (`backend/routers/settings.py:generate_mcp_api_key` → `backend/config.py:save_settings` → `mcp-server/config.py:get_mcp_api_key`) found no reproducible code defect — the persistent `api_key_configured: false` reports almost certainly trace to a config/deployment difference between the two containers' `/config` mounts, which until now was indistinguishable from a real bug at the API surface. New tests: 5 in `mcp-server/tests/test_config.py` (`TestGetMCPApiKeyStatus`) and 5 in `mcp-server/tests/test_server.py` (`TestHealthEndpoint` reports-status branch) lock the contract; `MCPSettingsSection.test.tsx` adds 4 component tests for the diagnostic UI. `get_mcp_api_key()` keeps its original `str`-returning signature so existing callers (auth middleware, `ecm_client`) are untouched. Cherry-picked from `release/v0.16.1` (`bd-ix1g6`).
- Stats v2 User Watch Time panel no longer attributes Dispatcharr-side viewer rows to the ECM admin/API account. ECM's local `users` table and Dispatcharr's `users` table are separate namespaces with coincidentally-overlapping integer ids — when ECM `users.id=3` is the local admin ("claude") and Dispatcharr `users.id=3` is a real human viewer, the Stats panel renders the human's viewing sessions under the admin's name (the panel joins `session_telemetry.user_id` against ECM's `users` table). New env var `ECM_TELEMETRY_EXCLUDE_USERS` (comma-separated, default empty/unset = no filtering, no behavior change) suppresses `_write_session_telemetry` rows whose Dispatcharr-side `user_id` OR resolved username matches any token (case-insensitive). New Prometheus counter `ecm_session_telemetry_rows_excluded_total{reason="excluded_user"}` surfaces suppression rate honestly — skipped writes are counted, not silently zeroed. Per-row `[BANDWIDTH] Skipped telemetry write for excluded user user=… reason=excluded_user` debug log for operator traceability. Backfill of existing mis-attributed rows is operator-driven (NOT auto-applied at startup): `docker exec ecm-ecm-1 python3 -c "import sqlite3; c=sqlite3.connect('/config/journal.db'); n=c.execute('DELETE FROM session_telemetry WHERE user_id=?', (3,)).rowcount; c.commit(); print(f'deleted {n} rows')"` (substitute the local admin's `users.id`). ([bd-uqbob])
- Stats v2 Providers panel polish — "Time spent per provider" + "Bitrate by provider" charts are now self-documenting. The time-spent chart was a single-bucket stacked `AreaChart` rendering one tall "Total" stack with no Y-axis label, so a PO couldn't tell from the chart which provider was which or what the Y-axis values meant. It's now a per-provider `BarChart`: one bar per provider with the provider name on the X-axis, watch minutes on the Y-axis, an explicit "Watch minutes" axis label, and a one-line description above the chart. The bitrate chart already rendered per-provider time-series correctly with auto-scaled tick formatting (bps / Kbps / Mbps), but had no Y-axis label and no caption explaining what the metric was; both are now in place — Y-axis labeled "Bitrate (auto-scaled)" and a one-line description noting the formula derivation (`SUM(bytes_delta) * 8 * 1000 / SUM(poll_interval_ms)` per `(provider, time_bucket)`, equivalently `bytes / seconds * 8`). Backend bitrate + watch-time endpoints are unchanged — both already return per-provider rows and the bitrate formula was already correct; two new contract-shape regression tests (`test_response_row_shape_matches_documented_contract`) pin the field names the panel binds to. ([bd-tknci], [bd-zrk05])
- Stats v2 provider resolver: channel-streams URL-match fallback for the upstream-id case. kbgey's URL fallback parses the trailing `<id>.ts` integer from the active stream URL, but empirically that integer is the **upstream M3U provider's** stream id (e.g. Infinity's `85796`), NOT Dispatcharr's stream row id — `get_streams_by_ids([85796])` returns nothing, the resolver previously gave up with `reason=stream_not_found_url_derived`, and `provider_id` stayed NULL (Unknown bucket). The resolver now falls back to `GET /channels/<channel_id>/streams`, matches by URL substring, and uses the matched stream's `m3u_account_id`. Per-poll cache + small cross-poll LRU keeps the API call rate bounded. ([bd-5g7kx])
- Stats v2 session_telemetry: anonymous `user_id=0` (and `"0"`) no longer poison the per-poll batch commit. Dispatcharr returns `user_id="0"` for anonymous viewing sessions; `session_telemetry.user_id` is INTEGER NULL with an FK to `users.id`. The 0 sentinel doesn't exist in `users` → `sqlite3.IntegrityError` at `session.commit()` rolled back the ENTIRE batch including correctly-resolved rows. New `_coerce_session_user_id(raw) -> Optional[int]` helper normalizes 0 / "0" / "" / None / negative ints / bools / floats / unparseable strings to None; positive ints / int-parseable strings pass through. Defense-in-depth: `_write_session_telemetry` now also catches `IntegrityError` at commit time and logs `[STATS_V2] session_telemetry batch FK violation observed_at=... rows_attempted=...` so any future FK trip-up doesn't silently zero-out a batch. ([bd-gbxmj])

### Fixed (prior)
- Stats v2 provider resolver — URL fallback when Dispatcharr's `/proxy/ts/status` omits `stream_id`. The skqln.14 resolver previously gave up with `reason=no_stream_id` for any active channel where Dispatcharr didn't surface `stream_id` directly, leaving `provider_id=NULL` (the "Unknown" bucket). In dev, this hit 91% of polls (214 of 235). The resolver now also extracts the stream id from the URL path's trailing `<id>.ts` segment (`r"/(\d+)\.ts(?:\?|$)"`) and feeds it into the same `get_streams_by_ids` batch the resolver already runs per poll — no additional API calls. URL-derived lookups that miss log `reason=stream_not_found_url_derived` so ops can differentiate from the direct-stream-id miss case. ([bd-kbgey])
- Stats v2 Users panel — daily watch-minutes chart now displays dates in the operator's **local timezone** and visually marks today as "in progress" (amber dot vs teal for completed days), with a caption explaining the UTC bucketing + 10s update cadence. Resolves the misleading appearance of watch-time "going down" across the UTC date boundary when yesterday-UTC is complete but today-UTC is still accumulating. Server-side aggregation stays UTC; the relabeling anchors each bucket at noon UTC then formats in the operator's tz to pick the most-overlapping local day. ([bd-1qxo9])

### Fixed (existing entry continues below)
- Stats v2 Providers panel polish: provider rows now display the actual M3U account name instead of `Provider <id>`. Frontend side-loads the existing `/api/providers` endpoint at panel mount, builds an id→name map, and uses it in chart legends, heatmap row labels, and data tables. Falls back to `Provider <id>` for unmapped IDs (e.g., during the fetch); NULL stays "Unknown". ([bd-vjv7k])
- Stats v2 Providers heatmap polish: column labels rotated -45° (anchored at column center) and default `cellSize` bumped 32px → 48px; column-label band 24px → 80px to fit rotated text. Resolves the "everything super compressed" overlap when real channel names ("Discovery Channel", "ESPN HD") are wider than the column. The `.heatmap` container's existing `overflow-x: auto` handles wider heatmaps via horizontal scroll. Heatmap primitive interface preserved — `cellSize` prop still overridable for the time-of-day use case. ([bd-yteek])

### Added
- Stats v2 rollup tables + nightly prune (ADR-007 implementation): Alembic migration `0009` adds 3 new tables — `session_telemetry_user_daily` (PK `(user_id, channel_id, day)`) for the GH-62 Users panel rollup, `session_telemetry_provider_daily` (PK `(provider_id, channel_id, day)`) for the GH-59 Providers panel rollup, and `telemetry_rollup_state` (marker table per rollup_name with last_completed_day/run_status/run_error). Nightly scheduled task `stats_v2_rollup` (registered via `task_registry`) aggregates the previous day's raw `session_telemetry` rows into both rollup tables (idempotent via `INSERT OR REPLACE`), updates the state marker, and **after successful rollup** prunes raw rows older than 30 days (per ADR-007 retention) — prune is **skipped on rollup failure** so raw data isn't lost. NULL provider_id raw rows bucket into the `'unknown'` sentinel string in the provider rollup; NULL user_id raw rows are excluded from the user rollup (no behavioral subject to attribute, documented on the model). Watch-seconds use the DISTINCT-`(channel, observed_at)` collapse pattern; bytes_delta_sum and buffer_event_count are summed across raw rows since they're per-poll-per-client measurements. 5 new Prometheus metrics: `ecm_telemetry_rollup_last_success_timestamp{rollup_name}`, `ecm_telemetry_rollup_duration_seconds{rollup_name}`, `ecm_telemetry_rollup_days_processed{rollup_name}`, `ecm_telemetry_raw_rows_pruned`, `ecm_telemetry_rollup_errors_total{phase}` — all bounded-cardinality. ([bd-7i2vv])

### Known issue
- Operator-facing Stats v2 telemetry opt-out: new env var `ECM_STATS_TELEMETRY_OPT_OUT` (default `false`, truthy enum `true|1|yes|on` case-insensitive). When set, `BandwidthTracker._collect_stats` short-circuits the Stats v2 write path immediately after legacy `ChannelBandwidth`/`BandwidthDaily`/`UniqueClientConnection` writes — skipping `_resolve_provider_ids` (saves the Dispatcharr `get_streams_by_ids` call), `_collect_buffer_events` (saves the `get_system_events` call), and `_write_session_telemetry` (skips all `session_telemetry` row writes). Read per-poll so runtime flips take effect on the next cycle. One-shot startup INFO log when enabled: `[STATS_V2] telemetry opt-out is ENABLED — no session_telemetry data will be collected`. Documented in `docs/user_guide/stats/stats-v2-history-cutover.md`. Closes the CONDITIONAL item in the privacy sign-off (`bd-skqln.8`) requiring an operator-global toggle. ([bd-tp1pd])
- Stats v2 Providers panel (GH-59): new **6th panel** in the Stats tab — `ProvidersPanel` — wired to skqln.16's 4 provider-stats endpoints. Four visualizations: buffering events by provider (multi-series line), time spent per provider (stacked area), channels-by-provider heatmap (reuses skqln.17's `Heatmap` primitive + Tol-bright colorblind-safe palette), and bitrate-by-provider over time (multi-series line). Window selector (7d/30d/90d) drives all four; bucket selector (hour/day) drives the two time-series charts. a11y matches skqln.6: semantic `<h3>` heading, data-table fallbacks for all four charts (visually-hidden `<table>` with `<caption>`, "Show chart data" toggle), `role="status" aria-live="polite"` empty states, `aria-label` on selectors, WCAG-AA contrast via theme tokens, focus-visible outlines. NULL `provider_id` rows surface as a labeled "Unknown" bucket in both chart legend and data table. **Admin-only**: non-admin callers see the admin-access notice (`role="note"`), no API calls. Closes `bd-39ao6` (GH-59 per-provider stream stats tracker) together with `bd-skqln.16`. ([bd-skqln.18])
- Stats v2 SLOs + Prometheus alert rules + deployment-safety runbooks. New SLOs in `docs/sre/slos.md`: **SLO-7** (telemetry write success rate, 99.5% over 30d), **SLO-8** (provider attribution rate, 95% over 24h, warn-only — degraded resolution surfaces as "Unknown" bucket, not a fault), **SLO-9** (Stats v2 query latency, p99 < 2s + p95 < 800ms on `/api/stats/*`, 7d). New alert rules in `docs/sre/prometheus_rules.yaml`: `ECMStatsTelemetryWriteFailing` (page on 30m sustained), `ECMStatsTelemetryWriteFailingWarn` (ticket on 5m), `ECMStatsProviderResolutionDegraded` (warn-only), `ECMStatsQueryLatencyHigh` + `ECMStatsQueryLatencyP99High` (warn), `ECMStatsRowCountGrowthAnomaly` (warn — 2× the 7-day rolling avg over 24h). New runbooks: `stats-v2-write-failures.md`, `stats-v2-provider-resolution-degraded.md`, `stats-v2-row-growth.md`, `stats-v2-deployment-safety.md`. The obsolete dual-write divergence alert from the bead's original spec is omitted by design — skqln.3 step (d) retired the legacy ChannelWatchStats writer, so the modern data-consistency SLI is the resolver success rate (SLO-8). ([bd-skqln.11])
- Stats v2 user-facing documentation: `docs/user_guide/stats/users-panel.md` (Users panel operator guide — admin-only gate, multi-client DISTINCT collapse explanation, polling cadence, 5 named caveats) and `docs/user_guide/stats/metric-glossary.md` (every user-facing Stats v2 number defined and verified against the shipped code — `total_watch_seconds`, `session_count`, `last_watched`, `buffer_event_count`, `provider_id` / "Unknown" bucket, `bytes_delta`, derived-only `bitrate_bps`, plus the 5 Prometheus metrics from `bd-skqln.12`). Cross-linked from `docs/user_guide/index.md` and `docs/user_guide/stats/index.md`. Screenshot placeholders marked TODO. ([bd-skqln.9])
- `docs/security/privacy_signoff_stats_v2.md` — post-implementation privacy sign-off for Stats v2 (Security Engineer review against the pre-impl threat model from `skqln.7`). **Status: APPROVED WITH CONDITIONS**. PASS on 5 of 7 review items (data classification, logging discipline, metric cardinality, admin-only auth boundary, external data flow). CONDITIONAL on 2 items (retention policy enforcement awaits `bd-7i2vv` rollup + prune; operator opt-out awaits `bd-tp1pd`) — both in flight. Includes auditable evidence trail (file paths + line numbers + test names) for each PASS item, the release-gate hand-off rule for the conditional items, and an evidence-of-record for the post-skqln.3 dual-write scope deviation. ([bd-skqln.8])
- Stats v2 GH-59 performance benchmarks (follow-up to skqln.10): 4 new pytest-benchmark tests at `backend/tests/performance/test_perf_provider_stats.py` covering the `/api/stats/providers/*` aggregations from skqln.16 (buffering, watch-time, channel-heatmap, bitrate). Reuses the existing 250k-row Zipfian-distributed fixture from skqln.10. Baseline numbers (dev workstation): heatmap median 28.6ms, watch-time 99.3ms, buffering 84.9ms, bitrate 105.9ms — all 7×–28× under the 800ms p95 target. The CI workflow (`.github/workflows/perf-benchmarks.yml`) picks them up automatically; same `--benchmark-compare-fail=median:20%` regression gate. ([bd-zgsbn])
- Stats v2 performance baseline + CI regression gate (`bd-skqln.10`): new `pytest-benchmark` suite at `backend/tests/performance/` covering the watch-time-by-user query (`bd-skqln.5`'s `GET /api/stats/watch-time`). Deterministic 250k-row synthetic fixture in CI (50 users × 5k polls, seed `1_234_567`, Zipfian provider distribution with ~5% NULL provider — already shaped for the 4 GH-59 follow-up benchmarks); 5M-row stretch fixture available via `ECM_PERF_ROW_COUNT=5000000` for local pre-release sanity. New GitHub Actions workflow `.github/workflows/perf-benchmarks.yml` runs **same-runner baseline-vs-PR comparison** (`--benchmark-compare-fail=median:20%`) — sidesteps heterogeneous-runner timing noise that committed-baseline JSONs are prone to. Default test workflow gains `--ignore=tests/performance` so the perf suite runs only in its own job. `.gitignore` excludes `.benchmarks/` stores. Baseline numbers (250k rows, AMD Ryzen 7 PRO 8845HS, Python 3.12.3): min/median/max ≈ 352/363/368 ms, σ ≈ 5.8 ms. Follow-up bead `bd-zgsbn` files the 4 GH-59 provider-aggregate benchmarks now that `bd-skqln.16` shipped — the fixture is already engineered for them. ([bd-skqln.10])
- Stats v2 observability instrumentation: 5 new Prometheus metric families on the existing `/metrics` endpoint — `ecm_session_telemetry_writes_total{result=success|failure}` (counter), `ecm_session_telemetry_write_duration_seconds` (histogram), `ecm_session_telemetry_row_count` (gauge for storage-growth alerts), `ecm_provider_resolution_total{result=resolved|unresolved}` (counter; wires skqln.14's `[STATS_V2] provider_resolution_*` log emission into Prometheus), and `ecm_stats_query_duration_seconds{endpoint, granularity}` (histogram; endpoint = matched FastAPI route pattern, NOT resolved path, so `user_id`/`channel_id` never become label values — bounded ~5×3 = 15 series). **Cardinality discipline**: `user_id`, `channel_id`, `session_id`, `target_id`, `client_ip`, `trace_id` are NEVER metric labels — parametrized test asserts the rule across every Stats v2 metric. `provider_id` is allowed (bounded <20 per SRE pre-clearance). The bead's original dual-write-divergence SLI is omitted by design: skqln.3 step (d) retired the legacy `ChannelWatchStats` writer, so there is no dual-write to compare; the modern data-consistency SLI is the resolver success rate (`ecm_provider_resolution_total{result=resolved}` / total), target ≥95% steady state. SLO definitions, alert rules, and runbook entries land in `bd-skqln.11` (unblocked by this). ([bd-skqln.12])
- Stats v2 Users panel (GH-62): new **5th panel** in the Stats tab — `UserStatsPanel` — wired to skqln.5's watch-time API. Primary table = per-user totals (`user | total minutes | last watched`) with a per-user drill-down showing the channel breakdown (`channel name | total minutes | last watched`). Date-range selector with 7/30/90-day presets. One daily-trend line chart (watch-minutes, 30-day window). a11y: semantic `<h3>` heading consistent with sibling Stats panels, **chart data-table fallback** always-rendered as visually-hidden `<table>` with `<caption>` (screen readers cannot read SVG; "Show chart data" toggle reveals it visually), `role="status" aria-live="polite"` empty-state announcement, explicit `aria-label` on date-range select and drill-down buttons, WCAG-AA contrast via theme tokens (`var(--text-primary)`) overriding Recharts defaults, focus-visible outlines, keyboard traversal verified. Non-admin callers see a **"User watch-time statistics require admin access"** notice (`role="note"`) rather than the panel silently disappearing — keeps the feature discoverable while honoring the admin-only auth boundary from skqln.5. ([bd-skqln.6])
- Stats v2 per-provider read APIs (GH-59): four new endpoints on `/api/stats/providers/*` backing the upcoming Providers panel — `GET /buffering` (time-series of `buffer_event_count` grouped by `(provider_id, time_bucket)`; params `window=7d|30d|90d`, `bucket=hour|day`), `GET /watch-time` (total per-provider watch time over window; aggregates `SUM(poll_interval_ms)` grouped by `provider_id`), `GET /channel-heatmap` (2D rows×cols grid: providers × top-N channels by bytes; default `top_n=50`, hard cap `500`; two-pass query so the cross-provider top-channel set is selected once then re-grouped by provider), `GET /bitrate` (derived bitrate time-series: `SUM(bytes_delta)*8*1000 / SUM(poll_interval_ms)` per `(provider_id, time_bucket)`). Queries 2 and 4 reuse the DISTINCT-`(provider_id, channel_id, observed_at)` collapse pattern from skqln.3 migration 0008 / skqln.5 to guard against multi-client overcount; queries 1 and 3 sum inherently per-poll columns and don't need the collapse. NULL `provider_id` rows surface as a single "Unknown" bucket row on every endpoint (SQLite's GROUP BY NULL handling) — never silently excluded. Response envelope matches skqln.5: `{data, meta: {from_iso, to_iso, total_rows, window, ...}, pagination: null}`. Property-based test confirms per-provider watch-time sum equals per-user watch-time sum over the same universe (consistency invariant). **Admin-only** per PO directive 2026-05-13; non-admin callers receive 403. ([bd-skqln.16])
- Stats v2 buffer-event ingestion: `BandwidthTracker._collect_stats` now polls Dispatcharr's `GET /api/core/system-events/?event_type=buffering` once per cycle and increments `session_telemetry.buffer_event_count` for matched channels. Cross-poll dedup by `event.id` via a 10k-entry LRU (system-events feed re-delivers recent events; without cross-poll dedup the same event would be counted N times). Provider attribution piggybacks on skqln.14's `_resolve_provider_ids` — one shared resolver call per poll, not two. Count attributes to exactly **one row per (channel_uuid, observed_at) bucket** (the first emitted client-row gets the count, sibling rows write 0) so `SUM(buffer_event_count) GROUP BY provider, time_bucket` stays well-defined for the future per-provider read APIs without per-client double-counting. Events for channels that don't appear in the current poll's snapshot are dropped with `[STATS_V2] buffer_event_unmapped_channel` log. ([bd-skqln.15])
- Stats v2 provider attribution: `BandwidthTracker._collect_stats` now resolves the active stream's `m3u_account_id` for every channel in every poll and writes it as `session_telemetry.provider_id` (the data foundation the upcoming Providers panel and per-provider stats endpoints will read). Resolution piggybacks on the same `DispatcharrClient.get_streams_by_ids()` call ECM already uses for stream metadata — one batched API call per poll, not N. Resolver cache is scoped to a single poll cycle so a stream's failover hop is picked up next poll without staleness. Unresolvable channels (no active stream, mid-failover, or Dispatcharr returns the stream-without-provider) surface as `provider_id = NULL` and feed the "Unknown" bucket in the future provider panel — failures are logged with `[STATS_V2] provider_resolution_failed channel=... stream=... reason=...` and an SLI counter (`[STATS_V2] provider_resolution resolved=X unresolved=Y`) lands per-poll so `skqln.12` can wire it to Prometheus later. ([bd-skqln.14])
- Stats v2 watch-time-by-user read API: two new endpoints on the `/api/stats` namespace — `GET /api/stats/watch-time` (per-user totals, optional `group_by=total|day`, optional `user_id` filter and `from`/`to` ISO-8601 UTC range) and `GET /api/stats/watch-time/{user_id}` (per-user breakdown by channel; `channel_name` side-loaded from `UniqueClientConnection` with a `Channel <uuid[:8]>...` fallback). Both read `session_telemetry` directly (the view doesn't expose `user_id`) and use a `DISTINCT (user_id, channel_id, observed_at)` subquery to guard against multi-client overcount. Response envelope: `{data, meta: {from_iso, to_iso, group_by, total_rows}, pagination: null}`. **Admin-only**: non-admin callers receive 403 from both endpoints regardless of which `user_id` they query. ([bd-skqln.5])
- Stats v2 keystone: BandwidthTracker now writes per-poll viewing telemetry to `session_telemetry` (the foundation for the Users/Providers panels and per-provider stats). Alembic migration `0007` corrects `session_telemetry.channel_id` from `Integer` to `String(64)` UUID for consistency with every other channel-keyed table and with ADR-007's rollup schemas. Alembic migration `0008` adds the `channel_watch_stats_v` SQL view — a read-compat aggregation over `session_telemetry` that exposes `channel_id`, `total_watch_seconds`, and `last_watched` (the columns that faithfully derive from per-poll observation). Migration 0008 uses a `DISTINCT (channel_id, observed_at)` subquery so multi-client concurrent viewing does not silently inflate watch time. ([bd-skqln.3])
- `docs/user_guide/stats-v2-history-cutover.md` — operator-facing note explaining that Stats v2 metrics begin on the day v0.17.0 deploys and prior watch-time history is not reconstructable. The `accept-the-gap` backfill policy is documented in `docs/database_migrations.md`. ([bd-skqln.3])
- Stats v2 foundation: new `session_telemetry` table and Alembic migration `0006` — per-session bandwidth/buffer-event telemetry; the data source for the upcoming Users panel. Internal schema only, no UI yet. ([bd-skqln.2])

### Changed
- BandwidthTracker no longer writes to `ChannelWatchStats` — that aggregate table is now served by the `channel_watch_stats_v` view over `session_telemetry`. The legacy `ChannelWatchStats` table schema is retained for pre-cutover rows; no production code reads it post-skqln.3. Readers repointed in this release: `popularity_calculator._gather_metrics`, `BandwidthTracker.get_top_watched_channels` (the `/api/stats/top-watched` endpoint), and `BandwidthTracker._log_watch_stop_events`. `ChannelBandwidth` and `BandwidthDaily` writes are unchanged — they have independent live readers (per-day breakdown charts, bandwidth summaries). ([bd-skqln.3])
- Channel popularity formula's `watch_count` axis now uses `COUNT(DISTINCT session_id)` (distinct viewing sessions per channel) instead of the legacy state-transition counter. Operators may notice channel rankings shift visibly on the first calculator run after upgrade — a one-time event documented in `stats-v2-history-cutover.md`. The other three axes (`watch_time`, `viewers`, `bandwidth`) and the default weights are unchanged. ([bd-skqln.3])
- `ECM_SESSION_TELEMETRY_WRITE_ENABLED` environment-variable kill-switch retired. The flag's job ended when legacy writes were removed; `BandwidthTracker._write_session_telemetry` now runs unconditionally. Operators who set the env var manually can remove it from their compose / .env — it is silently ignored. ([bd-skqln.3])

## [0.16.0] — 2026-05-12

_This is the shipping 0.16.0. An earlier 0.16.0 build was cut on 2026-04-20 and rolled back before any consumer pulled it (see `[0.16.0-yanked]` below); everything from that attempt is included here, with the blocking bugs fixed._

### Added
- Auto-creation **Stream sort → Quality (resolution)** breaks ties at equal resolution using ECM **M3U account priorities** (same values as M3U Manager Save Priorities / Provider Order). Per-rule **`quality_tie_break_order`** (`desc` = higher priority first, default). Per-rule **`quality_m3u_tie_break_enabled`** (default on) disables that tie-break so equal-resolution ordering falls back to stream-id order only; Rule Builder and bulk-edit expose a checkbox and gate the direction dropdown. Alembic revision **`0005`** adds `quality_tie_break_order` and `quality_m3u_tie_break_enabled` on `auto_creation_rules` (legacy `database._run_migrations` remains for installs predating Alembic); YAML export/import, backup restore, and duplicate carry the fields (`enhancedchannelmanager-3j9su`).
- Auto-creation rule analyzer — advisory linter for auto-creation rules. Surfaces structural and regex-style configuration bugs (`UK|` regex matches everything; `^4K` typed under "Contains" matches nothing; `^\^4k` double-escape typo; OR-arms that drop a `normalized_name_in_group` guard; `merge_streams` targeting a channel group with zero channels) without running the rule. Two endpoints: `POST /api/auto-creation/rules/analyze` analyzes the live DB; `POST /api/auto-creation/rules/analyze/from-bundle` accepts a debug-bundle tar.gz upload and never touches the DB so it's safe for support diagnosis of any user's bundle. New MCP tool `analyze_auto_creation_rules(bundle_path=None)` exposes both. All findings are warnings; rule saves are never blocked. Three new advisory codes added to `backend/regex_lint.py` (`REGEX_TRIVIALLY_MATCHES_ALL`, `OPERATOR_VALUE_LOOKS_LIKE_REGEX`, `REGEX_REDUNDANT_ESCAPE_CARET`) on top of the strict bd-eio04.7 codes; new `severity` field on `LintViolation` defaults to `error` for back-compat. Documented in `docs/channel_pipeline_rule_analyzer.md` (`bd-0gntx`).
- `docs/adr/ADR-004-release-cut-promotion-discipline.md` — mandates short-lived `release/vX.Y.Z` branches + merge-commit PR + a seven-item pre-cut gate (G1a–G7) for all `dev`→`main` promotions. Narrow hotfix carve-out with a mechanical ceiling (>2 hotfixes between cuts triggers a mandatory incident review). Directly closes the 0.16.0-rollback root cause (zero-open-P0/P1 gate) and the PR #82 scope-sprawl pattern (non-release PRs to `main` forbidden) (`bd-4lk1q`).
- `docs/adr/ADR-005-code-security-gating-strategy.md` — extends CodeQL to run on PR-to-`dev` and push-to-`dev` (currently only fires on `main`-adjacent events); enforces delta-zero via GitHub Code Scanning merge protection rules on both `dev` and `main`; disables "Allow administrators to bypass required status checks" on both branches. Three-week phased rollout (advisory → dirty-base remediation → enforcement). Dismiss-with-comment policy with only two Phase-1 categories (false-positive / test-only); "won't fix" intentionally excluded until a mechanical co-sign enforcer exists (`bd-sm3n3`).
- Structured JSON logging on stdout with `trace_id` correlation middleware (honors inbound `X-Request-ID`, generates UUIDv4 otherwise, echoes in response header). `bind_context()` helper for attaching structured fields to long-running tasks (`bd-ak1db`, PR #80).
- Prometheus `/metrics` endpoint exposing `ecm_http_requests_total`, `ecm_http_request_duration_seconds`, `ecm_health_ready_ok`, and `ecm_health_ready_check_duration_seconds`. HTTP middleware labels by matched FastAPI route pattern so cardinality stays bounded. `/metrics` is unauthenticated by design (Prometheus scrapers have no session context) — hardening via reverse-proxy allowlist or bearer-token scrape credential is a separate future bead (`bd-ak1db`, PR #80).
- Alembic schema migration system with baseline revision `0001` capturing the current SQLAlchemy metadata (36 tables). `alembic upgrade head` wired into app startup; legacy pre-Alembic DBs are `stamp`ed rather than re-DDLed (`bd-c5wf5`, PR #81).
- `GET /api/health/schema` public endpoint returning `current_revision`, `head_revision`, `up_to_date`, `foreign_keys_enabled`, and `journal_mode` — required by DBAS restore/sync to gate backup imports on schema version (`bd-c5wf5`, PR #81).
- `docs/database_migrations.md` authoring conventions (`bd-c5wf5`, PR #81).
- `docs/runbooks/` scaffold with template and v0.16.0 hard-rollback exemplar (`bd-bwly4`).
- New Settings → Normalization Rules → "Apply to existing channels" button with dry-run diff preview + per-row rename/merge/skip actions. Exposes POST /api/normalization/apply-to-channels endpoint (PR #107, bd-u9odj, GH-104).
- Auto-creation per-rule flag `match_scope_target_group` (default off) that restricts the duplicate channel-name lookup in `create_channel` to the rule's target group. Two rules targeting different groups can now create separate channels with the same name instead of merging into an existing channel in another group. Opt-in, backwards-compatible (bd-r9mtd, GH-92). Adds Alembic revision `0002`.
- Auto-Creation rules — bulk edit. Multi-select rules and apply per-section changes (options, normalization groups, channel/stream sort, orphan cleanup, Merge Streams prune) in one action. Adds POST `/api/auto-creation/rules/bulk-update`; omitted fields are left unchanged. (PR #99)
- `GET /api/journal` now accepts an optional `?batch_id=` query parameter that filters to a single bulk operation's journal rows. Hits the `idx_journal_batch_id` index shipped in bd-dmu8w; an unknown `batch_id` returns `200` with an empty list (not 422 — `batch_id` is just a filter). Composes with the existing `?search=` filter via AND. Closes the integrator gap that bd-pf82j flagged in `docs/api.md` and obviates direct DB lookups for forensic batch correlation (PR #143, bd-s4sph).
- `.github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE/release.md` — release-cut PR template per ADR-004 §Implementation Sketch §3. Invoked via `gh pr create --base main --head release/vX.Y.Z --template release.md`. Carries the verbatim G1a–G7 pre-cut gate checklist, separate G1a-Justifications and G1b-Waivers sections matching the formally-waived semantics in `docs/shipping.md`, the hotfix-coexistence note (G7 tiebreaker), and post-merge tasks (tag + release + back-merge + dev counter bump). Default repo PR template (`.github/pull_request_template.md`) remains the catch-all for non-release PRs (`bd-tvlxz` item 3).
- `.github/workflows/release-cut-gate.yml` — Phase 2 mechanical CI enforcement of ADR-004 pre-cut gates G1a (zero open P0/P1 beads), G1b (zero open HIGH/CRITICAL CodeQL alerts), G5 (CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]` promoted to `[X.Y.Z]` with today's UTC date and a fresh `[Unreleased]` above), G6 (`frontend/package.json` version matches `release/vX.Y.Z` branch name), and G7 (no other release-cut or hotfix PR open against `main`). Triggered on PR open/sync/edit/reopen against `main`; classifies release-cut PRs by title-regex (`^Release vX.Y.Z$`) AND head-branch-regex (`^release/vX.Y.Z$`); short-circuits to a passing check for non-release PRs so branch protection isn't blocked on hotfix/feature PRs. Installs `bd v0.49.6` (statically-linked Go binary from upstream `gastownhall/beads` releases, pinned to maintainer-local v0.49.x line) for G1a. Added as the `Release Cut Gate` required status check on `main` branch protection — failure of any gate blocks the cut PR (`bd-3d0tv`).

### Changed
- All MCP tools now route every backend call through the declarative endpoint-contract registry (`mcp-server/_endpoint_contracts.py`) — Phase 2 of `enhancedchannelmanager-vtghg` migrates the remaining 11 tool domains (`channel_groups`, `epg`, `export`, `m3u`, `normalization`, `notifications`, `profiles`, `stats`, `streams`, `system`, `tasks`) plus `resources/overview.py` onto `ECMClient.call_endpoint(...)`, bringing the registry to ~100 endpoints (99 today). The contract test's tool-source guard is now **FAIL-mode across the whole MCP surface**: any raw `client.<verb>("/api/...")` literal in `mcp-server/tools/*.py` / `resources/*.py` that isn't `# contract-exempt:`-marked fails the build — only a handful of multi-call / cross-domain flows and the dynamic-key M3U group-settings PATCH remain exempt. Mutating tools now report the **actual resulting state** rather than just "OK": `update_channel`/`create_channel` echo the response object's `name`/`channel_number`/`channel_group_id`; `update_epg_source`/`update_m3u_account` echo the saved `name`/`url`; `bulk_remove_streams` reports the post-update stream count from the response; `delete_channel_group`/`delete_saved_backup`/`delete_task_schedule`/`mark_notifications_read`/`delete_all_notifications`/`apply_profile_to_channels` do a cheap one-call read-back and WARN if the change didn't take (the GH #221 "200 OK, nothing changed" failure mode). Drift the migration surfaced and fixed: the streams tools sent `group`/`provider_id` query params to `GET /api/streams` (backend wants `channel_group_name`/`m3u_account` — silently ignored); `delete_channel_group`/`match_streams_to_channels`/`build_channel_lineup` filtered `GET /api/channels` by `group_id` instead of `channel_group` (the GH #221 class again); `get_journal` paginated via `limit` instead of `page_size` (ignored); `get_epg_grid` sent `channel_id`/`limit` query params that `GET /api/epg/grid` doesn't accept (now filtered/truncated client-side); and `create_task_schedule` sent `{cron_expression}` to a backend that has no cron-style scheduling and requires `schedule_type` — the tool's signature changed from `cron_expression` to `schedule_type` plus the interval/time/day fields the backend actually accepts. Behaviour is otherwise plumbing-only (`bd-vtghg` Phase 2).
- The `channels` and `auto-creation` MCP tools now route every backend call through a declarative endpoint-contract registry (`mcp-server/_endpoint_contracts.py`) instead of hand-rolled `client.post("/api/...", json_data={...})` literals. `ECMClient.call_endpoint(Endpoint, path_args=, body=, query=)` enforces — at call time, always on — that the body/query keys a tool sends are a subset of the keys the registry declares (a tool that sends `group_id` when the backend wants `channel_group_id` now raises `ContractError` loudly instead of silently no-op'ing at the backend), and delegates to the existing verb methods so it inherits their timeout/`detail`-surfacing error handling. A new backend test (`backend/tests/integration/test_mcp_tool_contracts.py`) cross-checks the registry against the live OpenAPI spec — `(method, path)` exists, `request_fields ⊆` the request-body schema's properties (the GH #221 catcher), `query_params ⊆` declared query params, `response_is_list` matches — plus a tool-source guard that FAILs on any un-migrated raw `/api/` literal in the two migrated files (the other ~11 tool domains are inventoried for Phase 2). This is Phase 1 of `enhancedchannelmanager-vtghg`, the structural fix for the drift class behind GH #221-225 (`group_id` vs `channel_group_id`; the `{"rules":[...]}` envelope; missing bulk endpoints; swallowed 422 detail). Plumbing/no behaviour change except three drift fixes the migration surfaced: `list_channels`/`build_channel_lineup` passed `group_id` as the channels-list query param (backend wants `channel_group`); `merge_channels` sent `{target_channel_id, source_channel_ids}` to `POST /api/channels/merge`, which never accepted `target_channel_id` (it now routes through `POST /api/channels/bulk-merge`, which has the "keep target, absorb sources" semantics); and `bulk_commit_channels` read `result["idMappings"]`, which `BulkCommitResponse` doesn't expose (it's `tempIdMap`/`groupIdMap`) — that key always read empty (`bd-vtghg` Phase 1).
- `docs/backend_architecture.md` expanded with an Observability section covering logging schema, metric naming conventions, cardinality rules, `bind_context` usage, and the process for adding new metrics (`bd-ak1db`, PR #80).
- `docs/shipping.md` — replaced the "Release Workflow (Merging to Main)" section with the ADR-004-authoritative Cut Mechanics (steps 0–10), a verbatim Pre-Cut Gate Checklist (G1a–G7) for pasting into release-cut PR descriptions, and a new Hotfix Path subsection covering the one carve-out from the "no non-release PRs to `main`" rule. The "When User Says 'Ship the Fix'" dev-cycle section is unchanged (`bd-tvlxz`).
- `docs/runbooks/v0.16.0-rollback.md` — reordered the two force-push paths: Option 2 (temporary `allow_force_pushes` flip) is now primary per ADR-005 (admin-bypass disabled on `main`), with Option 1 (admin bypass) retained as a fallback only for the transitional window. Added a mandatory post-action verification step (`gh api ... | jq '.allow_force_pushes.enabled'` must return `false`) and a design note considering a short-TTL wrapper that auto-reverts via `trap` regardless of outcome (`bd-tvlxz`).
- `docs/shipping.md` — added a §G1b "formally waived" semantics subsection in the Pre-Cut Gate Checklist clarifying that a HIGH/CRITICAL alert is waived for G1b only when **both** (a) it is dismissed in the GitHub Security tab with a non-empty rationale matching ADR-005's permitted dismissal categories (false-positive-with-evidence or test-only sink; "won't fix" remains excluded) AND (b) the release-cut PR description carries a cross-reference line citing the alert number, dismissal category, and dismissing user. Belt-and-suspenders gate that mirrors and extends ADR-005's Dismiss-With-Comment Policy item 1 to the release-cut surface. Resolves the ambiguity flagged in `bd-tvlxz` item 5 (`bd-tvlxz`).
- Auto-creation `POST /api/auto-creation/run` and `POST /api/auto-creation/rules/{id}/run` are now async-enqueue endpoints — they pre-create the `AutoCreationExecution(status="running")` row, return `202` with `{execution_id, status, ...}` immediately, and dispatch the engine call as a supervised background task (`asyncio.create_task` + strong-ref set + done-callback for GC safety + wrapping `try/except` that marks the row `failed` with `error_message` so failures are observable, not lost). The frontend `useAutoCreationExecution.runPipeline` hook polls `GET /api/auto-creation/executions/{id}` every 1s (30-min safety cap, `AbortSignal`-aware) and splices snapshots into the executions state for incremental UI updates. `fetchExecutions()` moved into a `finally` block so the list refreshes on both success and error paths. Per-rule endpoint now pre-validates rule existence (returns `404` instead of FK-violation `500`). With this change, `/api/auto-creation/` is removed from `_TIMEOUT_EXEMPT_PREFIXES` in `backend/main.py` — the 30s defense-in-depth that bd-zv6pi exempted as a hotfix is restored, and a regression test guards against re-introduction. Completes the follow-up flagged in the bd-zv6pi Fixed entry below (PR #134, bd-enfsy).
- `PUT /api/auto-creation/rules/{id}` and `POST /api/auto-creation/rules/bulk-update` now perform write-time foreign-key validation on `normalization_group_ids` against the `normalization_rule_groups` table. A submission referencing a missing or deleted group returns `422` with `detail.invalid_normalization_group_ids` listing every offending ID; previously the IDs were stored as-is and surfaced later as a silent "no normalization happened" runtime symptom. Validation runs after the rule lookup but before scalar mutations on PUT (no partial state on a bad ID), and after the rules-by-id fetch but before the per-rule mutation loop on bulk-update (single 422 rolls back the whole batch — verified by the integration test asserting zero journal entries + zero scalar mutations on rollback). Delta-on-write semantics: only IDs being newly submitted are validated, so future group deletions cannot retroactively break already-stored rules. POST `/api/auto-creation/rules` (create endpoint) has the same gap; tracked separately as bd-j5p4k for symmetric coverage (PR #145, bd-i75ax).
- `POST /api/auto-creation/rules` (create endpoint) now performs the same write-time foreign-key validation on `normalization_group_ids` as PUT and bulk-update — closes the symmetric coverage gap flagged in bd-i75ax. Same `_validate_normalization_group_ids` helper, same `422` shape with `detail.invalid_normalization_group_ids`. All three rule write paths (POST create, PUT update, POST bulk-update) now reject invalid normalization-group references at write-time instead of silently storing them (PR #150, bd-j5p4k).
- Auto-creation debug bundle now ships a `normalization_rules.yaml` file alongside `rules.yaml`. Every NormalizationRuleGroup is emitted in priority order with its rules nested (priority-ordered, with full condition/action fields and `tag_group_name` resolved). Group `id` is preserved so the cross-reference from `rules.yaml`'s `normalization_group_ids` resolves directly; numeric per-rule ids and timestamps are stripped to keep the YAML readable. `manifest.json` gains `normalization_group_count` and `normalization_rule_count` for at-a-glance counts. Without this, "normalization isn't stripping X" reports could not be diagnosed from the bundle alone — the bundle had the auto-creation rule referencing groups [1,2,5,6,7,8] but no record of what those groups actually did. Container smoke-test produced a 5,156-byte `normalization_rules.yaml` for a 5-group install. Closes the follow-up gap flagged on the bd-cns7j ship (`bd-cns7j` follow-up).
- The `ecm-mcp` sidecar now uses the modern **Streamable HTTP** transport at **`/mcp`** instead of the deprecated SSE transport at `/sse` + `/messages/`. This fixes GH #218 — `mcp-remote` (the bridge Claude Desktop uses) runs mandatory OAuth discovery before connecting, and the MCP Python SDK 1.27.0's SSE handler rejected that probe with a 421, so Claude Desktop couldn't connect; the Streamable HTTP transport handles it cleanly. The SDK's DNS-rebinding (Host-header) protection is disabled on the transport so the sidecar stays reachable from any host by IP or hostname — the static API key remains the access control. **Breaking for existing configs:** update any client pointing at `http://HOST:6101/sse?api_key=...` (or `"type": "sse"` in a `.mcp.json`) to `http://HOST:6101/mcp?api_key=...` (and `"type": "http"`); Claude Desktop now connects via `npx mcp-remote http://HOST:6101/mcp?api_key=... --allow-http`. API-key auth is unchanged (`?api_key=` query param or `Authorization: Bearer`). The `mcp` SDK pin is untouched (1.27.0 already supports Streamable HTTP). Thanks to @swarthyplacebo for the report and diagnosis (`bd-uvh0a`, GH #218).
- New auto-creation rules now default **`match_scope_target_group`** to **on** (was off). When a Create Channel action has `if_exists=merge` (or `merge_only`) and ECM finds an existing channel with a matching normalized name, the lookup is now restricted to the rule's target group by default — so the rule creates a new channel in its target group instead of merging into a same-name channel in some other group (the "channels updated, 0 created" footgun GH #226 hit). **Existing rules keep their stored value (off) — there is no migration; nothing changes for rules you already have.** Toggle the per-rule **Merge lookup scope** option off to restore the original all-groups lookup. The rule analyzer now emits a `MERGE_SCOPE_NOT_TARGET_GROUP` advisory (`info`) on existing rules that still have it off and use a merging Create Channel action, so you can decide whether the all-groups behavior is intentional (documented in `docs/channel_pipeline_rule_analyzer.md` and `docs/user_guide/channel-pipeline/debugging-rules.md`). Relates to GH-92 (the flag's original opt-in introduction). Thanks to @Koalamanx for the report (`bd-p6ko9`, GH #226).

### Fixed
- Alembic revision **`0005`** (`auto_creation_quality_m3u_tie_break`) — SQLite `upgrade head` no longer fails with **`CircularDependencyError`** when adding `quality_tie_break_order` and `quality_m3u_tie_break_enabled` in one batch (each column is added in its own `batch_alter_table`). If Alembic previously failed and startup fell back to **`create_all()`**, those columns can already exist while **`alembic_version`** stayed at **`0004`**; the migration now **introspects** the table and **skips** `add_column` for existing columns, avoiding **`duplicate column name`**. Downgrade **drops** each column only if present. Thanks to @stevencoutts for the fix (`enhancedchannelmanager-m2k7p`; original columns shipped with `enhancedchannelmanager-3j9su`).
- **Auto-creation `stop_processing` action** no longer terminates the entire Pass 2 loop. The `if stop_processing: break` at the end of the per-stream block in `AutoCreationEngine._process_streams` Pass 2 exited the whole `sorted_entries` loop, so the first stream whose winning rule had a `stop_processing` action ended Pass 2 for **all** remaining streams (`streams_matched=1` every run when any rule used the action). Changed to `continue`: Pass 1 already resolves exactly one winning rule per stream, so `STOP_PROCESSING` has no "further rules" left to stop in Pass 2 — it is now a per-stream no-op and all matched streams are processed. The inner per-action loop is unchanged (the action's own description is "stop processing further *rules*", not further actions of this rule). Regression test in `backend/tests/unit/test_auto_creation_engine.py` (`bd-iqm50`, GH #225).
- **MCP `update_channel` / `create_channel`** now send `channel_group_id` instead of `group_id`. The backend channel PATCH forwards keys straight to Dispatcharr (whose channel field is `channel_group_id`) and POST `/api/channels` uses `channel_group_id` — FastAPI/Dispatcharr silently dropped the unknown `group_id` key, so the tool returned `200` but the channel never moved groups. The tool's public `group_id` argument is unchanged; only the wire field was fixed. Tests in `mcp-server/tests/test_tools.py` (`bd-7q9l3`, GH #221).
- **MCP `list_auto_creation_rules`** no longer raises `str object has no attribute get`. `GET /api/auto-creation/rules` returns `{"rules": [...]}`; the tool iterated the dict's keys (`"rules"`, a string) and called `.get()` on it. Now unwraps the envelope defensively, matching the `analyze` tool in the same module. Test in `mcp-server/tests/test_tools.py` (`bd-pvw35`, GH #222).
- **MCP `bulk_add_streams_to_channel`** no longer times out on slow hardware for batches of ~10 streams. It used to fake a bulk op by looping `POST /api/channels/{id}/add-stream` once per stream — 2 Dispatcharr roundtrips × N. Added a real `POST /api/channels/{id}/add-streams` (plural) backend endpoint that fetches the channel once, appends every new stream (dedup, request order preserved), and PUTs once — one Dispatcharr roundtrip total — and wired the tool to it (with a generous 120 s timeout). Documented in `docs/api.md`. Router tests in `backend/tests/routers/test_channels.py`; tool test in `mcp-server/tests/test_tools.py` (`bd-02xjj`, GH #223).
- **MCP `bulk_commit_channels`** (and every `ECMClient` HTTP method) now surface the response body's `detail` on a `4xx`/`5xx` instead of a bare "HTTP 422". FastAPI's auto-validation `422` carries a structured `detail` list whose `loc` pinpoints the bad operation index and field; `ECMClient`'s `HTTPStatusError` handler discarded it. The bulk-commit operation schema (the `type` enum values and the fields each op uses) is now documented in `docs/api.md` under `POST /api/channels/bulk-commit`. Tests in `mcp-server/tests/test_tools.py` (`bd-mjtxn`, GH #224).
- **Normalization rules with Compound (OR) logic** no longer strip the entire channel name when the *first* condition mismatches but a later condition matches. `NormalizationEngine._match_compound_conditions` only captured the action's match span from condition 1 (the `i == 0` guard); for an OR rule where condition 1 missed and condition 2 hit, `primary_match` stayed `None`, fell back to `match_end = len(text)`, and a **Strip Prefix** / **Remove** / **Replace** action then operated on the whole string → blank channel name. Now the first *matching*, non-negated condition's span is used regardless of index — which also fixes the analogous AND-logic case where condition 1 is a negated guard. Repro from GH #217: rule Logic=OR, cond1=`Starts With VariantA` (mismatch), cond2=`Starts With VariantB` (match), action=`Strip Prefix` → channels matching `VariantB` became empty strings. New regression tests in `backend/tests/unit/test_normalization_compound_conditions.py`. Thanks to @swarthyplacebo for the report and the diagnosis (`bd-hssif`, GH #217).
- **Scheduled tasks:** enabling or disabling a task in the UI now persists to the database. `TaskRegistry._save_task_to_db` was restoring `instance._enabled` from the previous row and never writing the flag back, so `PATCH /api/tasks/{id}` had no effect after save or reload. **YAML Backup** was especially visible because it defaults to off (`default_enabled=False`). Unit test: `test_sync_to_database_persists_enabled_for_existing_task` (`bd-7k2n9`).
- Removed two unused imports from `backend/auto_creation_rule_analyzer.py` (`Any` from typing; `lint_pattern_advisory` from regex_lint). Both were imported speculatively while drafting the bd-0gntx Phase 1 module — the advisory regex linting goes through `lint_conditions_json_advisory` which calls `lint_pattern_advisory` internally, so the direct import is dead. No behavior change. Follow-up to the v0.16.0-0074 ship (`bd-0gntx` follow-up, bd-4c02g).
- Auto-creation debug bundle no longer fails with "Failed to generate debug bundle" on large catalogs. The previous `GET /api/auto-creation/debug-bundle` walked the full Dispatcharr catalog inline — ~150 sequential page fetches plus ~150 sequential stream-detail batches — and on a 15K-channel install (a single PPV rule pulling everything into one group) blew the 30s `ECM_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` budget so the response never reached the browser. Replaced with a 202+poll pair matching the bd-enfsy `/run` shape: `POST /api/auto-creation/debug-bundle` returns `{job_id, status: "running"}` immediately and dispatches a supervised background task; `GET /api/auto-creation/debug-bundle/{job_id}` returns JSON status while running, JSON `{status: "failed", error}` on failure, or the tar.gz `application/gzip` attachment when ready (job evicted on read; 30-min TTL prune for abandoned jobs). Inside the worker, page fetches and stream-detail batches now run via `asyncio.gather` with a concurrency-8 semaphore so even multi-thousand-channel catalogs finish in seconds. Frontend `autoCreationApi.ts` gains `startDebugBundle()` + `pollDebugBundle()` + `generateAndFetchDebugBundle()`; both `SettingsTab` and `AutoCreationTab` Debug Bundle handlers switched to the new helper. 7 new backend tests cover the 202 contract, status while running, completed download with eviction, failed-status surfacing, 404 unknown id, sub-5s enqueue under stuck worker, and TTL pruning; 6 new frontend tests cover enqueue, binary download, failed-status throw, 404 throw, running→completed flip, and the full enqueue→poll→download flow. Container smoke-test confirmed enqueue in 1.8ms and a 571-channel bundle delivered in 531ms (`bd-cns7j`).
- Auto-creation YAML export (`GET /api/auto-creation/export/yaml`) coerces **`quality_tie_break_order`** / **`quality_m3u_tie_break_enabled`** to plain `str`/`bool` before `yaml.dump`, so tests using `MagicMock` rule rows cannot leak non-serializable placeholders into the export (`enhancedchannelmanager-3j9su`).
- Settings sub-page memory leak (Channel Normalization / Channel Defaults) reduced ~2,400 KB/s → near-zero on idle. Follow-up to the render-loop fix in PR #209: with the loop fixed, a second leak family emerged from `@dnd-kit/core`'s `useRect()` hook, which attaches a `MutationObserver` to `document.body` with `subtree: true` for every mounted sortable element. With ~30 sortable rows mounted on Channel Normalization plus a heavy notification toast stream (reporter had 20 active "Export Publish" task-completion notifications driving the 2 s poller tier), every toast mount/unmount fired `childList` records to all 30+ observers — millions of `MutationRecord` instances retained, with `MutationRecord` dominating the heap-snapshot diff (+234 MB Size Delta over 30 s). Fix: sweep all 9 `<DndContext>` mount sites across 8 files (`SubstitutionPairsEditor`, `ChannelDetail`, `ChannelsPane` ×2, `NormalizationEngineSection` ×2, `EPGManagerTab`, `SettingsTab` ×2 in Channel Defaults) to gate the `<DndContext>` mount on a local `isReorderMode`/`isEditMode` toggle. Drag handles only render when the user enters reorder/edit mode (via a "Reorder" button per section, exited via "Done"); idle navigation does not mount any sortable observers. Browser-verified on Chromium: 0 `MutationObserver` instances on `#settings/normalization` idle (pre-fix: 19), 60 s heap slope −453 KB/s (pre-fix: +2,400 KB/s on reporter's Edge 147). All 1231 frontend tests pass; lint + typecheck clean. Reporter reverification on Edge 147 / Win 11 is the authoritative signal — Chromium-Playwright cannot reproduce the absolute leak rate due to scheduler differences (`bd-5xg17`, gh #207, follow-up to PR #209).
- Settings tab no longer leaks memory (reported up to 5 GB on Edge before browser pressure relief). Two changes break a cross-component render loop that armed when the `ecm:open-task-editor` sessionStorage key was set (NotificationCenter writes it on Edit-Schedule clicks; ScheduledTasksSection only clears it after the matching task loads, so a stale or unmatched taskId stranded the key indefinitely): (1) the `useEffect` in `SettingsTab.tsx` that consumes the key was missing a dependency array and therefore re-ran every render — adding `[]` deps confines it to mount, breaking the loop driver; (2) `useHashRoute.setHash` always allocated a fresh `HashRoute` object literal and called `pushState` + `setRoute` even when the route was unchanged, so any caller invoking it with the same value (e.g., the buggy effect calling `setActivePage('scheduled-tasks')` with `activePage === 'scheduled-tasks'`) churned `history.length` and re-rendered every iteration — `setHash` now bails out when `tab` and `settingsPage` match the current route. Same family as the recharts Stats loop in `bd-a7l1z`; latent under React 18 batching, runaway under React 19.2.5 concurrent rendering. Browser verification with the trigger primed: 7 settings sub-page navigations produced exactly 7 history entries (pre-fix: tens of thousands within seconds), heap stable across idle and navigation (`bd-5xg17`, gh #207).
- Scheduled task email alerts now deliver via the SMTP Email alert method (recipients in `to_emails`) instead of being a no-op, and Notification Settings now exposes an "Email Alert Recipients" field to configure those recipients in the UI (`bd-bq27w`, `bd-lfmuw`).
- Scheduled task **success** emails (and shared alert metadata) no longer label **Auto-Create Channels** and **Stream Probe** with generic “Total Items” / “Success Count” that disagreed with the execution UI (e.g. tens of thousands “items” vs dozens of channel changes). `task_engine.py` now emits task-specific fields (streams evaluated/matched, channels created/updated, groups, conflicts, execution id / pipeline mode for auto-creation; streams scheduled/ok/failed/skipped and black-screen/low-FPS counts for probe) and human-readable success lines aligned with `TaskResult.message` / probe totals. `tasks/stream_probe.py` adds quality counters to `TaskResult.details`; `stream_prober.py` final external alert uses the same naming. Unit tests in `test_task_notification_formatting.py` (`enhancedchannelmanager-e3ts6`).
- Auto-creation `/run` and `/rules/{id}/run` no longer return `504 Gateway Timeout` on large catalogs. The bd-w3z4h request-timeout middleware (30s default) did not exempt `/api/auto-creation/`, so pipeline runs that exceeded 30s were cancelled client-side even though the work continued to completion server-side (the engine commits as it progresses). Added `/api/auto-creation/` to `_TIMEOUT_EXEMPT_PREFIXES` in `backend/main.py` alongside the other inherently long-running prefixes (`/api/tasks/`, `/api/backup/`, `/api/ffmpeg/`). Secondary symptom — the Execution History panel not auto-refreshing until browser reload — resolves with this fix because the frontend's post-run `fetchExecutions()` now runs on the success path instead of being skipped by the error. Follow-up refactor to convert `/run` to an async enqueue + polling pattern tracked as bd-enfsy (bd-zv6pi).
- Auto-creation no longer renumbers foreign-group channels that were matched via PR #107's normalized-name fallback. A rule with `sort_field` set would pick up any pre-existing channel found via the new normalized-search path — even channels owned by a different rule — and Pass 3 (channel renumber) would then reassign their channel numbers to fit its own sort. Fix: gate the Pass 3 append so a rule only tracks channels it created this run OR that were already in its pre-run managed set; cross-rule matches via fallback are still merged/skipped correctly but no longer trigger a foreign renumber (bd-yj5yi, GH-104 regression from PR #107).
- Auto-creation now strips letter-superscripts (ᴿᴬᵂ → RAW, ᴴᴰ → HD) from channel names it creates, while still preserving numeric superscripts (ESPN² stays ESPN²). PR #61 added a `preserve_superscripts=True` flag to `normalize()` that short-circuited ALL superscript conversion — including letter-superscripts used as quality-tag cruft — so auto-creation output retained `ᴿᴬᵂ`/`ᴴᴰ` even when Settings → Normalization's "Test" button stripped them. Fix splits the internal superscript map into `LETTER_SUPERSCRIPTS` (always converted) and `NUMERIC_SUPERSCRIPTS` (preserved when flag set); `convert_superscripts()` gains a `preserve_numeric` argument; the `preserve_superscripts` call-site in `normalize()` now routes through the letter-only path. Backward-compatible: the public `SUPERSCRIPT_MAP` still exists as the union of both (bd-yui1k, supersedes coarser PR #61 behavior).
- Stream probing now works on HTTPS streams. The ffprobe/ffmpeg `-protocol_whitelist` was missing `tls` (and `crypto`), so every HTTPS `.ts`/`.m3u8` source failed with `[tls @ ...] Protocol 'tls' not on whitelist ...!` and `Invalid argument`. Added `tls,crypto` to the whitelist in `backend/stream_prober.py`, `backend/ffmpeg_builder/probe.py`, and `backend/routers/stream_preview.py`. Neither is a user-supplied URL scheme (they are internal demuxers chained from `https://` / encrypted HLS), so the existing threat model against `file://`, `data:`, `concat:`, `subfile:`, etc. is preserved. New regression tests lock `tls` and `crypto` into the whitelist and assert dangerous schemes stay out (GH-106, bd-4awsl).
- Auto-creation rule duplicate preserves stream/channel sort, normalization groups, orphan cleanup, and option toggles — duplicates now match the original's configuration instead of silently dropping fields (PR #94, external contributor stevencoutts, bd-x3lto).
- Auto-creation no longer creates duplicate channels when an existing channel's name differs only by a normalization-matched suffix (e.g., "RTL ᴿᴬᵂ" and incoming "RTL"). Root causes: normalization engine wasn't initialized unless at least one rule opted in via normalize_names=True, and the channel-lookup map only indexed stored-name → normalized. Fix: always initialize engine when any NormalizationRuleGroup is enabled; add symmetric normalized-search + core-name fallbacks in _find_channel_by_name (PR #107, bd-u9odj, GH-104).
- Frontend test `NormalizationEngineSection.applyToChannels.test.tsx` no longer hangs in CI — the `useNotifications` test mock returned a fresh object on every call, which invalidated the component's `loadData` `useCallback` and triggered an infinite re-render loop that kept the test stuck in the loading state. Mock now returns a stable singleton (PR #107, bd-u9odj).
- Auto-Creation Pass 3.5 stream reorder now runs on channels modified via `merge_stream`/`merge_streams_prune` — previously silent when Channels Created = 0, leaving streams unsorted after a pure-merge run. (PR #113, enhancedchannelmanager-f55hm, gh-113)
- Auto-Creation name-based stream sort now works when probe stats are unavailable — the reorder path now seeds stream names from the channel's stream payload so natural-order and alphabetic sort have something to sort on. (PR #113, enhancedchannelmanager-f55hm, gh-113)
- Auto-Creation quality (resolution) sort now honors `deprioritize_failed_streams` — black-screen / failed / low-fps streams no longer promote to the top ahead of working higher-resolution streams. (PR #113, enhancedchannelmanager-f55hm, gh-113)
- `journal_entries` table now has the indexes its access patterns require. bd-91mcq introduced per-entity journal rows with a shared `batch_id` for bulk auto-creation operations, but the table only had indexes on `timestamp DESC`, `category`, and `action_type` — so the forensic "show me everything in batch X" lookup full-scanned, and "history for entity N" queries fell back on the timestamp index. Adds Alembic revision `0004` creating `idx_journal_batch_id` on `(batch_id)` and `idx_journal_entity` on `(category, entity_id, timestamp DESC)`; mirrors them in the SQLAlchemy model so model + migration stay in sync (drift test confirms). Also raises `CleanupTask.journal_days` default from `30` → `90` to align with `journal.purge_old_entries`'s existing 90-day default — bulk operations now amplify journal row volume N-fold, and the inconsistent retention default would have masked unbounded table growth. CleanupTask was already wired into the scheduler; no integration changes required (PR #133, bd-dmu8w).
- Stats tab no longer crashes with React error #185 (Maximum update depth exceeded) on every load. Recharts 3.x bundles `react-redux` + `@reduxjs/toolkit` for internal state; under React 19's stricter concurrent rendering, the chart's `YAxis` → `RenderedTicksReporter` `useEffect` re-dispatched `setRenderedTicks` with a fresh `ticks` array reference on every render, which forced a re-render that re-ran the effect, looping until React aborted with #185 inside the ErrorBoundary scoped to the Stats tab. The trigger was the React 18.3.1 → 19.2.5 bump in `ca6e5019` (bd-in620, 2026-04-24); the recharts 3.7.0 → 3.8.1 bump in `62ea5c5f` (2026-04-16) seeded `RenderedTicksReporter` itself, but the loop only manifested under React 19. Pin `recharts` to `2.15.4` — the latest 2.x line, declares React 19 support in peerDeps, and pre-dates the redux refactor entirely. Our chart usage (`LineChart`/`AreaChart`/`BarChart` + `XAxis`/`YAxis` + `Tooltip` + `ReferenceLine` + `ResponsiveContainer`) is all stable across the 2.x→3.x boundary; tsc clean and 110 stats panel tests pass against 2.15.4 (bd-a7l1z).

### Security
- `backend/routers/backup.py` — canonicalize-and-verify (`resolve()` + `relative_to()`) applied to `download_saved_backup` and `delete_saved_backup` as defense-in-depth alongside the existing `_BACKUP_FILENAME_RE` guard. Closes CodeQL py/path-injection alerts 1416-1419 (CWE-22/23/36/73/99). Pattern mirrors the zip-restore remediation in the same file. New tests cover traversal, absolute paths, symlink-escape, and null bytes (PR #98, bd-0a1pr).
- `backend/cloud_storage/onedrive_adapter.py` — Pydantic + adapter-level validators on `tenant_id` (GUID or verified domain) and `drive_id` (base64url-style) prevent SSRF via Microsoft Graph URL interpolation. Closes CodeQL CRITICAL py/partial-ssrf alerts 1361, 1362 (CWE-918). New tests at `backend/tests/test_cloud_storage.py` cover validator unit coverage + HTTP 422 at the cloud-target API (PR #101, bd-zbt74).
- `backend/auto_creation_schema.py` — three write-time `re.compile(<user_input>)` sites (condition regex validation, set_variable pattern validation, name_transform_pattern validation) now route through `safe_regex.compile`, which enforces a length cap and uniform compile-error surface. User-controlled regex from auto-creation rule definitions can no longer pin a worker via crafted ReDoS patterns. The bd-91mcq auto-creation bulk merges expanded request volume through these paths; this hardening closes the exposure window. Removes the three `# nosemgrep: no-bare-re-on-dynamic-pattern` annotations + their `TODO(enhancedchannelmanager-ltjyx)` markers. Backward-compatible: error message substrings (`"Invalid regex"`, `"Invalid name_transform_pattern"`) preserved for existing test/UI assertions. New `TestSafeRegexMigrationWriteTimeValidation` (10 tests) covers oversize-pattern rejection at all three sites + boundary-at-cap acceptance (PR #136, bd-ltjyx).
- `backend/routers/m3u_digest.py` — two write-time `re.compile(<user_input>)` sites validating `exclude_group_patterns` and `exclude_stream_patterns` from request bodies now route through `safe_regex.compile`. Same ReDoS class as bd-ltjyx, different surface (M3U digest endpoint instead of auto-creation rule endpoint). Removes the two `# nosemgrep` annotations. New tests in `TestUpdateDigestSettings` cover oversize-pattern rejection on both fields plus a `TestRouterUsesSafeRegex` source-level revert guard (PR #135, bd-3u6p0).
- Eight CodeQL py/stack-trace-exposure HIGH alerts (1412-1415, 1350-1353) remediated across `backend/routers/{backup,channels,export,health,settings}.py` + `backend/cloud_storage/`. Pattern: `logger.exception(...)` for full trace correlated by `X-Request-ID` + return only the exception class name (`type(e).__name__`) in the API response — never `str(e)` (which can include connection strings, file paths, or credentials). For `ImportError` paths in cloud_storage adapters, `e.name` (the missing module name) is used so the response stays useful without leaking interpreter paths. **Material risk closed**: alert 1414 in `health.py` — `/api/health/ready` is in `AUTH_EXEMPT_PATHS`, meaning DBAPI exception messages were echoed unauthenticated; this PR closes that. ADR-005 Phase 1 disallows "won't fix" dismissal so every open stack-trace alert needed a real fix. Six new sanitization tests + 1 updated, each asserting both the positive contract (response equals exception class) and the negative contract (the secret-bearing message body MUST NOT appear in the response) (PR #160, bd-m8i9q).
- `backend/export_manager.py` + `backend/routers/export.py` — defense-in-depth canonicalize-and-verify backstop for path traversal in the M3U/XMLTV export download paths. New `_safe_export_path(profile_id, *parts)` helper resolves and verifies containment under `EXPORTS_DIR.resolve()` via `Path.relative_to()`, raising `ExportPathError` on escape. Wired into `ExportManager.get_export_path` and `ExportManager.cleanup` (cleanup short-circuits before `rmtree` on escape). Also re-verifies path containment in `download_m3u`/`download_xmltv` before serving. CodeQL py/path-injection alerts 1354-1359 were already dismissed as false-positive (FastAPI int path-param + Pydantic `FILENAME_RE` allowlist make traversal unreachable upstream), but per ADR-005 sequencing 2 and the bd-0a1pr precedent the property is now guaranteed at the sink rather than depending on caller hygiene. New `TestExportPathInjection` (9 tests) mirrors `TestSavedBackupsPathInjection` from bd-0a1pr — traversal payloads, absolute paths, null-byte, backslash, cleanup sentinel-file regression, symlink-escape (PR #159, bd-h5rfv).

### Documentation
- `README.md` MCP setup section — disambiguates `api_key` (Dispatcharr REST token) from `mcp_api_key` (ECM MCP key) with a callout table, explicit "do not touch `api_key`" guidance in the key-rotation flow, a note clarifying that the `?api_key=` URL query parameter in client configs is the `mcp_api_key` value, and a cross-reference to the `/health` `api_key_status` diagnostic for post-rotation troubleshooting. Closes GH #273 (`bd-6gm3l`).
- `docs/api.md` — `POST /api/auto-creation/rules/bulk-update` now documents three previously-undocumented behaviors that shipped in earlier merges: the rejected-fields contract (`conditions` and `actions` return 422 per bd-gjoe5), the single-fetch response shape `{rules, updated_count}` (bd-bh1hh), and the `batch_id` correlation primitive — every bulk operation writes N per-entity journal rows sharing one 8-char `batch_id` (bd-91mcq), retrievable today via direct `journal_entries.batch_id` lookup against `idx_journal_batch_id` (bd-dmu8w; a `?batch_id=` query param on `GET /api/journal` is tracked as bd-s4sph for a future release). Also adds a Journal-section paragraph documenting the actual `GET /api/journal` filter set. Surfaces two write-time validation gaps as separate beads (bd-s4sph + bd-i75ax). Style matches existing inline-narrative blocks (PR #139, bd-pf82j).

## [0.16.0-yanked] — 2026-04-20 (rolled back)

**This build was rolled back** before any external consumer pulled the tag. The GitHub Release, git tag, and GHCR image for `0.16.0` were deleted; `:latest` was retagged back to the `v0.15.2` multi-arch index. Rollback executed per `bd-vgm4l`; see `docs/runbooks/v0.16.0-rollback.md` for the procedure. The PO chose rollback over a forward hotfix because open P0/P1 bugs needed to clear before a release could ship. The work originally cut here re-ships in the **[0.16.0] — 2026-05-12** release above, with the blocking bugs fixed.

## [0.15.2] — 2026-04-16

### Security
- Upgraded `vite` out of the vulnerable 7.0.0–7.3.1 range, resolving three high-severity advisories affecting the frontend dev tooling:
  - [GHSA-4w7w-66w2-5vf9](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-4w7w-66w2-5vf9) — path traversal in optimized-deps `.map` handling.
  - [GHSA-v2wj-q39q-566r](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-v2wj-q39q-566r) — `server.fs.deny` bypass with queries.
  - [GHSA-p9ff-h696-f583](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-p9ff-h696-f583) — arbitrary file read via dev server WebSocket.
- No runtime code changes; only the frontend build tooling is affected. Dev server users should upgrade promptly.

## [0.15.1] — 2026-03-31

### Added
- Login rate limiting — 5 attempts per minute per IP via slowapi on both local and Dispatcharr login endpoints.
- Security headers: `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`.
- Client IP in login-failure logs (includes `X-Forwarded-For` for forensics).

### Changed
- **Password policy now follows NIST 800-63B** — dropped composition rules (uppercase/lowercase/number), replaced the 33-word list with the 10k common-passwords list, uniform 8-character minimum for all users including admin-created.
- CORS tightened — explicit method and header allow-lists instead of wildcards.

### Fixed
- EPG matching: channel names with a number prefix (e.g. `535 | ESPN`) were normalizing to `535espn` instead of `espn`, causing zero matches against EPG data.

### Security
- Injection / SSRF (OWASP A03/A10):
  - ffmpeg protocol allow-list on all invocations — blocks `file://`, `data://`, `concat:` and other dangerous protocols.
  - URL-scheme validation at input time on M3U, Xtream Codes, and EPG source endpoints — only `http://` and `https://` allowed.
  - Zip backup path canonicalization — defense-in-depth `resolve()` check alongside existing traversal guards.
- Logging hardening (OWASP A09):
  - 500-response scrubbing — global handler returns generic `Internal server error` to clients; real error stays in server logs.
  - Validation-error log redaction — `Authorization` / `Cookie` headers and auth-endpoint request bodies redacted from logs.

## [0.15.0] — 2026-03-29

### Added
- **Export & Publish** — generate M3U/XMLTV playlists from channel profiles and publish to cloud targets (S3, WebDAV, etc.) on a cron schedule. Includes channel selector, playlist preview, cloud-target management, and publish history.
- **Low-FPS detection** — streams below a configurable threshold (5/10/15/20 FPS) are flagged with an amber icon and deprioritized in Smart Sort. Zero-overhead, always-on, configurable in Settings → Maintenance.
- **Black-screen detection** — optional ffmpeg `signalstats` check after each probe flags dark/blank streams with a purple icon. Configurable sample duration (3–30s) in Settings → Maintenance.
- **Normalize Names engine** — tag-based rules engine for cleaning up stream names during bulk channel creation. YAML import/export, edit/revert controls, drag-and-drop priority ordering.
- **Backup & Restore** — full configuration backup/restore from Settings, with restore option during the first-run setup wizard.
- **Merge Channels** — combine two or more channels into one, consolidating their streams.
- Auto-creation enhancements — EPG logo-source action, Probe Streams action, Set Channel Profile action; Smart Sort option in rule sort dropdown; rule selection in Auto-Create schedule; separate stream sort from channel sort per rule.
- PUID/PGID support for Docker container user identity.
- TV Guide print view.
- JWT authentication in Swagger UI with `/swagger` redirect.
- Debug bundle for troubleshooting auth issues.

### Changed
- Server-side migration of EPG matching, stream normalization, and edit consolidation (previously client-side).
- Settings UI consistency — compact dropdowns, unified admin section CSS, proper field ordering.
- Scoped reprobe to last scheduled probe's channel groups.
- Capped Dispatcharr HTTP client connection pool.
- All GitHub Actions updated to Node.js 24-compatible versions.
- Migrated 161 `console.log` call sites to the structured logger.

### Fixed
- Persistent 503 when stream prober not initialized — prober now self-heals and auto-creates.
- `assign_logo` from EPG and duplicate rule priorities.
- Radio buttons, undefined CSS variables, and card backgrounds in admin tabs.
- Push-down renumbering, menu overflow, task-card grid.

### Removed
- ~2,000+ lines of dead code across backend and frontend, including duplicate `formatDate` implementations.

### Security
- Secured backup endpoints; improved token handling.
- Fixed npm audit vulnerabilities in `brace-expansion`, `flatted`, `picomatch`.

## [0.14.0] — 2026-03-06

Highlights: black-screen detection during probes; failed-stream re-probe scheduled task; auto-creation per-rule `skip_struck_streams`, provider order, and channel-number sort; pattern-builder UI for normalization rules; configurable HTTP/HTTPS ports via env vars. Numerous auto-creation and notification-center bug fixes. Full notes: [GitHub Release v0.14.0](https://github.com/MotWakorb/enhancedchannelmanager/releases/tag/v0.14.0).
